===Original===
To

Frances Brincklerbee

===Chinese===
献给

弗朗西丝·布林克勒比（Frances Brincklerbee）

## I / I

===Original===
As she lay floating in the grey river that flows between sleeping and waking, Maggie Campion knew, without remembering why, that it was a happy day. And when she opened her eyes, the sunlight falling on the carpet in stripes of pale warm gold, the warm buff walls, even the fat little buff potichomanie flagons with their crimson rosebuds, all held a secret happiness﻿—what was it? The looped-back muslin curtains were like ladies in billowing white, curtseying to each other, two in each window, and even Maggie’s stout scuffed little shoes on the floor where she had left them when she undressed, pointed their toes in the first position of dancing.
She lay pressing her hands together under the blankets, floating in this still bright bliss. She remembered now what it was. It was Papa’s birthday, and he was coming home from New York.

===Chinese===
她躺在那条流过睡乡与醒境的灰色河流里，随波漂浮，玛吉·坎皮恩（Maggie Campion）还没想起缘由，就知道今天是快乐的一天。待她睁开眼睛，一道道浅淡暖金色的阳光落在地毯上，暖米黄色的墙壁，连那对矮墩墩的米黄色彩釉仿瓷（potichomanie）小壶——上头描着绯红的玫瑰花苞——也都各怀一分隐秘的欢喜。是什么呢？挽起的平纹纱窗帘像一位位身穿蓬蓬白裙的淑女，正在互相屈膝行礼，每扇窗口两位；连玛吉睡前脱衣裳时丢在地板上的那双粗实磨破的小鞋，鞋尖也各自朝外，摆出舞蹈的第一姿势。

她把手掌合在一起压在毯子底下，漂浮在这一片安静明亮的至福里。她这会儿想起来了。今天是爸爸（Papa）的生日，他要从纽约（New York）回来了。


===Original===
Maggie was ten years old, with light eyes looking out from under dark scowling brows, brown hair that fell straight and limp from curling-rags even as they were unrolled, and a face covered with freckles. She was more like a boy than a girl, everyone said. She was always carrying hop-toads about in her hat, or tearing her petticoats climbing trees and sliding down the ice house roof. She was sometimes as bold as brass and sometimes one crimson blush of shyness, and she had the strangest ways of showing people that she loved them﻿—boasting in front of them in a loud gruff voice, making awful faces, twisting one leg around the other, or standing on the sides of her feet.
Six-year-old May slept beside her in the big bed carved with oak-leaves and acorns, under the picture of the guardian angel hovering above the little brother and sister gathering wild flowers at the edge of the precipice. May had short bright brown curls foaming all over her head, and brown eyes with long curled lashes, and she knew perfectly well what Mamma’s friends meant when they exclaimed, “Oh, *what* a little b-e-a-u-t-y!” She loved her pretty clothes, and never tore them as Maggie did, but would stroke her small muff or her best blue sash as another little girl might stroke a kitten; and Mamma had been dreadfully troubled once to find her kissing her best bonnet goodnight. When May loved people she told them so, flinging her arms around them and kissing them again and again, which they found at first charming and presently exhausting, for she never knew when to stop, and always had to be disentangled, like a burr or a kitten, and carried weeping from the room.

===Chinese===
玛吉十岁，浅色的眼睛从乌黑紧锁的眉骨底下望出来，一头棕发哪怕刚拆掉卷发纸，也照样直塌塌地垂着，脸上缀满雀斑。人人都说她像男孩，不像女孩。她不是把蟾蜍装在帽子里四处提溜，就是爬树蹭破了衬裙，或是顺着冰窖屋顶出溜下来。她有时胆大包天，有时又害羞得满脸涨成一片绯红；她向人表露喜爱的方式古怪得很——当着人家的面粗声大气地自吹自擂，做吓人的鬼脸，一条腿缠着另一条腿拧麻花，或是踩着自己双脚的外侧站着。

六岁的梅（May）睡在她身旁那张雕着橡树叶和橡子的大床上，床头挂着一幅画：守护天使翱翔在悬崖边采野花的小兄妹头顶。梅一头短短的亮棕色卷发，满脑袋翻腾如泡沫，棕色的眼睛配着长长的卷睫毛；妈妈（Mamma）的朋友们惊叹“哎呀，真是个小—美—人—儿！”时，她心里清楚得很这话是什么意思。她爱惜自己漂亮的衣裳，从不像玛吉那样撕破它们，还会抚摩自己的小手筒或最体面的蓝腰带，那样子就像别的小姑娘抚摩小猫；有一回妈妈发现她睡前对着自己最好的那顶软帽亲晚安吻，简直愁坏了。梅爱谁就对谁说，张开胳膊搂住人家，亲了又亲，起初人人觉得她招人疼，不多时便吃不消，因为她从来不知道何时该住手，末了总得像摘苍耳、逮小猫似的把她从人家身上解下来，哭着抱出屋去。


===Original===
Sometimes, when there was company of an evening, Papa would pick her up out of bed and carry her downstairs in her nightgown to dance on the top of the piano, while Mamma played, not quite accurately, but with a lot of ripple and splash, and Papa sang in the voice that pierced so thrillingly the heart of his eldest daughter, lying awake in the dark:

> “ ‘Sound, sound, the tambourine,
 

 Welcome now the gipsy star;
 

 Strike, strike the mandoline,
 

 And the light guitar;
 

 When the moon is beaming bright,
 

 The gipsies dance, the gipsies dance;
 

 ’Neath the moonbeams’ glittering ray,
 

 Now their figures glance.
 

 See, see, they trip along,
 

 O’er the green, o’er the green,
 

 List, list, the cheerful song,
 

 To the merry, merry, merry, merry, merry, merry, merry,
 

 merry, merry, merry, merry, merry,
 

 tambourine!’ ”

And excited little May would hold up her long nightie and dance, while the company applauded. But she generally ended in tears. “May is very high-strung,” Mamma would say, gently complacent.

===Chinese===
有时晚上家里来了客，爸爸会把她从床上抱起来，让她穿着睡袍下楼，站到钢琴顶上去跳舞；妈妈弹琴，弹得不算太准，却满是涟漪与浪花；爸爸唱起歌来，那嗓音刺得他那躺在黑暗里醒着的大女儿心头一阵阵激颤：

> “ ‘敲响吧，敲响铃鼓，
> 迎接那吉普赛之星；
> 弹响吧，弹响曼陀林，
> 还有那轻巧的六弦琴；
> 趁月华皎皎明亮，
> 吉普赛人起舞，吉普赛人起舞；
> 在月光的粼粼银辉里，
> 身影儿翩翩回翔。
> 看哪，看哪，他们款款而行，
> 过青茵，过青茵，
> 听哪，听哪，歌声多么欢欣，
> 献给那欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、
> 欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、欢快的、
> 铃鼓！’ ”

兴奋的小梅于是提起长长的睡袍跳起舞来，客人们鼓掌喝彩。但她多半要以哭收场。“梅这孩子，神经太娇了，”妈妈总这么说，语气温柔而自得。


===Original===
Lily, who was four, lay in a cot beside the big bed, as fat and fast asleep as a milk-white kitten. Her hair was palest silky yellow, curling up in little duck-tails from her fat neck, and her round eyes, so tight shut now, were like Mamma’s, as blue as flower petals. She trotted through childhood’s endless days on fat legs that could never catch up with Maggie and May, calling always, “Wait! Wait! Wait for Lily!”
Maggie, lying there, heard from below the swish, swish of Albert’s broom, sweeping the porch; the squeak of the pump-handle as old Chloe filled the kettle; Trusty barking at the starlings. The beautiful day had begun.
The three little Campions spent the morning in the kitchen, drawn by the smell of baking cake, as bees are drawn to apple-blossoms, being stepped on and bumped into, stealing almonds, scraping icing-bowls and licking the sugary, buttery batter from the wooden cake spoons, until old Chloe shooed them out as if they were chickens.

===Chinese===
四岁的莉莉（Lily）睡在大床边的一张小床上，胖乎乎的，睡得正沉，像一只乳白的小猫。她的头发是极淡的、丝一般的黄，从胖脖颈那儿翘起一撮撮小鸭尾；那双圆圆的眼睛——此刻闭得紧紧的——生得像妈妈，蓝得像花瓣。她迈着两条胖腿，一路小跑着打发童年那些没完没了的日子，永远也追不上玛吉和梅，嘴里总在喊：“等等我！等等我！等等莉莉！”

玛吉躺在床上，听见楼下传来艾伯特（Albert）扫帚扫廊子的刷刷声，老克洛艾（Chloe）灌水壶时水泵把手的吱呀声，还有特拉斯蒂（Trusty，意为“忠心”）冲着椋鸟汪汪吠叫。美好的一天开始了。

坎皮恩家三个小的整个上午都泡在厨房里，叫烤蛋糕的香味勾着，就像蜜蜂叫苹果花勾着一样；她们被人踩来踩去、撞来撞去，偷杏仁，刮糖霜碗，舔木蛋糕勺上又甜又油的生面糊，直到老克洛艾像轰小鸡一样把她们轰出去。


===Original===
Dressed alike in brown merino frocks and bibbed black aprons edged with quilling, with long tucked drawers showing beneath their full skirts, they sat squeezed together in the door of the kitchen shed, eating the hot little try-cakes with which old Chloe tested the oven. At their feet, on the rose-red bricks, silky black Trusty sat and watched each vanishing mouthful with drooling jaws and an agony of longing in his eyes. The pale spring sunshine lay on them delicately warm; starlings, shining and black as wet ink, swayed in the tree tops; and the weeping willow, hanging over the whitewashed cabins across the road where the negro servants slept, was turning brightest yellow green. The small hot cakes with their crisp brown lace-like edgings were so delicious. Oh, everything was so nice! And Papa was coming home!
Great things were being done inside, for Uncle Willie and poor Aunt Priscilla and Cousin Lizzie and Cousin Sam were coming to dinner because it was Papa’s birthday.

===Chinese===
三个女孩穿得一模一样：棕色美利奴呢裙，围涎式黑围裙，滚着细褶边，鼓鼓的裙子底下露出打密褶的长衬裤。她们挤坐在厨房披屋门口，吃着老克洛艾试炉子的小热饼。脚下玫瑰红的砖地上，坐着黑缎子似的特拉斯蒂，淌着口水，眼巴巴看着一口口下肚的饼，眼里满是苦苦的渴望。春日淡淡的阳光照在她们身上，暖得轻柔；椋鸟黑得发亮，像湿墨似的在树梢间摇荡；路对面白灰墙的小屋——黑仆们睡觉的地方——上方那株垂柳，正泛出最鲜亮的黄绿色。脆边烤得焦黄、赛过花边的小热饼多好吃呀。啊，样样都这么好！爸爸就要回家了！

屋里正办着大事，因为今天是爸爸的生日，威利叔叔（Uncle Willie）、可怜的普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）、丽齐表姐（Cousin Lizzie）和萨姆表哥（Cousin Sam）都要来吃晚饭。


===Original===
“Do you *really* think you ought to have us, Margaret?” Cousin Lizzie had asked. “In your condition?” For Mamma was expecting another baby in two months.
It seemed to Mamma not quite﻿—well﻿—*delicate*﻿—of Lizzie to keep reminding her of her condition. She herself never spoke of it except reluctantly in answer to Papa. There was some excuse for him, he wanted a son so intensely, and then gentlemen were different. It was a fact that complicated life, but could not be denied. But Lizzie, with her sharp eyes and sharp tongue, was dreadfully embarrassing.
“I’ll give you just what we’d have ourselves, Lizzie.” Mamma had lied gently; and she wrote out the menu and carried it about tucked into her bodice like a love letter.
“Mock turtel soup, boiled turky with oyster sauce, roasted ham, chicken-pie, roast goose with applesauce, smoke-tongue, beets, cold-slaw, squash, salsify, fried celery, almond pudding, mince pie, calf’s foot jelly, blancmange.”

===Chinese===
“你真觉得该请我们来吗，玛格丽特？”丽齐表姐当时问，“你眼下的身子，也行吗？”妈妈再有两个月就要添小宝宝了。

在妈妈看来，丽齐一而再、再而三地提她有身子的事，未免有点不——唔——知趣。这种事她自己从来不说，除非爸爸问起，才勉勉强强答上一句。爸爸倒还情有可原，他盼儿子盼得那么切，再说男人们又不一样。这是个把日子搅复杂了、却否认不掉的事实。可丽齐，眼睛尖，舌头也尖，实在叫人难堪极了。

“我给你们备的，就跟我们自己平日吃的一个样，丽齐。”妈妈温温柔柔地撒了个谎；她把菜单写出来，掖在胸口衣襟里，走到哪儿带到哪儿，像掖着一封情书。

“素甲鱼汤，牡蛎汁煮火鸡，烤火腿，鸡肉派，苹果酱烧鹅，熏牛舌，甜菜根，凉拌菜丝，南瓜，婆罗门参，炸芹菜，杏仁布丁，碎果甜派，小牛蹄冻，牛奶冻。”


===Original===
There was a beautiful cut-paper trouser-frill for the roasted ham, and the crust of the chicken-pie, meltingly, tenderly brown, was ornamented with pie-crust stars and squiggles. As for the blancmange, the little girls had never seen anything so charming. It had been moulded in blown eggshells, and lay in a nest of clear amber jelly and lemon peel cut in thin strips to look like straw.
Of course, today of all days, poor Aunt Priscilla had to come to help, and that always delayed things so. She came with her beautiful Cashmere shawl all huddled about her round shoulders, and her hair spraying out of torn places in her net, and her shabby old Adelaide boots that drove Uncle Willie nearly crazy. He wanted her to dress fashionably, and she *couldn’t*, no matter how hard she tried. She used to tell Mamma she couldn’t, sitting and eating a piece of cake or drinking wild cherry bounce, while the tears trickled down her cheeks.
Now, when poor Priscilla appeared, Mamma said, “Botheration!” softly, under her breath; but she didn’t really mind, for Priscilla was the only one of Papa’s relations who made her feel quick and clever and sure of herself; and she moved twice as briskly, with an important little frown, as soft as a wrinkle in cream, between her eyebrows, after Priscilla came.
But she had to get her out of the kitchen, for old Chloe’s puckered black face was getting crosser every minute. So they went into the conservatory to cut some flowers for Papa’s welcoming.

===Chinese===
烤火腿配着一圈漂亮的剪纸花边，形如裤脚的镶褶；鸡肉派的酥皮，焦黄酥嫩得要化了，上面用派皮缀着星星和弯弯绕绕的花纹。至于那道牛奶冻，小姑娘们从没见过这么招人喜欢的东西。它是用吹空的蛋壳成形的，卧在一窝清亮的琥珀冻里，四周码着切成细条的柠檬皮，扮作稻草。

当然啦，偏偏挑今天，可怜的普丽西拉婶婶非得来帮忙不可，而她一来总要耽搁事。她来的时候，那条漂亮的克什米尔披肩胡乱裹在圆肩膀上，发网破了洞，头发从洞眼里支棱出来，脚上蹬一双寒酸的旧阿德莱德靴——威利叔叔简直叫这双靴子逼得要发疯。他要她穿得时髦，可她再怎么使劲也办不到。她常对妈妈说自己实在办不到，一边坐着吃块蛋糕，或是喝点野樱桃浸酒，眼泪顺着腮帮子一串串往下淌。

如今只要可怜的普丽西拉一露面，妈妈就会压着嗓子轻轻说一声“真要命！”；可她心里并不真嫌，因为在爸爸这边的亲眷里，唯有普丽西拉能让她觉得自己手脚麻利、脑子灵光、有主见；普丽西拉来过之后，她走动起来要比平时利落一倍，两眉之间蹙起一道郑重其事的小皱，那皱褶柔和得像奶油上的一道纹。

不过她还是得把她从厨房里请出去，因为老克洛艾那张皱巴巴的黑脸，一分钟比一分钟气鼓鼓。于是两人进了花房，为迎爸爸剪些花。


===Original===
Mamma loved her conservatory so! Papa said she loved it better than she loved him, but, of course, that was only his fun. There were the delicate drooping ferns; the bloodred foliage of the dragon-plant; the fuchsias, trained like umbrellas, all tasselled with crimson and purple, umbrellas gorgeous enough to hold over the heads of Chinese emperors. And begonias with crimson-lined, silver-spotted leaves; intense blue and purple velvet disks of cinerarias; creamy calla lilies; and the little pouches of the calceolarias, golden, crimson, maroon and rose colored, mottled and flecked﻿—moneybags for the elves. All along one side were the spice-scented, winter-flowering carnations, with their flakes and veinings and marblings of color﻿—yellow edged with a fringe of rose, rosy pink and carmine. *La Pureté* and *La Pureté Variée*. Mamma could never understand what Papa found funny in “Variegated Purity”; but then she couldn’t understand most of the jokes that amused him, although she always gave them her gentle smile, puzzled and polite.
Snip went her scissors through the stem of a tea-rose. Snip! That was a little bit of myrtle. And then back into the parlor to arrange charming unaesthetic bouquets﻿—rosebuds and fuchsia, an airy tendril of vine, a spray of wax-white lemon blossoms, with glossy dark leaves﻿—while Aunt Priscilla followed, talking in a mild steady trickle.

===Chinese===
妈妈多爱她的花房啊！爸爸说她爱花房胜过爱他本人，不过那当然只是他的玩笑话。花房里有柔弱低垂的蕨草；有龙血树血红的叶子；有倒挂金钟，整修得像一把把小伞，缀满绯红与深紫的流苏——那样的伞，华美得足够给中国皇帝遮在头顶。还有秋海棠，叶片衬着绯红的脉、撒着银白的点；有瓜叶菊，一圆盘一圆盘浓蓝浓紫的丝绒；有乳白的马蹄莲；还有蒲包花的小荷包，金黄的、绯红的、枣红的、玫瑰红的，斑斑点点——那是给小精灵装金子的钱袋。沿着一面墙根，摆满了冬天也开花、散着香料甜气的康乃馨，颜色有片斑、细纹、大理石纹——黄的镶一道玫瑰色边，玫瑰粉的，胭脂红的。《纯白》（La Pureté）啦，《杂色纯白》（La Pureté Variée）啦。妈妈永远弄不明白，“杂色的纯白”有什么好笑的；不过话说回来，逗他发笑的那些笑话，她多半也弄不明白，尽管她总要还它们一个温柔的微笑，又困惑，又客气。

咔嚓，她的剪刀剪断一枝茶玫瑰的茎。咔嚓！这回是一小枝桃金娘。随后回到客厅，去插那些迷人却不讲章法的花束——玫瑰苞配倒挂金钟，一缕轻盈的藤蔓卷须，一枝蜡白的柠檬花，衬着油亮的深色叶子——普丽西拉婶婶跟在后头，声调温和，涓涓不断地说着话。


===Original===
“So I had my new dress laid out on the bed, and that new fancy dinner-cap I got in Philadelphia, the black lace one with the magenta ribbons, all ready to surprise Willie. I meant to have them on when he came home to tea, but I got to reading a new book by that Mr. Wilkie Collins﻿—‘Sister Rose; or, The Ominous Marriage,’ it’s called. I read about it in Godey’s. It said: ‘has merit and is neatly printed,’ so I knew it would be good﻿—so anyway﻿—what was I talking about?”
“Willie,” Mamma replied. She hadn’t been listening, but she knew that all Aunt Priscilla’s conversation rippled around that name.
“Oh, yes, Willie!” And she said the name tenderly, as if her heart was giving it a little kiss. “Well, so I hadn’t an *idea* how late it was; and the first thing I knew, there was Willie home and Henry Allen with him﻿—come to tea﻿—and me in my old crocheted Zouave because it was so chilly, and my hoops off. And nothing for tea but chip-beef because I told the girl I’d make some whips for dessert﻿—she don’t seem to have much success with her desserts﻿—girls aren’t what they used to be﻿—no faculty, and inde*pen*dent! And even the green ones asking two dollars a week.”
“Don’t talk to me about *girls*!” cried Mamma, meaning *do*.
“And so Willie﻿—*O‑oh!*”
Priscilla’s hands flew to her mouth, her mild kind eyes swam with tears, and the small nose that looked like a button in the middle of a puffy cushion flushed pink. On the floor lay the glass swan her sleeve had brushed from the table; and Mamma looking at it, felt her eyes fill with tears too. She had never thought about it much before, but, now that it was broken, it seemed to her that it had always been her favorite ornament. Poor little swan! It lay there with its fragile neck snapped right in two, with the violets old Toot had brought in from the cold-frame scattered around it, and a dark patch beside it on the crimson carpet that looked as if it had been bleeding. Yet with those pale bewildered eyes looking at her so beseechingly, she could only repeat to herself the family saying:
“Poor Priscilla means so well!”

===Chinese===
“我那会儿把新裙子摊在床上，还有在费城买的那顶新式晚餐帽——黑蕾丝的，缀着品红缎带——都拾掇好了，要给威利一个惊喜。我本打算等他回家吃茶点时就穿戴上的，结果我读开了那位威尔基·柯林斯先生的一本新书——叫《罗丝妹妹，或不祥的婚姻》。我在《戈迪》杂志上看到过对它的介绍，说‘颇有可取之处，印制精美’，我就知道这书准好看——所以呀——咦，我刚才说到哪儿了？”

“威利。”妈妈答道。她并没在听，可她知道，普丽西拉婶婶的话题转来转去，都围着这个名字打转。

“哦，对，威利！”她说这个名字的声气那么温柔，仿佛她的心正在轻轻亲它一下。“唉，我压根儿没觉出天有多晚了；猛可里，威利到家了，还带着亨利·艾伦（Henry Allen）——回来吃茶点——我还穿着那件旧钩针祖阿夫短外套，因为天儿凉，裙箍也卸了。茶点上除了几片干牛肉什么都没有，因为我跟那女佣说，甜点我来做搅打果泥——她做甜点好像总不见起色——如今的女佣不比从前喽——没脑子，还独——立——得很！连生手丫头都开口要一礼拜两块钱了。”

“可别跟我提女佣！”妈妈叫道——嘴上说别提，意思是快讲。

“于是威利——哎呀！”

普丽西拉的双手飞快捂住嘴，温厚的眼睛里蓄满泪水，那只小鼻子——活像蓬松靠垫正中钉着的一颗纽扣——涨得通红。地板上躺着那只玻璃天鹅，是她的袖子从桌沿带落的；妈妈望着它，只觉自己眼里也涌上了泪。从前她没怎么在意过这只天鹅，可如今它碎了，她倒觉得它一向是自己最心爱的摆设。可怜的小天鹅！它躺在那儿，纤巧的脖子齐齐断成两截，老图特（Toot）从阳畦里剪来的紫罗兰撒了它一身，身旁深红地毯上一小片发暗的印子，看着就像它流出的血。可是望着那双正对着自己、苍白茫然、苦苦哀怜的眼睛，她只能在心里一遍遍念叨那句家传的话：

“可怜的普丽西拉，她可是一片好心啊！”


===Original===
She was dreadfully tired by the time Aunt Priscilla went home! But the parlor was decked satisfactorily for Papa’s return. She loved her parlor, almost as much as her conservatory. Each fat chair was a friend, the sofa was a lover who said to her, “Come, lie in my arms.” To walk on the carpet was to walk on crimson roses. Between looped-back crimson window-curtains hung cages of canaries and lovebirds that she had tamed with the endless patience of indolence combined with a sweet nature, and taught to perch on her shoulder and peck at lumps of sugar held between her lips. Under the cages green iron plantstands held geraniums soft as butterfly wings, their velvet leaves banded with chocolate color, growing in pots covered with putty into which she had pressed acorns and little pine-cones. She had crocheted the blue and green and scarlet worsted covers for the goose-egg baskets in the windows, each holding a little bunch of flowers or a feather of fern; she had made the wax pond lilies floating on their mirror pools under glass shades. And even a picture hanging on the wall was hers﻿—a castle on a lake, with mountains and clouds for background. The sky and water were painted; and the mountains were of gray sand; the rocks, of red sand; and a road of yellow sand, sprinkled on glue. The castle was made of white birch-bark, with its dark reddish lining used for the parts in shadow, and the windows and doors painted in with ivory black; and, springing from the moss of the foreground, were trees﻿—bits of untwisted rope with the strands divided at the top to make the limbs. Cousin Lizzie and Aunt Priscilla had made sand-pictures, too. The directions said you could have white clouds, storm clouds, or sunset clouds, but pointed out that sunset clouds were more difficult and required more patience to paint. Mamma had been contented with white clouds, but Cousin Lizzie had done a sunset. As for poor Priscilla’s, hers had ended in nothing much but glue all over everything in the house.
And now to rest!

===Chinese===
等普丽西拉婶婶回家去的时候，她已经累得不行了！好在客厅布置停当，尽可以迎爸爸回来了。她爱她的客厅，几乎赶得上爱她的花房。每一把胖墩墩的椅子都是一位朋友，那张沙发是个情人，对她说：“来，躺进我怀里。”走在地毯上，就是走在绯红的玫瑰上。挽起的绯红窗帘之间，挂着金丝雀和相思鸟的笼子，那是她以懒人特有的无穷耐心，加上一副天生的好性子，把它们驯得服服帖帖，教会它们停在她肩头，啄她双唇间衔着的方糖。笼子底下，绿铁花架上摆着天竺葵，软得像蝴蝶翅膀，丝绒般的叶片上箍着一道巧克力色，栽在抹了油灰的花盆里，油灰上按满了她嵌进去的橡子和小松果。窗边鹅蛋篮的蓝的绿的鲜红的绒线罩子是她亲手钩的，每只篮里插一小束花，或是一片蕨羽；玻璃罩下浮在镜面水池上的蜡睡莲，也是她做的。连挂在墙上的一幅画都出诸她的手——湖上一座城堡，衬着群山与云。天空和湖水是画的；山是灰沙子；岩石是红沙子；一条路是黄沙子，撒在胶水上。城堡用白桦树皮做成，向阴的部分用树皮深红的里子，门窗拿象牙黑描出来；前景的青苔里则探出棵棵树木——是一截截拆散的绳头，绳股在顶上岔开，权当枝桠。丽齐表姐和普丽西拉婶婶也做过沙子画。做法说明上讲，云可以画白云、乌云或晚霞，但又指出晚霞更难画，须得更大的耐性。妈妈画白云就心满意足了，丽齐表姐却画了一幅晚霞。至于可怜的普丽西拉那幅，到头来什么也没画成，只落得满宅子上上下下全是胶水。

这下可以歇歇了！


===Original===
But almost before she had settled herself on the sofa before the fire, Cousin Lizzie Blow came rustling in, wearing a new black bonnet with scarlet verbenas under the brim. Her black eyes, darting about the room, made Mamma see a curtain looped unevenly, a dead geranium leaf, and the dark patch on the carpet where the glass swan had fallen.
“I came to drive Mr. B. home﻿—he rode over the mare he sold Victor last week. He’s out in the stable-yard with old Toot; and Maggie’s there too, superintending affairs, trying to stand like Mr. B. and Toot, with her legs apart and her hands behind her back. I verily believe she’s such a tomboy because Victor was so frantic for a son. I do hope that he’s﻿—that you’re not going to be disappointed again!”

===Chinese===
可她还没在炉火前的沙发上坐安稳，丽齐·布洛表姐（Lizzie Blow）便窸窸窣窣地进来了，戴一顶新的黑软帽，帽檐下衬着猩红的马鞭草。她那双黑眼睛在屋里扫来扫去，扫得妈妈也跟着看见：窗帘有一边挽得不匀，有一片枯了的天竺葵叶，还有地毯上玻璃天鹅摔落的那块深色印子。

“我来接 B. 先生（Mr. B.）回家——他骑着上礼拜卖给维克托（Victor）的那匹母马过来的。这会儿在外头马厩院子里，跟老图特一起呢；玛吉也在那儿监工，学 B. 先生和图特的样子站着，两腿撇开，双手背在身后。我实在相信，就因为维克托盼儿子盼得发了狂，才把她纵成这么个假小子。我真是盼着他——盼着你这回别再落空！”


===Original===
Mamma’s peach-like cheeks flushed a deeper pink, and her blue eyes looked as if they were going to fill with tears. Goodness knew she had done her best to give Victor a son! In the eleven years of their marriage she had had seven babies, and was expecting another. Victoria (named for Papa and the Queen of England, but mostly for Papa) Anna Louisa, Sophia, and Adelaide had breathed and died, and Mamma often thought of them with tears. But then she had tears for so many things that Papa never knew whether her eyes were red because she had been thinking of the lost babies, or because the sponge-cake had gone flat, or because she had been reading “Miss Proctor’s lovely, darling book of poems.”
What more could she do to give Victor a son than have babies and pray? Even the little girls were helping, having been taught to remind God, at the end of all their prayers, that they would like a brother. Yet Cousin Lizzie lifted her eyebrows and said, “Poor Victor!”
However, everyone knew that Lizzie Campion had been madly in love with her cousin, and had only married Sam Blow to show she didn’t care when Victor married Margaret Southmayd. So Mamma, remembering, thought, “Poor Lizzie!” complacently, and felt better.

===Chinese===
妈妈桃子似的脸颊晕上更深的粉红，蓝眼睛看样子又要满含泪水了。天知道，为了给维克托生个儿子，她已尽了全力！他们结婚十一年，她生过七个孩子，如今又怀着一个。维多利亚（为纪念爸爸和英国女王而起的名字，不过主要是爸爸）、安娜·路易莎、索菲娅和阿德莱德，都只喘息了一阵便夭折了，妈妈常常想着他们就掉泪。不过话说回来，让她掉泪的事情也实在太多，爸爸永远弄不清她眼睛红，究竟是因为又想起了那些没了的孩子，还是因为海绵蛋糕塌了膛，还是因为读了“普罗克特小姐那本可爱的心肝宝贝诗集”。

除了生养和祈祷，她还能靠什么给维克托添个儿子呢？连几个小姑娘都在帮衬——她们受了调教，每回祷告到末了，都要提醒上帝一声，说她们想要个弟弟。丽齐表姐却偏偏挑起眉毛说：“可怜的维克托！”

不过谁都清楚，丽齐·坎皮恩当年疯了一样恋着自己这位表亲，后来所以嫁给萨姆·布洛（Sam Blow），不过是维克托娶了玛格丽特·索斯梅德（Margaret Southmayd），她要做出一副满不在乎。于是妈妈想起这层，心安理得地想：“可怜的丽齐！”心里便舒坦了些。


===Original===
“I saw Priscilla scuttling along looking like an old peddler woman. How *can* she go about so? Willie gives her fifty times the spending money Mr. B. gives me﻿—almost as much as Victor gives you﻿—and what’s more, she spends it, and buys elegant clothes, and yet *look* at her!”
“Poor Priscilla,” Mamma murmured automatically.
“Poor Priscilla fiddle-dee-dee! Poor Willie, I say. You wouldn’t believe how pretty she was when he married her. She said she’d been here helping you. I can imagine the help!”
Her hands flew about, pleating her little lace handkerchief, smoothing it out again, drumming a tune on the table. Her black eyes slipped this way and that.
“I suppose Victor’ll be home on the four o’clock train?”

===Chinese===
“我刚才瞧见普丽西拉急急慌慌一路小跑，那模样活像个挑担子的老货婆。她怎么就能那样出门见人呢？威利给她的零用钱，是 B. 先生给我的五十倍——差不多赶上维克托给你的了——而且她还真花，还真买高贵的衣裳，可你再瞧瞧她那副样子！”

“可怜的普丽西拉。”妈妈下意识地低声道。

“可怜的普丽西拉，呸！要我说，可怜的是威利。他娶她那会儿她有多漂亮，你简直不会相信。她说她上这儿来给你帮忙来着。那种‘帮忙’我可想象得出！”

她那双手闲不住，捏着小蕾丝手绢折出细褶，又抚平，又在桌面上嗒嗒地敲一支曲子。黑眼睛溜过来，溜过去。

“我猜维克托是搭四点钟的火车回来吧？”


===Original===
Whenever she spoke Victor’s name, it was as if strong hands that she loved﻿—his hands﻿—took hold of her heart and twisted it. It hurt her so that some day she felt she would fall down dead of the pain, and yet she was always in a fever to say his name, over and over again; in a fever to see him, to hurt herself watching his dark ugly face with its bittersweet smile, and the smiling passion in his eyes when he looked at his wife.
“Yes, he wrote and said to have Toot meet him.” Mamma’s hand sought in her bosom for Papa’s letter. She brought out the dinner menu first, but the letter was there too.
“He’s been staying at the Fifth Avenue Hotel, but he went twice to Delmonico’s and had turtle soup.”

===Chinese===
每逢她说出“维克托”这名字，就仿佛有一双她所爱的有力的手——他的手——攥住她的心拧了一把。疼得她暗想，总有一天自己会疼死过去；可她又总是发着热似的，要把这个名字说了一遍又一遍；发着热似的要见他，好望着他那张黝黑的丑脸、那又苦又甜的笑，望着他看向妻子时眼里含笑的炽情，把自己疼个够。

“是啊，他来信说要图特去接他。”妈妈的手伸进怀里去摸爸爸的信。先掏出来的是那张晚餐菜单，好在信也在里头。

“他一直住在第五大道旅馆（Fifth Avenue Hotel），不过去德尔莫尼科（Delmonico's）吃了两回，吃的甲鱼汤。”


===Original===
“Oh, I don’t doubt he’s had a gay time,” said Cousin Lizzie, getting up and moving about the room restlessly, crushing a sprig of lemon-verbena between nervous fingers, flapping over the songs on the piano. “Have you anything new? ‘Oh Let Me Shed One Silent Tear’﻿—‘Too Late, Too Late’﻿—you *do* like merry songs, don’t you, Margaret? How does this go?

> ‘Not lost forever, though by fate now parted,
 

 Not lost forever, though we meet no more;
 

 They do not wander lone and brokenhearted﻿—!’

Yes, I’m sure he’s had a good time. Victor’s always known how to amuse himself very well.”

===Chinese===
“哦，他过得快活，这我可不怀疑。”丽齐表姐说着站起身，心神不定地在屋里走来走去，焦躁的手指间捻着一小枝柠檬马鞭草，把钢琴上的乐谱哗啦啦地翻来翻去。“有新歌吗？《啊，让我洒一滴无声的泪》——《太迟了，太迟了》——你还就爱这种欢快的歌，对不对，玛格丽特？这首是怎么唱的来着？”

> ‘命运虽将你我分开，并未永远失去，
> 从此虽不再相见，并未永远失去；
> 他们并不孤身漂泊、肝肠寸断——！’

“是啊，我敢保他玩得痛快。维克托向来最会给自己找乐子。”


===Original===
“He had to go on business,” Mamma replied, displeased and dignified. “Very important business. He told me all about it before he went.”
And he had, for although he had no illusions about his wife’s mentality, he liked to talk to her about the things that interested him. He had talked to her all one evening about the business that was taking him to New York; and Mamma had said, “Yes, love,” and “*Well!*” and “I’m sure you’re right,” when the tone of his voice seemed to call for such remarks. As a matter of fact she hadn’t listened to a word, for she had been crocheting a floral card-basket that called for a great deal of counting. The bottom was a star of white on claret that shaded to violet on the border; and there were crocheted dark and light green leaves, and straw-colored poppies shading into claret and violet, all spangled with dewdrops of little glass beads. It was only by the greatest effort she had kept from counting aloud, “single chain over the last single chain under the same chain, four chain, single chain under chain before the second double chain, seven chain.” Papa, kissing the top of her head, as sleek and brown as a horsechestnut under its chenille net, had assured her that it was a great relief to find that she agreed with him.
“Well, I must fly, love!” cried Cousin Lizzie. “Mr. B. will be waiting for me. ‘Not lost forever, though by fate now parted﻿—’ No, not lost forever, only until dinner time!”

===Chinese===
“他是去办事。”妈妈答道，不悦而端庄，“顶要紧的正事。临走之前他原原本本都对我说过了。”

他也确实说过了，因为虽说对太太的脑力不抱幻想，他仍旧喜欢把自己的兴致讲给她听。有一整个晚上，他都在讲那趟要去纽约办的事；妈妈呢，在他的语气似乎该应一声的当口，说一句“是啊，亲爱的”“哦！”“我敢保你说得对”。其实她一个字也没听进去，她正钩一只花卉名片篮，那活计要数数个没完。篮底是酒红底子上的一颗白星，到边上渐渐晕成紫罗兰色；还有钩出来的深绿浅绿的叶子，麦秆黄的罂粟花，渐渐晕进酒红和紫罗兰，通体缀着小玻璃珠子做的露珠。她是费了九牛二虎之力，才憋住没把针数念出声来：“在上一短针的同一针目上钩短针，四针锁针，第二针长针前的锁针上钩短针，七针锁针。”爸爸亲了亲她的头顶——雪尼尔发网底下，那颗头油光溜滑、棕褐得像颗马栗——并向她担保说，发现她与自己所见相同，实在叫人大大宽慰。

“好啦，我得飞啦，亲爱的！”丽齐表姐嚷道。“B. 先生该等急了。‘命运虽将你我分开，并未永远失去——’是啊，并未永远失去，只失到晚饭时分！”


===Original===
And at last Mamma could drop on the sofa, her bright blue skirts swelling up around her. On the table near by, in a workbasket petticoated with pinked frills of violet and maize-colored ribbon, lay her sewing; on it lay also the book she was reading, drawn to it by the heroine’s name being Margaret, just like hers. “ ‘Margaret, Marchioness of Miniver,’ by Lady Clara Cavendish, authoress of ‘Lisa, or The Mesmerist’s Victim,’ ‘The Divorce, a Tale of Fashionable Life,’ ‘The Woman of the World,’ etc.” Perhaps, presently, she would sew a little, or read. If she read she would have to hide her book before Papa came home, for she knew he would hope to find her reading “The New Gymnastics for Men, Women, and Children,” all full of dumbbells. He was so modern and full of upsetting ideas﻿—exercise, and air, insisting on having a window in the children’s bedroom lowered an inch or two at night, although Mamma pointed out to him again and again that everyone knew the night air was injurious.
But just for the moment she only wanted to lie still.
Oh, how nice, how nice! She made little nestling motions, settling deeper on her sofa, letting comfort surround her softly. She was all soft and smooth and round﻿—so round that she sometimes thought, but only thought, of drinking vinegar. She lay there, stretching and stirring a little at first from sheer comfort, then growing motionless; heavy and soft and white, a lady made of white velvet and stuffed with down.

===Chinese===
妈妈总算能一屁股陷进沙发里，鲜亮的蓝裙子在身周鼓胀开来。旁边桌上，针线篮罩着紫罗兰色和玉米黄缎带剪出锯齿边的流苏，活像穿了条衬裙，里头搁着她的针线活；针线活上还压着她正读的那本书——当初引她读它的，是女主角的名字就叫玛格丽特，跟她的一模一样。“《玛格丽特·米尼弗侯爵夫人》，克拉拉·卡文迪什女勋爵（Lady Clara Cavendish）著，另著有《丽莎，或催眠师的牺牲品》《离婚——一则上流社会故事》《社交场上的女人》等。”待一会儿，她也许要做点针线，或是读读书。要读书，就得赶在爸爸回家前把书藏起来，因为她知道，他满心指望撞见她读的是《男女老幼新体操》，满书都是哑铃。他那么摩登，满脑子搅动人心的主意——锻炼啦，空气啦，硬要人夜里把孩子们卧室的窗子放低一两寸，尽管妈妈一遍又一遍地提醒他：夜里的空气伤人，这是人人皆知的。

只是眼下这一刻，她只想安安静静躺着。

啊，多好啊，多好啊！她小小地拱动了几下，往沙发深处又偎了偎，任舒坦温温柔柔地把自己包围。她整个人又软又滑又圆——圆得她有时会想起喝醋，不过也只是想想罢了。她躺在那儿，起先纯粹因为太舒坦，还伸一伸、动一动，渐渐便一动不动了；又沉、又软、又白，一位白天鹅绒做的、塞满羽绒的夫人。

## II / II

===Original===
Victor Campion met Margaret Southmayd a few years after he graduated from Harvard, when he went to visit her brother Henry, who had been a classmate.
There had been guests in his honor on the afternoon before he went home—young men a little standoffish and suspicious of the stranger who had so much money, and had made the “Grand Tour,” and was just home from England and inclined to talk about the opening of the Crystal Palace in Hyde Park; arch young ladies not at all standoffish, tossing their ringlets and shrugging their shoulders and making “saucy” replies. There had been strawberries and cream, and croquet with tiny mallets and huge balls banded with bright colors that they sent trundling through wide wickets. The shadows grew long on the gold-green turf, and the girls’ great crinolines, swinging and swaying as they bent above their balls, took on the tender colors of flowers at twilight. And through the laughter and the calling there sometimes sounded a note of sadness, though they were all so happy.
Now they were gone; and Henry had gone with them to get the mail, while Victor and Margaret collected the strawberry saucers and put the mallets and balls to bed in their box in the dark summerhouse that smelled of moss and old rotting wood.

===Chinese===
维克托·坎皮恩（Victor Campion）从哈佛（Harvard）毕业几年后，去探访老同学亨利·索斯梅德（Henry Southmayd），就在那时认识了亨利的妹妹玛格丽特·索斯梅德（Margaret Southmayd）。

回家前一天下午，主人家为他设了宾客聚会——年轻的男客们对这位生客有点儿敬而远之、满怀猜疑：他的钱那么多，游历过欧陆“大旅行”（Grand Tour），刚从英国回来，张口就想谈海德公园水晶宫开幕的盛况；年轻的小姐们倒一点儿也不疏远他，甩着长卷发，耸着肩膀，回话回得“俏皮”得很。席上有草莓拌奶油，还有槌球戏——小小的木槌，配上箍着一道道亮色彩条的大球，骨碌碌推过宽宽的球门。金绿色的草坪上，影子越拉越长，姑娘们巨大的裙撑在俯身击球时摇来摆去，染上了暮色里花朵般柔嫩的颜色。而在笑声和呼喊声中间，有时会透出一丝忧伤的调子，虽说人人都那么快活。

这会儿人都散了；亨利也随大伙取邮件去了，只剩维克托和玛格丽特收拾草莓碟子，把木槌和球送回幽暗凉亭里它们那只盒中安睡——亭子里一股青苔和陈年朽木的气味。


===Original===
She wanted to talk to him, to say clever things to make him laugh, as Alice and Emily had. He had been with those two all afternoon—and tomorrow he would be gone forever. But she wasn’t good at beginning conversations, and she never knew where to look when his dark eyes were on her; so she kept at a little distance, turning her head away, pretending to be busy.
“Here’s another ball!” he called, picking it up out of the wet pink and silver border of cinnamon pinks. And he said it as if he were telling her a tremendous and beautiful and funny secret that only they two would ever know, so that she forgot to be shy, and came smiling to his side.
In her spreading white muslin and rose-colored ribbons, she seemed to him like a slender apple-tree in blossom. He was a modern young man, but he did not like the modern girl. It was the time of the bloomer agitation, and every paper you picked up was full of caricatures of ladies, with bonnets and ringlets, to be sure, but also with trousers, bull dogs, and cigars. And there were jokes about ladies “popping the question,” or if they did not ask for the gentleman’s hand in marriage, at least asking for the next polka or deux temps. There were even jokes about colleges for women, and caricatures of professors with bloomers under their gowns and mortarboards on top of their lace caps. But there was nothing of the new woman about Margaret. She no more wanted equal rights than did the rose, sweet and cool in its broad fresh leaves at her belt. At the very mention of bloomers her white lids curtained her blue eyes, her cheeks grew pinker; and, when she went to church to confess her sins in her innocent voice, she wore a bonnet all rosebuds and lace instead of one of those fast round hats that were becoming so fashionable.

===Chinese===
她想跟他说话，想像艾丽斯（Alice）和埃米莉（Emily）那样说些聪明话逗他发笑。整整一下午，他都跟那两位待在一处——而明天，他就要永远地走了。可她不善于起话头，他那双黑眼睛一望住她，她就不知道眼睛该往哪儿搁；于是她只稍稍退开些，把头别过去，装出一副手头有事的样子。

“这儿还有一只球！”他喊道，把它从湿润的石竹花境里拾起来——那一圈肉桂石竹，粉红里衬着银白。他说这话的神气，仿佛正告诉她一个天大的、美妙的、逗趣的秘密，天底下永远只有他们两个知道；她于是忘了害羞，笑吟吟地走到他身边。

她一身舒展的白纱裙，系着玫瑰色的缎带，在他眼里就像一株开满了花的纤细苹果树。他是位摩登青年，却不喜欢摩登女郎。那正是“灯笼裤”（bloomers）风波闹得沸反盈天的年头，随手抄起哪份报纸，都登满了淑女们的漫画像——头上固然照旧是软帽和长卷发，身上却也添了长裤、斗牛犬和雪茄。还有种种笑话，说淑女们“开口求婚”；或者说，纵然不求男士娶她，至少也要抢先约下一支波尔卡或是二步舞（deux temps）。甚至还有拿女子学院打趣的，漫画里的女教授长袍底下穿灯笼裤，蕾丝软帽顶上还架一顶学位方帽。可玛格丽特身上，没有半点儿“新女性”的影子。她想要平等权利的心思，并不比别在她腰间、衬在肥厚鲜嫩叶子里的那朵玫瑰更多——那玫瑰甜甜的、凉凉的。一有人提起灯笼裤，她白色的眼睑便帘幕似的遮下蓝眼睛，脸颊涨得更粉；她上教堂、用她那天真的嗓音忏悔罪愆时，戴的是缀满玫瑰花苞和蕾丝的软帽，而不是那种越来越时兴的轻佻圆帽。


===Original===
“See, here’s a firefly,” he said, and showed it to her on his finger, its little green glow shining and darkening, shining and darkening. She bent over it, showers of ringlets falling like curtains on either side of her pretty face. The dusky rose in the sky was deepening, and little pale moths were fluttering over the flowers. In the valley below them where a white mist was rising, the frogs began to call. They still stood close together, their heads bent to the firefly’s small green lamp; but they had forgotten it, forgotten everything except that her whole being was crying silently, “Tomorrow we will be apart!” and his was answering, “Tonight we are together.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，这儿有只萤火虫。”他说，把它举在指尖上给她看，那一点小小的绿光明了又暗，明了又暗。她俯身去看，一绺绺卷发像帘幕似的，纷纷垂落在漂亮脸蛋的两侧。天上的暗玫瑰色越来越浓，小小的白蛾在花间扑扇。他们脚下的小山谷里，白雾正在升起，青蛙叫起来了。两人依旧紧紧挨着站在一起，头低垂着，凑向萤火虫那盏小小的绿灯；可他们早把它忘了，把一切都忘了，只知道她的整个生命在无声地呼喊：“明天我们就要分开了！”而他的整个生命在回答：“今夜我们还在一起。”

---

===Original===
The night before her wedding Margaret spent in weeping. In the first place, the Southmayds were dreadfully poor, and, instead of lace, her wedding-veil beneath its orange buds would be plain illusion. And then Mrs. Southmayd had had “a little talk” with her daughter that was as terrifying as it was unilluminating; and, after vague wide circlings about some secret so sinister that it could not be mentioned, it had ended in floods of tears on both sides.
Her trembling voice and ice-cold fingers, when they were being married, filled Victor with an anguish of love. But presently, tying on her white silk going-away bonnet, an artless advertisement with its lace veil and orange-blossoms, while her bridesmaids fluttered about her, envious and excited, and her tall, dark lover waited for her below, she grew more calm, became, in fact, complacent. And although his passionate love never seemed to her “quite nice,” she returned it with placid affection.

===Chinese===
婚礼前夜，玛格丽特是在哭泣中度过的。头一件，索斯梅德家穷得厉害，她那顶缀着橘子花苞的新娘头纱，用的不是蕾丝，只是寻常的薄纱。再一件，索斯梅德太太跟女儿进行了一场“小小的谈话”，吓人有余，开窍不足；她围着某个罪恶得提不得的秘密，含含糊糊地兜了老大一圈，末了以母女俩的滂沱泪雨收场。

行婚礼的时候，她那发抖的嗓音、冰凉的指尖，叫维克托心里涨满一阵带着痛楚的爱。可是不多时，待她系上那顶白丝绸的出门帽——配着蕾丝面纱和橘花，天真得无异于一纸告示——伴娘们围着她扑来扑去，又眼热又兴奋，她那高大黝黑的情郎正在楼下等她——她反倒镇定了，说实在的，是心满意足了。他那炽烈的爱，在她看来从来有点儿“不甚得体”，她却报之以恬静的柔情。


===Original===
He had fallen in love with her greatly because she was not “strong-minded”; and indeed Margaret was never what Miss Florence Nightingale called “a female ink-bottle.” But, after they were married, he tried for a little while to improve her mind.
“Would you like me to read to you, darling?”
“Oh, that would be very nice!”
But first she would have to get her footstool, and her work from upstairs, and her little quilted jacket of rose-colored silk bordered with swansdown, in case she grew chilly. And then:

===Chinese===
他深深地爱上她，多半正因为她不“头脑强硬”；的确，玛格丽特从来不是弗洛伦斯·南丁格尔小姐（Florence Nightingale）所谓的那种“女墨水瓶”。可是成亲以后，他还是试着花了一小段日子，想给她长长心智。

“亲爱的，愿意我念点儿什么给你听吗？”

“哦，那可太好了！”

可她先得取脚凳，从楼上取针线活，还得穿上那件小小的玫瑰色丝绡绗缝外衣——滚一道天鹅绒毛边——以防着凉。然后：


===Original===
“Oh, Victor! My thimble! It’s up on my toilet table!”
And she would look so helpless and appealing that he would tear upstairs, three steps at a time, and bring it down to her.
“Now then, I really *am* ready!”
He would begin to read words that stirred the depths of his heart with their beauty:

> “ ‘Bright star, would I were steadfast as thou art—
 

 Not in lone splendor hung aloft the night,
 

 And watching—’”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，维克托！我的顶针！落在梳妆台上了！”

她那副孤立无援、可怜巴巴的神气，惹得他噔噔噔冲上楼去，一步三级，给她取下来。

“好啦，这下我可真的齐了！”

他便开始诵读那些句子，它们的美搅动着他心灵的深处：

> “ ‘明亮的星，但愿我像你一样坚定——
> 不在孤独的辉煌里高悬夜空，
> 长夜守望着——’ ”


===Original===
“Oh, Victor love! Before you get *really* into it! Tell me, if you were me, would you have field flowers on your new leghorn straw, or just a white lace veil and mauve ribbons? Field flowers *and* a lace veil would be *too* much, wouldn’t they? Or *wouldn’t* they?”

> “ ‘No—yet still steadfast, still unchangeable,
 

 Pillowed upon my fair love’s ripening breast,
 

 To feel forever its soft fall and swell,
 

 Awake forever in a sweet unrest,
 

 Still, still to hear her tender-taken breath,
 

 And so live ever—or else swoon to death.’”

===Chinese===
“哦，亲爱的维克托！趁你还没真正念进去！告诉我，你要是我，你那顶新买的意大利麦秆帽上，是缀野花呢，还是只配白蕾丝面纱加淡紫缎带？野花再配蕾丝面纱就太过了，对不对？还是说，不过分？”

> “ ‘不——却依旧坚定，依旧不变，
> 枕在我美丽爱人日渐丰满的胸脯上，
> 永远感受它温柔的起落与涨动，
> 永远醒着，在一场甜蜜的不安之中，
> 依旧，依旧听她轻柔的呼吸，
> 就这样永生——不然便昏死过去。’ ”


===Original===
Surely she must cry “It’s about us. It’s just exactly like us!”
She hadn’t listened to a word. The fire was so bright, her chair was so soft, and the sound of the rain on the windows made her sleepy. But when his voice stopped she rose out of her warm abstraction to make a little humming sound that meant, “How very pretty.”
“Did you like it, my darling?”
“Yes, thank you, Victor. It was sweetly pretty. Oh, and just while I remember it—would you like a tipsy parson for dessert tomorrow? You hardly touched your syllabub today, and I *do* so want to please you!”
It seemed to him so touching that she should want to please him that he could have fallen at her feet to worship her. But he gave up trying to improve her mind.

===Chinese===
她准会喊出来的：“这说的是我们呀！这说的分毫不差就是我们呀！”

其实她一个字也没听进去。炉火那么旺，椅子那么软，雨点敲窗的声音又声声催困。待他的声音一停，她才从暖烘烘的恍惚里浮上来，轻轻哼出一声，意思是：“多好听呀。”

“喜欢吗，亲爱的？”

“喜欢，谢谢你，维克托。又甜又美，好听极了。哦，趁我还记得——明天想来一份‘醉牧师’当甜点吗？今天你那杯乳酒冻几乎没动，我多想让你高兴呀！”

她这样一心想讨他欢心，他感动得简直要拜倒在她脚下顶礼膜拜。可是，提高她心智的尝试，他放弃了。


===Original===
He gave up trying to make her exercise her body even sooner, although on their honeymoon he had bullied and coaxed her into a bath in the ocean. She rather fancied herself in her gipsy hat and her bathing-dress, with her feet looking so white and small under the blue flannel ruffles; but going into the cold, rough water was dreadful! Walking, except for gentle ramblings about the garden, tired her, she said; and the very thought of a horse filled her with panic. As for skating, thank goodness, by the time the river froze, she knew she was going to have a baby, and could lie on her dear, soft sofa for hours and hours at a time without being urged to do anything.
So Victor went “down state” with his brother Willie and Sam Blow to shoot reedbirds, and railbirds, and ducks in the marshes, rowed across the river to fish in the New Jersey creeks, skated on the river in winter nearly up to Philadelphia, and galloped over the fields foxhunting.
“You ought to join in Victor’s pleasures more, my dear, unless you’re anxious to lose him,” Lizzie Blow advised Margaret. “It’s a poor plan to let a man find out how well he can amuse himself without you.”

===Chinese===
要她锻炼身体，他放弃得还要早。虽说蜜月里他连吓带哄，才劝得她下海洗过一回海水澡。她戴着吉普赛帽、穿着浴衣，蓝法兰绒的褶边底下那双脚显得又白又小——那模样她自己倒相当得意；可是要走进冰冷汹涌的海水里，多可怕呀！散步呢，除了绕着园子悠悠地转转，她说多走就累；一想到马，她就满心惶恐。至于滑冰——谢天谢地，等河面上冻的时候，她已经知道自己要添小宝宝了，尽可以一连好几个钟头躺在她那亲爱的软沙发上，再没人催她干什么了。

于是维克托便同弟弟威利（Willie）和萨姆·布洛（Sam Blow）一道“下州”去，在沼泽地里打苇莺、秧鸡和野鸭；划船过河，到新泽西（New Jersey）的小河汊里垂钓；冬天顺着冰封的大河一路滑，几乎滑到费城（Philadelphia）；还策马驰过田野，追猎狐狸。

“亲爱的，你要是不想失掉维克托，就该多参与参与他的玩乐，”丽齐·布洛（Lizzie Blow）劝玛格丽特道，“让男人发现没有你、他自己也能玩得多么痛快——这可不是个好主意。”


===Original===
Margaret’s eyes filled with tears. She *did* join in Victor’s pleasures! With her own hands, she made him sandwiches of ham, home-cured in the old grey smokehouse that huddled under its mantle of heaven-blue wistaria; she ate, with cries of delight, the fat little reedbirds he shot; she patted his horses, timidly offering them apples while she tried to hide her terror at their bared teeth and jerking heads; and she saw that drinks were always ready when he and Willie and Sam came trooping in, steaming punch when they were cold and muddy, frosty mint juleps when they were hot and dusty. And it was to “The Maples” they always returned, not to “Meadowbrook,” the Blows’ house, nor to “Riverview,” where Willie and poor Priscilla lived in expensive muddle. Remembering this helped Margaret to bear Lizzie’s trying remarks with fortitude. And she would rather have died than tear around the country as Lizzie did on Sam’s wildest horses, with her cheeks like fire, making such a figure of herself.
Victor had brought Margaret home to his father’s house in Delaware; and two years later the elder Mr. Campion died, leaving the place to his son. She loved it, inside and out; the trees so tall they seemed to hold the sky, the gold-green lawn, the carriage sweep, and the fountain in front of the house, with its great sheaf of white and green iron calla lilies and leaves, each lily sending up a jet of water. She loved the big grey house with its cupola and porches; the round beds of asters that were blooming when Victor brought her home, the pears that were dropping, heavy and golden from the golden sun. She loved the view of the river, blue, or slate-colored, or café au lait, with its sailing ships. She loved the negro servants, their soft voices and ready laughter and melancholy songs. She loved her deep carpets, the snowdrift of her bed, the delicious food that old Chloe cooked. And oh, she did love her pretty clothes, after piecing and contriving and making over her old dresses for so long! Her gowns of apple-green glacé, black velvet, mauve and primrose silk, her black lace shawl with its swirling ferns and deeply petalled edge, her white lace shawl as delicate as frost-work; her little parasols the shape and color of hyacinth bells; and the pearl and primrose tinted gloves; and small white mists of cobweb pocket-handkerchiefs, fragrant from lavender fagots.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特的眼里顿时涌满了泪。她怎么没参与维克托的玩乐！她亲手给他做火腿三明治——火腿是自家老灰熏房里腌熏的，那熏房蜷缩在一棚天青蓝紫藤的花幕底下；他打来的肥嫩小苇莺，她一边欢呼一边吃；她拍拍他的马，怯生生地递上苹果，一面极力掩住看见马龇牙、猛甩头时的惊惶；他和威利、萨姆成群结队涌进家门时，她总打点得酒水样样现成——他们又冷又泥，是热气腾腾的潘趣酒；又热又灰头土脸，是挂着霜的薄荷茱莉普。而且他们回的永远是“枫树庄”（The Maples），不是布洛家的“草溪庄园”（Meadowbrook），也不是“河景宅”（Riverview）——威利和可怜的普丽西拉（Priscilla）在那儿过着花销浩大、一团乱麻的日子。想起这一层，玛格丽特便有力量坚忍地受住丽齐那些刁钻难堪的话。至于像丽齐那样，骑着萨姆最野的马在乡间横冲直撞，两颊烧得像火，大出风头——她宁可死了也不干。

维克托把玛格丽特带回特拉华（Delaware）他父亲的宅子；两年后，老坎皮恩先生故去，家业留给了儿子。她爱这宅子，里里外外都爱：那些树高得仿佛擎着天空；金绿色的草坪；门前的马车环道，屋前的喷泉——一大捧白铁绿铁打成的马蹄莲和莲叶，每朵花心里喷起一股水柱。她爱那座灰色大宅，连同它的眺楼和游廊；爱那几圆坛紫菀——维克托接她回家时正开着花；爱那些正坠落的梨，沉甸甸、金灿灿，像从金色的太阳上坠下来。她爱眺望河上的景致——河水时而碧蓝，时而石板灰，时而又像一杯牛奶咖啡，河上帆影往来。她爱那些黑仆，爱他们软和的嗓音、随时迸发的笑声和忧郁的歌谣。她爱她那厚厚的地毯，爱她那张雪堆似的床，爱老克洛艾（Chloe）烧的好饭食。还有啊，拼拼凑凑、翻翻改改捱了这么久之后，她多爱她那些漂亮衣裳啊！苹果绿的加光绸长裙，黑丝绒长裙，淡紫的和樱草黄的绸裙；黑蕾丝披肩，蕨叶纹打着旋，垂着层层深瓣的花边；白蕾丝披肩，纤巧得赛过霜花细工；小小的阳伞，一柄柄风信子铃铛的形状和颜色；珍珠色和樱草黄的手套；蛛网纱的小白手绢，一小团一小团的白雾，透着薰衣草香枝熏出的香。


===Original===
And she loved Victor, too. She loved him very much, indeed.
As she had told him, she did so want to please him! But there was only one thing he didn’t have that he wanted, and that was a son.
So kneeling in church in her best shawl and bonnet, and by the broad bed in her long-sleeved high-necked nightgown and lace-frilled nightcap, she sent up prayers detailed as marketing lists:
“Dear God, please bless Victor and Baby” (or “Maggie and Baby,” or “Maggie and May and Baby,” as time went on) “and let her not have so much trouble with her teething; and bless Mamma and Papa, and bless Henry, too, but don’t let him ask Victor for any more money; and make old Chloe not so cross; and make me good and make my sore throat well; and keep us in safety and happiness and give us a little boy. Amen.”

===Chinese===
她也爱维克托。千真万确，她非常非常爱他。

正如她对他说过的，她多想让他事事称心！可他想要而还没有的东西只有一样——一个儿子。

于是，在教堂里，她披着最好的披肩、戴着最好的软帽跪下；在宽阔的大床边，她穿着长袖高领的睡袍、戴蕾丝褶边的睡帽跪下，把祷告一份份呈上去，详尽得像采买单：

“亲爱的上帝，求你保佑维克托和小宝宝”（日子久了，也作“玛吉（Maggie）和小宝宝”，或“玛吉、梅（May）和小宝宝”）“别让她出牙出得太受罪；保佑爸爸妈妈，也保佑亨利，可别让他再向维克托要钱了；叫老克洛艾别那么凶；叫我学好，叫我的嗓子快点好；保我们平安喜乐，再赐我们一个小男孩。阿门。”

## III / III

===Original===
Nobody could keep away from the parlor, because Papa was there, home again. Old Chloe, in a clean yellow print sprigged with scarlet and a fresh turban, brought eggnog; Albert carried in logs for the fire; and old Toot, though he knew perfectly well he had no business to, looked in to say that he was trying to teach the little yellow cow's two-day old calf to suck. As for the little girls, they couldn't get near enough to Papa. But it was to Mamma that all his being was pouring out, pouring out.

They gave him his birthday presents right away—they simply couldn't wait any longer. Mamma had embroidered him a pair of slippers—tigers! They quite frightened Lily, the tigers stared at her so, and showed such red tongues. It had been a great piece of work, calling not only for black Berlin wool, and white, and light and dark scarlet, but seven different shades of brown. And then such a question as to whether the background should be bright sky-blue or pale green. All the little girls had given their opinions.

Then she had had her own hair and theirs made into a watch-fob for him; and even that wasn't all, for old Toot had driven them to Wilmington one day to the photographer's, and they had had their *Cartes de Visite* taken. The little girls could have spent hours gazing at their pictures, and Mamma herself took a good many looks.

===Chinese===
谁也没法儿不往那间客厅里凑，因为爸爸在那儿——他回家了。老克洛艾换了身干净的黄底印花布衫，缀满猩红的小花朵，头上包一条新头巾，端来了蛋奶酒；艾伯特抱进生火的木柴；老图特呢，明明清楚得很自己没什么正经事，也探进头来，说他正想法子教那头黄母牛出生才两天的小牛犊吮奶。至于几个小姑娘，恨不得整个人贴到爸爸身上去，怎么也贴不够。可爸爸整个身心的倾泻、倾泻，倾泻的对象却是妈妈。

大家当即就把生日礼物送给了他——他们实在再也等不下去了。妈妈给他绣了一双拖鞋——绣的是老虎！莉莉简直被吓坏了：那些老虎那么样瞪着她，还吐出那么红的舌头。这可是件大工程，不但要黑色的柏林绒线（Berlin wool），白的，深浅两样的猩红，还得有七种深浅不同的棕色。接着又有一道天大的难题：底子该用鲜亮的天蓝呢，还是浅绿。小姑娘们个个都发表过意见。

随后，她又用自己的头发和孩子们的头发，给他编成一只怀表坠；这还不算完，有一天老图特赶车送她们去威尔明顿上照相馆，照了名片照（Cartes de Visite）。几个小姑娘对着自己的照片，一看就能看上几个钟头，妈妈自己也看了好许多回。

===Original===
Maggie had made him a small, flat silk pincushion, shaped like a fish, with net scales, and fins and tail of pins. It had seemed beautiful to her while she was making it—a little blue fish with silver tail and fins. She could imagine the sensation it would have made swimming among the mud-colored minnows in the brook. But now she wanted to give Papa something precious, marvelous—a milk white charger, or a ruby as big as a pigeon's egg. She wanted to do something hard to risk her life, to lose her life for him. And instead of saying, "Happy birthday with my love, Papa dear," prettily, as Mamma had told them all to do, she almost threw her present at him, scowling dreadfully and muttering, "Here's something—I guess it's horrid."

May had made him a pincushion, too—it was all the little sisters could think of. But hers was a lute, with a small round spot of black velvet glued on to look like the opening, and long stitches of gold thread for the lute strings. She and Maggie had been rather troubled because they were both giving him pincushions, but, fortunately, it turned out that that was exactly what Papa had been longing for.

Lily's present was an allumette stand filled with colored paper allumettes. It was of fluted wire covered with cerise and Napoleon blue chenille, and of course, Mamma had made it, really, guiding Lily's fat little hand in the weaving of a strand or two. But Lily thought she had made it all herself, and told Papa so.

===Chinese===
玛吉给他做了一只小小的扁平绸针插，做成一条鱼的样子，鳞片是网纱的，鱼鳍鱼尾由大头针排成。做着的时候，她觉得它美极了——一条蓝色小鱼，银色的尾巴银色的鳍。她想象得出，它游进小溪那群泥色鲦鱼中间，会引起怎样的轰动。可这会儿她想送爸爸的是贵重的、了不得的东西——一匹乳白色的骏马，或者一颗鸽子蛋大的红宝石。她想干一件难事，去冒生命的危险，为他把命舍掉也在所不惜。于是她没有像妈妈嘱咐大家的那样，甜甜地说一声“亲爱的爸爸，带着我的爱祝您生日快乐”，而是几乎把礼物朝他摔了过去，眉头拧得怕人，嘴里咕哝道：“给你个东西——我看糟糕透了。”

梅也给他做了个针插——小姊妹们想得出的就只有这个。不过她那只是一把琉特琴，琴身上贴着一小圆块黑天鹅绒，算是音孔，一长行一长行的金线针脚就算琴弦。她和玛吉都挺犯愁，因为两人送的都是针插；幸而后来发现，爸爸朝思暮想盼的恰恰就是这个。

莉莉的礼物是一只火柴架，插满了彩色纸火柴。架子由槽纹铁丝弯成，缠着樱桃红和拿破仑蓝两色雪尼尔绒线；当然啦，其实是妈妈做的，只把着莉莉胖乎乎的小手，让她也编了一两股。可莉莉认定全是她亲手做的，还这么告诉了爸爸。

===Original===
And Papa had presents for them, too—presents from New York.

“Oh, Victor love, you shouldn’t have! We didn’t expect *presents*!” Mamma cried, looking as pleased and excited as her little daughters.

“Oh yes, we *did*, Mamma!” protested May.

There were enamel lockets, black, with forget-me-nots, for the little sisters. They had on light blue chambray dresses, low necked, with scalloped ruffles, white muslin aprons, and their best long lace-edged drawers, and the lockets looked so pretty, tied on with long blue ribbons. And then there were three wax dolls, dressed as babies, and as pink as if they had just gotten out of boiling hot baths. Maggie thought that she would rather be chopped up into little tiny pieces than let Papa guess that she had hoped her present would be a bow and arrow.

===Chinese===
爸爸也给大家带了礼物——从纽约带来的礼物。

“哦，亲爱的维克托，你太不该破费了！我们可没想到会有礼物！”妈妈喊道，那副又欢喜又雀跃的样子，跟她的小女儿们一模一样。

“哦，不，我们想到了，妈妈！”梅分辩道。

给小姊妹们的是黑底珐琅小盒坠，上面烧着勿忘我花。她们穿的是浅蓝钱布雷布连衣裙，低领口，镶扇贝形的荷叶边，罩着白纱围裙，里面是最好的一条条蕾丝花边长衬裤；盒坠用长长的蓝缎带系上，好看极了。另外还有三只蜡娃娃，穿成襁褓婴儿的模样，粉扑扑的，仿佛刚从滚烫的热水澡里捞出来。玛吉心想，宁可把自己剁成小小的碎丁，也不能让爸爸猜到，她原先盼的礼物是一副弓箭。

===Original===
“That’s all I have for you three.”

“There’s another package in the hall,” suggested May, in case Papa had forgotten it.

“Oh, fie, pet, don’t be greedy!” Mamma reproved her, shocked.

Mamma’s present was a parure of flowers; a wreath for the hair, a bouquet for the corsage, and sprays for the skirt. They were made of cornflowers and wheat, and they came from Paris!

“Is it true that shawls are out, and everyone is wearing zou-zous?” Mamma wanted to know. But Papa hadn’t noticed.

However, he had noticed lots of other things; the heavy traffic, the stages, the newsboys and apple-venders shouting in the streets. Homesick for the country, he had walked one day all the way to the Central Park, and seen the beautiful bird houses, each with its name painted on it, “City Hall,” “Battery,” and so on, built for the sparrows imported two or three years before from England. And at his hotel he had made the trip up to his room in the Vertical Railway, going straight up like a frog in a well-bucket.

“Oh, Victor! How dreadfully dangerous!” cried Mamma, pressing her hands above her heart and looking quite faint. “Thank goodness, we have you safely back!”

“I wouldn’t be afraid to ride in the Vertical Railway,” Maggie boasted, but May copied Mamma, pressing her hands to her apron bib and crying, “Oh, Papa! I’d have been frightened to death!”

===Chinese===
“给你们三个的，就这些了。”

“门厅里还有一个包裹呢。”梅提醒道，生怕爸爸忘了它。

“哎呀，啧啧，小宝贝，可别贪心！”妈妈又惊又责备地说。

给妈妈的礼物是一整套花饰（parure）：发间一顶花环，胸前一带花束，裙上几枝花彩。都是矢车菊配麦穗做的，而且是从巴黎来的！

“是不是真的，披肩已经不时兴了，人人都改穿‘祖祖服’（zou-zous）了？”妈妈很想知道。可爸爸没留意过这个。

不过，别样东西他留意了许许多多：熙攘的车马，公共马车，还有在街上吆喝的报童和卖苹果的小贩。他惦念乡间，有一天一路步行到中央公园（Central Park），看见那些漂亮的鸟舍，每座上面都漆着自己的名字——“市政厅”呀，“炮台”呀——都是给两三年前从英国引进的麻雀盖的。在旅馆里，他还坐“垂直铁道”（Vertical Railway）上楼回房间走了一遭，直上直下，活像井桶里的一只青蛙。

“哦，维克托！多吓人，多危险呀！”妈妈喊道，双手按在心口上方，人眼看要晕过去。“谢天谢地，你总算平平安安回来了！”

“坐垂直铁道我才不怕呢。”玛吉夸口道。梅却学妈妈的样，双手按在围裙的护胸上，喊道：“哦，爸爸！我会给吓死的！”

===Original===
Papa had brought home some new music, “Dream on, Lillie,” and “If Thou hast Crushed a Flower,” a duet called “Home of My Youth,” and two humorous songs, “The Girls are not so Green” and “What they do at the Springs.”

“I hope they’re not *too* comical, love,” said Mamma, remembering one called “Skedaddle” that had been.

They tried over the duet together.

> “ ‘Home of youth! all thy pleasures
 
 
 Are impressed on my heart—
 
 
 Ere they fade from my mem’ry
 
 
 Life itself must depart.’ ”

Oh, beautiful voices, rising and falling together! May had taken her new doll to the kitchen to show Chloe and Martha, and Lily had trotted after her; but Maggie leaned against Papa, weak and heavy with love, feeling through all her body his kind, careless hand on her shoulder.

> “ ‘In the land of the stranger,
 
 
 Sighs and tears are but mine—’ ”

Mamma sang the heartbroken words in a voice like the chirping of a contented canary, and Papa’s voice swelled out, jubilant, triumphant:

> “ ‘Sighs and tears are but mine!’ ”

and he looked at her, smiling, forgetting Maggie, forgetting everything but Mamma.

===Chinese===
爸爸带回家一些新乐谱：《梦下去吧，莉莉》（Dream on, Lillie）、《你若碾碎了一朵花》（If Thou hast Crushed a Flower），一支名叫《我青春的家》（Home of My Youth）的二重唱，还有两首诙谐歌，《姑娘们可没那么嫩》（The Girls are not so Green）和《他们在温泉做些什么》（What they do at the Springs）。

“但愿它们别太逗了，亲爱的。”妈妈说，想起从前有一支叫《溜之大吉》（Skedaddle）的，就逗过了头。

他们一起试唱了那支二重唱。

> “ ‘青春的家！你的种种欢愉，
> 深深印在我心上——
> 趁它们还未从记忆淡去，
> 生命便不能先亡。’ ”

哦，多美的歌声，一同扬起，一同低回！梅带着新娃娃到厨房给克洛艾和玛莎（Martha）看去了，莉莉蹬蹬蹬地跟在她后头；只有玛吉偎在爸爸身上，被爱泡得又软又沉，隔着整个身子，觉出他那只慈爱而漫不经心的手搭在自己肩头。

> “ ‘身在异乡作客，
> 叹息与眼泪唯我独尝——’ ”

妈妈唱着这些令人心碎的句子，嗓音却像一只心满意足的金丝雀的啭鸣；爸爸的歌声随之扬起，欢欣鼓舞，得胜凯旋一般：

> “ ‘叹息与眼泪唯我独尝！’ ”

他望着她，微笑着，忘了玛吉，忘了一切，心里只有妈妈。

===Original===
“How have you been, my darling?”

“Nicely, thank you, Victor.”

“Margaret, Margaret—!”

“Sam brought over your new horse,” she said quickly, smiling and embarrassed.

“I must go out and have a look at her. I’m afraid I shouldn’t have bought her, Margaret—my business didn’t turn out quite as well as I thought it was going to.”

“Oh, but you wanted that horse so much! I’ll save some way—I’d rather fix up my old hoops and make them do another year than have you go without!”

===Chinese===
“这些日子你可好，亲爱的？”

“很好，谢谢你，维克托。”

“玛格丽特，玛格丽特——！”

“萨姆把你的新马牵过来了。”她赶紧说，笑着，有点儿窘。

“我得出去瞧瞧它。恐怕我不该买它，玛格丽特——我这趟生意做得不像原先想的那么顺。”

“哦，可你多想要那匹马呀！我总有法子省出来——宁可把我的旧裙撑修修再撑一年，也不能叫你缺了这个！”

===Original===
How funny and sweet and touching she was! He loved her so that he could hardly bear it.

“Margaret—I have something to show you—”

It was in the big parcel in the hall—how had he ever gotten it home in the steam-cars? When the paper fell away, there pranced a rocking-horse, so bright, so freshly painted that it was still a little sticky, dappled grey and white, with wide scarlet nostrils.

“For the baby!”

“For *Lily*?” But she knew he didn’t mean for Lily. Her head drooped, her eyelids fell. She was dreadfully embarrassed.

“No—for our little boy.”

===Chinese===
她多么可笑，多么甜美，又多么动人啊！他爱她爱得几乎承受不住。

“玛格丽特——我有个东西给你看——”

东西在门厅的大包裹里——他是怎么坐着火车把它弄到家的？包装纸剥开，一匹摇木马神气活现地立在那儿，那么鲜亮，漆新得还微微发黏，灰白相间的斑纹，两个鼻孔张得老大，猩红猩红的。

“给小宝宝买的！”

“给莉莉的？”可她知道他不是给莉莉的。她的头垂了下去，眼睑落了下来。她窘极了。

“不——给咱们的小儿子。”

---

===Original===
They all went out on the lawn to see Papa try the new mare. The sky was full of ragged clouds, streaming and free, and the river was covered with whitecaps. How the wind blew! It sent the water from the iron calla lilies streaming to one side in thin veils, it banged the shutters, it blew the little girl’s skirts so that you could see their drawers up to their waists. They jumped up and down, wild with excitement, and they could feel their new lockets jumping too, tap, tap.

“That’s a beautiful mare!” screamed Cousin Lizzie to Mamma, trying to make herself heard above the wind. The Blows had come as Papa was trotting down the road, and stood with Mamma on the steps watching him galloping back.

“Full of spirits—nervous as a witch!” Cousin Sam shouted. “That’s the kind Victor likes.”

===Chinese===
一家子都出到草坪上，看爸爸试新母马。满天是破絮般的云，飞也似的，自由自在，河面翻满了白色浪头。风刮得多大呀！它把铁马蹄莲里喷出的水刮成薄薄的纱幔，斜斜地飘向一边；它撞得百叶窗砰砰作响；它掀起小姑娘们的裙子，叫人把她们的衬裤一直看到腰际。她们蹦上蹦下，兴奋得发了狂，还觉得出新盒坠也在胸口一跳一跳，嗒，嗒。

“好一匹漂亮的母马！”丽齐表姐顶着风尖声喊给妈妈听。布洛一家是爸爸沿着大路小跑出去的时候来的，这会儿同妈妈一起站在台阶上，望他飞奔回来。

“浑身是劲儿——疯得像女巫似的！”萨姆表哥（Cousin Sam）喊道，“维克托中意的正是这一种。”

===Original===
Papa tore into the driveway, Trusty yelping behind him, and flung up a welcoming hand to them just as the wind caught a little white shawl from his wife’s shoulders and flung it flapping into the mare’s face. As she swerved with a great buck, he shot into the air and fell headfirst on the road.

“God, his neck’s broken!” Sam Blow cried: and Lizzie began to scream, thin and high above the wind, like a rabbit in a trap. But no one paid any attention to her, for Margaret had gone crumpling down onto the steps.

In the last hour of Victor Campion’s birthday his son was born.

===Chinese===
爸爸旋风似的冲进车道，特拉斯蒂（Trusty）汪汪叫着追在后面；他正朝大家扬手致意，恰恰在这时，一阵风卷起他妻子肩头的一条小白披肩，啪啪地甩在了母马脸上。母马猛地尥了个大蹶子，身子一歪，他一下子被抛上半空，头朝下摔在了大路上。

“天哪，他脖子摔断了！”萨姆·布洛喊道；丽齐尖叫起来，那声音又细又高，压过了风声，像落进陷阱的兔子。可谁也没顾上理她，因为玛格丽特已经瘫软着倒在了台阶上。

在维克托·坎皮恩生日的最后一个钟头，他的儿子降生了。

## IV / IV

===Original===
One of the cherry trees in the garden was dead, and over it grew a wistaria vine, lavendar-blue, a heaven-colored tent. The long sprays of flowers fell down like a curtain, the light that came through was stained a lovely color, and there was a lavender-blue carpet of fallen petals on the grass. The bees in the wistaria made a sound like the sound in a seashell, only louder, but they never bothered the little girls. Old Toot said they wouldn’t, and they didn’t.

“Dem bees ain’ gwine bodder you, ’outen you bodder dem.”

===Chinese===
花园里有一棵樱桃树枯死了，一株紫藤爬满了它，开出薰衣草似的蓝花，像一顶天空颜色的帐篷。长长的花串垂挂下来，宛如一道帘幕，透过帘幕的光线被染成一种可爱的颜色，草地上铺着一层落花，像薰衣草蓝的地毯。紫藤深处的蜜蜂发出一种海螺里才有的嗡嗡声，只是更响些，可它们从来不搅扰小姑娘们。老图特说它们不会，它们就当真没有。

“蜜蜂不会来搅扰你们，除非你们先去搅扰它们。”


===Original===
Black Susannah brought the baby out, lying on his pillows in the big wash-basket, and left him under the wistaria where his sisters were playing. Fat little Lily tried to give him some of her bread and butter and sugar, poking it at his mouth, and he lifted his feeble voice in a mewing cry.

“*Lily!*” cried Maggie, outraged and important. “Naughty! You *mustn’t* give Baby things to eat! Was he frightened, then? Did he want his Maggie?”

She knelt by the basket smiling at little Victor; and in her old brown dress, with her thin face gentle and bright with love, she looked like the youngest shepherd, kneeling to worship the Baby who lay in a manger.

May came and knelt on the other side, putting out a finger for the tiny groping fist to close around.

“Look! He’s holding on to me! He loves me best because he knows I love him best!”

===Chinese===
黑人苏珊娜（Black Susannah）把小宝宝抱了出来，让他枕着自己的小枕头躺在大洗衣篮里，搁在紫藤底下姐姐们玩耍的地方。胖乎乎的小莉莉想把抹了黄油、撒了糖的面包分他一点，往他嘴里戳，他便细声细气地哼出一声猫叫似的啼哭。

“莉莉！”玛吉喝道，又气恼又俨然一派大人的威严。“淘气！不准拿东西给宝宝吃！他是吓着了吗？是不是想要他的玛吉了？”

她在篮子旁跪下来，笑吟吟地望着小维克托；她穿着那件旧棕色裙子，瘦瘦的小脸因爱意而温柔发亮，看上去活像最年幼的那个牧羊人，跪着礼拜卧在马槽里的圣婴。

梅走过来在另一边跪下，伸出一根手指，让那只四处摸索的小拳头攥住。

“看！他抓住我啦！他最爱我，因为他知道我最爱他！”


===Original===
“You do not love him best!”

May affected not to hear, but said to the baby in a high small voice, copying Mamma and Aunt Priscilla:

“*Did*dun he know I loved him besty, besty, best? *Did*dun he then? He says, ‘yes,’ *he* knew!”

“You do not love him best!”

“I do so!”

“I love him best because I’m oldest, so now, miss! And, if you ever say I don’t, I’ll knock you down!”

Lily didn’t understand about the baby.

===Chinese===
“你并不是最爱他的！”

梅装作没听见，只学着妈妈和普丽西拉婶婶的腔调，尖起细细的嗓门对宝宝说：

“他是不是知道呀，我最疼他啦，最疼、最疼、最最疼？是不是呀？他说：‘知道呢，’他早知道啦！”

“你并不是最爱他的！”

“我就是最爱！”

“我最大，最爱他的当然是我，就是这样，小姐！你要是敢说半个不字，我就把你打倒在地！”

莉莉闹不清这小宝宝的来历。


===Original===
“Did Papa bring him from New York?” she asked; for she remembered the big parcel in the hall the day Papa came home. She and May had picked a tiny hole in the wrapping paper, feeling dreadfully guilty and as nervous as two mice, until suddenly a large eye stared out at them and they had fled in terror. It might have been the baby.

“Yo’ ma find him in de ga’den undeh a cu’ant bush,” Chloe told them; but May and Lily had looked for babies among the currant bushes every day since, and hadn’t found one, so they were beginning to doubt it. Aunt Priscilla said Dr. Chase had brought him in his bag; and Cousin Jennie Blodgett, who stayed with them while Mamma was ill, and let them dress up in her bonnet and shawl, and made them cup-custards that they didn’t like very much but ate politely, told them the stork had brought him, and showed them the stork’s picture in “Hans Andersen.”

===Chinese===
“是爸爸从纽约把他带回来的吗？”她问；因为她还记得爸爸回家那天，门厅里摆着的那只大包裹。她和梅在包装纸上戳了个小窟窿，愧疚得要命，又紧张得像两只小老鼠，冷不防一只大眼睛瞪出来望着她们，吓得两人撒腿就逃。那说不定就是这小宝宝呢。

“你们妈呀，是在园子里醋栗丛底下捡着他的。”克洛艾告诉她们；可打那以后，梅和莉莉天天都到醋栗丛里找小宝宝，一只也没找着，心里便渐渐犯起嘀咕来。普丽西拉婶婶说，是蔡斯医生（Dr. Chase）用提包把他带来的；妈妈卧病期间来照看她们的珍妮·布洛杰特表姐（Cousin Jennie Blodgett）——她让她们戴她的软帽、披她的披肩打扮着玩，还给她们做过杯装蛋奶冻，她们不大爱吃，却也客客气气地吃了——说他是鹳鸟送来的，还翻出《安徒生童话》（Hans Andersen）里鹳鸟的图画给她们看。


===Original===
Mamma said God had sent an angel with the baby to comfort them because Papa had gone to heaven.

There seemed to be a difference of opinion among the authorities.

Maggie was inclined to believe the Dr. Chase theory, for she had seen him go into Mamma’s room with his black bag; and later, lying awake on a tear-soaked pillow, she had first heard that little mewing cry. But, since Mamma thought it was God and an angel, she wasn’t going to tell her it was only Dr. Chase.

They thought at first little Victor would slip away like Victoria, Anna Louisa, Sophia and Adelaide. He was so tiny, so weak. Wrinkled and red as a poppy-bud, he lay wrapped in warm blankets, slept, woke to weep, and slept.

===Chinese===
妈妈说，因为爸爸上天堂去了，上帝特意派了一位天使，抱着小宝宝来安慰他们。

这些权威之间，看来是意见不一。

玛吉比较倾向于蔡斯医生那一说，因为她亲眼见过他提着黑提包走进妈妈的房间；后来她睁着眼睛躺在泪湿的枕头上，头一回听见那声小猫叫似的啼哭。不过，妈妈既然认定是上帝和天使，她就不打算告诉妈妈，说那不过是个蔡斯医生。

起初大家都以为，小维克托也会像维多利亚、安娜·路易莎、索菲娅和阿德莱德那样悄悄溜走。他那么小，那么弱。他皱巴巴、红彤彤的，活像一粒罂粟花苞，裹在暖毯子里躺着，睡了，醒来哭一场，又睡。


===Original===
“We will both go to Victor,” said Mamma pathetically, propped among her pillows, while her tears fell into her bowl of arrowroot.

“Try just a *spoon*ful of the wine jelly, love!” begged Cousin Jennie, with tears on her own cheeks.

“How can you ask me, Jennie?” Mamma’s eyes reproached her.

“For little Victor’s sake!”

For little Victor’s sake, she tried; and presently, somehow, the saucer was empty.

===Chinese===
“我们娘儿俩这就一起到维克托那儿去了。”妈妈悲悲切切地说，靠坐在一堆枕头里，眼泪一滴一滴落进她那碗竹芋粉糊里。

“哪怕就吃一小勺酒冻呢，亲爱的！”珍妮表姐央求道，自己腮帮上也挂着泪珠。

“你怎么开得了这个口，珍妮？”妈妈的眼睛在责备她。

“看在小维克托的份上！”

看在小维克托的份上，她试了试；不多一会儿，也不知怎么的，碟子竟见了底。


===Original===
For little Victor’s sake, they would all do anything. He was the center of the household, the center of the universe. In April, a great fleet sailed down the river; further south battles were fought; presently summer added its heat to the fever that burned in the prisons; the sun set in melting pink and gold; the stars shone in the sky; but these things were unnoticed. Victor sneezed his first tiny sneeze like a kitten’s; he smiled his first smile; he was carried to church in his high-waisted, puff-sleeved christening robe that Mamma had embroidered all over with incredibly tiny flowers and leaves, and was given his father’s name. He was the man of the house, and his women surrounded him, worshipping.

===Chinese===
看在小维克托的份上，一家人什么都肯做。他是这个家的中心，是宇宙的中心。四月里，一支庞大的舰队顺河而下；更南边的地方正打着仗；不久，夏天又给监狱里焚烧般的热病添上暑气；太阳熔成一片粉红金黄沉落下去；星星在天上闪亮；可这些都无人留意。小维克托打出了第一声小小的喷嚏，像小猫打的一样；他绽出了第一个微笑；他穿上高腰、泡泡袖的受洗袍——满袍都是妈妈绣的花草，细小得叫人不敢相信——被抱到教堂去，得了他父亲的名字。他就是这宅子里的男人了，他的女人们围着他，一心一意地敬拜着他。


===Original===
Uncle Willie tried to explain the state of Papa’s financial affairs to Mamma.

“Do you mean we’re *paupers*?”

“No, no!” Good heavens, was she going to cry again? “You’re very well-to-do, only Victor didn’t leave as much as I expected him to. The war’s hit us both; though nothing to compare to any number of others.”

“But I don’t see how the war concerns us﻿—we haven’t any slaves.” For Victor’s grandfather had freed his slaves by manumission long before, though they had gone on living with the family, and were buried, when they died, at the feet of their masters and mistresses in the family burying-ground.

“Well﻿—there are other considerations,” said Uncle Willie, prudently skipping on. “But the point is, you won’t be able to go on quite as you have been going. Have you any idea what you’ve been spending a year?”

===Chinese===
威利叔叔想法子向妈妈讲解爸爸财务上的境况。

“你是说，我们沦为贫民了？”

“不，不！”天哪，她该不会又要哭了吧？“你们家底很殷实，只是维克托留下的没有我原先指望的那么多。这场战争你我都挨了打击，虽说比起许许多多别人家来，又算不得什么。”

“可我不明白这场战争跟我们有什么相干——我们家又没有奴隶。”因为维克托的祖父早已立下释放文书，把奴隶全都放了；虽说他们后来依旧跟着一家人过活，去世的时候，也葬在自家的墓园里，安卧在自家老爷太太的脚下。

“唔——还有别的种种考量，”威利叔叔说，审慎地把话头岔了过去。“可要紧的是，你们没法儿再照原来的样子过下去了。你心里有没有数，一年你们要花掉多少钱？”


===Original===
“I’m afraid I don’t understand anything about figures,” Mamma replied complacently.

“No, ladies aren’t to be expected to. But, of course, you’ll want to be near your father and mother now, and a smaller house would make a great difference.”

“Oh, no, Willie! I could never leave The Maples.”

“But it’s a big place, and expensive to keep up.”

“Dearest Victor would wish us to stay,” said Mamma with placid finality. It was her answer to everything. “Dearest Victor would wish it,” or “Dearest Victor wouldn’t,” and simply meant “I will” or “I won’t.”

“God give me patience!” Uncle Willie cried within himself; and he thought quite kindly of Aunt Priscilla, who at least knew she didn’t know anything.

===Chinese===
“只怕数目字上的事我一窍不通，”妈妈答道，语气里还带着几分怡然自得。

“是啊，对太太小姐们原就不能指望这个。不过，你如今总归想住得离你父亲母亲近些，换座小一点的宅子，情形就大不一样了。”

“哦，不，威利！我永远也离不开枫树庄（The Maples）。”

“可那宅子太大，维持起来花费不小。”

“最亲爱的维克托会愿意我们留下的。”妈妈说，语气平静，却一锤定音。这是她对一切事情的回答。“最亲爱的维克托会愿意”，或者“最亲爱的维克托不会愿意”，意思无非是“我愿意”，或者“我不愿意”。

“上帝赐我耐心吧！”威利叔叔在心里喊道；他倒挺宽厚地想起了普丽西拉婶婶——她至少还知道自己什么都不知道。


===Original===
“We can economize in so many ways, now that darling Victor’s gone,” Mamma said through her pocket handkerchief, when Willie’s conscientious floods of figures ceased. “The meat bills will be *nothing*! Gentlemen have such hearty appetites. But I’m sure I don’t care if I never see a roast of beef again﻿—just a little dish of creamed sweetbreads now and then, and we have our own chickens. And *clothes*! The price of a gentleman’s top hat alone! And, perhaps, I might possibly let Susannah go﻿—she’s very lazy. I don’t know what girls are coming to nowadays,” she added, but absently, not receiving or expecting an answer. She had said it so often, and would say it so often again.

“But Victor wouldn’t want me to give up the conservatory, or the horses and carriages. And I suppose, after all, the children should have roast beef﻿—and Susannah *is* very fond of the baby.”

“Well, you’ll still save on poor old Victor’s hats,” said Uncle Willie sardonically.

“Oh, yes! And coats as well, and waistcoats and﻿—and other things.” She blushed brightly. She had nearly said “trousers” to a gentleman.

“The woman’s a fool,” he thought, though not with any shock of discovery. But the May day was warm and beautiful, the lilies of the valley growing beside the porch sent up their perfume, and Mamma looked pretty and appealing, though plump, in her black dress and little white crêpe “Marie Stuart” bonnet, so that presently, his nose buried in mint and the frost thick on his glass, he was thinking indulgently, “Poor little woman!”

===Chinese===
等威利那出自责任心、滔滔不绝的数字总算止住了，妈妈才隔着小手绢开口：“亲爱的维克托去了，我们可以从好多地方省出钱来。肉钱会省到不值一提！绅士们的胃口多好呀。不过说真的，往后就是再看不见一炉烤牛肉，我也不在乎——隔三差五来一小碟奶油牛胸腺就成，鸡是自家养的。还有衣裳！光一位绅士的大礼帽就得多少钱！还有，兴许，苏珊娜我也可以打发走——她懒得很。如今这些姑娘们，真不知道要变成什么样了，”她又添了一句，却是心不在焉的，既没听着谁答话，也没指望谁答话。这话她已经说过不知多少回，往后还要再说上不知多少回。

“可维克托不会愿意我舍弃花房，也不会愿意我舍弃那些马和马车。再说，孩子们到底还是该吃烤牛肉的——而且苏珊娜又实在疼这小宝宝。”

“唔，可怜的老维克托的帽子钱，你总归还是省下了。”威利叔叔讥诮地说。

“哦，是啊！还有外套，还有马甲，还有——还有别的东西。”她脸上飞起鲜亮的红晕。她差点儿当着一位绅士说出“长裤”这个词来。

“这女人是个傻瓜，”他想，不过这发现也没让他多么震惊。可五月的这天温暖明媚，廊子边上长着的铃兰送来阵阵香气，妈妈穿着黑裙子，戴一顶小小的白绉纱“玛丽·斯图亚特”（Marie Stuart）软帽，虽说丰腴，却显得又好看又叫人怜爱；于是不多时，他的鼻尖埋进薄荷枝里，杯壁上结着厚厚的白霜，他心里宽厚地想道：“可怜的小女人！”

## V / V

===Original===
The baby wailed and drooped through the heat of his first summer; but, when Mamma cut long sprays of pungent chrysanthemums for the brass vases that stood on the altar to the glory of God and in memory of Mary Clarissa Campion, when burrs pattered down from the chestnut trees, and the frost touched the persimmons with orange and vermilion, he grew stronger, looked about him with interest, and presently was amiably plunging from outstretched hands to outstretched hands.

Other events beside Victor’s first steps, his first words, his teething, and his whooping-cough took place, though none seemed so important. Trains grew on to dresses, and crinolines were full at the bottom instead of the top, so that ladies turned from tulips to morning glories. Pamela bonnets, like saucers with strings, became so fashionable that even Mamma, who prided herself on being conservative, succumbed; although she only wore hers in the carriage, considering it far too conspicuous and coquettish to wear when she was on foot. Lizzie Blow stopped going to church and took up Spiritualism, tipped tables, and asked for one loud rap if “yes,” two if “no.” One day, when the stars were full of portent, the Prince and Princess of Wales were married in London and old Toot died in his whitewashed cabin under the weeping willow. His place was taken by Caesar, who, under Mamma’s direction, planted ribbon borders and the fashionable new pincushion beds here and there about the place; and two new iron urns on the terrace boiled over with petunias and sent up jets of ornamental grass. In winter, when she could not be among her flowers, Mamma learned new stitches in knitting and crochet, and everything in the house became covered with wool-work as things in damp countries are covered with mold﻿—even the bird cages dripped crochet borders. And, when she was not busy with other things, she wound bandages and felt badly about the war﻿—almost as badly as she felt about Victor’s croup and the naughty way in which Lily had taken to stealing sugar.
===Chinese===
小宝宝熬着他头一个盛夏的酷热，哭哭啼啼，蔫蔫萎萎；可是，等妈妈剪下气味辛烈的菊花长枝，插上圣坛的铜花瓶——那花瓶供在那里，为的是荣耀上帝，并纪念玛丽·克拉丽莎·坎皮恩（Mary Clarissa Campion）——等栗子球的刺壳噼噼啪啪从栗树上掉下来，寒霜给柿子染上橙色与朱红，他便一天天壮实起来，饶有兴致地四下张望，不多久就能笑呵呵地从这双伸出的手里，扑腾到那双伸出的手里去了。

除了维克托的初学走路、初开口说话、出牙和百日咳，别的种种事情也在发生，只是没有一件显得这样要紧。裙裾添起了拖尾，克里诺林裙撑下部放宽、上部收窄，女士们便从郁金香变成了牵牛花。帕梅拉软帽——活像系了帽带的茶碟——风行一时，连以稳重守旧自许的妈妈也缴了械；只是她那顶专在坐马车时才戴，若要戴着走路，她嫌太惹眼、太轻佻。丽齐·布洛不再上教堂，改奉了通灵术，玩起转桌，请它“是”就大声敲一记，“否”就敲两记。有一天，满天星象凶兆丛生，威尔士亲王和王妃在伦敦成了婚，老图特则在垂柳底下他那间刷白的小屋里咽了气。接替他的是凯撒（Caesar），他在妈妈的指点下，在园子里东一片西一片种上缎带花境和时新的针垫花坛；平台上两只新添的铁瓮里，矮牵牛沸沸扬扬满得直往外溢，观赏草一束束像喷泉似的往上蹿。到了冬天，没法守着她的花儿，妈妈学起编织和钩针的新针法，家里样样东西都罩上了毛线绣，就像潮湿地带的东西长霉——连鸟笼都垂挂着钩针花边。手头没别的事的时候，她就卷绷带，为战争难过——难过得几乎跟为维克托的哮吼、为莉莉新学会的偷糖毛病一样。

===Original===
Terrible and stirring names sounded through those days, piercing even her dreamy mistiness, the Seven Days’ Battles, Fredericksburg and Gettysburg, Lookout Mountain and Missionary Ridge, the Battle of the Wilderness. Her eyes would brim over with tears, and whispering, “Oh, the poor mothers!” she would catch her son to her breast.

The soldiers went south past The Maples, on foot, by train, or sailing down the river in great fleets of many-masted schooners. The sails of the full-rigged ships swelling in the wind, shining in the sun, stirred the heart and made war wonderful and romantic; but the tired men plodding past in clouds of dust on the road were different. Looking at them, the bugle-calls sounded faintly, the glory dimmed, and pity filled the heart. The little girls watched them from behind the hedge, and once Lily thought she saw Papa marching past, and ran out to him. But she got mixed up in the marching legs and knew she was lost forever until a kind soldier with a grey mustache picked her up, smudged the dust and tears on her face with a hand as spiritually gentle as it was physically rough, and swung away to become one of those countless tiny figures fighting together against the infinite and dramatic background of space, stars, thunder, and eternity or annihilation.

The talk of war was everywhere﻿—monitors, the draft, buying substitutes. Between North and South, they felt pressure from both sides. Mr. Brown was ridden on a rail by masked men﻿—which side were they on? No one knew. Cousin Willie, supposed to have southern sympathies, was threatened with tarring and feathering, but so was that violent Yankee, Mr. Farley. The war charged the air, changing everything, giving everything an excitement, an emotional tensity. Wellington Carter was being stupid at the store, breaking the candles and dropping the change on the floor﻿—and before the candles all were burned he was being buried at Mount Pleasant with military honors. Sorrow was the common state, and boys who had marched away to fife and drum came home by the streets of silence.
===Chinese===
那些日子里，一个个可怕而撼人心魄的名字响个不停——七日战役，弗雷德里克斯堡与葛底斯堡，卢考特山与传教士岭，荒野战役——连她那迷迷蒙蒙的恍惚劲儿也被穿透了。她眼睛里总会涌满泪水，喃喃说着“哦，那些可怜的母亲们！”，把她的儿子一把搂进怀里。

士兵们打枫树庄旁开赴南方，有的步行，有的坐火车，有的乘着一艘艘多桅帆船结成的大船队顺流而下。张满帆的船，帆在风里鼓胀，在日光下闪耀，看得人心头发烫，把战争衬得又美妙又浪漫；可大路上拖着步子、在滚滚尘土里走过的疲惫士兵就不同了。望着他们，军号声听着就远了，光辉也就黯了，心里涨满的是怜悯。小姑娘们躲在树篱后面望。有一回，莉莉以为瞧见爸爸正随队伍走过，便朝他跑去。可她一头扎进迈动的大腿中间，只知道自己这下永远迷路了，直到一个蓄灰胡子的和气士兵把她抱起来，用一只手抹去她脸上的尘土和眼泪——那手粗鲁得很，心肠却温柔得很——然后一转身走开去，变成那千千万万细小身影中的一个，同其余的人一起，在苍穹、星辰、雷霆、永生或寂灭那无垠而壮阔的背景上厮杀。

战争的言谈无处不在——铁甲炮舰啦，征兵抽丁啦，出钱雇人顶替啦。夹在北方与南方之间，他们两头都感到逼迫。布朗先生（Mr. Brown）被蒙面人架上横木游了街——他们是哪一边的？没人知道。据说心向南方的威利叔叔，吃到了泼柏油、粘羽毛的恐吓，可那个暴烈的北方佬法利先生（Mr. Farley）也同样吃到。战争给空气充上了电，改变着一切，叫一切都带上一种亢奋、一种情绪上的紧张。惠灵顿·卡特（Wellington Carter）还在铺子里犯傻——碰断蜡烛，把找的零钱撒在地上——还没等那些蜡烛一根根点完，他已葬进芒特普莱森特（Mount Pleasant），得了带军礼的哀荣。悲伤成了常态；伴着横笛与军鼓开拔出去的少年们，是打一条条无声的街道回家的。

===Original===
“Thank God, Victor is a baby!” Mamma cried, listening to the church bell tolling. “And after all the suffering this one has brought, surely, surely, there’ll never be another war!”

The war was like distant thunder; threatening, warning, although the sun shone on the still garden. But it was Maggie in whose heart it echoed loudest.

Aunt Priscilla bought one of the new green-grey Rogers’ groups, and put it in the bay window along with the draggled lace curtains, the hanging baskets of sick ferns, and the parrot in his dirty cage; and Maggie would steal in to look at it, aflame with pity for the wounded scout, wandering in the swamp supported by a ragged negro. So sick, so sick, the veins standing out terribly on his arm under a tourniquet. Once, when no one was near, she touched her lips softly, quickly to that tortured hand, while a wordless passion flooded her to help﻿—she didn’t know how, she didn’t know who﻿—just to help. It was only her adoration of her little brother that kept her from running away and trying to enter the army disguised as a drummer boy.
===Chinese===
“感谢上帝，维克托还是个小娃娃！”妈妈听着教堂敲响的丧钟，哭喊道。“这个孩子已经惹来这许多苦楚，往后肯定、肯定再不会有另一场战争了！”

战争像远方的雷声，虽有太阳照着静谧的花园，它仍旧威胁着、警告着。可这雷声在心里回响得最响的，是玛吉。

普丽西拉婶婶买了一座新出的绿灰色罗杰斯群像（Rogers' groups），摆进凸窗，跟那拖沓的蕾丝窗帘、吊着病恹恹蕨草的吊篮、脏笼子里的鹦鹉放在一处；玛吉常常悄悄溜进去看它，为那受伤的侦察兵心疼得火烧火燎——他由一个衣衫褴褛的黑人搀着，跋涉在沼泽里。病得那样重，那样重，胳膊上扎着止血带，带子底下的血管怕人地凸起着。有一回，四下无人，她把嘴唇轻轻地、飞快地贴上那只受难的手，一股无以言传的激情涌遍全身，她要去救助——不知怎么救，不知救谁——只是要去救。若不是割舍不下她的小弟弟，她早就离家出走，扮成个鼓手小子混进军队里去了。

===Original===
Then one April day, when Caesar was showing the children a nest of blue eggs in a hawthorn tree and Mamma was admiring her tulips, Uncle Willie rode over to tell them President Lincoln had been assassinated.

It was like a sad, exciting story in a book to them, it was not reality. Their world was The Maples; and the road, the river, the yellow day-lilies in the tall wet grass along the meadow fence, marked the world’s ends.
===Chinese===
后来，四月里的一天，凯撒正指给孩子们看山楂树上一窝蓝莹莹的鸟蛋，妈妈正在赏她的郁金香，威利叔叔骑马过来告诉他们，林肯总统遇刺了。

这对他们来说，像书里一个又悲惨又刺激的故事，不是真事。他们的世界就是枫树庄；大路，河流，草场栅栏边高高的湿草里黄灿灿的萱草花，便标出了世界的尽头。

===Original===
Year after year, the frogs thrilled out their song, the ferns uncurled their woolly balls, the sweet rain fell, and summer came, just as if Papa had not died. Mamma was so happy with the children, the garden, the lawn, gold-green in the summer sunshine, the golden lazy days. She would never admit, never even realize consciously that his death meant a strain removed; but even his loss, dimmed by a few years, added to the perfection and harmony of her days like minor chords of music.

Her black crêpes melted into violet muslins; less and less often she said to the awed and solemn little girls, “You *do* remember dear Papa, don’t you, darlings?” More and more often the cornucopia, held before his picture by a bronze hand in a neatly fluted cuff, was empty of its pansies (for thoughts). Nothing was left of his passion, his jokes, his funny songs, his sudden flashing tempers, his love﻿—nothing but a sigh, and a few tears from Mamma on a wet day, when the children or the servants had been tiresome or when she had a headache; his watch-pocket still hanging over the big bed in her room; and Maggie’s missing him.

Sometimes it seemed as if Maggie were the only one who ever thought of Papa now. But someone else remembered.
===Chinese===
一年又一年，青蛙抖擞精神唱起歌，蕨草舒展开毛茸茸的拳芽，甜丝丝的雨落下来，夏天到了，就仿佛爸爸没有死过。妈妈有孩子们，有花园，有草坪——夏日阳光下金绿金绿的草坪——有那些金灿灿、懒洋洋的日子，好不快活。她永远不会承认，甚至从来不会清醒地意识到，他的死意味着卸下了一层紧绷；然而就连失去他这件事，隔上几年淡下去之后，也像乐曲里的小调和弦，给她日子的圆满与和谐添上一笔。

她的黑纱渐渐化成了紫罗兰色的薄纱裙；她对那几个心怀敬畏、屏声敛气的小姑娘，越来越不常说“你们可记得亲爱的爸爸，记得的吧，宝贝们？”；他画像前那只丰饶角——由一只青铜的手举着，袖口熨帖地打着褶边——里面供的三色堇（花语是“思念”）越来越常常是空的。他的炽热，他的玩笑，他滑稽的歌，他电光石火般发作的脾气，他的爱——什么都不剩了，只剩一声叹息，只剩雨天里、孩子们或仆人闹得人心烦、或是她头疼时，妈妈落下的几滴眼泪；只剩他的挂表袋还悬在她房中那张大床上；还有玛吉的想他。

有时候，玛吉觉得如今仿佛只有她一个人还记得爸爸。可是还有一个人也记得。

===Original===
Poor Aunt Priscilla needed matches and cheese from the store, so she stood at her back door and feebly called towards the stable:

“Washington! Oh, Washington!”

But Washington, fat and black as a blackbird too fat and old to fly, felt the afternoon was over-hot for a drive, and remained where he was, comfortably hidden, mimicking his mistress under his breath:

“Wash’n’ton! Oh, Wash’n’ton!”
===Chinese===
可怜的普丽西拉婶婶要打发人到铺子买些火柴和奶酪，便站在后门口，朝马厩那边有气无力地喊：

“华盛顿！喂，华盛顿！”

可是华盛顿（Washington）——又肥又黑，像一只肥得、老得飞不动了的乌鸫——觉得这午后出车未免太热，便待在原处不动，舒舒服服地藏着，压低嗓子学舌他的女主人：

“华盛顿！喂——华——盛——顿！”

===Original===
So Aunt Priscilla started on foot for the store, trailing her magenta draperies through the dust, her face turning from mauve to purple as she plodded along. She was thinking she would have to give up and sit down in the ditch in the shade by the side of the road, when Cousin Lizzie Blow in her little low pony carriage came rolling along in a cloud of dust, and picked her up.

“You’ll have a stroke if you don’t take care,” she said, her eyes slipping over Priscilla’s wet purple face, the point lace collar pinned on all crooked, and the lunch crumbs nested in the folds of her bodice. She herself was exquisitely neat. Her black and scarlet looked as impossible to disarrange or dim as the black and scarlet of a ladybug; and she was cool-looking, too, except for her red cheeks, one a little redder than the other, almost as red as the poppy-colored Cashmere of her Garibaldi bodice.
===Chinese===
于是普丽西拉婶婶只得步行去铺子，洋红色的裙裾一路拖过尘土，脸也一步一步从藕荷色涨成了紫色。她正想着索性认命，坐进路边背阴的沟里歇歇脚，丽齐·布洛表姐驾着她那辆低低的小马车，卷着一蓬尘土赶了上来，把她捎走了。

“你再不当心，可要中风的。”她说，眼睛扫过普丽西拉汗津津的紫红脸蛋、别得歪歪扭扭的针绣蕾丝衣领、前襟褶子里安窝的午餐碎屑。她自己却整洁得一丝不苟：那一身黑配猩红，就像瓢虫身上的黑与红，休想弄乱一星、磨暗一毫；她瞧着也凉快，只是两颊通红，一边比另一边还要红些，红得几乎跟她加里波第式（Garibaldi）上衣那罂粟红的开司米一个样了。

===Original===
“What on earth are you walking for on such a day, with your stable full of horses?”

“I wanted some cheese﻿—the mice are so bad and Willie likes it, too﻿—me, but it’s hot!” She mopped her face with a worked collar she had snatched up, mistaking it for a handkerchief. “I couldn’t find Washington﻿—I guess he was busy somewhere, and I don’t like to stop him. He says the work’s almost too much for him anyway. Wasn’t last night awful? I couldn’t stay in bed﻿—I sat by the window and fanned myself until after two. Willie says it was the hottest night in ever so long.”

“Was it? I didn’t notice.”

“Didn’t *notice*! Why, what ever were you doing?”

“I was talking to Victor.”

“Precious little fellow!”

“Oh, he seems a right nice little boy in spite of the way they spoil him﻿—too bad his ears stick out so dreadfully. Margaret ought to do something. But I don’t mean him. I mean his father. He was speaking to me last night.”
===Chinese===
“这么热的天，你家马厩里满满一圈马，怎么倒走起路来了？”

“我想要点儿奶酪——家里耗子闹得凶，威利也爱吃——唉哟，这天真热！”她抓起一条绣花衣领错当手帕，直往脸上擦。“我找不着华盛顿——我猜他在哪儿忙着呢，我又不愿打断他。他总说这些活计本来就够他呛。昨儿夜里不是热坏了么？我在床上待不住——坐在窗口给自己打扇，一直打到两点多。威利说，这是多少年来最热的一夜。”

“是么？我没留神。”

“竟没留神！咦，你到底在干什么来着？”

“我在跟维克托说话呢。”

“乖乖的小家伙！”

“哦，虽说他们把他惯得没了样子，他瞧着倒真是个挺不错的小男孩——只可惜那两只耳朵招风招得太厉害了。玛格丽特早该想个法子才好。可我不是说他。我是说他父亲。昨儿夜里，他在跟我说话呢。”

===Original===
Aunt Priscilla gave such a start she nearly went over the side of the pony carriage into the dusty yarrow. Of course, she knew about Lizzie’s spiritualism﻿—but she hadn’t mentioned it for ever so long﻿—and *this*﻿—oh, *dear*!

“He talks to me every night. It all began with the table tipping﻿—don’t you remember the afternoon you did it with me?”

Did she remember? Could she ever forget? She hadn’t slept for nights afterwards, and she had been almost afraid to go to church on Sunday, for fear God would strike her dead for being a necromancer. And Cousin Lizzie told her that was nothing to what the table had done afterwards, when she was alone. Aunt Priscilla had a vision of it running after Cousin Lizzie like a little dog, leaping up and down stairs on its mahogany legs and three claw feet, rapping out dreadful messages. Willie and Priscilla had gone to see the Blows one evening, and, when Lizzie set out homemade wine and sponge-cake on that table, Priscilla nearly had hysterics. She would as soon have eaten sponge-cake off a coffin.
