Mr. Lacey bringing her gloxinias for the conservatory, large bells of white and violet-blue.

Mr. Lacey in his tam-o’-shanter on a botanizing stroll, telling them all the wildflower names.

Mr. Lacey reading Tennyson and Longfellow aloud to her—so much prettier than that Keats that Papa had been so fond of, and you never felt nervous about what might be coming next.

Mr. Lacey in his blue flannels and his straw sailor hat with a blue ribbon taking them on the river in his sailboat so prettily named “Swallow.”

===Chinese===
莱西先生给她送大岩桐来，添进花房，一盏盏白色和紫蓝色的大铃铛。

莱西先生戴着他那顶苏格兰圆便帽（tam-o'-shanter）出去采集花草，一路把野花的名字讲给他们听。

莱西先生给她朗诵丁尼生和朗费罗——比爸爸生前偏爱得很的那位济慈悦耳多了，而且你永远不必提心吊胆，不知道下一句会冒出什么来。

莱西先生穿着蓝法兰绒衣裤，戴一顶缀蓝飘带的草水手帽，带他们上河去，坐他那艘帆船——船名起得好听极了，叫「燕子号」（Swallow）。

===Original===
Mr. Lacey—no, the pictures grew too intimate. Mamma, diffused with a warmth not of the day, put her hands over her eyes. Soft waves surged over her body, and the heart in her breast swelled until it held the earth, the sky, the sun and moon and stars. She forgot toilet-tables and how much a man might help a little boy; she forgot cooking sherry and the proprieties. She was no longer thirty-nine, fat, the mother of four children, nor was Mr. Lacey a dapper little gentleman with a sunny temper and an assured income. They were the hidden heart of the world, the pulse of life, the creators, through which life flows in its endless circle.

===Chinese===
莱西先生——不成了，这些画面越来越亲昵了。妈妈周身漫起一股暖意，那不是天气的暖，她把双手掩在眼睛上。柔软的浪潮从她身上涌过，她胸中的心涨啊涨的，直到把大地、天空、日月星辰都装了进去。她忘了梳妆台，忘了男人能怎样帮一个小男孩；她忘了做菜的雪利酒，忘了体统规矩。她不再是三十九岁、发胖、生养了四个孩子的她，莱西先生也不再是那个脾气晴朗、收入稳当、衣冠楚楚的小个子绅士。他们是世界藏着的心，是生命的脉搏，是造物的人，生命经由他们，环流不息。

===Original===
Bowls of roses on little tables of papier maché inlaid with mother of pearl, perfumed the darkened parlor in honor of Mr. Lacey. Other roses, artificial, adorned the ornament like the skirt of a lady’s ballgown that filled the empty fireplace, looking as if the lady’s head were stuck up the chimney, looking for swallows, perhaps, or stars. Under the bronze Arabs and camels standing beneath palm trees from which wax candles rose, the chilly marble mantelpiece had been put into a red flannel petticoat embroidered in yellow chain-stitch; and the chairs were dressed decently in tatted tidies. Everything in the room was well covered except the Venus de Milo in the darkest corner, that had been a Christmas present from the Blows. Was the statue *quite* nice, Mamma wondered? But it would have been so embarrassing explaining to Sam and Lizzie, if she had hidden it, and she remembered that Papa had said there were ever so much worse ones abroad.

===Chinese===
一碗碗玫瑰，摆在镶螺钿的纸胎小桌上，熏香了放下帘子、暗沉沉的客厅，为的是敬莱西先生。另一些玫瑰是假花，装点着那件填满空壁炉的摆饰，活像一位贵妇舞裙的裙幅；瞧那样子，仿佛这位贵妇的脑袋直挺挺地捅上了烟囱，在找燕子——找星星也未可知。青铜的阿拉伯人和骆驼立在棕榈树下，树梢间升起蜡蜡烛；在这底下，冷冰冰的大理石壁炉台裹上了一条绣黄色链式花边的红法兰绒衬裙，椅子们也体体面面地罩上了梭结花边的小罩巾。满屋的东西都遮盖得妥妥帖帖，只有最暗角落里那尊米洛的维纳斯（Venus de Milo）光着——那是布洛家（the Blows）送的圣诞礼物。这尊像究竟算不算体面，妈妈暗自纳闷。可要是把它藏起来，又得跟萨姆和丽齐解释，那多尴尬；何况她记得爸爸说过，国外比这不像样的多得是。

===Original===
The parlor windows had new terra-cotta curtains, and lace ones as well. The old crimson curtains had been hung upstairs in the schoolroom. Their folds concealed Victor curled up in a corner of the window seat, sleepy from his morning of fishing on the glaring river. Neither May or Lily knew he was there.

===Chinese===
客厅的窗子挂上了新的陶土色窗帘，还配了蕾丝纱帘。原先那副猩红窗帘，挂到楼上的课室里去了。帘褶里藏着维克托，蜷在窗座的一角，早上在那条白晃晃的大河上钓了一晌鱼，这会儿困得不行。梅（May）也好，莉莉（Lily）也好，谁都不知道他在那儿。

===Original===
Lily ought to have been practicing “Convent Bells at Twilight” on the piano with its fan of puckered green silk in the front, and the swinging candle-holders. But it was so hot, and four flats were so hard. Besides, she wanted to talk to May, who was cutting out a picture of a statue of Psyche to paste on green “velvet” cardboard. Lily watched the quick flashing of the sharp scissors. If she had been doing it she would have torn the delicate winged figure, or pasted it on crooked, or messed the glue. She did things clumsily, as if her fingertips were numb, not like May’s that seemed as sensitive as the fingertips of a blind person.

===Chinese===
莉莉本该在那架钢琴上练《暮色中的修道院钟声》（Convent Bells at Twilight）——琴身前脸绷着一扇抽褶的绿绸，琴上还带着能摇转的烛台。可是天太热，四个降号的调子又太难。再说，她有话要跟梅说；梅正剪一幅普赛克（Psyche，灵魂的化身）雕像的图片，要贴到绿「丝绒」纸板上去。莉莉望着那把快剪子刷刷地闪。要是换了她，准会把那带翅膀的纤细人影剪破，要不就贴歪了，再不然把浆糊弄得一塌糊涂。她做事笨手笨脚，仿佛指尖是麻木的，不像梅的那双手，灵敏得像盲人的指尖。

===Original===
“May!”

“Mm,” said May, abstracted.

“May, do you think Mamma is going to—that Mamma and Mr. Lacey are going to—you know!”

“Oh, Lily! Don’t!” May blushed brightly, delicately, and began to laugh; and Lily, blushing and laughing too, swung this way, that way on the fringed plush mushroom of the piano stool.

“Wouldn’t it seem—I don’t know—*funny*, if they got married? I sort of think maybe they will, though, don’t you?”

The sisters looked at each other, shaking with nervous laughter. They were curious, uneasy, and mildly unhappy. But Lily would have quite loved kind Mr. Lacey, if only the others had, and May had a sudden flashing vision of gloves from Paris, a trip to Italy.

===Chinese===
“梅！”

“嗯。”梅心不在焉地应了一声。

“梅，你说妈妈是不是要——妈妈和莱西先生是不是要——你知道的！”

“哎呀，莉莉！别说了！”梅亮亮地、娇娇地红了脸，笑起来；莉莉也红着脸笑，在琴凳那缀流苏的长毛绒圆蘑菇座上，这边一晃，那边一晃。

“他们要是结了婚，会不会显得——我也说不上——「滑稽」？不过我倒有点觉得，没准儿真会的，你说呢？”

姐妹俩对望着，神经质的笑得直抖。她们好奇，不安，还有点儿不快活。不过，只要旁人都喜欢，莉莉原会满心喜欢和气的莱西先生的；梅呢，眼前倏地闪过巴黎来的手套、一趟意大利之行。

===Original===
“*Who is that beautiful girl with the wistful eyes, looking out to sea?*”

“*That is May Campion, the stepdaughter of the rich Mr. Lacey. He showers every luxury upon her, they say.*”

“*And yet that lovely face is the saddest I ever saw in my life.*”

===Chinese===
“*那位眼睛脉脉含愁、凭海远眺的美丽姑娘是谁？*”

“*那是梅·坎皮恩（May Campion），阔佬莱西先生的继女。听说他把她娇宠得锦衣玉食、要什么有什么。*”

“*可那样一张可爱的脸，却是我平生所见最悲伤的一张脸。*”

===Original===
“May—do you think Mr. Lacey ever—ever kisses Mamma?”

“Lily, how can you? You’re perfectly awful! Stop, for mercy’s sake!”

But they couldn’t stop laughing. Lily’s head went down with a clash on the piano keys, and her hair curtained her burning cheeks, while May’s hands shook so she had to put down the little picture—Psyche, the butterfly-winged, Psyche, the soul. But it was not only laughter that made her hands shake. Thinking about kissing did, too. She thought so much about being kissed. She longed passionately to be kissed, and would have died rather than admit it, for Mamma had brought her daughters up with great propriety. The prince of her secret dreams, in pink tights with a white ostrich tip in his toque, had long since given way to a dark handsome cruel Roman emperor, in snowy toga and imperial purple mantle, with his brows drawn together over his dark eyes. And she was a Christian virgin, exquisite, slender, white as a stalk of fire-white lilies. He tortured her and she adored him, he pressed his beautiful scornful mouth to hers, and she melted away with delight. At night she would lie thrilling, trembling, burying her face in the pillow so that the little moans of ecstasy she could not keep back, should not waken Lily, sleeping so quietly beside her.

===Chinese===
“梅——你说莱西先生会不会——会不会亲妈妈？”

“莉莉，你怎么说得出口？你简直坏死了！行行好，别说了！”

可她们还是笑个不停。莉莉一头磕在琴键上，哐啷一声，头发帘子似的遮住她烧得滚烫的脸颊；梅的手抖得厉害，只好把手里的小画儿放下——普赛克，蝴蝶翅膀的普赛克，灵魂的普赛克。不过叫她手抖的，不光是笑。想亲吻的事也叫她手抖。她老想着被人亲吻。她火热地渴望着被人亲吻，却宁死也不肯承认，因为妈妈把女儿们调教得规规矩矩、极守本分。她秘密梦里的那位王子——粉红紧身裤，小帽上插一支白鸵鸟翎——早就让位给一位黝黑、俊美、冷酷的罗马皇帝了：雪白的托加袍，绛紫的御氅，黑眼睛上头眉头拧着。而她是个基督徒贞女，纤巧，苗条，白得像一茎火焰般莹白的百合。他折磨她，她崇拜他；他把他那美丽而轻蔑的嘴压到她的嘴上，她便欢喜得消融了。夜里她躺着，心惊神荡、浑身发抖，把脸埋进枕头，好叫那些忍不住的小声呻吟，不至于惊醒在身边静静睡着的莉莉。

===Original===
Now that she was growing up she sometimes “tried on” different young men in her imagination, in the place of her emperor. And although she looked on her love life as the reality, Mamma’s as the dream, she tried to imagine Mr. Lacey kissing Mamma—oh—*horrid!* So prim and pecky! Or else soft and playful, which would be worse. A kiss wouldn’t be a kiss unless it hurt you, made you want to swoon, to die! But Mr. Lacey kissing Mamma would be as passionate as Mamma’s canary pecking at a cherry hung between the bars of his cage. The idea was so silly—so—oh! It made her feel hysterical. She said, to stop her thoughts:

===Chinese===
如今她渐渐长成大姑娘了，有时便在想象里把一个个青年男子拿来「试穿」一番，顶替她那位皇帝的位置。虽说她把自己的爱情生活看作现实，把妈妈的看作一场梦，她还是试着想象了想象莱西先生亲吻妈妈——哦——可怕！那么干巴巴、啄一口了事！再不然就是软塌塌、闹着玩儿的，那就更糟。亲吻若不能叫你疼、叫你想晕过去、想去死，那还算什么亲吻！可莱西先生亲妈妈，那股热烈劲儿，顶多跟妈妈那只金丝雀隔着笼条啄一颗挂着的樱桃差不多。这念头太傻了——太——哦！她简直要歇斯底里了。为了截住自己的念头，她说：

===Original===
“Lily! Be an angel and make some lemonade. I’m dead with this heat.”

Lily pretended for a moment that she couldn’t stop laughing; but seeing that May was all through, she answered amiably:

“I will if you’ll come with me and ask Martha for the lemons. She’s cross as two sticks having company to tea on such a hot day.”

===Chinese===
“莉莉！做个天使，去调点柠檬水吧。我热得要死了。”

莉莉有一会儿还装着刹不住笑；可一看梅已经笑完了，便和和气气地答道：

“我去——可你也得陪我一起找玛莎要柠檬。这么热的天家里来客吃茶，她正一肚子的火呢。”

===Original===
They went off together, just in time, for Victor could no longer hold in his sobs, and they burst loudly on the empty schoolroom. Mamma was going to marry Mr. Lacey! The idea had never entered his head, and the shock was terrific. Between the forces of life that like a wave lifted Mamma and Mr. Lacey before toppling over and submerging such little in-the-way creatures as himself, and the power of the midsummer sun, that had poured itself all morning on his bare head, he was unbearably shaken. Sobbing, he leaned from the window and was sick into the fern bed far below.

===Chinese===
她们一道走了，走得正是时候，因为维克托再也憋不住了，哭声炸响在空荡荡的课室里。妈妈要嫁给莱西先生了！这个念头他从来没有往脑子里想过，这一惊非同小可。一边是生命的力量，它像一道浪，把妈妈和莱西先生高高地掀起来，再哗地翻倒过去，把像他这样碍事的小东西卷进水底；一边是仲夏太阳的威力，一上午都倾泻在他光着的脑袋上——夹在这两股力量中间，他抖得受不住了。他抽抽噎噎地探身出窗，朝底下老远的蕨草圃里吐了。

---

===Original===
Out in the steaming heat of the “truck patch” Maggie was picking raspberries. It was so hot. The grape leaves, green on one side, pale grey suede on the other, that she had just picked to line the raspberry baskets, were already limp. There was a singing sound in her ears, and now and then blackness and a bursting of bloodred stars floated before her eyes.

She knew from the way Mamma was acting that she was going to marry Mr. Lacey. She had been so shy and smiling, looking like a happy little girl. She had even tried to tell Maggie something, after Mr. Lacey’s coachman brought over a note this morning, but Maggie, fiercely loyal to Papa, had been forbiddingly silent and sullen.

“That little lady-killer to take Papa’s place!” she thought bitterly, desperately. And, yet even to her who loved him so, Papa had grown far away and dim—a dear dream that faded even as she tried to remember.

The moist heat rolled over her like waves. Two cabbage butterflies, one pure white, one faintly veined with green, a Mr. Lacey and a Mrs. Campion of the insect world, quivered over the raspberry bushes, and a dust-colored toad gave a languid hop as she nearly stepped on him, but no other creatures were in sight anywhere; humans were in their darkened houses, birds in their shadowy green ones. Pushing the hair up from her wet forehead with the back of her wrist, Maggie went on picking raspberries for Mr. Lacey’s tea.

===Chinese===
外头，「菜园子」（truck patch）里热气蒸腾，玛吉正在采覆盆子。天热极了。葡萄叶一面绿、一面是淡灰的麂皮绒，她刚摘了来给覆盆子篮子垫底，这会儿已经蔫了。她耳朵里嗡嗡地响，眼前不时一阵发黑，炸开一朵朵血红的星。

瞧妈妈的做派，她就知道妈妈要嫁给莱西先生了。妈妈那样羞答答、笑吟吟的，活像个快活的小姑娘。今儿早上莱西先生的车夫把条子送来之后，妈妈甚至试着想跟玛吉说什么来着，可玛吉死心塌地地向着爸爸，一脸拒人千里的沉默和阴沉。

「叫那个迷死女人家的小白脸儿，来顶爸爸的位置！」她又恨又绝望地想。可是，就连对她这样爱爸爸的人，爸爸也已经远了、淡了——一场亲爱的梦，她越是追着想记住，它越是褪色。

潮热像浪头一样从她身上滚过。两只菜粉蝶，一只纯白，一只隐隐透着绿纹，是昆虫世界里的一对莱西先生和坎皮恩太太，在覆盆子丛上颤巍巍地飞；一只土灰色的癞蛤蟆，险些被她踩着，懒懒地蹦了一蹦。除此之外，四下里再看不见一个活物：人躲在放帘遮暗的屋里，鸟躲在绿荫荫的窝里。玛吉用手腕背把湿额头上的头发撩上去，接着采她的覆盆子，为莱西先生的茶点采着。

## XI / XI

===Original===
Mamma planned to be surprised in the garden, but as the time for Mr. Lacey’s arrival drew near, she grew shy and stayed with the girls on the porch. She wouldn’t let them out of her sight. She felt dreadfully nervous.

She was wearing her white flounced, bustled dress with the little black bows all over it, and her black fringed sash. Black ribbons were tied around her wrists, and an onyx cross on a black velvet ribbon tobogganed on her bosom. She had tucked a crimson rose in her great bird’s nest of braids﻿—would *he* think that was flighty? But just as she began to take it out, his carriage wheels were heard on the drive, and she had only time to fling one arm about Victor, one about Lily, who were showing signs of running away, before Martha brought him out on the porch.

===Chinese===
妈妈原本盘算着在花园里让他「突如其来」地吓自己一跳，可离莱西先生（Mr. Lacey）到的时辰越近，她越害起臊来，索性同姑娘们一道待在廊子上。她一眼也不肯离开她们。她紧张得不得了。

她穿着那身缀着白褶、垫着腰垫的裙子，满身小小的黑蝴蝶结，系一条黑流苏腰带。手腕上绑着黑缎带，一条黑丝绒带子拴的缟玛瑙十字架，在胸口一滑一滑地出溜。她那鸟窝似的一大蓬发辫里，掖了一朵深红的玫瑰——他会觉得这轻佻吗？可她刚要伸手去摘，车道上已经传来他马车的轮声；眼见维克托（Victor）和莉莉（Lily）露出要溜的苗头，她只来得及一边一条胳膊搂住一个，玛莎（Martha）就把他引到廊子上来了。

===Original===
A new Mr. Lacey, surely! This Mr. Lacey is too spotless, too creaseless, too twinklingly tidy, ever to have been used before. He has never run, ridden on smoky trains, gone to bed, burnt his mouth, fallen down, or shed tears. He has just been created, he and his silver-grey suit, his shoes as polished as black glass, his linen so white that it makes you blink, the cluster of dark geranium in his buttonhole. Or else he has just been done over from tip to toe, with a new wig and whiskers of glossy nut-brown silk, and a fresh coat of pink paint for his face, and of sky-blue paint for his eyes.

No, his eyes are not sky-blue paint, but the real sky itself, and those are real stars shining in them.

===Chinese===
准是一位崭新的莱西先生！这一位莱西先生太一尘不染、太不见一道褶子、太闪闪发亮地齐整了，先前断断没有被使用过。他从来没有跑过步，没有坐过冒烟的火车，没有上床睡过觉，没有烫过嘴，没有摔过跤，也没有掉过泪。他是刚刚才造出来的——他本人，连同他那身银灰色的衣裤、那双擦得像黑玻璃一般亮的鞋、那白得叫你直眨眼的亚麻衬衫，还有纽扣眼里那一簇深色天竺葵。再不然，他就是刚从头到脚翻修过一遍：换了一副崭新的假发和栗棕色亮绸一样的连鬓胡子，脸上新刷了一层粉红油漆，眼睛里新刷了一层天蓝油漆。

不对，他的眼睛不是天蓝的油漆，而是真正的天空本身，里头亮着的，是真正的星星。

---

===Original===
“My prediction is that in a hundred years we will all be going about in balloons,” said Mr. Lacey.

“Oh, dear!” said Mamma. “Nothing would in*duce* me!”

That was the end of that. What could they talk about now?

===Chinese===
“我预言，一百年之后，我们人人出门都要乘气球了。”莱西先生说。

“哎呀，天哪！”妈妈说。“说什么也劝不动我！”

这个话头就到了头。眼下还能谈些什么呢？

===Original===
Literature! That was always a nice topic. But one had to be careful with young ladies present. Hawthorne was all right if one stuck to “The Marble Faun,” but there was always “The Scarlet Letter” lurking in the background. He tried George Eliot, who had just written a novel called “Middlemarch.”

“We’ve read ‘Mill on the Floss,’ ” said May. Mamma didn’t know about Mr. Lewes, but the girls did, and that got them through the dull parts.

“Quite an authoress, quite an authoress!” Mr. Lacey decided kindly.

“I hardly like to let the girls read her,” said Mamma. “It sounds so *fast*﻿—a lady calling herself George! Pass the biscuits, please, Martha.”

===Chinese===
文学！这向来是个好题目。不过有年轻小姐们在座，得留点儿神。谈霍桑（Hawthorne）还稳妥，只要守住《大理石牧神》（The Marble Faun）不跑题；《红字》（The Scarlet Letter）却总在暗地里埋伏着。他便试了试乔治·艾略特（George Eliot），她刚写完一部小说，名叫《米德尔马契》（Middlemarch）。

“我们读过《弗洛斯河上的磨坊》。”梅（May）说。刘易斯先生（Mr. Lewes）那档子事妈妈不知道，姑娘们却知道，多亏了这个，沉闷的地方才熬得过去。

“真是位女作家，真是位女作家！”莱西先生好心地下了定评。

“我真不大乐意让姑娘们读她的书，”妈妈说。“听着太轻佻了——一位太太，管自己叫乔治！玛莎，请把饼干递一递。”

===Original===
The conversation fainted again, and again Mr. Lacey leapt forward with restoratives. President Grant and Mr. Home, the Spiritualist, helped him out; and, at the mention of Dr. Livingstone and Mr. Stanley, the Congo rolled through their minds, with variations, for none of them had very clear ideas about it. But Mr. Lacey had to do the work, for Mamma was nervous and the children silent.

He tried lighter topics, and described the ladies of fashion he had seen at Saratoga last month, with hair dressed so high, with the bonnets perched on top such explosions of feathers and flowers, that it seemed as if the gentlemen escorting them should have walked on stilts. “The *height* of fashion!” cried Mr. Lacey, expecting laughter. For once his lady failed him, murmuring, “*Do* have some more salad,” but he didn’t mind. Above all things he admired sensibility in Lovely Woman.

===Chinese===
谈话又晕了过去，莱西先生又一次抢上前去灌救药。格兰特总统（President Grant）和招魂术士霍姆先生（Mr. Home）帮他支应了过去；一提到利文斯通博士（Dr. Livingstone）和斯坦利先生（Mr. Stanley），刚果河便从他们各人的脑子里滚滚淌过——各淌各的道，因为谁对它都没有个清楚的概念。可是出力的总得是莱西先生：妈妈心慌，孩子们又不作声。

他换了个更轻快的题目，讲起上个月他在萨拉托加（Saratoga）见到的时兴太太们：头发梳得高高耸起，帽子就蹲在头顶上，羽毛花朵炸开一团又一团，瞧那光景，陪同的绅士们简直该踩着高跷走路才是。“时尚的绝顶！”莱西先生喊道，一心等着哄堂大笑。这一回他的女士没接住，只低低说了句：“千万再添点儿沙拉。”可他并不介意。天下万事里，他顶顶看重「可爱女性」的善感。

===Original===
“Eat your nice sweetbread, darling,” Mamma urged Victor tenderly, smiling at him. She wanted, oh, she wanted to show them all how much she loved them. The warmth and fragrance of her love poured out over her little boy.

“Victor caught a big fish this morning, for our lunch.”

“Caught a big fish, did he? *This* big, eh?” Mr. Lacey measured off improbable lengths of air. “What, not that big, young Sir Piscator? Well, I suppose the bigger fish got away﻿—I thought so, I thought so! You’re a true fisherman!” He slapped his napkined knee, and his laughter pealed out. Even Mamma had to laugh a little in spite of Victor’s gloomy face. Mr. Lacey was so droll.

===Chinese===
“把你那份可口的小牛胰吃了，宝贝。”妈妈温存地劝维克托，朝他微笑着。她多想——啊，她多想让他们人人都看看，她是多么爱他们。她那爱的暖热与芬芳，倾洒在她的小男孩身上。

“维克托今天早上钓了条大鱼，给咱们当午饭。”

“钓了条大鱼，是吗？有——这——么大，嗯？”莱西先生在空气里比出一段又一段大得离谱的尺寸。“怎么，没那么大吗，年轻的垂钓爵士（Sir Piscator）？唔，我看哪，更大的那条准是跑掉啦——我就知道，我就知道！你是位真正的渔夫！”他一巴掌拍在自己蒙着餐巾的膝盖上，笑声当啷啷地响出来。饶是维克托一张脸阴沉沉的，妈妈也忍不住笑了少许。莱西先生多逗趣啊。

===Original===
Panic-stricken, she would have stayed with the children all evening. But Mr. Lacey was firm. He wanted to be shown the roses. With Brownie at their heels, they strolled across the grass, and the hemlock hedge that separated the lawn from the garden hid them.

The girls didn’t want to be waiting there on the river porch when Mamma and Mr. Lacey came up from the garden. They took their round straw mats and sat on the grass under the trees in front of the house. The western sky was a sheet of pure gold, and against it the weeping willow by the servants’ cabins across the road was a dark fountain﻿—a fountain springing and falling, springing and falling forever. Somebody from one of the cabins began to play the banjo, languidly, for it was still so hot. The notes spattered out, seemed drops from that fountain pouring up against the golden sky that faded as they watched it, and then grew dark. The music stopped, everything was quiet but the insects that sounded as if they were mechanical insects being wound up for tomorrow.

===Chinese===
她吓坏了，恨不得一晚上都守在孩子们身边。可莱西先生主意已定。他要人领他去看看玫瑰。布朗尼（Brownie）跟在他们脚后，他们缓步走过草地，那道隔开草坪和花园的铁杉树篱，把他们遮没了。

妈妈和莱西先生要从花园里上来了，姑娘们可不想守在河边廊子上等。她们拿了各自的圆草垫，坐到屋前树下的草地上。西天是一整幅纯金；衬着它，大路对面仆人小屋旁那棵垂柳，是一挂黑色的喷泉——一挂永远喷起又跌落、喷起又跌落的喷泉。不知哪间小屋里，有人懒懒地弹起了班卓琴，天还是热得很。琴音点点滴滴溅出来，像那喷泉的水珠，逆着金色的天空泼上去；她们望着，那片金色淡下去，又暗下去。琴声停了，四下里静悄悄的，只剩虫鸣——那声音像上了发条的机关虫，正在给明天上紧发条。

===Original===
There was a crunch of steps on the drive﻿—of course, Mamma and Mr. Lacey thought the girls would be on the porch, they were all hiding from each other﻿—and the gentleman’s voice said cheerfully:

“I wonder how it will feel to be Paterfamilias to three lively young ladies and a young hopeful!”

Lily’s nervous giggle escaped before she could cram her hands against her mouth, and the voice sank to a murmur, the steps withdrew. Presently they heard his carriage roll away; and Mamma was standing in the lighted doorway calling: “Children! I want you!”

No need to tell them what had happened, her face was so beautiful and radiant. She was like a great white water-lily that opens softly to show a golden heart, a great white cloud, gold in the sun. Floating, floating, far away from earth. Even the hard knot of pain in Maggie’s breast melted as she hugged Mamma.

===Chinese===
车道上响起一串沙沙的脚步声——当然了，妈妈和莱西先生还当姑娘们在廊子上呢，他们这是一家人彼此躲着呢——跟着听见那位绅士喜滋滋地说：

“真不知道，给三位活泼的小姐加一位有望头的小子当一家之长（Paterfamilias），会是怎样一番滋味！”

莉莉还没来得及把双手捂上嘴，一声神经质的咯咯已经漏了出去，那话音低成了咕哝，脚步退远了。不多时，她们听见他的马车碾出院子；妈妈正站在灯亮的门口喊：“孩子们！我要你们来！”

出了什么事，用不着谁来告诉她们，她那张脸那么美，那么光彩焕发。她像一朵大白睡莲，轻轻开放，露出金色的心；又像一大朵白云，映着太阳的金。飘着，飘着，离地远远的。就连玛吉（Maggie）胸口那团硬硬的痛，搂着妈妈的时候也化开了。

===Original===
“Where’s my Victor?”

“I put him to bed, Mamma. He wasn’t feeling very well.”

“Poor little man! I’ll see if he’s asleep. Girls﻿—”

“Yes, Mamma.”

“Darlings, I *do* love you so much!”

===Chinese===
“我的维克托呢？”

“我打发他上床了，妈妈。他有点儿不舒服。”

“可怜的小人儿！我去瞧瞧他睡着了没有。姑娘们——”

“哎，妈妈。”

“宝贝们，我可真是好爱好爱你们哪！”

===Original===
Victor was awake, a huddle of misery. His head was wet, he was sure he was going to be sick again. Half waking, half sleeping dreams filled the room. Mr. Lacey’s body in its silver-grey suit﻿—but the head was the head of the catfish he caught for Mamma﻿—the dark grey of wet slate, with long fleshy feelers like drooping mustaches﻿—a face as terrifying, as evil, as the Chinese devils in the Foreign Mission book at Mr. Page’s. And from the face came the purring sound that had come from the catfish lying in the bottom of the boat﻿—the frightening purring that had gone on until Jake hit it over the head.

Now the catfish face was laughing at him, the silver-grey sides of Mr. Lacey’s suit were shaking. And over the bed, past the windows, head-down from the ceiling, floated Mamma and the girls, laughing at him, too﻿—

They melted away, he could see his clothes over a chair, the faint glimmer of the looking-glass in the light from the hall. It glimmered like water﻿—water gently rocking the boat in which he sat fishing. Suddenly the red bob in the circle of bright water wobbled and went under﻿—a bite! And there, jerking and leaping at the end of his line, was a tiny Mr. Lacey, grey suit, brown sidewhiskers, geranium buttonhole, streaming wet and jerking on the hook as if he were dancing the Highland fling. Funny little Mr. Lacey! But, lying kicking in the bottom of the boat, he began to grow, to swell, blotting out the river, blotting out the sky; and screaming with laughter he began rocking the boat from side to side﻿—further﻿—further﻿—until the water came pouring in﻿—

Victor wrenched himself back into full consciousness, calling “*Mamma!*” But no use to call for Mamma﻿—she was in the garden with Mr. Lacey, and Lily and May said she was going to get married and be with him always. The waking dreams crept back, knitted together with the heat, the sound of the night insects, the sheet all screwed up into humps and ridges, his stomach that felt so sick no matter how hard he hugged his knees against it, his hot head that swelled up and floated off like a balloon.

===Chinese===
维克托醒着，蜷作一团苦楚。他的头汗津津的，他知道自己又要吐了。半醒半睡的梦塞满了屋子。莱西先生的身子，裹在那身银灰衣裤里——脑袋却换成了他替妈妈钓的那条鲶鱼的脑袋——湿石板一般的深灰，垂着长长的肉须，像耷拉的两撇胡子——一张脸又吓人又凶恶，像佩奇先生（Mr. Page）家那本海外传教书里的中国恶鬼。那张脸上发出咕噜咕噜的声响，同那天躺在船底的鲶鱼发出来的一模一样——那阵吓人的咕噜声，一直响到杰克（Jake）照它头上敲了一下才停。

这会儿那张鲶鱼脸正冲着他笑，莱西先生那身银灰衣裤的两侧笑得直颤。床上方，窗户外，妈妈和姑娘们头朝下从天花板上飘过来，也都在冲他笑——

他们消融了，他看得见自己的衣裳搭在椅背上，门厅灯光里那面镜子微微发亮。它亮得像水——水轻轻地摇着他坐着钓鱼的那条船。忽然，亮晃晃一圈水当中，红色的浮子一晃，沉了下去——咬钩了！线的那一头，又蹿又跳的，是个小小的莱西先生：灰衣裳，棕色连鬓胡子，纽扣眼里的天竺葵，浑身淌着水，在钩子上一挣一挣，像在跳苏格兰高地舞（Highland fling）。有趣的小莱西先生！可是躺在船底乱蹬乱踹着，他开始长大，开始膨胀，把河遮没了，把天也遮没了；他尖声大笑，把船身左摇右晃——再晃——再晃——直到水哗哗地灌进来——

维克托猛地把自己拽回全然的清醒，喊了一声“妈妈！”可是喊妈妈也没有用——她在花园里陪着莱西先生呢，莉莉和梅说了，她要结婚了，从此跟他长长久久。醒着的梦又悄悄爬回来，同炎热纺在一起，同夜虫的鸣声纺在一起，同拧成一疙瘩一道棱的床单纺在一起，同他怎么死命把膝盖抵住也照旧翻搅欲呕的肚子纺在一起，同他那颗热得涨起来、像气球一样飘走了的热脑袋纺在一起。

===Original===
And then Mamma really came. She put down her candle, and the moths began to fly around it crazily. Her shadow and Victor’s streamed up the side of the wall, tremulous and huge. She knelt by the bed and put her arms around his body that stiffened under her touch.

“Oh, you naughty boy, you ought to be sound asleep!” But she was glad he wasn’t, really. Bright tears went slipping down over the brightness of her smile.

“Victor, dearest little boy, I have some news for you, something that’s going to make Mamma and all of us so happy. Look, Mamma’s crying, but it’s only because she’s so happy﻿—isn’t that silly? You’re going to have a new Papa﻿—won’t that be lovely, darling? A dear new Papa to love us all and take care of us﻿—Mr. Lacey﻿—”

Victor was as rigid as iron. His face, his large ears, his thin little neck turned scarlet. His whole body was scarlet under his nightshirt. And suddenly like a cork flying out of a bottle a loud “*No!*” burst from him, and another and another. She couldn’t soothe him, she couldn’t stop him. Scarlet, furious, he went on crying “No! No! No! No!” as if his cries were torn out of him by terror and despair.

===Chinese===
后来妈妈真的来了。她放下蜡烛，飞蛾便绕着烛火疯狂地打转。她的影子和维克托的影子顺着墙壁流上去，颤颤巍巍，大得吓人。她跪在床边，伸出双臂搂住他，他的身子在她的手底下僵住了。

“哎呀，你这个顽皮孩子，这会儿本该睡得香甜才对！”其实她心里倒高兴他没睡着，真的。亮晶晶的泪珠，从她亮晶晶的笑容上滑下来。

“维克托，最亲爱的小男孩，我有个消息告诉你，一件要让妈妈和我们大家都幸福的事。瞧，妈妈哭了，可那只是因为她太幸福了——傻不傻？你要有一位新爸爸了——那多好呀，宝贝？一位亲爱的新爸爸，来爱我们大家、照应我们大家——莱西先生——”

维克托僵得像块铁。他的脸、他的大耳朵、他细细的小脖子全红了。睡衣底下，他整个身子也是猩红的。忽然，像瓶塞崩出瓶口，一声响亮的“不！”从他身上迸出来，接着又是一声，又一声。她哄不住他，也拦不住他。他红得透亮，怒得发狂，只是一个劲儿地喊“不！不！不！不！”仿佛那些喊声是被恐惧和绝望从他身上硬生生撕出来的。

===Original===
“Hush, darling, hush! He wants to take us all across the ocean in a great big ship, as big as this *house*﻿—won’t that be fun? Perhaps we’ll see a whale! Think of that! And Mamma will love Victor a thousand *thousand* times as much as ever﻿—”

She tightened her arms about him, she tried to draw his head down on her breast, rocking back and forth with broken murmurs of “sh-sh, sh-sh.” But nothing would stop those tearing cries.

And at last she promised. He was safe. He lay in the big chair by the window, exhausted, giving now and then a long shuddering sob﻿—but safe, safe! Mamma had promised.

===Chinese===
“嘘，宝贝，嘘！他要带我们大家坐上一艘好大好大的轮船漂洋过海，那船大得跟这座房子一样——多有意思呀！说不定咱们还能看见鲸鱼呢！想想看！妈妈爱维克托，要比从前多上一千再加一千倍——”

她把双臂收得更紧，想把他的头揽到自己胸口，前前后后地摇着，嘴里断断续续地呢喃“嘘——嘘，嘘——嘘”。可是什么都止不住那撕人心肝的哭喊。

末了她答应了。他安全了。他躺在窗边的大椅子里，筋疲力尽，不时发出一声长长的、打着哆嗦的抽噎——可是安全了，安全了！妈妈答应了。

===Original===
She washed his hot face with cool water and cologne, she took off his hot, rumpled nightie and put on a fresh one. Sitting there while she made his bed with fresh cool sheets he heard a cock crow far away, and saw a faint streak of light along the river.

In bed again﻿—so cool, so still﻿—no sound but a June-bug bumbling against the ceiling. Funny old June-bug! He tried to lift a hand to make sure that Mamma was still there, but his arms, his legs, all his body had turned into yards and yards of silk the color of the river in the moonlight, flowing in such soft folds and billows in his cool fresh bed. He felt something wet fall on his face, and just managed a faint “Mamma!” before the cool silver depths closed over him.

Next morning Mamma was all washed white, like a flower that has been rained on and rained on and rained on. Only her poor eyes and nose were pink, as if her pretty pink cheeks had run in the wash and streaked and stained all the wrong places. All morning she sat at Papa’s desk between the dining-room windows, writing and tearing up, writing and tearing up, and at last Albert was sent off with a note, and with Brownie, but not the gloxinias, which hadn’t done very well. Mamma put Mr. Lacey’s letter and one velvety dark gloxinia bell into her Bible, and for a long time she cried whenever she looked at them. She never saw Mr. Lacey again.

===Chinese===
她用凉水和古龙水洗了他滚烫的脸，脱下他又热又皱的睡衣，换上一件干净的。他坐在那里，看她铺上又凉又新的床单，听见远处一声鸡啼，望见河边淡淡一线曙光。

又躺回床上——这么凉，这么静——除了天花板上嗡嗡乱撞的一只金龟子（June-bug），再没有一点儿声响。滑稽的老金龟子！他想抬起一只手，好确认妈妈还在；可是他的胳膊、他的腿、他整个身子，都变成了好几码好几码月光下河水颜色的丝绸，在又凉又新的床上，流成那样柔软的褶皱和波纹。他觉出一点湿的东西落在脸上，勉强发出一声轻轻的“妈妈！”清凉的银色深处便在他头顶上合拢了。

第二天早晨，妈妈整个人被洗得发白，像一朵被雨淋了又淋、淋了又淋的花。只有她那可怜的眼睛和鼻子还是粉红的，仿佛她那漂亮的粉红脸蛋儿在浆洗里化了色，洇出一道道一条条，全染错了地方。一上午，她坐在饭厅两扇窗之间爸爸的书桌前，写了又撕，撕了又写，末了打发艾伯特（Albert）带一张条子出门，布朗尼跟着一道去，大岩桐却没有让带——那几盆长势不大好。妈妈把莱西先生的信和一朵天鹅绒似的深色大岩桐铃铛夹进她的《圣经》，此后好长一段日子，她一看见它们就哭。她再没有见过莱西先生。

## XII / XII

===Original===
Miss Hessie Farley was going to be Airy Fairy Lilian in half a minute, and was in a panic because she couldn’t find her blue scarf and parasol anywhere. Mrs. Farley, who was helping behind the scenes, asked, “Why didn’t you hold on to them?” and tears of fury sprang to her daughter’s eyes. While the ladies seethed, Mr. Bates and Prentice Page stood ready to pull back the hitchy curtains, and had a good look at the audience through the sides. Flap, flap, flap went the fans, like flapping wings all over the hall, but the birds were too heavy to rise.

It was Maggie, just through being Mariana in the Moated Grange in a draped crimson portière with her back hair flowing, who saw a pale blue tail hanging down from Aunt Priscilla, sitting on a box. The parasol was under her, too. Airy Fairy Lilian took up her position and put on her smile, the curtains hitched back, and there was another of the Tennyson Tableaux.

===Chinese===
再过半分钟，赫西·法利小姐（Miss Hessie Farley）就要上场扮「空灵仙子莉莲」（Airy Fairy Lilian）了，偏偏她的蓝纱巾和阳伞哪儿也寻不着，急得她慌了神。在后台帮着张罗的法利太太（Mrs. Farley）问了句：“你怎么不拿好了？”女儿眼里顿时迸出狂怒的泪珠。太太们正乱作一团，贝茨先生（Mr. Bates）和普伦蒂斯·佩奇（Prentice Page）已经站在一边，随时准备把那老是卡壳的幕布拉开，还从侧面把台下的观众好好打量了一番。扇子啪嗒、啪嗒、啪嗒地扇着，像满场拍打的翅膀，只是这些鸟儿太沉，飞不起来。

正是玛吉（Maggie）——她刚演完壕沟庄院里的玛丽安娜（Mariana in the Moated Grange），身上裹一条当裙子的深红绒布门帘，脑后的长发披散着——一眼看见一条淡蓝的尾巴，从坐在一只箱子上的普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）身子后头垂了出来。阳伞也压在她屁股底下。空灵仙子莉莲站好位置，挂上微笑，幕布向两边一挂，又是一幅丁尼生「活人画」（Tennyson Tableaux）。

===Original===
They were to raise money to buy a new melodeon for the Sunday school, and everyone was helping. Aunt Priscilla was old Mother Hubbard—not that that was Tennyson, but she always was Mother Hubbard, whatever the tableaux were. She had her costume—a chintz skirt with poppies on it, a red sateen lace-up-the-front bodice, and Willie’s mother’s straw poke-bonnet trimmed with tea-colored ribbons. It did for Martha Washington, too, at fancy-dress parties, or, if she left off the poke bonnet and floured her hair, for Marie Antoinette. But she liked to be Mother Hubbard because she had taught her terrier Tiny to sit up and beg, and he could be Mother Hubbard’s Dog. Every time anything was planned someone said, “Now *this* time we really *can’t* have Mrs. Willie Campion as Mother Hubbard, and somebody just must tell her so, that’s all there is about it!” But what were they to do when she said timidly and eagerly, “Do you want Tiny and me to be Mother Hubbard and her Dog? It wouldn’t be a bit of trouble, if you do, because I have my costume.”

===Chinese===
这场演出是为了筹钱给主日学校买一架新簧风琴（melodeon），人人都在出力。普丽西拉婶婶照例扮老哈伯德大妈（Mother Hubbard）——这本不是丁尼生，可不管活人画演的是什么，她永远都是哈伯德大妈。行头是她现成的：一条印满虞美人的印花布裙，一件前襟系带的红色羽缎紧身衣，还有威利（Willie）妈妈那顶缀着茶色缎带的麦秸大檐帽（poke-bonnet）。化装舞会上拿这身扮玛莎·华盛顿（Martha Washington）也使得；要是摘掉大檐帽、往头发上扑些白粉，扮玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）也行。可她顶喜欢扮哈伯德大妈，因为她把自己的小猎犬「小不点儿」（Tiny）调教得会直立起来讨食，它可以当哈伯德大妈的狗。每逢筹划什么节目，总有人说：“这回咱们可真不能再让威利·坎皮恩太太扮哈伯德大妈了，说什么也得有人跟她挑明了，没得商量！”可她怯生生又热切切地来一句：“你们要不要我和小不点儿来扮哈伯德大妈和她的狗？要是要的话，一点儿也不费事，因为行头我是现成的。”——这叫他们可怎么办呢？

===Original===
Mrs. Webster sang “Sweet and Low” with such tremolo it seemed as if the staff and the stems of the notes on her sheet of music must be scalloped instead of straight; and Mr. Bates’ voice welling out in “Break, break, break” made the ladies sigh and smile.

> “‘But the tender grace of a day that is dead
> Will never come back to me.’”

===Chinese===
韦伯斯特太太（Mrs. Webster）唱《甜蜜与低吟》（Sweet and Low），颤音颤得那么重，仿佛她那张歌谱上的谱线和音符的符干，都该是扇贝花边而不是直线了；贝茨先生的嗓音在《拍岸，拍岸，拍岸》（Break, break, break）里滚滚涌出，惹得太太们又叹息又微笑。

> 「逝去之日那温存的光华，
> 再也不会回到我身旁。」

===Original===
Mamma, sitting in the audience between Victor and Lily, thought of a summer night three years ago. Where was he now? The tears welled into her flower-blue eyes. But soon she had to laugh, for Carrie Trotter was giving a recitation from “The Widow Bedott Papers”—such a comical one about pumpkin pie. Of course, that wasn’t Tennyson either, but then it didn’t do to be too particular.

===Chinese===
妈妈坐在观众席上，维克托（Victor）和莉莉（Lily）一边一个地夹着她，她想起三年前的一个夏夜。他如今在哪儿呢？泪水涌上她那双花儿般湛蓝的眼睛。不过不一会儿她就忍不住笑了，原来是嘉莉·特罗特（Carrie Trotter）在朗诵《贝多特寡妇文集》（The Widow Bedott Papers）里的一段——讲南瓜馅饼的那段，滑稽得很。当然，这也不是丁尼生，可话又说回来，凡事犯不着太较真。

===Original===
The curtain jerked back again—oh, what a charming tableau! Fannie Leaf and Prentice Page were “Come Into the Garden, Maud,” and they had rented a rustic gate from a photographer in Wilmington, and twined real climbing roses over it. Fannie knew how pretty she looked under the roses, in her pink satin and Roman pearls, and she felt so happy that she couldn’t keep from laughing. She shook so that the arch shook, too, and some of the petals came drifting down and caught in her yellow curls.

===Chinese===
幕布又猛地一扯——哎呀，多迷人的一幅活人画！范妮·利夫（Fannie Leaf）和普伦蒂斯·佩奇演的是《到花园来吧，莫德》（Come Into the Garden, Maud）：他们从威尔明顿一家照相馆租来一座乡下柴门，门上真的缠满了活的攀缘玫瑰。范妮知道自己衬在玫瑰底下有多俏丽——粉缎子衣裙，罗马珍珠——她美得心花怒放，想忍也忍不住地要笑。她笑得直颤，连柴门都跟着颤，颤得花瓣簌簌飘落，挂在她金黄的鬈发上。

===Original===
The last tableau was the loveliest of all, “The Lady of Shalott.” May was the Lady, lying all in white on packing boxes covered with draperies. She had Mamma’s white satin wedding slippers on her little feet, and on her breast beneath her crossed hands lay all the Ascension lilies from the garden, sacrificed by Mamma for this great hour. Edward Post was Sir Lancelot in a Tam o’ Shanter with a plume from his Aunt Jo’s best hat; and Robert Leaf was the Dumb Oarsman (for they had mixed in some of Lancelot and Elaine) in Fannie’s tan cape, with the packing-box draperies arranged to hide his boots and trousers.

===Chinese===
最后一幅活人画最美不过：《夏洛女郎》（The Lady of Shalott）。梅（May）扮女郎，一身雪白，躺在蒙着帷幔的几只包装箱上。她那双小脚上，套着妈妈的白缎结婚拖鞋；交叉的双手底下、胸口上，躺着花园里全部的升天百合——妈妈为了这个盛大的时刻，把它们全牺牲了。爱德华·波斯特（Edward Post）扮朗斯洛爵士（Sir Lancelot），戴一顶苏格兰软帽（Tam o’ Shanter），插一根从乔姨妈（Aunt Jo）最好那顶帽子上取下来的羽毛；罗伯特·利夫（Robert Leaf）扮沉默的桨手（他们还揉进了些《朗斯洛与伊莱恩》（Lancelot and Elaine）的情节），披着范妮的棕褐色斗篷，包装箱上的帷幔专门布置过，好遮住他的靴子和裤子。

===Original===
> “‘But Lancelot mused a little space;
> He said, “She has a lovely face;
> God in his mercy lend her grace,
> The Lady of Shalott,”’”

read Mrs. Allen with great expression; and May was so beautiful that Lily wept unrestrainedly, and Edward Post looked down at her as if he really loved her.

But she couldn’t get him to sound as if he had when she tried, ever so hard, afterward, while Mrs. Webster and Mr. Bates finished things up with a duet of “Tears, Idle Tears” and Maggie helped poor Aunt Priscilla hunt for the slippers she had kicked off in a moment of relaxation.

===Chinese===
> 「朗斯洛沉吟了片刻；
> 他说：“她的面容多么姣好，
> 愿仁慈的上帝赐她恩宠，
> 这位夏洛女郎。”」

艾伦太太（Mrs. Allen）念得声情并茂；梅美得叫莉莉抑制不住地直流泪，爱德华·波斯特低头凝望她，仿佛真心爱着她。

只是事后任凭她怎样苦苦地试，也没法叫他的口吻听上去真像那么回事；这当儿，韦伯斯特太太和贝茨先生以一支《眼泪，无端的眼泪》（Tears, Idle Tears）的二重唱收了场，玛吉则帮着可怜的普丽西拉婶婶，找那双她一时松快下来踢脱了的拖鞋。

---

===Original===
“What were you thinking about while our tableau was going on?”

“About a fly that was tickling my nose. What were you?”

“I shan’t tell you.” (A fly, indeed!)

“Oh, come on!”

“You’d just laugh.”

===Chinese===
“咱们那幅活人画演着的时候，你在想什么呀？”

“想一只直痒痒我鼻子的苍蝇。你呢，想什么？”

“我才不告诉你。”（苍蝇？亏他说得出口！）

“哎呀，说嘛！”

“说了你准要笑。”

===Original===
> (“‘So sad, so fresh, the days that are no more’”

warbled Mrs. Webster, boomed Mr. Bates.)

“I know I must have looked perfectly *crazy*—”

“I know what you’re fishing for, young lady, and it won’t hurt you to work a little for it,” thought Edward, and added aloud cruelly:

“Who’s that pretty girl in pink?” He was a new young man, and had just come to live with his Aunt Jo and Uncle Henry Allen while he worked in Wilmington.

“Which one? *Oh!* Oh, you mean her? That’s Fannie Leaf. Do you think she’s pretty? Goodness, men are funny!”

And she laughed, but she looked so much as if she would rather cry that he decided she should have her compliment at once.

“Yes, I think she’s pretty as a picture—I think she’s the second prettiest girl here.”

“Who’s the prettiest?” asked May, reviving like a wilted flower put into water, and trying to look as if she couldn’t guess.

“Don’t you really know?”

===Chinese===
> （「如此哀伤，又如此清新，那些一去不返的日子」

韦伯斯特太太颤声唱着，贝茨先生嗡嗡地和着。）

“我知道，我方才那模样准是疯得没法看了——”

“我明白你在钓什么，小姑娘；自个儿下点功夫才换来，对你没坏处。”爱德华心里想着，嘴上却狠巴巴地接口道：

“穿粉衣裳的那个俊姑娘是谁？”他是位新来的年轻人，刚搬来同乔姨妈和亨利·艾伦姨父（Uncle Henry Allen）一起住，在威尔明顿做事。

“哪一个？啊！哦，你说她呀？那是范妮·利夫。你觉得她好看？天哪，男人可真怪！”

她也笑了，可那神气分明是宁可哭一场，于是他拿定主意，恭维话得立刻给她。

“是啊，我觉得她好看得像画上人儿——我看她是这儿第二俊的姑娘。”

“那谁是第一俊的？”梅问，像一株蔫了的花重新插进水里活了过来，还竭力装出一副猜不出的样子。

“你真的不知道？”

===Original===
“May, you haven’t seen Aunt Priscilla’s slippers, have you?” asked Maggie, coming up.

“My sister Maggie, Mr. Post.”

> “‘—deep as love,
> Deep as first love, and wild with all regret,’”

sang Mrs. Webster and Mr. Bates.

“How do you do, Mr. Post. Have you, May? She took them off because they were so tight—” She moved away.

“Mercy, I wish *these* slippers were too tight! They’ve been falling off all evening!”

“That’s because you have such tiny feet.”

“Oh, now you’re making fun of me—”

===Chinese===
“梅，你没瞧见普丽西拉婶婶的拖鞋吧？”走上来的玛吉问。

“这是我姐姐玛吉，波斯特先生。”

> 「——深得像爱，
> 深得像初恋，翻腾着万千悔恨，」

韦伯斯特太太和贝茨先生唱道。

“您好，波斯特先生。你见着了吗，梅？她脱下来是因为鞋太紧了——”她说着走开了。

“老天，我倒巴望这双拖鞋也太紧呢！一晚上它们就没停过往下掉！”

“那是因为你的脚太小啦。”

“哦，你这是取笑人家——”

===Original===
He took her out to the carriage. She was dreadfully excited. Mamma tried to put a sensible wrap about her gleaming shoulders, but she twitched it off impatiently.

“Oh, *Mamma!* I don’t need that!”

(To put a stuffy old cape about moon-silver and lilies! She lifted her face a little, to the stars.)

“There’s a chill in the night air, honey. Well, goodnight, Mr.—er—goodnight! *What’s* the young man’s name, May?”

“Sh, Mamma! He’ll hear you. Post. He’s the Henry Allens’ nephew.”

“I thought Stewy Grant was going to be Sir Lancelot.”

“He fell out of a cherry tree this morning and knocked out a front tooth, so he wouldn’t, and Mrs. Allen got Mr. Post. He’s coming over for archery on the lawn next Saturday afternoon.”

===Chinese===
他送她上马车去。她兴奋得不得了。妈妈想把一件实惠的披肩，裹上她莹莹发光的肩膀，她却不耐烦地一扭身抖掉了。

“哦，妈妈！我用不着那个！”

（拿一件闷死人的旧斗篷，去裹月光似的银辉和百合花！她把脸微微仰起来，朝着星星。）

“夜里空气凉呢，宝贝。好啦，晚安，先生——呃——晚安！这位年轻人叫什么名字来着，梅？”

“嘘，妈妈！他要听见啦。波斯特。是亨利·艾伦家的外甥。”

“我还当斯图伊·格兰特（Stewy Grant）要扮朗斯洛爵士呢。”

“他今天早上从樱桃树上摔下来，磕掉一颗门牙，就不肯演了，艾伦太太才请了波斯特先生。下星期六下午，他要到咱们草坪上来射箭。”

===Original===
Already she was walking down the aisle on his arm—shimmer of satin, foam of lace, and an armful of lilies. They left the church, they entered the carriage. He and she, alone.

“*You were carrying lilies when first I saw you—do you remember?*”

“*I remember, my Lancelot!*”

“*My pure, white lily—my wife!*”

And Mamma, as she answered, “That will be very nice, pet,” was holding her first grandchild in her arms.

===Chinese===
她已经挎着他的胳膊，走在教堂的甬道上了——缎子的流光，蕾丝的雪沫，满怀的百合。他们走出教堂，上了马车。他和她，只他们两个。

「你抱着百合——我头一回见你，你正抱着百合——你还记得吗？」

「我记得，我的朗斯洛！」

「我纯白的百合——我的妻！」

而妈妈一面答着「那敢情好呀，宝贝」，一面已经把她的头一个孙儿抱在了怀里。

===Original===
“We’ll make some ice-cream to have when you finish your archery.”

“Oh, Mamma, *no*!” May’s voice was anguished. “Goodness, what would he *think*?”

“He’d think it was very nice, if I know anything about boys.”

“Oh, Mamma, he isn’t a *boy*! And he’d think we were so *crazy* about having him come—!”

“But, honey, we could just act as if we were going to have it for supper anyway.”

“Oh, *no*—!”

“All right, we needn’t have it, I’m sure! I just wanted to make things pleasant for your friends.” Mamma was dignified, but tears were near.

“May, I think you’re horrid when Mamma plans a treat for you,” Maggie said severely.

“Well, I know, it’s lovely of Mamma, but he’d think we *cared* whether he came or not!”

“And *you* don’t care! Oh, my, no! Oh, I guess not!” jeered Victor.

===Chinese===
“咱们做点儿冰淇淋，等你们射完箭好吃。”

“哦，妈妈，不行！”梅的声音里带着苦楚。“天哪，他会怎么想呀？”

“他会觉得这很好——男孩子是怎么回事，我总算还懂一点儿。”

“哦，妈妈，他可不是个『男孩儿』！再说他会觉得，咱们盼他来盼得都要发疯啦——！”

“可是宝贝，咱们只消装出本来就要留着当晚饭吃的样子嘛。”

“哦，不行——！”

“好吧，那就不做，我担保不做了！我不过想叫你的朋友们高兴高兴。”妈妈端着架子，眼泪却已在眼边打转。

“梅，妈妈好心张罗疼你，你却这样子，我看你太不像话了。”玛吉严正地说。

“唉，我知道，妈妈疼我，多好呀。可他会觉得，咱们太把他来不来当回事啦！”

“你才不当回事呢！哦，天哪，才不呢！哦，想也不当吧！”维克托讥笑道。

===Original===
> “May’s got a beau,
> Oh, oh, oh,
> May’s got a beau
> Oh, oh, oh!”

“Mamma! Make him *stop*! He thinks he’s so funny!” May wailed, and burst into a storm of tears.

===Chinese===
> 「梅有了情郎，
> 嗷，嗷，嗷，
> 梅有了情郎
> 嗷，嗷，嗷！」

“妈妈！叫他住口！他还当自己多逗呢！”梅哭喊着，眼泪泼洒成一场暴风雨。

---

===Original===
Old Major ambled along. It was late, so late that the tollgate was lifted for the night, and Lily was fast asleep in the back of the carriage. Maggie was driving. They didn’t like to keep Albert up now that he had all the work to do.

> “‘Tears, idle tears, I know not what they mean,’”

Maggie sang to herself, silently, inside her head. Then she thought of Aunt Priscilla and her pale blue tail. May was still shaking with sobs—what had been the matter with them all? Such a fuss about that Mr. Post—but she was ashamed of herself for having been so cross and bossy.

The sky was swarming with stars—so many, so bright, it made you feel—oh—funny, to look at them. Mm‑m, how sweet! Those were the mock-orange bushes in Miss Perry’s yard. Queer how their fragrance made you feel so happy and yet as if you wanted to cry at the same time. Another smell, not so nice—the tavern pigpen. Pee-*yugh*!

===Chinese===
老梅杰（Major）迈着溜蹄，不紧不慢地一路走着。夜深了，深得连收税卡口的闸杆都为过夜吊起来了；莉莉在马车后座上睡得沉沉的。赶车的是玛吉。如今一应活计都归了艾伯特（Albert）一个人，他们不忍心再叫他熬着等门。

> 「眼泪，无端的眼泪，我说不清它们意味着什么，」

玛吉在心里无声地独自唱着。随后她想起普丽西拉婶婶和她那条淡蓝的尾巴。梅还在抽抽噎噎地哭——他们这是全都怎么了？为一个波斯特先生，就这样大动干戈——不过她也怪不好意思的，方才自己那么凶、那么霸气。

满天星星挤挤挨挨——那么多，那么亮，望着望着，叫人心里觉着——哦——说不出的怪。嗯——真香！那是佩里小姐（Miss Perry）家院子里的山梅花。真怪，那香气叫人这样欢喜，又叫人同时想哭。还有一股味儿，就不那么好了——酒馆的猪圈。噗——臭！

## XIII / XIII

===Original===
He came on Saturday afternoon long before anyone expected him. Mamma was lying down, May was tying a rose-colored velvet ribbon around her curls, and Maggie, who had been helping Martha preserve strawberries, was trying to get the stains off her fingers with the grated lemon rinds left from the lemon-icing cake Mamma had made that morning. It was Lily, strolling around the corner from the kitchen with her mouth full of buttered biscuit, who saw him on his bicycle with the huge front wheel and the tiny back one come wobbling up the drive between the pine trees.

“Is this the Campions’ house?” he shouted.

Lily, crimson with embarrassment, made muffled sounds and would have run away, if Edward hadn’t fallen off cheerfully into a bed of peonies.

“My bicycle’s new, and I don’t know how to ride it very well yet. I hope you’ll excuse my smashing your flowers.”

Lily swallowed her mouthful of biscuit with a gulp that forced the tears into her eyes, and became vehement through shyness.

“Oh, my yes, we *hate* those peonies. Mamma’ll be *glad* you fell off in them﻿—oh, I didn’t mean *that*! I mean she hates *these*, we *all* do, the pretty ones are all down in the garden, the big white ones with the pink bottoms﻿—”

Oh, what had *possessed* her? What would he *think* of her? As soon as the others came down, she rushed off and flung herself into a game of solitary croquet, pretending to be deeply absorbed, and passing from one agony of blushes to another.

===Chinese===
星期六下午，他来得比谁预想的都早。妈妈正躺着歇晌，梅（May）在往鬈发上系一条玫瑰红的丝绒缎带，玛吉（Maggie）帮玛莎（Martha）熬草莓酱刚忙完，正拿当天早上妈妈做柠檬糖霜蛋糕剩下的碎柠檬皮，搓指头上沾的渍色。是莉莉（Lily），嘴里塞满抹了黄油的软饼，正从厨房那边绕过屋角溜达出来，一眼看见他骑着那辆前轮老大、后轮小得可怜的自行车，在两行松树夹峙的车道上摇摇晃晃地驶上来。

“这是坎皮恩家吗？”他喊道。

莉莉臊得满脸通红，喉咙里呜噜呜噜说不出话，眼看就要逃走，幸亏爱德华（Edward）乐呵呵地连人带车，一头栽进了一畦芍药里。

“我这自行车是新买的，我骑得还不大熟。压坏了你们的花，还请你多多包涵。”

莉莉把满嘴的软饼一咕咚咽下去，噎得眼圈里都逼出了泪，害臊之下反倒激切起来。

“哦，可不是嘛，我们最恨那些芍药了。你栽在它们里头，妈妈保准高兴——哦，我不是那个意思！我是说她恨的是这些，我们全恨，好看的全在下面花园里，那些白白净净、粉着心子的大朵的——”

天哪，她这是着了什么魔？他会把她看成个什么样的人？等旁人一下楼，她赶紧一溜烟跑开，一头扎进一个人的槌球游戏里，装出一副全神贯注的样子，脸颊却一阵红过一阵，烧个不了。

===Original===
They had set up a target on the lawn near the beech tree. Edward and May shot against Maggie and Victor, and Mamma sat in a rustic chair, watching them, chaperoning the young people. The pleated ruffles of her skirt flowed out on the grass beside her, crisp fan after fan. In her spreading white, with her cooing voice, she was like one of Victor’s pigeons. How nice the girls looked, she thought, in their tight basques and three-ruffled skirts, their bustles, and sashes, their trains and long tight sleeves. Fashions had never been so pretty. Nobody would have dreamed that Maggie’s tobacco-brown dress had started life as a crinoline skirt of Aunt Priscilla’s. But how sunburned the child was! She must wear her hat more, and rub cucumbers on her face at night.

Maggie shot well when she got over trying to hide her stained fingers. Edward had to work hard to beat her. He looked at her with dawning interest as she drew her bow. Her straight, strong body in its tight brown sheath, like a young tree; her face, the golden rose of peaches above the white of her stiff little linen turn-back collar; her hair in a thick bang over her dark eyebrows and clear light eyes.

===Chinese===
他们在山毛榉树旁的草坪上立起了箭靶。爱德华和梅一方，对玛吉和维克托（Victor）一方；妈妈坐在一把粗木椅上望着他们，看顾着这几个年轻人。她裙子上打着褶的荷叶边在身旁草地上层层漾开，一折又一折脆生生的扇面。她一身铺展的白，加上那咕咕哝哝的软嗓子，活脱是维克托鸽子群里的一只。姑娘们多俊啊，她想——紧身的小袄、三道荷叶边的裙子、腰垫、饰带、拖裾，还有又长又紧的袖子。时装从来没有这么好看过。谁也想不到，玛吉那身烟棕色的裙子，前身竟是普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）的一条克里诺林衬裙（crinoline）。只是这孩子晒得太黑了！她该多戴戴帽子，夜里多拿黄瓜擦擦脸。

玛吉一旦不再费心思藏那染了色的手指，箭就射得又准又稳。爱德华要赢她，得下死力气。她开弓的时候，他望着她，兴味一点点萌生。她那笔直结实的身子裹在紧绷绷的棕色衣裙里，像一株小树；她的脸，是桃子金红的玫瑰色，衬着小小的硬挺亚麻翻领的一圈白；她的头发厚厚地覆在额前，盖着乌黑的眉毛和清亮浅色的眼睛。

===Original===
Over and over again May had to jerk him back to her.

“Oh! Oh!”

“What’s the matter?”

“Oh, a *bee*! Oh, make it go away!”

But she was really calling:

“Come back to me! Don’t be Maggie’s! Come back to me!”

For she was nineteen, a whole year older than Mamma had been when she was married, and she was terrified sometimes for fear life had passed her by.

===Chinese===
梅只得一遍又一遍，把他拽回到自己身边来。

“哎呀！哎呀！”

“怎么啦？”

“哦，一只蜜蜂！哦，快把它赶走！”

可她心里真正喊的是：

“回到我这儿来！别去做玛吉的！回到我这儿来！”

要知道她已经十九了，比妈妈当年出嫁足足大一岁，她有时怕得要命，唯恐这辈子已经打她身边过去了。

===Original===
When they were tired of archery, they went up on the porch and ate cherries. Albert had brought a big basket of them, great shining black worlds, each bursting with the sun, the rain, the earth; each holding hidden in its seed a tree with roots and branches, with snowdrifts of blossoms, and green leaves, and a universe of crimson black worlds each hiding in its heart another tree.

“Just throw your stones into the lily-of-the-valley bed﻿—we always do,” said Maggie. She and Edward ate their cherries, ever so many of them. May put hers on for earrings, and decorated Edward, too; and Victor threw his into the air and caught them in his mouth.

===Chinese===
射箭射腻了，他们便上廊子去吃樱桃。艾伯特（Albert）拎来一大篮，一颗颗亮晶晶的黑球，是一个个小世界，每一颗都涨满着太阳、雨水和泥土；每一颗的核里又都暗藏着一棵树，有根，有枝，有雪堆似的繁花和青青的叶子，还有一个绯红黑色小世界组成的宇宙，每个小世界心里又各藏着另一棵树。

“核儿只管吐在铃兰花坛里——我们向来这么着。”玛吉说。她和爱德华吃樱桃，吃了好些好多。梅把自己的核儿当耳环挂上，还把爱德华也装点了一番；维克托把核儿抛上半空，用嘴去接。

===Original===
Mamma had slipped away, and presently there came a tinkling, and she brought out a frosty pitcher of raspberry shrub, and the fresh lemon-icing cake. She and May tried to act as if it happened every afternoon, but Victor, who was showing off by walking on the porch railing, nearly fell over in his delight, and shouted rapturously to the round blue figure knocking the croquet balls about.

“Lil-lee!”

“What-ee?”

“Cake and raspberry shru-hub!”

Conversation, that had been wandering happy and silly and free, resumed its ball and chain.

===Chinese===
妈妈悄悄溜了进去，不一会儿，一阵叮叮当当，她端出一壶沁凉挂霜的树莓果饮（raspberry shrub），还有新鲜的柠檬糖霜蛋糕。她和梅竭力装出天天下午都有这一出的样子，可是正在廊栏上走给人看的维克托，乐得险些一个倒栽葱，欢天喜地朝那个敲着槌球玩的圆滚滚蓝影子喊：

“莉——莉！”

“什——么？”

“蛋糕和树莓果——饮！”

方才那东拉西扯、又快活又傻气、自由自在的闲谈，重新套上了铁球和锁链。

===Original===
“This is a wonderful view of the river you have, Mrs. Campion.”

“Yes, we’re very fond of our view. Do you like Delaware, Mr. Cobb?”

“Mr. *Post*, Mamma!”

“Oh, me! How silly of me! What do you suppose made me call you Mr. Cobb? Of course, there was Papa’s friend with the red hair who had the racehorses, Carrot-Top Cobb they used to call him; but you aren’t a bit like him, with your dark hair and everything. Heigho! I haven’t thought of him for years﻿—let me fill your glass. Do you think that you’re going to like Delaware?”

“Yes, indeed, thank you, though I haven’t seen much of it yet. Do you go to Wilmington often?”

“Well, not *very* often. Victor goes to school every day on the steam-cars, to Rugby Academy, but, of course, not in summer; and the girls have some very pleasant friends in town, haven’t you, girls?”

“Yes, Mamma.”

“But it’s a long drive, and we find plenty to do out here. Oh, yes, you must, just a *little* piece! But we always drive in once a week to market﻿—Victor, precious! I *wouldn’t* eat any more, darling; you’ll spoil your supper! No, honey, Mamma said *no*﻿—well, just a teeny piece. We generally go Saturday, but this week we went Wednesday, so we didn’t go this morning.”

“Aunt Jo drove in.”

“How is your aunty, Mr. Cobb?”

“Mr. *Post*, Mamma.”

===Chinese===
“你们这儿望河的景致真好，坎皮恩太太。”

“是啊，我们顶喜欢这片景致。你喜欢特拉华（Delaware）吗，柯布先生？”

“是波斯特先生，妈妈！”

“哎呀，瞧我！我多糊涂呀！你猜是什么叫我喊你柯布先生的？哦，对了，爸爸有位红头发的朋友，养赛马的，人家从前管他叫「红毛顶」柯布（Carrot-Top Cobb）；可你跟他半点儿也不像，你这一头黑头发，哪儿样都不像。嗐！我多少年没想起他了——来，我再给你斟上。你觉得往后你会喜欢特拉华吗？”

“喜欢，真的，谢谢您，虽说我还没怎么见识过。你们常去威尔明顿（Wilmington）吗？”

“唔，也说不上常去。维克托每天坐火车上学，上拉格比学校（Rugby Academy），当然啦，夏天不去；姑娘们在城里也有几位顶要好的朋友，是不是呀，姑娘们？”

“是的，妈妈。”

“不过车马一趟远，我们在乡下也有的是事做。哦，对了，你一定得再吃一块，就小小一块！我们倒是每礼拜赶一趟车进城上市场——维克托，小祖宗！不能再吃了，宝贝，晚饭都要给你吃坏了！不行，宝贝儿，妈妈说了不行——唉，好吧，就指甲盖大的一小片。我们往常礼拜六去，这礼拜礼拜三去过，所以今儿早上没去。”

“乔姨妈（Aunt Jo）赶车进去了。”

“你姨母可好，柯布先生？”

“是波斯特先生，妈妈。”

===Original===
Presently he had to go, and they all went out on the front porch to watch him climb up on his bicycle. Victor and Maggie saw themselves skimming along on it with the clouds foaming about their heads; Mamma and May said the very notion made them feel faint; and Lily, blushing, tried not to look at the crushed peonies.

“Goodbye,” Edward said to Maggie, smiling at her, taking her hand in his.

What is happening? What is this feeling that floods them both? Two drops of water touch, and are one.

===Chinese===
不多一会儿他就得走了，大家都到前廊去，看他爬上那辆高高的自行车。维克托和玛吉仿佛看见自己骑着它一路飞掠，云彩在头顶翻腾起沫；妈妈和梅说，光那么一想她们就要晕了；莉莉红着脸，竭力不去看那畦压烂了的芍药。

“再见。”爱德华对玛吉说，朝她微笑着，把她的手握进自己手里。

这是怎么了？这是漫过他们两个身心的，是一种什么样的感觉？两滴水珠一碰，便成了一滴。

---

===Original===
It was the most wonderful summer. The Sun said, “Lift up your hearts,” and the flowers answered, “We lift them up unto the Lord.” The big creamy roses had never been so fragrant, the river had never been so blue, the nights had never held such swarms of stars.

They got out the Chinese lanterns, lopsided, green and yellow and raspberry red, so crushed and dusty in their box in the attic, such miracles of beauty alight and afloat in the blue summer night. By their light, they ate the watermelons they had sailed across the river for, and popped the seeds at each other.

Edward came on Sunday evening to sing hymns with the Campions. While Mamma played the accompaniments, selecting one nice chord for the bass, which she stuck to through thick and thin, he would sing so loudly, so tunelessly, with such a sweet, serious expression on his face that Maggie would melt with love and laughter. Lily taught him to play chopsticks on the piano, and his Aunt Jo complained to Mamma that she was nearly driven crazy by his practicing.

===Chinese===
那是最最美妙的一个夏天。太阳说：「抬起你们的心来。」花儿们应道：「我们把它举向主。」奶油色的大朵玫瑰从没这么香过，河从没这么蓝过，夜空也从没兜住过这么成群结伙的星星。

他们把纸灯笼拿了出来——歪歪斜斜的，绿的、黄的、树莓红的，在阁楼的盒子里压得瘪瘪的、蒙满灰尘，可一点亮起来、飘荡在蓝色的夏夜里，竟是一个个美的奇迹。就着灯光，他们吃起特意撑船过河去驮来的西瓜，还彼此噗噗地吐瓜籽。

礼拜天傍晚，爱德华来同坎皮恩家一起唱圣歌。妈妈弹伴奏，给低音部单挑了一个好听的和弦，从此千难万难死守着它不放；他就放开嗓子唱，唱得那么响、那么不成调，脸上偏又挂着那么又甜美又郑重的神情，惹得玛吉又是爱意消融，又是忍俊不禁。莉莉教他用钢琴弹那支《筷子》（chopsticks），乔姨妈向妈妈诉苦，说他练琴练得她差点儿发疯。

===Original===
Fourth of July! Victor was up before the sun, exploding his torpedoes on the bricks by the kitchen shed. That last one, found in the sawdust when he thought he had used them all! It sounded twice as loud as any.

Every now and then he had to go into the house to look at the rockets and Roman candles, lying there wrapped in lovely pastel colors, waiting for darkness. Then out again. The red and gold of the firecracker packages were as exciting as clashing Chinese gongs, and sometimes braided in with the bunches of red firecrackers was a yellow one, or a green one. Bang! Bang! And there was his toy pistol, and the round brown cardboard boxes full of magenta caps. Bang! Mamma lay in her darkened room with a handkerchief wet with eau-de-cologne on her forehead, and saw Victor with his right hand torn off﻿—Victor blinded.

May gathered red and white Sweet-William and blue cornflowers for the dining-room table; and Albert made a big freezer of raspberry water-ice for the evening, when the Blows, and the Willie Campions, and Mr. and Mrs. Allen and Edward Post were coming for the fireworks.

The light never lingered so long as on Fourth of July; but at last it grew dark.

===Chinese===
独立日（Fourth of July）到了！维克托赶在太阳前头就起了床，在厨房棚子边的砖地上摔他的摔炮。还有最后那一枚——他本以为全放完了，却在锯末里又翻出来的那一枚！响声比哪一枚都大一倍。

他隔一阵儿就忍不住要进屋去，看看那些冲天炮和罗马烛（Roman candle），它们裹在一身可爱的粉彩纸衣里，躺在那儿等着天黑。然后再跑出去。鞭炮包装上那红红金金的颜色，像铛铛乱敲的中国锣一样撩人，一挂挂红鞭炮里，有时还编着一支黄的，或者一支绿的。砰！砰！还有他的玩具手枪，还有一只只圆滚滚的棕色纸盒，装满了洋红色的火药纸帽。砰！妈妈躺在遮暗的房间里，额头上敷着浸透古龙水的手帕，看见维克托的右手给炸掉了——维克托瞎了。

梅采了红的白的须苞石竹（Sweet-William）和蓝莹莹的矢车菊，供饭厅桌上插瓶；艾伯特做了一大桶树莓冰糕（water-ice），留着晚上用——晚上布洛一家（the Blows）、威利·坎皮恩一家（the Willie Campions）、艾伦夫妇（Mr. and Mrs. Allen）和爱德华·波斯特（Edward Post）都要来看烟火。

天光从没有像独立日这天这样磨蹭着不肯走；可它终于还是黑了下来。

===Original===
“Victor! *Victor!* Oh, honey, be careful!”

“It’s only a Roman candle, Mamma!”

“I won’t let him blow himself up, Mrs. Campion.”

“Oh, dear! Are you sure that it’s safe? Take care of him, Edward!”

(*Take care of my darling, take care of my life and my love!*)

The Roman candles burst softly into balls of ice-green, rose and blue. Over by the hedge were white flashes of teeth and rolling eyes, black faces melting into the night, soft voices full of sad cadences, broken by sudden yells of laughter, quickly hushed because of the white folks further up on the lawn.

Rushing up, tearing the darkness, the rockets ascended unto heaven, burst, spread into ferns of fire, blossomed into golden stars and tears that floated, melted, and were gone.

“Maggie? Where is Maggie?”

“Here I am, Edward.”

That was all he needed. He must be sure she was there, feeling everything with him.

===Chinese===
“维克托！维克托！哦，宝贝，小心！”

“不过是支罗马烛嘛，妈妈！”

“我不会让他把自己炸上天的，坎皮恩太太。”

“哎呀，天哪！你担保这东西安全吗？照顾好他，爱德华！”

（照顾好我的宝贝儿，照顾好我的性命、我的爱！）

罗马烛轻轻地炸开，绽成一团团冰绿、玫瑰红和蓝的火球。树篱那边，白牙一闪一闪，眼珠骨碌碌转，黑黑的脸庞融进夜色里，软软的嗓音带着忧伤的调子，不时被猛然爆出的笑声打断，又赶忙压下去——因为再往草坪上头去，就是白人们坐的地方了。

冲天炮呼啸着腾起，把黑暗撕开，一路升上高天，砰然炸裂，散成火做的蕨叶，开出金色的星星和眼泪，飘着，化着，没了。

“玛吉呢？玛吉在哪儿？”

“我在这儿呢，爱德华。”

他要的就只是这个。他必须确定她在那儿，同他一起感受着这一切。

===Original===
Edward persuaded them to go bathing in the river at high tide. The girls used the boathouse as a dressing-room, sitting on the edge of the wobbly gunning skiff. The boathouse was musty smelling and dark, but through the cracks between the boards you could see the blue river dancing. There were old starch boxes full of cartridge shells, and wooden decoy ducks, some without their heads, that had belonged to Papa. Lily was afraid of the wasps that built their nests in the corners, but May was never afraid of them unless she was with a young man.

Getting out of their tight dresses, their shoes and stockings, stepping from circle after circle of petticoat, calling “Don’t look!” to each other, as they took off corset covers, drawers, corsets shaped like hourglasses, chemises, and long ribbed shirts, getting into their blue flannel bathing-suits with the white braid on the sailor collars stained café au lait by the river, they felt like butterflies, light, free as air, escaped from their thick cocoons.

Maggie could swim a little, and so could Victor, though for Mamma he drowned each day they bathed. Lily pretended to swim, but she was really hopping along with one foot on the bottom. But May wanted Edward to teach her.

===Chinese===
爱德华撺掇大家趁涨潮到河里洗浴。姑娘们把船屋当更衣室，坐在那条摇摇晃晃的猎鸭小划子沿上。船屋里一股霉味儿，黑黢黢的，可从木板缝里望得见蓝莹莹的河水在跳舞。屋里搁着几只旧淀粉箱，装满弹壳，还有爸爸从前那些木头诱饵鸭，有几只丢了脑袋。莉莉怕那些在角落里搭窝的黄蜂，梅却从来不怕——除非身边带着位年轻先生。

她们脱下紧绷绷的衣裙、鞋子和长袜，从一圈又一圈的衬裙里跨出来，一面互相喊着“不许看！”，一面褪下束胸小马甲、衬裤、沙漏似的紧身褡、无袖衬衣和罗纹长内衣，钻进蓝色法兰绒的泳衣——水兵领上那道白镶边，早叫河水染成了咖啡加奶的颜色——这时候，她们觉得自己像蝴蝶，轻轻的，自由得像空气，是从厚厚的茧里逃了出来。

玛吉会游一点儿，维克托也会，虽说他们每回下水，他在妈妈心里总要淹死一回。莉莉装作在游，其实是一只脚点着河底往前蹦。可梅要的是爱德华亲手教她。

===Original===
“Oh! Oh! Edward! Oh, I’m going down! Oh, don’t let me go down!”

He disentangled her clinging arms.

“Now look here, May, do you want to learn, or don’t you? *Kick!* Pull up! *Kick!* Pull up! Keep your head down and imagine you’re a frog.”

*A frog!* The little mermaid floats in the foam, the Lady of Shalott drifts down the river, and all the prince sees, all Sir Lancelot sees, is a frog!

So she gave up bathing in the dirty old river; and, because she was unhappy, became disagreeable and distant, until she began an affair with a new young man named Ralph Wither, who taught her the names of the stars.

===Chinese===
“哎呀！哎呀！爱德华！哦，我要沉了！哦，别让我沉下去！”

他把死缠住他的两条胳膊解开来。

“我说，梅，你到底想不想学？踢腿！划水！踢腿！划水！头埋下去，就当自己是只青蛙。”

青蛙！小美人鱼浮在泡沫里，夏洛女郎顺流漂去，可王子眼里看见的，朗斯洛爵士眼里看见的，竟是一只青蛙！

于是她再也不到那条脏兮兮的老河里洗浴了；她心里不痛快，人就变得别扭而冷淡，直到她同一位新来的年轻人有了那么一段情——那人名叫拉尔夫·威瑟（Ralph Wither），教她认星星的名字。

===Original===
They all went crabbing after supper on the tremulous pier that ran out from Uncle Willie’s boathouse. The blue crabs scrabbled and waved their claws in the old peach-baskets, the river clucked coolly under the pier, and May cried:

“Oh, Ralph! Help me! Oh, I’ve got such a big old one I’m frightened to death!”

“I don’t see why you’re so scared of the crabs, May. You never used to be,” said Lily, who always thought that what people said and what they meant were the same.

Edward and Maggie sat together, swinging their feet over the water. A light warm wind flowed over them, and up from the grey river into the grey sky floated an enormous apricot-pink moon. Silently they watched it, and then looked at each other, smiling faintly.

===Chinese===
晚饭后，他们全体到威利叔叔（Uncle Willie）船屋外那条颤巍巍的栈桥上去钓蟹。青蟹在旧桃子筐里乱爬乱挥螯，河水在桥下凉丝丝地咯咯轻响，梅喊道：

“哎呀，拉尔夫！帮帮我！哦，我逮着一只好大的老家伙，吓死我啦！”

“我不明白你干嘛这么怕螃蟹，梅。你从前可不这样。”莉莉说——她向来以为，人家嘴上说的和心里想的是一回事。

爱德华和玛吉并肩坐着，四只脚在水面上悠荡。一阵轻暖的风从他们身上拂过，灰色的河面上，一轮杏子粉红的巨月冉冉浮起，升向灰色的天空。他们默默地望着它，随后相视一笑，笑意浅浅。

## XIV / XIV

===Original===
Now that they could only afford to keep Albert outside and Martha in the house, Mamma and the girls had ever so much more to do. They even had to let lame Joseph go. He had swept the porches and limped around the table in his white house-jacket; as Mamma said, he was awkward and slow, but he *did*. But they really couldn’t afford him any more.

And Martha wasn’t what she used to be. Whenever the weather was hot she groaned and held her side and mumbled so Mamma couldn’t understand a word.

“What, Martha?”

“Ah doan feel right good. Ah done got a misery.”

“Where is it? Does your head ache?”

“No, ma’am, taint ezzackly mah *haid*, but Ah doan feel like puttin’ out much today.”

“None of us do in this hot weather, but you don’t hear Miss Maggie and me going on as if we were dying.”

Mumble.

“What, Martha?”

But all you could hear was grumble, mumble, grumble. You couldn’t be sure she was being impertinent, but then you couldn’t be sure she wasn’t. She had been so cross and disobliging this morning that Mamma had punished her by making the piccalilli herself, with Maggie’s help.

===Chinese===
如今家里只雇得起屋外的艾伯特（Albert）和屋内的玛莎（Martha），妈妈和姑娘们要操持的事就多得多了。连跛脚的约瑟夫（Joseph）也不得不辞退了。他一向包扫各处廊子，穿着白色仆役短上衣，一瘸一拐地绕着饭桌伺候；照妈妈的说法，他人是笨，是慢，可他到底是做了呀。只是他们实在再雇不起他了。

再说玛莎也不比从前了。天一热，她就哼哼唧唧，捂着腰肋，咕咕哝哝，叫妈妈一个字也听不清。

“什么，玛莎？”

“我不大舒坦。我这身上闹起毛病来了。”

“哪儿不舒坦？是头疼吗？”

“不是，太太，倒不全是头的事，就是今儿个不大想卖力气。”

“这么热的天谁都不想动，可你听不见玛吉小姐（Miss Maggie）跟我这样哼哼唧唧、闹得跟要死要活似的。”

咕哝。

“什么，玛莎？”

可你听得见的，只有嘟囔、咕哝、嘟囔。你说不准她这是在顶撞，可你也说不准她不是。她一早就这样横竖不顺、爱答不理，妈妈便罚了她——亲自下手做酸辣菜酱（piccalilli），由玛吉打下手。

===Original===
Oh, what a day! The flies crawled ticklingly over their faces or buzzed despairingly from the saucer of molasses Martha had put on the windowsill to catch them﻿—poor things, how they struggled. Mamma’s face was crimson and wet as she bent over the hot stove, wiping her forehead with the back of her wrist, and her head buzzed like a thousand flies. Maggie chopped cabbages and peppers until her hands were shaking, carried leftovers out to the pig-bucket﻿—*pugh!*﻿—and felt as if the whole house was a bubbling pot of piccalilli, with herself boiling in the midst.

Later that afternoon, when the jars were all filled, she and Edward lay in canvas chairs under the pear tree. It was so hot, so still, a storm was coming. Pears had fallen in the grass, some of the ripest had burst, and wasps crawled over them. She was tired and languid, not only from her morning over the stove. Even the still air was heavy, pressing them down in their chairs, pressing the pears down on the grass until the juice ran out of their broken sides.

“Look, Maggie,” said Edward, speaking slowly, like someone half asleep. A butterfly, blue in the sunshine, black in shadow, had drifted through the heat and settled on a spot of squashed pear. She answered him﻿—or did she? She was heavy with love for him as the yellow pears in the tree were heavy with sweetness. When he took her in his arms, she was so weak she could not have stood without him. His lips moved slowly over her face.

A blue-black curtain of clouds rolled over the sky, the wind turned the river to lead, flattened the grass in the fields, pelted down the pears. Up at the house shutters were banging, people were running to the windows, slamming them down before the storm should break. The wind kindled Maggie’s cheeks, strained back her thin, dark blue dress until she was a flying victory. And now she was all alive again, and light as a mounting flame, and Edward too was flaming﻿—

===Chinese===
哦，这是怎样的一天哪！苍蝇麻酥酥地在她们脸上爬来爬去，要么就在玛莎搁在窗台上粘它们的那碟糖蜜里绝望地嗡嗡挣扎——可怜的东西，扑腾得多厉害。妈妈俯在热炉子跟前，脸红得像火烧，汗津津的，只拿手腕背抹额头，脑袋里嗡嗡直响，像有一千只苍蝇。玛吉剁白菜、剁辣椒，直剁到两手发颤，又把残羹剩饭拎去猪食桶——呕！——只觉得整座房子成了一锅咕嘟咕嘟翻滚的酸辣菜酱，她自己就在锅当中滚煮着。

挨到午后晚些，罐子都装满了，她和爱德华（Edward）躺在梨树下的帆布椅里。天那么热，那么静——要有雷雨了。梨子落在草里，熟透的有几只绽了口，黄蜂在上面爬。她倦恹恹的，浑身没力气，不光是在炉子跟前站了一早上的缘故。连纹丝不动的空气都沉甸甸的，把人往椅子里压，把梨往草上压，压得果汁从绽口的破边里沁出来。

“你看，玛吉。”爱德华说，话音慢慢的，像个半睡半醒的人。一只蝴蝶，太阳地里是蓝的，影子地里是黑的，飘过那片热气，落在一小摊压烂的梨上。她应了他一声——也许并没有？她心里盛满了对他的爱，沉甸甸的，正像树上的黄梨盛满了甜。他把她搂进怀里时，她软得没有他扶着就站立不住。他的嘴唇缓缓地摩挲过她的脸。

蓝黑色的云幕滚滚卷过天空，风把河水刮成一片铅色，把田里的草压伏在地，把梨子噼里啪啦打落下来。屋子那头百叶窗乒乓乱响，人们奔向窗口，赶在暴雨砸下来之前把窗子关严。风点燃了玛吉的双颊，把她单薄的藏青裙衫向后绷得紧紧的，绷得她整个人成了一尊振翅欲飞的胜利女神。这一下她又全活了过来，轻快得像一蓬腾起的火苗，爱德华也在熊熊燃烧——

---

===Original===
> “ ‘Je-ru-sa-lem the go-ol-den,’ ”

boomed Mr. Bates, up and down﻿—*much* too loud, thought Miss Martin at the organ, it really drowned out all the rest of the choir. Something tactful would have to be said again﻿—oh dear!

> “ ‘With milk and honey blest﻿—’ ”

The choir was marching out. Stewy Grant wondered if he could possibly hold in his sneeze until they got into the robing room﻿—those wild asters and goldenrod sprays on the altar!

> “ ‘Beneath thy contem-pla-shuhun﻿—’ ”

That was where Mrs. Webster’s voice soared higher than the steeple!

> “ ‘Sink heart and voice opprest.’ ”

Then, muted by the closed door, “A‑men,” and Stewy’s sneeze. Mumble, mumble, mumble, and then another “A‑men.” Miss Martin set the organ pealing, and the congregation could lift up its heads from the pew backs and go out into the sunshine.

===Chinese===
> 「耶——路——撒——冷，黄——金——的——城——」

贝茨先生（Mr. Bates）放开嗓门轰隆着，一起一落——太响了，实在响得过分，风琴跟前的马丁小姐（Miss Martin）心想，这简直把唱诗班其余的人全淹没了。回头得再想句得体的话说说——哎呀，愁人！

> 「奶与蜜，福泽滋润——」

唱诗班正列队退场。斯图伊·格兰特（Stewy Grant）心里琢磨着，能不能把喷嚏憋到进了更衣室再打——祭坛上那些野紫菀和一枝黄花的花枝，呛死人了！

> 「在你——的——默——观——之下——」

唱到这一句，韦伯斯特太太（Mrs. Webster）的嗓音直往上蹿，蹿得比教堂的尖顶还高！

> 「心与声俱沉，郁郁其伤。」

随后，隔着关上的门，闷闷地传来一声「阿们」，还有斯图伊的喷嚏。咕哝，咕哝，又一阵咕哝，然后又是一声「阿们」。马丁小姐让风琴叮叮咚咚扬起来，会众这才把头从长椅背上抬起来，走出去，走进阳光里。

===Original===
Mamma was ashamed of herself﻿—or at least she knew she ought to be﻿—but she hadn’t heard a word of the sermon, for thinking of Edward and Maggie, and they had wandered even into the prayers and the benediction. She held quite a reception on the church lawn.

“Yes, indeed, we’re very much pleased,” said she. “Of course, it’s sad to lose a daughter; but as you say, I’ll be gaining a﻿—Good morning, Mrs. Farley, isn’t it a lovely morning? Good morning, Hessie dear, how sweet you look! I never saw such a girl﻿—a new dress every time I see you. Yes, indeed, we’re very much pleased, he’s a very dear boy. Good morning, Mr. Leaf. Yes, indeed. They’re walking home﻿—yes, we’re very fond of him. I don’t know what I’ll do without her, but then﻿—oh, not until next autumn *any*way. Lily pet, run back and see if Mamma left her fan in the pew﻿—*oop!*﻿—don’t fall over your feet, honey. Good morning, Miss Martin! Thank you! Yes, indeed﻿—!”

“Goodness! You’d think nobody in the world had ever got engaged before,” Miss Hessie Farley said gloomily to Mrs. Farley as they walked away. “Such a *fuss*﻿—!”

“Oh, yes, he’s *nice*, but can you i*mag*ine feeling sentimental about him?” May asked her friends.

Poor Aunt Priscilla said it was exactly like Willie and her all over again, and offered her grandmother’s lace wedding-veil. But she couldn’t find it anywhere, until﻿—*pop!*﻿—while she was screaming, “Work, for the night is coming,” in Sunday school, she remembered that she had put it over some plates of caramels up in the attic to keep off the flies. She had made them for a surprise for Willie, that’s why she’d hidden them up there; and then, of course, she’d forgotten them. She was so afraid she’d forget again that she kept saying, “Caramels﻿—caramels,” to herself all through the psalms, all through the sermon.

===Chinese===
妈妈自觉惭愧——至少她知道自己该惭愧——可布道她一个字也没听进去，一心只在想爱德华和玛吉，连祷告词和祝福文里，那两个人影都在她心头出没。她在教堂草坪上简直开起了一场招待会。

“可不是，真是的，我们欢喜得很。”她说。“当然啦，女儿家要走，做娘的伤心；可就像您说的，我这也算得了个——早上好，法利太太（Mrs. Farley），今儿早上多好的天哪！早上好，亲爱的赫西，你多俊哪！我从来没见过这样的姑娘——回回见你都穿新衣裳。可不是嘛，我们欢喜得很，他是个顶招人疼的孩子。早上好，利夫先生（Mr. Leaf）。是啊，真是的。他们走着回去呢——是啊，我们都喜欢他。没有她我可不知道怎么过哟，不过话又说回来——哦，反正怎么也得到明年秋天呢。莉莉（Lily）宝贝，跑回去瞧瞧妈妈把扇子落在长椅上没有——哎哟！——宝贝儿，别叫自己的脚绊倒了。早上好，马丁小姐！谢谢您！可不是嘛——！”

“天哪！照这么着，还当天底下头一回有人订婚呢。”赫西·法利小姐（Miss Hessie Farley）一面走远，一面闷闷地对法利太太说。“瞧这通张罗——！”

“哦，是啊，人是挺好，可你们能想象得出，对他动起感情来是什么滋味？”梅（May）问她的女友们。

可怜的普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）说，这简直跟她和威利（Willie）当年一模一样，于是献出了她外婆的花边新娘头纱。可她翻遍各处也找不着，直到——啪！——她在主日学校里正扯着嗓子高唱《做工吧，黑夜将临》（Work, for the night is coming），猛地想起来：头纱叫她罩在阁楼里几盘焦糖上了，为的是挡苍蝇。那焦糖本是做来给威利一个惊喜的，这才藏到那上头去；跟着呢，自然就忘了。她生怕再忘，便一路自顾自念叨「焦糖——焦糖」，念过一篇篇诗篇，念过整场布道。

===Original===
“Hear my prayer, O Lord: (caramels) and let my crying come unto thee.

“Hide not thy (caramels) face from me in the time of my trouble: incline thine ear unto me when I call; (caramels, caramels, caramels).”

They had been there nearly a year, so they probably wouldn’t be very good. Still, it wouldn’t hurt just to try them.

The mice had tried them, and tried the veil, too. But May, who was clever with her needle, mended it so that you wouldn’t have known.

Victor was pleased with the engagement because he thought now he could ride Edward’s bicycle.

“Margaret drove over to announce the forthcoming nuptials,” Cousin Lizzie said to Cousin Sam. “That means a silver tea-set. Really, the woman’s ridiculous! They haven’t a penny between them, and she was clucking like old Speckle when she’s laid an egg!”

===Chinese===
“主啊，求你垂听我的祷告：（焦糖）愿我的哀声达到你面前。

“在我遭难的日子，求你不要（焦糖）向我掩面；我呼求的时候，求你侧耳而听；（焦糖、焦糖、焦糖）。”

它们在阁楼上搁了快一年，多半好不到哪儿去了。不过嘛，只管尝上一尝，总不碍事。

老鼠们倒先尝过了，连头纱也一并尝了。好在梅的针线最巧，补得妥妥帖帖，叫人瞧不出一点破绽。

维克托（Victor）对这门亲事满心欢喜，因为他盘算着，往后可以骑爱德华的自行车了。

“玛格丽特赶着车过来，通报这桩指日可待的婚事，”丽齐表姐（Cousin Lizzie）对萨姆表哥（Cousin Sam）说。“这就意味着，该破费一套银茶具了。说真的，这女人可笑！他们俩合起来一个大子儿也没有，她还咯咯哒个没完，活像老斑花（Speckle）下了蛋！”

===Original===
Mamma had tried to make Maggie and Edward drive to the Blows with her, but they escaped to the autumn garden. He was trying to learn the names of the flowers because she loved them so.

“Chrysanthemum, Edward.”

“Chrysanthemum. What’s this brown one?”

“Chrysanthemum, too. Zinnia.”

“Zinnia. Oh, Maggie, you beautiful girl!”

Her heart cried, “Oh, darling, darling, *darling*, say it again!” But aloud she said scornfully:

“You must enjoy hearing yourself talk!”

“And what’s this?”

“A zinnia, just the same as it was two minutes ago.”

And they looked at each other laughing, shining with inward light. They were always laughing﻿—laughter charged with excitement, laughter that left them trembling.

===Chinese===
妈妈本想拉上玛吉和爱德华，一块儿赶车去布洛家，他们两个却溜进了秋天的花园。他正用心学各种花名，因为她太爱花了。

“菊花，爱德华。”

“菊花。这棕色的一种呢？”

“也是菊花。百日菊。”

“百日菊。哦，玛吉，你这美丽的姑娘！”

她的心喊着：「哦，亲爱的，亲爱的，亲爱的，再说一遍吧！」嘴上却轻蔑地说：

“你准是爱听自己说话！”

“这是什么？”

“还是百日菊，跟两分钟前一模一样。”

两人相视而笑，从里向外透着光。他们总是笑个不停——那笑里带着电流，笑过之后，浑身还在打颤。

===Original===
“What’s the joke, children?”

“Nothing, Mamma, really and *truly* there isn’t any joke.”

And there wasn’t, but Mamma could never believe it, and her feelings were dreadfully hurt. But they couldn’t stop. Sitting in church, going to supper at the Blows, they didn’t dare look at each other, because they were bursting to laugh.

===Chinese===
“孩子们，笑什么呢？”

“没什么，妈妈，真的，千真万确没什么笑话。”

也确实没什么，可妈妈说什么也不信，心里委屈得不行。可他们就是忍不住。坐在教堂里，去布洛家吃晚饭，他们谁也不敢看谁，因为一看对方，就要笑破了肚皮。

===Original===
He laughed at her, and she loved it, even when he laughed at things she meant perfectly seriously. Because he never laughed at the wrong things. He understood so wonderfully that sometimes she thought she was dying of happiness. When she told him, shy, even with him, the things other people would have waited for her to finish﻿—the word, the broken sentence﻿—the tears came into her eyes as he answered, “I know, my darling.”

Even when they fought, when they were furious at each other, they felt as if sparkles and flames were running over them, leaping towards each other.

Lily couldn’t understand it at all. She thought when people were engaged they exchanged locks of hair and held each other’s hands. Two pale pink hearts tied together with a pale blue ribbon. How *could* Edward and Maggie talk to each other the way they did?

===Chinese===
他笑话她，她也欢喜，哪怕她明明是一板一眼说的正经话，他也照笑不误。因为他从不在不该笑的地方笑。他善解人意到这般地步，有时她觉得自己幸福得快要死了。她害羞，连对他也害羞，说那些换了别人必得等她说完的事——一个字眼，半截句子——他只答一句“我知道，亲爱的”，泪水便涌上了她的眼睛。

就连吵架的时候，彼此气得七窍生烟的时候，他们也觉得火星和火焰在周身乱窜，噼噼啪啪，朝着对方扑过去。

莉莉全然闹不明白。她以为人订了婚，就是互换一绺头发，彼此牵牵手。两颗淡粉红的心，用一条浅蓝缎带系在一处。爱德华和玛吉怎么能那样跟对方说话呢？

===Original===
“It’s tremendously stimulating,” Edward explained.

“Edward makes me so *mad*!” added Maggie. They smiled at each other, and again the invisible lightning leapt.

“You mustn’t be so selfish, children,” Mamma reproved them. “You mustn’t grumble so when people ask you out. It’s very kind of them.”

So they went to evening parties; and Mamma, beaming, handed Edward around like a plate of delicious cake.

===Chinese===
“这来劲儿得很呢。”爱德华解释道。

“爱德华气死我啦！”玛吉补了一句。两人相视一笑，那道看不见的闪电又倏地跳起。

“孩子们，不许这样自私，”妈妈训斥他们。“人家请你们出去做客，不许这么嘟嘟囔囔。人家是一片好意。”

于是他们去赴晚间聚会；妈妈满面春风，把爱德华满场递送，像递一盘人人爱吃的好蛋糕。

===Original===
“Mrs. Holly, this is Mr. Post! Cousin Jennie, I want you to know Edward﻿—”

And Maggie, in her flounced and frilled white ball gown with a bodice like a black satin corset, was saying:

“Thank you﻿—thank you ever so much. Yes, isn’t he? We don’t know yet. Thank you, Cousin Jennie. Yes, indeed, I am. Oh, not for ever so long.”

“Maggie, hurry up! Stop talking and come!” And his voice sounded quite desperate. Oh, together again! “The Blue Danube”﻿—how sad, how sweet! Was she going to disgrace herself by crying at a party?

“Edward﻿—”

“Yes, darling, I know.”

===Chinese===
“霍利太太（Mrs. Holly），这位是波斯特先生！珍妮表姐（Cousin Jennie），我正要让你认识认识爱德华——”

玛吉呢，穿着镶荷叶边、缀褶裥花边的白纱舞裙，上身束得像一件黑缎紧身褡，也正应酬着：

“谢谢——真是多谢您了。是啊，可不是嘛。我们还说不准呢。谢谢您，珍妮表姐。可不是，我正是呢。哦，还得好久好久呢。”

“玛吉，快点儿！别聊了，快来！”他的嗓音听上去简直急得不行。哦，又在一起了！《蓝色多瑙河》——多么哀伤，又多么甜美！她该不会在舞会上哭出声来，出自己的洋相吧？

“爱德华——”

“嗯，亲爱的，我知道。”

===Original===
But sometimes, when he talked to Cousin Sam and Uncle Willie about fishing and shooting, he looked so interested, so absorbed, that she thought, “This is his real life; this is what really interests him.” Sadness and loneliness covered her, darkening the light. And then to find that he had suffered as much as she all those hours, those years, when they were not alone together!

One Sunday in spring a late snow fell, light and wet, so that everything was deep in swansdown. The scillas were already in bloom, and she cleared the snow from them to show him, while the flakes still sifted from the grey sky, melting on their cheeks.

He hardly looked at them, but why did the patch of blue flowers in the white snow make her so happy? And why did old Mrs. Latter suddenly pop into her head﻿—old Mrs. Latter, who had been dead for years? She hadn’t thought of her since the day Mamma took her there when she was a little bit of a girl﻿—how long ago? But now she saw the wrinkled, white cheeks like crumpled tissue-paper, with their big spots of moth-wing brown; smelled cologne﻿—

What was it? She *must* remember﻿—she mustn’t lose it. It was something that mattered tremendously to Edward and to her.

And just as she gave up, came the feeling of soft little hands on her heart, light as the touch of the snow. And through all the years between she saw, hanging on Mrs. Latter’s wall, the circle of scilla-blue, the circle of Heaven, and the snow-white baby holding his arms wide open﻿—waiting for her, holding out his hands to be taken.

===Chinese===
可有时候，他跟萨姆表哥和威利叔叔（Uncle Willie）聊打鱼聊打猎，聊得那么兴致勃勃、那么入神，她就暗想：“这才是他真正的生活；这才是他真心喜欢的事。”忧伤和孤单罩上她的心头，把光都罩暗了。结果到头来才发现——那些时辰，那些年头，他们不单独在一处的时候，他心里熬的，竟一点也不比她少！

春天里一个礼拜天，下了一场迟雪，又轻又湿，天地万物都深深陷在天鹅绒毛里。蓝钟花（scilla）已经开了，她扒开积雪捧给他看，雪片还在从灰蒙蒙的天上簌簌筛下来，落在他们脸颊上就化了。

他几乎没有看那些花。可为什么白雪里那一小片蓝花，叫她那样欢喜？为什么老拉特太太（Mrs. Latter）会突然蹦进她的脑子——死了好些年的老拉特太太？打妈妈领她上她家去的那天起——那时她还是个小不点儿的姑娘，是多少年前了？——她就再没想起过她。可这会儿，她看见那张皱巴巴的白脸，像揉皱的薄绵纸，纸上带着蛾翅棕色的大斑点；闻见一股古龙水——

那是什么？她非想起来不可——千万不能丢。那是件对爱德华和她都了不得要紧的东西。

恰在她要撒手放弃的当口，一双柔软的小手轻轻搭上了她的心口，轻得像雪的抚触。隔着这中间的年年岁岁，她看见挂在拉特太太墙上的那一幅：一圈蓝钟花似的蓝，一圈天堂，还有那雪白的婴孩，两臂张得开开的——等着她，伸出小手，等她去牵。

---

===Original===
One evening at the supper table Maggie said, too casually, too airily:

“Edward’s going out West.”

“*No!*” said Mamma. Lily stopped mopping up the chicken gravy with her bread, and stared with round blue eyes and round pink mouth.

“The company never sent such a young man before﻿—and it means quite a lot more salary.”

“Then you can get married sooner,” May said, smiling at her.

“Yes!” Oh, darling May! Maggie’s heart glowed with gratitude.

“Poor little girl,” said Mamma.

“I want him to go! He asked me if he should, and I told him to go.”

“Don’t you *mind*?” asked Lily.

(“*I have been sentenced to death next week.*”

“*Don’t you mind?*”)

“Would he like us to keep his bicycle for him?” Victor suggested in a small respectful voice. “I’d keep it oiled and everything, and I wouldn’t ride it and I wouldn’t let Jake ride it either. You ask him, Maggie.”

Under the table Maggie squeezed her brother’s hand. The *darling*!

===Chinese===
一天晚饭桌上，玛吉开了口，说得那样不经意，那样轻飘飘：

“爱德华要上西部去了。”

“不！”妈妈说。莉莉停住了拿面包蘸鸡汤的动作，瞪圆了蓝眼睛，粉红的小嘴张得圆圆的。

“公司从没外派过这么年轻的人——再说，这可是多出来一大截的薪水呢。”

“那你们就能早点儿结婚啦。”梅含笑望着她说。

“是啊！”哦，亲爱的梅！玛吉心里腾起一股感激的暖意。

“可怜的小丫头。”妈妈说。

“我要他去！他问我要不要去，我跟他说，去。”

“你不难过吗？”莉莉问。

（「我已被判处死刑，下星期执行。」

「你不难过吗？」）

“他愿不愿意把自行车留在咱们家，替他保管？”维克托小小的嗓音里透着恭恭敬敬，这样提议。“我会给它上油，样样照料好，我自己不骑，也不让杰克（Jake）骑。你去问问他，玛吉。”

桌子底下，玛吉捏了捏弟弟的手。这个小宝贝！

===Original===
But she didn’t realize that Edward was really going, as day after day went by, until tomorrow was the day.

Here he was, here, his coat, his cheek, his hair, here to be touched and felt, his arms holding her close. Now he is here! Tomorrow he will be gone. How can it be possible that of their own volition they will part, he will go, she will stay?

When she was in his arms, he felt as if he would never dare let her go. Suppose he should lose her. A bird lies in your hands, yours to keep for always. But open your hands and the bird is lost in the sky, and, if you wait forever, your hands will still be empty.

“Maggie﻿—it won’t be so terribly long﻿—”

“It won’t be﻿—a bit﻿—” She turned her head away and bent down a branch of the snowball bush, broke off a still green snowball, and slowly, carefully, began to pull it apart and make little heaps of the blossoms on the bench beside her, three in each heap. A tear splashed down beside them.

“You’re crying!”

“I’m not!” She turned to show him she wasn’t, tears streaming over her face, her body shaken by tearing sobs.

“Mag‑gie! Oh, Mag‑gie!”

From the porch Victor’s voice calling her. Then louder﻿—then far away.

“Mag‑gie!”

“Maggie, Maggie, promise you’ll always love me!”

Oh, she could promise that! She could even laugh as she promised.

“Maggie, where are you?”

“Here, Victor.”

“Oh!” The voice came to a standstill behind a hedge, sympathetically distant. “Well, Mamma said to tell you you must come in, she says to tell you it’s getting very chilly and you have a cold.”

“All right, we’re coming.”

Edward went down on the grass at her feet, clinging to her, burying his face on her knees. Night was coming, and lifting her lips from the dear head, the dark head, she saw each star in the sky change to a cross through her tears.

===Chinese===
可是日子一天一天过去，她一直没有真切地意识到爱德华真的要走了，直到明天，就是动身的日子。

他就在这儿，就在这儿，他的衣裳，他的脸颊，他的头发，就在手边，摸得着，触得到，他的胳膊紧紧搂着她。现在他还在！明天他就要走了。这怎么可能呢——他们竟要出自本心、自愿地分开，他走，她留下？

她偎在他怀里，他觉得仿佛永远不敢撒开手。万一失去她呢。一只小鸟躺在你的手心里，归你了，可以永远留下。可你一摊开手，鸟就没入天空再寻不见，你就是等上一世，手心里也仍旧是空的。

“玛吉——不会太久，不会久得吓人——”

“不久——一点儿也不——”她扭过头去，扳低一枝雪球花树，折下一朵还发青的雪球花，慢慢地、仔仔细细地把它拆散，将小花一小堆一小堆地码在她身旁的长凳上，每堆三个。一滴眼泪啪地落在花堆旁边。

“你哭了！”

“我才没有！”她转过脸来给他看她没哭，泪水却正满脸横流，撕心裂肺的呜咽抖动着她的身子。

“玛——吉！哦，玛——吉！”

门廊那边传来维克托喊她的声音。随后喊得更响——随后远了。

“玛——吉！”

“玛吉，玛吉，答应我，你要永远爱我！”

哦，这个她答应得出口！答应的时候，她甚至还能笑出来。

“玛吉，你在哪儿？”

“在这儿，维克托。”

“哦！”那声音在一道树篱后头停住了，知情识趣地隔着段距离。“嗯，妈妈叫我来告诉你，你得进屋了；她说告诉你，天越来越凉了，你还带着感冒呢。”

“好，这就来。”

爱德华伏倒在她脚边的草地上，紧紧偎着她，把脸埋进她的膝头。夜正在降临；她把嘴唇从那颗可爱的头颅上——那颗黑黑的头颅上——抬起来，透过泪水，她看见天上的每一颗星星，都变成了十字架。

===Original===
The family went to bed early, so that Maggie and Edward could be alone for their last, aching goodbyes. But Mamma couldn’t sleep. For the third time she lit her candle, and looked at Papa’s big watch that hung in the beaded pocket at the head of the bed. It was dreadfully late. He ought to have gone home ever so long ago.

“Well, I’m not going to worry about them,” she told herself. But she lay rigid, almost afraid to breathe, waiting for the sound of the front door closing and Maggie climbing the creaking stairs.

The house was coming alive﻿—here a whisper, there a patter, not of mice nor of rain. It was breathing, you could hear it sigh.

“Perhaps he’s gone home without my hearing,” thought Mamma. “Anyway, I’m just going quietly to sleep.”

So she got up and stole out into the hall and sat down on the blanket box at the top of the stairs. There wasn’t a sound, but the lamp in the downstairs hall was still burning, and by leaning over she could see his hat lying on the sofa. This was nonsense! *Such* an hour! What were those crazy children thinking of? She would just call over the stairs﻿—

What made the whole house feel so strange, trembling and alive? The banister seemed to quiver under her hand. The ticking of the clock on the stairs sounded like water falling, drop by drop﻿—water that could never be gathered up again.

Cautiously, silently, she got up, avoided the squeaking board, went back into her room, and closed the door.

===Chinese===
一家人早早上了床，好让玛吉和爱德华单独待着，作那最后痛断肝肠的道别。可妈妈睡不着。她已经是第三回点亮蜡烛，去看挂在床头串珠小袋里的爸爸那只大怀表。夜深得吓人。他早在好半天之前就该回家了。

“咳，我才不替他们操心呢。”她对自己说。可她直挺挺地躺着，大气也不敢出，等着听前门关上的声响，等着玛吉爬上嘎吱作响的楼梯。

这房子正慢慢活过来——这里一声低语，那里一阵轻碎的脚步，不是老鼠，也不是雨。它在呼吸，你听得见它叹息。

“说不定他已经走了，我没听见呢，”妈妈想。“不管怎么说，我这会儿就安安静静睡觉。”

于是她起身，蹑手蹑脚溜到过道里，在楼梯口的毯子箱上坐下来。一点声息也没有，可楼下过道的灯还亮着，她探身一望，看得见他的帽子搁在沙发上。这成什么话！都什么时辰了！那两个疯孩子到底在想什么呀？她只要隔着楼梯喊一声——

是什么叫整座房子显得这样异样，颤巍巍、活生生似的？楼梯扶手仿佛在她手底下微微发颤。楼梯上那座钟的滴答声，听着像水一滴一滴往下落——落下去就再也收不回来的水。

她小心翼翼、悄没声儿地站起身，避开那块踩上去会叫唤的地板，回自己房里去了，随手掩上了门。

## XV / XV

===Original===
Even Willie said the new cook was good. When he finished his second cup of coffee and said, “I’ve only had one cup, haven’t I?” Aunt Priscilla was so pleased that she told a story, and said, “Yes, just one,” as she poured out his third. Think of it! Three cups, when usually he only tasted it and pushed it aside.

“Isn’t your coffee all right, Willie?” she would ask him anxiously, and he would answer:

“Coffee, my dear? Oh—do you mean my cup of warmish dishwater?”

Three cups of coffee and two helps of fried chicken and three *big* pieces of cornbread, and when he finished breakfast he smiled and patted her cheek.

She felt so happy, like a good little girl who has been praised. When he went out, she got down on her knees in the dining-room, with her head on his chair, while flies buzzed around her and the parrot screamed, “Will-*lee*! Hello! Want a crack-*kah*? Hello! Oh dear!”

“Our Father which art in Heaven, thank thee *ever* so much for making Cobina a good cook and thank thee for Willie. Amen.”

===Chinese===
连威利（Willie）都说新厨子好。他喝完第二杯咖啡，说：“我才喝了一杯，是吧？”普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）欢喜得撒了个小谎，一边给他斟上第三杯，一边说：“是啊，才一杯呢。”你想想！三杯呀——往常他不过沾一沾嘴唇，就推到一边去了。

“威利，你的咖啡不对劲儿吗？”她总要这样担心地问他，他便回答：

“咖啡，亲爱的？哦——你是说我这杯温吞吞的洗碟子水吧？”

三杯咖啡，添了两回的炸鸡，还有三大块玉米面包；吃罢早饭，他笑眯眯地拍了拍她的脸蛋。

她心里欢喜极了，像受了夸奖的乖小姑娘。他出门以后，她在饭厅里跪下来，头伏在他的椅子上；苍蝇围着她嗡嗡打转，鹦鹉扯着嗓子尖叫：“威——利！哈啰！要块薄脆饼不？哈啰！哎呀老天！”

“我们在天上的父，千恩万谢，谢谢你把科比娜（Cobina）造成一个好厨子，谢谢你把威利赐给我。阿们。”

===Original===
She must do something nice for somebody, she felt so happy. The peaches were ripe on her own peach tree, the tree Willie had planted on her first birthday after they were married.
