She loved her little peach tree﻿—its pink blooming in spring was one of the few things that pierced through the mist that surrounded her, and its peaches were much better than any other peaches. Who should she take them to? Mrs. Holly must be ill, she hadn’t been in church on Sunday. And although the Hollys had dozens of peach trees, Aunt Priscilla filled her basket, wobbling on a kitchen chair. Beautiful miracles of pink and yellow velvet! She could have kissed each one. She did kiss the little tree, softly and shyly, and then looked around in a panic to make sure Washington hadn’t seen her.

===Chinese===
她这么欢喜，非得给谁做点儿好事才好。她自己那棵桃树上，桃子熟了——那棵树，是威利在他们婚后她过的头一个生日那天栽下的。她爱她的小桃树——春天里那一树粉红的花，是为数不多能穿透她周身迷雾的东西；树上的桃子，又比谁家的桃子都好得多。该送给谁呢？霍利太太（Mrs. Holly）准是病了，礼拜天没上教堂。虽说霍利家有的是几十棵桃树，普丽西拉婶婶还是踩着厨房的椅子、摇摇晃晃地摘满了一篮。粉红鹅黄、天鹅绒似的美丽奇迹！她恨不得一只一只都亲一遍。她当真亲了亲那棵小树，轻轻的，怯生生的，随即慌慌张张四下张望，生怕叫华盛顿（Washington）瞧见了。

===Original===
She came home from the Hollys walking on air. Over the brook﻿—my *good*ness, what wobbly stepping stones! Suppose she sat down in the water, wouldn’t those water-spiders be surprised, and the small fish slipping about like shadows? So cool, so clear, for two pins she’d go in paddling. That bright, bright moss at the side with the crystal runlets of water trickling over it, reminded her of the green velvet dress with the crystal beading that Willie had liked, ever so long ago.

Mushrooms! Growing in long white drifts on the short green grass of the pasture. She began to fill her basket, empty now except for its permanent contents of a key that had long ago lost its door, with a bit of dingy red ribbon tied to it; a paper screw of seeds (seeds of what? She hadn’t the least idea); an elderly list of blurred pencilling, headed “*Must do*”; and a few twists of grubby string. Transient knitting, Christmas cookies, eggs, mail, fruit, and flowers covered them from time to time, but these old residents remained unmoved.

Most of the mushrooms were too old, after all, black and wormy. She got enough big brown-lined umbrellas for a nice little dish, but she couldn’t find any of the silky white ones with the pink linings like the basketful Margaret and the girls brought her last week.

Then, by the edge of the wood, she saw the most beautiful one, all by itself, and further on, another. More beautiful than any of Margaret’s, all silvery, inside and out. She found five before she was through. They gleamed against the shadows of the wood, beautiful, lonely, and white﻿—angels of death.

And then she thought, why not stop and ask Margaret to lunch? Because she loved mushrooms, and here they were, and there was Cobina to cook them.

===Chinese===
从霍利家回来，她一路像踩着云端。过小溪的时候——我的老天，这些踏脚石晃得多厉害！要是她一屁股坐进水里，那些水蜘蛛还不得吓一大跳？还有那些小鱼，影子似的滑来滑去。水这么凉，这么清，她真恨不得下去蹚一蹚。溪边那一片亮晶晶、亮晶晶的青苔，一缕缕水晶似的水淌过上面，叫她想起好多年以前那条缀水晶珠子的绿丝绒裙子，威利喜欢过的那一条。

蘑菇！一溜一溜白茫茫的，长在牧场短短的绿草上。她动手往篮子里捡。篮子这会儿空了，只剩几样常驻的物件：一把早就没了门的钥匙，拴着一小截发乌的红丝带；一纸包种子（什么种子？她一点儿也说不准）；一张上了年头、铅笔字迹模糊的单子，抬头写着“必做事项”；还有几小团油污的细绳。织到一半的毛线活、圣诞小饼、鸡蛋、邮件、水果、鲜花，隔三差五在它们上面盖一阵，可这些老住户岿然不动。

到底还是大多数蘑菇都太老了，发黑，长虫。她捡够了那种褐色衬里的大伞盖，够做好好一小盘了；可那种缎子似的白蘑菇、伞里衬着粉红的，像上礼拜玛格丽特（Margaret）带着姑娘们给她提来的满满一篮——却一朵也找不着。

后来，在林子边上，她看见一朵顶美的，孤零零独自一朵；再往前，又是一朵。比玛格丽特的哪一朵都美，里里外外一片银色。一路捡到底，她一共找到了五朵。它们衬着林中的暗影微微发亮，美丽，孤单，洁白——死亡的天使。

接着她想，何不顺路请玛格丽特留下吃午饭呢？她爱吃蘑菇，蘑菇这不是现成的么，家里又有科比娜来做。

---

===Original===
“Really, Priscilla, she’s a treasure!”

“Willie says so, too,” said Aunt Priscilla, beaming. “No, the mushrooms are all for you﻿—they cook down so, don’t they? No, really, I never want anything else when I have corn-fritters, and Cobina says she wouldn’t be paid to eat them. Toads, she calls them﻿—did you ever? Short for toadstools, I guess. Now take them all or I’ll feel bad, I gathered them especially for you. Co‑o‑*bina*! Oh, Cobina, you might just let us have a few more of the corn-fritters﻿—do you think she looks all right? She’s so black it’s sort of hard to tell, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“说真的，普丽西拉，她真是个宝！”

“威利也这么说，”普丽西拉婶婶满面春风。“不，蘑菇全归你——它们一炒就缩得厉害，是不是？不，说真的，有玉米煎饼我就再不要别的了，科比娜还说给她钱她都不肯吃呢。她管蘑菇叫「蛤蟆」——你听说过吗？我猜是「蛤蟆菌」的简称吧。快都拿去，不然我心里要过意不去的，我是特意给你捡的。科——比——娜！哎，科比娜，再给我们添几个玉米煎饼好不好——你瞧她气色还好吧？她黑得那么实在，瞧不大出来，是不是？”

===Original===
“She’s better than Lizzie’s wonderful fancy cook,” said Mamma, mopping up the mushroom gravy on her biscuit. “What’s the matter with Lizzie, anyway, Priscilla? She’s acting very queer lately﻿—just *half* a cup﻿—oh, you bad girl, I said half!”

“Don’t tell, but Willie thinks she’s going crazy!” Aunt Priscilla’s eyes looked like pale blue glass marbles ready to pop out of her head. “He went over last Tuesday﻿—was it Tuesday? What day was it the old peddler woman came? Anyway, he wanted Sam to go down state after reedbirds with him, and he said you could hear Lizzie screaming clear out on the road; and, when he got up to the house, he heard Sam sort of yell, ‘You’d better be careful, my lady, or I’ll lock you up, that’s what I’ll do!’ It made Willie feel so queer he never stopped at all﻿—here, let me take away your plate, and I’ll get some chocolate cake.” She opened the lower doors of her husband’s desk, where she kept the cake box, the vanilla bottle, and the broken plates with the hummingbirds and morning-glories that she planned to mend some day.

===Chinese===
“她比丽齐表姐（Cousin Lizzie）家那位了不得的花哨厨子强多了，”妈妈说着，拿软饼抹着盘里的蘑菇浓汁。“不过丽齐表姐到底是怎么了，普丽西拉？她最近行事古怪得很——就半杯——哦，你这坏丫头，我说的是半杯！”

“可别对外人说，威利觉得她要发疯啦！”普丽西拉婶婶的两只眼睛瞪得像两粒淡蓝的玻璃弹珠，眼看就要从脑袋里蹦出来。“上礼拜二他过去了一趟——是礼拜二吗？那个上门的老货婆来是礼拜几来着？反正，他想约萨姆·布洛（Sam Blow）下州南边打稻鸡去，他说人还在大路上，就听得见丽齐的尖叫，一直传到外头；等他走到宅子跟前，又听见萨姆扯着嗓子嚷：「你给我小心着点儿，我的太太，不然我把你锁起来，我可说得出做得到！」威利心里别扭得什么似的，压根儿没停就走了——来，我把你的盘子撤下去，我去切块巧克力蛋糕。”她打开丈夫书桌的下层柜门，蛋糕盒就搁在里头，还有香草精瓶子，和几只裂了的盘子——画着蜂鸟和牵牛花的，她总打算着哪天粘一粘。

===Original===
“Priscilla﻿—excuse me﻿—it comes so quickly, this damp, warm weather﻿—but I don’t believe we’d better eat that, I’m afraid it’s molded just a *little*.”

“Why, so it has!” She looked at the beautiful cake, so rich and black, but with spots of mold, furry grey on the icing. “Do you think it would hurt? Well, I suppose it’s better to be safe than sorry.” Sadly she put the cake back in the box.

“Jo Allen has marked four dozen tea napkins for Maggie.”

“Has she?” She brightened up again, sprinkled sugar thickly on a piece of bread and butter, and settled to enjoyment. “You know I was thinking this morning of a green velvet dress I had the year I was married﻿—I believe if it was steamed it would make over into a right pretty sacque for Maggie. And Willie and I want to give her her great-grandmother’s silver teaspoons. Has Lizzie given her anything yet?”

===Chinese===
“普丽西拉——你可别怪我——这几天又潮又热，东西说坏就坏——那块蛋糕，我看咱们顶好别吃了，我怕它有一丁点儿长毛了。”

“哎呀，还真长了！”她端详着那块好看的蛋糕，乌黑厚重，糖霜上却斑斑点点起了毛，灰茸茸的。“你说吃了碍事不碍事？唉，小心总没错，免得后悔。”她怏怏地把蛋糕放回了盒子里。

“乔·艾伦（Jo Allen）已经给玛吉（Maggie）的四打茶巾绣好记号了。”

“是吗？”她又高兴起来，往一片涂了黄油的面包上厚厚撒了一层糖，安安稳稳受用起来。“你知道吗，我今天早上正想着我结婚那年的那条绿丝绒裙子——我看拿去蒸一蒸，给玛吉改一件怪漂亮的宽松短上衣还成。威利和我还想把她太外婆那几把银茶匙送给她。丽齐送过她什么没有？”

---

===Original===
Beautiful, lonely, and white, the Angel of Death. Whiter than fire, whiter than snow, the great wings curve above Mamma, their shadow covers her. The flies buzz, the parrot says “Oh dear!” and scratches its head, Aunt Priscilla scratches her head, too, and takes a half moon bite of bread and sugar.

===Chinese===
美丽，孤单，洁白，死亡的天使。比火更白，比雪更白，巨大的双翅在妈妈头顶弯拱下来，翅影罩住了她。苍蝇嗡嗡，鹦鹉叫着“哎呀老天！”挠挠头；普丽西拉婶婶也挠挠头，咬了半月形的一口蘸糖面包。

---

===Original===
Mamma strolled home﻿—the shortcut through the cornfield. The river had never been so blue. Over the stile and up through the garden﻿—two or three more days and the asters would be lovely﻿—oh, such a fine plant broken! That was Victor’s new puppy Bundle, bad little thing.

She stopped to gather a few nasturtiums, thinking, “I believe I could get a whole dress for Maggie out of Priscilla’s green velvet if I had it open over a satin underskirt.” Poor Priscilla! Such a goose, but so kind. She wouldn’t harm a fly.

She paused to eat a peach, looking thoughtful and thinking of nothing. Then across the lawn and into the house. And with her went the Angel of Death.

Stop and look at your flowers, little child, and I will wait for you. Eat your peach, there is no hurry. No need to hurry now, we are nearly there.

===Chinese===
妈妈缓步溜达着回家——抄玉米地里的近路。河从没这么蓝过。跨过栅栏踏蹬，上坡穿过花园——再过两三天，紫菀就开得好看了一哦，这么好的一棵花，折断了！准是维克托（Victor）的新小狗邦德尔（Bundle），坏东西。

她停下来摘了几朵旱金莲，心里盘算：“我看普丽西拉那块绿丝绒，做成敞襟的，底下衬条缎子衬裙，够给玛吉做一整条裙子。”可怜的普丽西拉！这么个糊涂人，心肠又这么好。她连一只苍蝇都不肯伤害。

她又停下来吃了只桃子，神情若有所思，脑子里其实什么也没想。然后穿过草坪，进了家门。死亡的天使随她一同进去。

停下来看看你的花儿吧，小孩子，我等着你。吃你的桃子吧，不用急。如今不用急了，我们就快到了。

===Original===
The house was still, for the children had gone on a picnic. She could hear her canaries hopping and swinging in their cages. How cool and fresh the darkened house was after Priscilla’s sunny dusty rooms. That was a good cook, though. She had enjoyed her lunch.

Should she go and tell Martha she was home? Better not, perhaps, in case she wasn’t expected so soon. It had been embarrassing all around, last week, after Martha had said there wasn’t any cold duck left, to come upon her feeding it to a strange young darkie. She was forty if she was a day, too, the silly! But Mamma certainly didn’t want to catch her at anything she would have to disapprove of.

She went up to her room and took off her dress. Just a little lie-down before supper.

The great wings drooped above her, closer, closer. “Go to sleep, little child. I will be here when you wake.”

===Chinese===
屋子里静悄悄的，孩子们野餐去了。她听得见她的金丝雀在笼子里蹦蹦跳跳、荡着秋千。普丽西拉家那些屋子又太阳又落灰，自己这拉暗了窗帘的家又多凉快、多清爽。不过话说回来，那真是个好厨子。这顿午饭她吃得很受用。

要不要去告诉玛莎（Martha）她回来了？也许还是不去的好，省得她想不到她这么早就回来。上礼拜就有过一回尴尬——玛莎明明说冷的烤鸭没有了，却叫她撞见，她正拿那鸭子喂一个陌生的年轻黑人小伙子呢。那丫头瞧着少说也有四十岁了，傻东西！不过妈妈实在不想再撞见什么她不得不拉下脸来数落的勾当。

她上楼回到自己房里，脱了外衣。晚饭前稍微躺一躺。

巨大的双翅在她头顶垂落下来，越来越近，越来越近。“睡吧，小孩子。你醒来的时候，我就在这儿。”

---

===Original===
Every night Maggie almost prayed that Edward would come to her in her dreams. Praying was done on your knees, in churches and by bedsides, beginning properly, “Our Father” or “O Blessed Lord,” and dealing in stately language with reformation or protection. Almost praying was the quick warm gush of gratitude or pleading: “Oh, *thank* you!”, when Edward’s letter was extra long, or “Please *please* let me dream of him,” every night as she fell asleep.

But she dreamed of Mamma and the girls, of old Benny Brown with his beard in a braid, of Lossie’s black baby, of Victor’s new puppy﻿—anybody, anything but Edward.

===Chinese===
每天夜里，玛吉都近乎祷告似的祈求爱德华（Edward）到她梦里来。祷告是正经事，要跪着做，在教堂里，在床边，规规矩矩以“我们在天上的父”或“哦，蒙福的主”开头，拿庄重的辞令求悔改、求庇佑。而近乎祷告，是那一股又急又暖的感激或恳求：“哦，谢谢！”——当爱德华的信格外长的时候；或者“求你了，求你让我梦见他”——每天夜里入睡的时候。

可是她梦见的偏偏是妈妈和姑娘们，是胡子编成辫子的老本尼·布朗（Benny Brown），是罗西（Lossie）家的黑娃娃，是维克托的新小狗——什么人、什么事都有，独独没有爱德华。

---

===Original===
The long-nosed market-woman who always had the nice cheese and the tight bunches of marigolds and red bee-balm was sitting up in a pine tree on the drive. Knock! Knock! Knock! Maggie could see the long sharp nose sticking out from her sunbonnet as she struck it against the tree trunk like a giant woodpecker. Knock! Knock!

But she’d ruin the pine tree! Shoo!

Spreading her shawl, the market-woman flapped through the air, and settled in another tree, far off. Knock! Knock! Knock! It was fainter now.

Maggie rose from the sea of sleep and drifted, nearly awake, on the surface. The knocking came again.

She sat up in bed, listening. How queer! It came low down on her door, so low that she saw, still half-dreaming, a dwarf in the dark hall, knocking.

===Chinese===
那个长鼻子的卖菜妇——总有上好干酪、总有扎得紧紧的万寿菊和红蜂香花的那一个——正端坐在车道旁一棵松树上。笃！笃！笃！玛吉看得见那又长又尖的鼻子从她的遮阳帽檐底下探出来，一下一下磕着树干，活像只大啄木鸟。笃！笃！

可她要把松树毁啦！去！去！

卖菜妇张开披肩，扑扇着飞过半空，落到远处另一棵树上。笃！笃！笃！这会儿声音轻了。

玛吉从睡眠的大海里浮起来，漂在水面上，半睡半醒。敲门声又响了。

她在床上坐起来听。多奇怪呀！声音落在门上很低的地方，低得她半梦半醒间看见一个矮人立在黑黢黢的过道里，敲着门。

===Original===
“Maggie﻿—”

It was Mamma, huddled on the floor, leaning against the door.

“Oh, Maggie, I’m so sick﻿—”

“Oh, poor Mamma! Come, let me get you back to bed.”

“My stomach aches so﻿—and my legs﻿—” She lay in her bed, her knees drawn up, whimpering a little, her blue beseeching eyes darkening with pain as pools darken in a storm.

===Chinese===
“玛吉——”

是妈妈，缩作一团坐在地板上，倚着门。

“哦，玛吉，我难受极了——”

“哦，可怜的妈妈！来，我扶你回床上去。”

“我肚子疼得厉害——腿也是——”她躺在床上，双膝蜷起，低低呜咽着；那双蓝色的哀恳的眼睛，正像水塘在暴雨里一泓泓暗下去似的，被疼痛一点点浸黑。

===Original===
Maggie waited in the kitchen for the water to heat. The loud hurrying tick of the clock, the slow drip of the water-tap, the dark high ceiling, high as the night, that the faint light of her lamp could not reach, made her feel weak with loneliness. She was frightened﻿—something was waiting in the dark behind her. “Edward﻿—!” she called across the miles.

Then a chill of terror crinkled over her as she heard Mamma scream.

Three days, three nights. That screaming, thin as a knife, that shaking, that hurling back and forth in the deep white bed. Nothing could counteract the poison of that dish Aunt Priscilla had offered her so lovingly. Dr. Chase couldn’t help her, no one could help her, though everyone came to ask, driving up with baskets of grapes and autumn flowers, and saying, “If there’s *anything* we can do﻿—!” Martha worked all day and most of the night, good as gold, feeling sad and excited and bursting with importance. On Sunday morning the congregation prayed for Margaret, stealing glances full of that strange excitement at the Campions’ empty pew.

===Chinese===
玛吉在厨房里等水烧热。时钟的滴答声又响又急，水龙头的水滴又慢又沉，黑黢黢的天花板高得像夜空，她那盏灯的微光怎么也够不着——这一切叫她孤单得浑身发软。她害怕——有什么东西正在她背后的黑暗里等着。“爱德华——！”她隔着迢迢千里喊了一声。

接着，她听见妈妈一声尖叫，一阵恐惧的寒意簌簌地爬满全身。

三天，三夜。那样的尖叫，薄得像刀子；那样的抽搐，那样的翻来倒去，在那张铺得雪白的大床上。普丽西拉婶婶满怀恩爱端给她的那一盘菜，毒性已没有什么能消解。蔡斯医生（Dr. Chase）救不了她，谁也救不了她，虽然人人都赶着车来问安，载来一篮篮葡萄和秋花，说着“但凡有什么我们能效劳的——！”玛莎白天连轴转，大半夜也不歇，乖得没挑儿，心里又难过又兴奋，浑身涨满一件大事在身的得意。礼拜天早晨，会众为玛格丽特祈祷，一边偷偷朝坎皮恩家空着的长椅张望，目光里满是那种说不清道不明的激动。

===Original===
“O Father of mercies and God of all comfort, our only help in time of need; look down from heaven, we humbly beseech thee, behold, visit, and relieve thy sick servant﻿—”

The peaceful grey walls with their moss-rosebuds and lace, the fresh, white curtains, the piled white clouds in the soft sky, looked at Mamma tranquilly. The stoppers of her perfume bottles threw splashes of rainbows over her white bed. And all this still rose and silver brightness, this sweet, accustomed peace, held in its heart the black whirlpool in which she was sinking.

===Chinese===
“哦，众怜悯之父，万安慰之上帝，我们患难中唯一的倚靠；求你从天上垂看，我们谦卑恳求你，眷顾、临到、宽解你患病的使女——”

安宁静谧的灰墙壁，缀着苔蔷薇花蕾的花边，簇新雪白的窗帘，还有柔和的天上堆叠的白云，都安安静静地望着妈妈。她那些香水瓶的瓶塞，在雪白的床上投下一片一片的虹彩。而这一切恬静的玫瑰色与银亮，这甘美惯常的安宁，心口里却衔着一个黑色的漩涡，她正在那漩涡里下沉。

===Original===
Comfort came to her twice through her pain, not in the thought of Papa, not in prayer. Once May brought in a vase of fragrant, pink tea-roses blurred with a silvery bloom of dew, with delicate sprays of dusk red leaves, and Mamma’s tortured and bewildered eyes saw them and loved them. And once she whispered, “Maggie, if I﻿—if anything should happen, promise you’ll always take care of Victor.”

“Oh, I will, I will!”

She smiled. For a moment she floated at peace, before the dark tumult sucked her in again.

She longed for Victor passionately, but she wouldn’t let him come to her, for fear of frightening him.

===Chinese===
在这痛苦之中，安慰来过两回——不在对爸爸的思念里，也不在祷告里。一回，梅（May）捧进来一瓶芬芳的粉红茶香月季，蒙着一层银蒙蒙的露水，衬着几枝纤细的暗红叶子，妈妈那双受尽折磨、茫然无主的眼睛看见了它们，爱上了它们。又一回，她低声说：“玛吉，要是我——要是有什么三长两短，你答应我，永远照看好维克托。”

“哦，我会的，我会的！”

她笑了。有一小会儿，她安安稳稳地漂着，随后黑暗的湍流又把她吸了进去。

她热烈地渴念着维克托，却不肯让他到跟前来，生怕吓着他。

===Original===
“Can’t I see Mamma, Maggie?”

“Not just yet, honey. Wait until she’s better.”

“Would she like Bundle?”

“Not just yet.”

So he would go and hide in the wagon-shed, behind the sea-blue farm cart, out of hearing of that thin tortured screaming. And Bundle, all big paws and soft, clumsy heaviness and sad, anxious eyes, was companion and comforter and handkerchief.

===Chinese===
“我不能去看看妈妈吗，玛吉？”

“这会儿还不行，宝贝儿。等她好点儿再说。”

“她会喜欢邦德尔吗？”

“这会儿还不行。”

于是他就到车棚里去躲着，藏在那辆海蓝色的农用大车后面，听不见那细声细气、受刑似的尖叫。而邦德尔——一身大脚爪，一身软乎乎、笨墩墩的沉甸甸，一双发愁不安的眼睛——就是他的同伴、他的安慰、他的手帕。

===Original===
Maggie passed through and beyond ordinary exhaustion. She saw everything, the tiniest things, the blue and silver star of beads on Papa’s red cloth watch-pocket, Aunt Priscilla’s small pear shaped tears that never stopped, the tufts of cotton in Martha’s ears, the tiny green-white spider on the black grapes Cousin Sam brought, with a queer bright clearness. Everything had become brilliant, intense, and strange, like the reflections and colors in a soap bubble just before it breaks.

She sat by the window at the end of the third night, watching the sky and the river turn the faint silver of a moth’s wing. Just at dawn a light wind touched her cheek, gentle and comforting. The sky kindled to a sheet of living rose, and the river was a golden river of life, flowing into her heart, flowing out from her heart as she watched, sitting there quietly, not knowing that Mamma was dead.

===Chinese===
玛吉穿过并越过了寻常的疲惫。样样都看得见，顶细小的也看得见：爸爸红布表袋上那颗蓝银相间的珠子星，普丽西拉婶婶那梨子形状的小泪珠、一颗接一颗落个不停，玛莎耳朵里塞的棉花团，萨姆表哥（Cousin Sam）送来的黑葡萄上那只绿莹莹泛白的小蜘蛛——全都带着一种奇异的、亮堂堂的清晰。样样东西都变得绚烂、紧绷而古怪，像肥皂泡破裂前一刻映出的光影和颜色。

第三夜将尽的时候，她坐在窗前，望着天空和河流泛出飞蛾翅翼般淡淡的银色。恰恰在破晓时分，一阵轻风拂过她的脸颊，温柔，熨帖。天边燃起一片活生生的玫瑰色，河成了一条金色的生命之河，流进她的心里，又从她的心里流出来——她坐在那里静静地望着，还不知道，妈妈已经死了。

## XVI / XVI

===Original===
Edward was coming. Maggie had never been so brisk and cross. How she ordered them about! And she was everywhere﻿—whipping cream for the charlotte russe, shouting to Albert to bring in more firewood, carrying pots of pinks and ivy from the conservatory into the parlour.

“*Lily!* Don’t put that wet pot on the table﻿—*mercy*! Now look at that!”

And she would fly for a cloth, with Bundle jumping around her, tumbling over his paws, wanting to play.

“Oh, Bundle, look-*kout*! Victor, for *pity’s-sake* put your dog somewhere where he won’t be under foot every second!”

“Oh, Maggie, please don’t be so cross and bossy!”

And then, giving the tumblers an extra polishing, she struck one so that it chimed and rang, faint, faint, a thread of exquisite sound, humming, like a bell deep under the sea.

And she was lost, listening to it, not hearing it, gazing with bright soft eyes through Mamma’s oil painting of ladyfingers and strawberries and a teapot, through the terra-cotta wall behind it, through the falling snow, through the sky. And there seemed to be a bloom on her, a radiance.

There were ferns of frost on the kitchen windows; the pump, to keep it from freezing, was wearing Mamma’s old red cloth opera cape with the cock feather collar, its handle sticking out like a sword. Nothing was quite its ordinary self, magic had touched The Maples.

===Chinese===
爱德华（Edward）要来了。玛吉（Maggie）从没这么风风火火过，也从没这么爱发脾气过。她把人支使得团团转！她无处不在——给夏洛特蛋糕（charlotte russe）打发奶油，喊艾伯特（Albert）再搬些柴火进来，把一盆盆石竹和常春藤从花房搬进客厅。

“莉莉（Lily）！别把那湿花盆搁在桌上——我的老天！瞧瞧，弄成什么样了！”

说着她便飞跑去拿抹布，邦德尔（Bundle）围着她蹦来跳去，叫自己的爪子绊得直打滚，一心只想玩。

“哎呀邦德尔，小——心！维克托（Victor），看在老天的份上，把你这狗搁到个地方去，别叫它每分钟都绊在人的脚底下！”

“哦，玛吉，求你别这么又凶又霸道好不好！”

随后，她把玻璃杯又格外擦拭一遍，拿起一只轻轻一磕，让它叮的一声荡开，轻啊，轻啊，像一缕精美之极的声音，嗡嗡不绝，像沉在海底深处的一口钟。

她听着听着就出了神，听而不闻，一双又亮又柔的眼睛，穿过妈妈那幅画着手指饼干、草莓和茶壶的油画，穿过画后面的陶红墙面，穿过漫天落雪，望进天空里去。她周身仿佛罩上了一层光晕，容光焕发。

厨房的窗玻璃上结满了蕨叶似的霜花；井泵为了防冻，裹着妈妈那件旧的红色呢料歌剧斗篷——公鸡毛的领子——泵把像一柄剑似的直戳出来。样样东西都不再是平日的老样子，魔法点过了“枫树庄”（The Maples）。

===Original===
It was almost time to start for the station when she came in tears to May.

“I can’t go to meet Edward.”

“Why not?”

“I look so hideous!”

“I have seen you look better,” said May candidly. “What have you been doing to your hair?”

“I tried to crimp it.”

“Well, never mind, I guess it’ll come out as you drive up. Take my frill﻿—that collar’s so stiff and horrid, and you can have my muff too, only *please* don’t get it all wet in the snow, and remember to hold the good side out and the rubbed side against you. Maggie﻿—black makes you look so washed-out﻿—I don’t suppose you’d take a red ribbon﻿—I just happen to have some if you would﻿—and wet it with cologne and rub it on your cheeks?”

“*May!*”

“Well, I’ve heard of girls doing it,” said May, blushing deeply.

“Only bad women paint their faces,” Maggie told her severely.

===Chinese===
动身去车站的时刻眼看就要到了，她却哭着来找梅（May）。

“我没法去接爱德华了。”

“怎么没法？”

“我这样子丑死了！”

“你是有过比这体面的时候。”梅坦率地说。“你的头发是怎么弄的？”

“我想把它夹卷来着。”

“好了，别往心里去，我想坐车一路颠过去，卷儿也就散了。戴上我的褶饰领吧——你那个领子又硬又难看；我的手筒也给你用，只是千万求你别叫雪把它打湿了；记着把好的一面朝外，蹭坏的那面贴着自己。玛吉——你穿黑色显得这么没血色——你肯不肯拿一条红缎带——你要是肯，我手边正好就有几条——拿古龙水浸湿了，往脸颊上搽一搽？”

“梅！”

“反正我听说过有姑娘这么干来着。”梅说，脸红得厉害。

“只有坏女人才搽脂抹粉。”玛吉严词告诫她。

===Original===
What if she had imagined everything? What if he didn’t come? What if there wasn’t any Edward? She was so afraid of that sometimes that it was agony, when he wasn’t there and no one was speaking of him. How to be sure, to be sure it was all real? And when she felt that way she couldn’t say “Edward,” because only suppose their faces had said, “Edward? Who’s that? We don’t know anyone named Edward.”

She would watch for Uncle Willie’s horse and carriage, bringing the mail, bringing a letter from him. From her window she could see the carriage far up the road, looking so different, somehow, from the way it looked when it was only going to church or market; and she would pretend to herself she hadn’t seen it, going on trying to do whatever she was doing, weak in the knees﻿—dizzy﻿—until someone called from downstairs:

“*Mag*-gie! *Let*-ter!”

She never got used to his letters. She would cry over them, kiss them﻿—practical Maggie!﻿—carry them crackling under her chemise, wake up in the night to feel them beneath her pillow, or light her candle to make sure that he had really written what she hardly dared believe she remembered. And no matter how often she reread them, the words were a shock of bliss, a flame of ecstasy.

===Chinese===
万一这一切都是她凭空想出来的呢？万一他不来呢？万一压根儿就没有什么爱德华呢？有时候她怕这个怕得苦不堪言——尤其是在他不在跟前、又没有人谈起他的时候。怎样才能确确实实地知道，这一切都是真的呢？每逢这样想，她连“爱德华”三个字都叫不出口，生怕一说出口，大家的脸上会现出这样的神气：“爱德华？那是谁？我们不认识什么叫爱德华的。”

她总要守着窗口，张望威利叔叔（Uncle Willie）的马车——它送信件来，送他的信来。从她的窗口，能望见马车在大老远的路上，不知怎么，那样子同它只是上教堂、赶集去的时候大不一样；她还要装作没看见，只管做着手头的事，膝盖发软——头发晕——直到楼下有人喊：

“玛——吉！来——信！”

他的信她永远习惯不了。她会对着信哭，亲它——讲实际的玛吉！——把它揣在贴身衬衣底下，窸窣作响；夜里醒来，伸手去摸枕头底下的它；或是点亮蜡烛，去证实他真的写了那些她记得、却几乎不敢相信自己记得住的话。而且不论重读多少遍，那些字句永远是一记福乐的震撼，一阵销魂的火焰。

===Original===
“What’s the news from the Far West?” Mamma would ask. She and Lily nearly burst with curiosity over each letter.

“Why﻿—I don’t think there is any, Mamma.”

No news in that fat letter! Well, really!

“Mamma doesn’t want you to tell her anything you’d rather keep to yourself, Maggie,” she said, hurt, and was more hurt when Maggie didn’t. But she never gave up.

“How is Edward﻿—or ain’t I allowed to ask that?”

“What? Oh﻿—oh, yes, thank you, Mamma,” Maggie answered vaguely, gently, looking at Mamma with dazzled eyes that did not see her.

But now it wasn’t a letter that was coming, it was Edward. And how could she make herself feel it? She was numb, she couldn’t even remember what he looked like. And probably he had forgotten what she looked like, too. How would they ever recognize each other? They should have arranged something﻿—“I will carry a sealskin muff and you wear a red carnation.”

===Chinese===
“遥远的西部有什么消息？”妈妈总要问。她和莉莉为每一封信好奇得几乎要胀破肚皮。

“这个——我想没有什么，妈妈。”

那么厚的一封信，竟然没有消息！嗬，真是的！

“妈妈并不想叫你把你宁可藏在心里的事说给她听，玛吉。”妈妈委屈地说；等玛吉果真什么也不说了，她就更委屈了。可她从不死心。

“爱德华好吗——还是这话我也不许问？”

“什么？哦——哦，好，谢谢您，妈妈。”玛吉恍恍惚惚、柔声柔气地答着，一双耀花的眼睛望着妈妈，却并没有看见她。

可这回要来的不是一封信，是爱德华本人。她怎样才能叫自己真切地觉出这一点呢？她是麻木的，连他长什么模样都记不起来了。多半他也把她长什么模样忘了。他们两个可怎么认出彼此呢？早该事先约好个记号——“我带一只海豹皮手筒，你佩一朵红康乃馨。”

===Original===
“Edward will be here any minute now!” She tried to wrench herself out of this numbness and feel the passion, the bliss that his coming should bring her, but she only felt as if she were going to be sick.

He couldn’t kiss her *really*, with everyone at the station taking such an interest. And in the carriage her hat got in the way, and besides they were both thinking of Albert’s back and Albert’s ears.

She forced warmth and brightness into her voice, winking to keep the tears back.

“What kind of a trip did you have?”

And he answered with the same false brightness:

“Oh, all right. You’re looking wonderful. How are the others?”

===Chinese===
“爱德华随时就到了！”她竭力把自己从这麻木里拽出来，去感受他的到来理应带给她的激情与福乐，可她只觉得像要恶心似的。

车站上人人那样关切地张望，他没法真正地吻她。马车里，她的帽子又碍事；再说，两个人心里都惦着前头艾伯特的背脊、艾伯特的耳朵。

她硬叫嗓音里透出热络和欢快，眨着眼把泪水逼回去。

“一路上走得还顺当吗？”

他也用同样虚假的欢快回答：

“哦，挺好。你气色好极了。家里人都好吧？”

===Original===
“All right. Victor has long trousers now, just for best. He can’t wait to show you! The girls took their sewing to Fannie Leaf’s, but they’ll be back for tea.”

“I thought May didn’t waste much love on pretty Fannie?”

“Oh, she goes over there a lot now﻿—she and Robert are having an affair.”

“That’s different! How about Ralph Wither?”

“He’s engaged to a Dover girl. Look, it’s snowing harder than ever! I was afraid it might make your train dreadfully late.”

“We were about ten minutes late, weren’t we?”

“About that, I think.”

*(Oh, where are you, where are you?)*

===Chinese===
“都好。维克托如今穿上长裤了，专门留着做客穿的那条。他等不及要给你看呢！姑娘们带着针线活上范妮·利夫（Fannie Leaf）家去了，喝茶前就回来。”

“我还以为梅不大肯把情分浪费在漂亮的范妮身上呢？”

“哦，她如今常往那边跑——她跟罗伯特（Robert）正有那么一段呢。”

“那可另当别论！拉尔夫·威瑟（Ralph Wither）呢？”

“他跟多佛（Dover）的一个姑娘订了婚。瞧，雪下得比先前更大了！我还直怕它把你坐的那班车误得厉害呢。”

“咱们晚点了十来分钟，是不是？”

“我想差不多。”

（哦，你在哪儿？你在哪儿？）

===Original===
“Well, here we are!” The pine branches swept down, dark and sad through the falling snow, the fields were white, and in the fountain in front of the house each iron calla lily was heaped with snow. The two strangers got out of the carriage and went into the house.

“This room is always cold in a storm﻿—I am cold.”

“Maggie, can’t we go up to the schoolroom?”

And they were alone, really alone, in the room they had always loved best. She lighted the logs in the black marble fireplace and the room was fragrant with burning pinewood and the geraniums, too spindly for downstairs, that looked out at the falling snow. There were the shabby books, the wobbly table with “A Lily Among Thorns,” propping up one leg, Mamma’s sewing-machine, the dressmaking dummy with her wire legs and tiny black pear-shaped head and high proud bust, wearing a white flannel dressing sacque with violet bows that May was making. The goldfish that lived in the garden pool in summer, swam in a washtub, in and out of their castle of stones, in and out of the waving water plants, gleaming sides of red and gold and silver, filmy floating tails. Miss Proctor’s poems, decorated with tear blisters and gingerbread crumbs, lay open on the window-seat, left by Lily.

===Chinese===
“好啦，到家了！”松枝沉沉地垂扫下来，在漫天落雪里显得幽暗凄清；田野一片白；屋前喷泉里，每一朵铁铸的马蹄莲上都堆满了雪。两个陌生人下了马车，走进屋去。

“这间屋子一遇上风暴天气就冷——我冷。”

“玛吉，咱们能不能上楼到课室去？”

于是他们单独在一起了，真正地单独在一起，在这间他们从来最爱的屋子里。她点亮黑色大理石壁炉里的柴，满屋便都香了——松木燃烧的香，还有天竺葵的香；那些天竺葵长得太瘦高，不配上楼下去见客，正望着窗外的落雪。破旧的书还在，摇摇晃晃的桌子还在——一条桌腿底下垫着那本《荆棘中的百合》（A Lily Among Thorns）；妈妈的缝纫机还在；做衣裳的人台也还在，铁丝的细腿，小小的黑梨子脑袋，高高昂起的前胸，身上罩着梅正在做的那件缀紫罗兰色蝴蝶结的白法兰绒晨衫。夏天住在花园水池里的金鱼，如今游在一只大木盆里，在石块垒成的城堡间穿进穿出，在飘摇的水草间穿进穿出，身侧闪着红、金、银的光，尾巴像轻纱般浮动。普罗克特小姐（Miss Proctor）的诗集摊在窗座上，书页叫泪水泡起了疱、粘着姜饼屑，是莉莉撂下的。

===Original===
They kissed each other with love and tenderness, clinging close, but the longed-for moment had gone﻿—had never been. Her hat, the men at the station, all the cloud that surrounds us, had kept them apart. And they had wanted it so that now they were too tired.

“Remember, Maggie?” And as he began to play chopsticks on the old piano, the shell of thin ice around her heart melted so that she was warm and alive and happy again, and able to make a terrific face at him.

“Maggie﻿—I have the most tremendous news!” He beat her palms lightly together as he talked, stopping now and then to kiss them. Happiness bubbled in every word, and she had never seen his eyes shine so.

“We can be married! We can be married right away! The company’s sending me to South America in a month﻿—it’s a wonderful chance﻿—it means everything, absolutely everything! It’s too good to be true! It won’t be easy﻿—not many girls could do it; but you can, Maggie, darling, darling! You won’t be afraid!”

She flamed to his words, answering him silently, burningly.

“I talked to a man who’d been out there﻿—I especially asked him if there were flowers, I thought you’d like to know; and he says there are. He says there are orchids growing right on the trees in sort of bunches, pale purple and greeny white, and they hold so much rain that the snakes climb along the branches and drink out of them﻿—maybe he was fooling me, but anyway that’s what he said. He said there were trees all covered with great big cream-colored flowers that smell fine﻿—I don’t remember the name, but I’ve got it written down somewhere for you. And you can get parrots for nothing, almost﻿—of course, they talk Spanish, but we could teach them. And he said the babies were awfully cunning, it’s so hot they don’t wear a stitch, and they’re all copper-colored. And, perhaps, he was exaggerating, but he said the ferns along the river were as big as trees﻿—”

The firelit walls faded, the falling snow, the ice-filled river. On another river they floated, locked in each other’s arms, floating on water like black satin under the great green lace umbrellas of the ferns, lit from beneath by a million fireflies, lit from above by all the stars. A pad, pad of feet in the shadows, and green eyes looked out at them﻿—the satin water parted and flowed back from a swimming snake. Terror and beauty and passion.

The wind sighed, bringing back the snow, clicking it gently against the window.

===Chinese===
他们相亲相爱地吻了，紧紧相偎，可盼望已久的那个时刻已经过去了——也许根本就不曾来过。她的帽子、车站上那些人、包在我们每个人周身的那团云雾，都把他们隔开了。他们把它盼得太苦，如今已疲惫不堪。

“还记得吗，玛吉？”他在旧钢琴上弹起《筷子》，她心上那层薄薄的冰壳便化了，她又温暖、又鲜活、又快活起来，还能冲他做一个狰狞的鬼脸。

“玛吉——我有一件天大的新闻！”他握着她的双手，说话间把它们轻轻地互相对拍，时不时停下来吻一吻。每个字里都咕嘟咕嘟冒着欢喜，她从没见过他的眼睛这么亮过。

“咱们能结婚了！马上就能结！公司一个月之内就派我去南美——这机会再好不过——它意味着一切，绝对的一切！好得叫人不敢相信是真的！日子不会轻松——没几个姑娘吃得了那份苦；可你吃得了，玛吉，亲爱的，亲爱的！你不会害怕的！”

他的话把她点着了，她默默地、灼灼地回应他。

“我问过一个去过那边的人——我还特意问了他那边有没有花，我想你乐意知道；他说有。他说兰花就直接长在树上，一丛一丛的，淡紫的和绿白的；花心里存得住雨水，存得那么多，蛇会顺着树枝爬上去，从花心里喝水——兴许他是在哄我，反正他是这么说的。他说有一种树，开满了奶油色的大花，香得很——名字我记不得了，不过我把它写在什么地方了，留着给你。还有鹦鹉，差不多不花钱就能到手——当然了，它们说的是西班牙话，可咱们可以教。他还说那边的小娃娃伶俐得不得了，天热得他们一丝不挂，个个都是紫铜色。还有——兴许他是吹牛——他说河边的蕨长得跟树一样大——”

映着炉火的四壁淡去了，落雪淡去了，冻实的河也淡去了。在另一条河上，他们相互搂着漂流，漂在黑缎子似的水面上，头顶是蕨类撑开的巨大的绿蕾丝伞，水下有百万萤火照亮，天上有满天星斗照亮。暗影里传来轻悄悄、轻悄悄的脚步声，一双绿眼睛朝他们望过来——缎子似的水面分开，又从一条游动的蛇身边合拢回去。恐怖，美，激情。

风叹息了一声，把雪又带回来，让它轻轻地叩着窗子。

===Original===
“That other fellow hates to leave.”

“Why does he?”

“Well, he wants to be with his family. He has a twelve-year-old boy, and it’s bad for children there, fevers, and no schools.”

The shell of ice closed about her heart again.

“But then﻿—Victor? What about Victor?”

“Well, *what* about Victor?”

“I can’t come if Victor can’t come with us﻿—I can’t leave Victor, Edward.”

===Chinese===
“另外那个家伙倒舍不得走。”

“他干吗舍不得？”

“唉，他要跟家里人待在一块儿。他有个十二岁的儿子，那边对孩子没好处——热病，又没有学校。”

冰壳又在她心上合拢了。

“那么——维克托呢？维克托怎么办？”

“维克托又怎么了？”

“要是维克托不能一块儿去，我就不能去——我丢不下维克托，爱德华。”

===Original===
“Don’t be absurd!”

“But I thought that we﻿—that he﻿—I thought﻿—”

“I’m sorry, but, of course, he can’t come with us.”

“Then you must go without me.”

“Leave you for five years? Maggie, you’re crazy! You can leave him here.”

“Oh, Edward, you know May and Lily﻿—I couldn’t leave him with them. May doesn’t think about a thing in the world but clothes and men, and Lily’s such a goose. Why, they can’t even take care of themselves, let alone Victor.”

“Well, leave him with someone else, some of your relations.”

“How could I leave him with Aunt Priscilla? He’s never been strong, and you know the way they live, meals any time or no meals, just a piece of moldy cake, and *dirt*﻿—! Edward, how can you ask me to leave him with her, when it was her carelessness killed Mamma?”

===Chinese===
“别说傻话了！”

“可我以为咱们——以为他——我以为——”

“对不起，可是他当然不能跟咱们去。”

“那你就自己一个人去吧。”

“丢下你五年？玛吉，你疯了！你可以把他留在这儿。”

“哦，爱德华，梅和莉莉那两个人你是知道的——我不能把他托给她们。梅这个人，天底下的事除了衣裳和男人一概不想；莉莉又是个呆头鹅。嗐，她们连自己都照管不来，更别说维克托了。”

“那就托给别人，你家总有别的亲戚。”

“我怎么可能把他托给普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）？他身子从来单薄，他们家的日子你又不是不知道——吃饭没个准点，有一顿没一顿，好的时候也就一块发了霉的蛋糕，还有脏——！爱德华，当初就是她的粗心害死了妈妈，你怎么还开得了口，叫我把他交给她？”

===Original===
“I know, darling, I do understand that. But they aren’t the only ones. Let him stay with the Blows.”

“I couldn’t. Cousin Lizzie doesn’t like him. She doesn’t like any of us. She was in love with Papa, and I think it made her hate Mamma and all of us, really. And she’s so queer﻿—I almost think she’s crazy. And Cousin Sam drinks dreadfully, and they hate each other. It’s terrible there, it frightens me. And Victor’s so sensitive, he’s always had such love and gentleness, it would kill him.”

“You’ve all of you always spoiled him, that’s the real trouble.”

“We have not! And if we have, is that *his* fault?”

“Anyway, taking him with us is out of the question.”

“I can’t leave him! I promised Mamma!”

“She had no right to let you.”

“She had! And anyway I couldn’t leave him.”

===Chinese===
“我知道，亲爱的，这个我明白。可亲戚又不只他们一家。让他住到布洛家（the Blows）去吧。”

“我不能。丽齐表姐（Cousin Lizzie）不喜欢他。咱们家里她谁都不喜欢。她从前恋着爸爸，我想就为这个，她恨妈妈，也恨我们大家，真的。她那个人又古怪——我几乎觉得她是疯了。萨姆表哥（Cousin Sam）又酗酒酗得凶，他们两口子彼此仇恨。那个家可怕极了，我害怕。维克托又那么敏感，他从小到大得到过的全是疼爱和温柔，那会要了他的命的。”

“你们大伙儿一向把他惯坏了，毛病就出在这儿。”

“我们才没有！就算惯了，难道是他的错？”

“反正，带他一块儿去是办不到的。”

“我丢不下他！我答应过妈妈！”

“她无权叫你许这种愿。”

“她有！再说我横竖丢不下他。”

===Original===
(“Where are you? I can’t find you! *Oh, where are you?*” And the other was crying, “Come back to me! I am lost! Come back!” But so far apart, they could no longer hear each other. They could hear nothing but anger, feel nothing but anger that filled the room so that the crimson curtains, the red geraniums, the fire-colored fish, the fire itself, seemed part of its blazing.)

“He’s always come between us﻿—always. Even the last night before I went west he was down after us, calling you away. I’d give up my work﻿—it isn’t that﻿—but you’re the only person in the world that matters to me, and I must be first to you or I won’t be anything. I won’t take just what you can spare me from Victor. If you love me, you’ll come.”

“He needs me.”

“I need you.”

“Not as much as he does﻿—no one needs me as much as he does.”

“It’s Victor or me, Maggie.”

===Chinese===
（“你在哪儿？我找不到你！哦，你在哪儿？”那一个也在哭喊：“回到我这儿来！我迷路了！回来呀！”可是隔得那么远，彼此再也听不见了。除了愤怒什么也听不见，除了愤怒什么也感觉不到；愤怒涨满了整间屋子，深红的窗帘、红的天竺葵、火色的金鱼、炉火本身，都仿佛成了这团烈焰的一部分。）

“他向来横在咱们中间——一向如此。连我动身去西部前的最后一夜，他都追下楼来缠咱们，把你叫走。我甘愿放弃我的工作——要紧的不是这个——可你是我在这世上唯一放在心上的人，我在你心里必须排第一，不然我什么都不要。你从维克托身上省下来匀给我的那一点点，我不稀罕。你要是爱我，就跟我走。”

“他需要我。”

“我需要你。”

“没有他那么深——谁需要我，也深不过他需要我。”

“要维克托还是要我，玛吉。”

---

===Original===
The baby lay in the big wash-basket under the wistaria vine wailing until she came to comfort him, the child ran to her from imagined terrors, the boy lifted his wet face and swollen eyes to her as she came out of the room where Mamma was dying. They held out their hands to her as the Child had held out his hands on the day of scillas and snow.

“It’s Victor, Edward.”

===Chinese===
婴儿躺在大洗衣篮里，在紫藤架下啼哭，直到她来安抚他；小孩子从凭空想象的恐怖里朝她奔来；少年在她走出妈妈弥留的那间屋子时，仰起湿漉漉的脸和哭肿的眼睛望着她。他们一个个朝她伸出手来，就像那个雪白的婴孩，在蓝钟花与落雪的那一天，向她伸出的双手一样。

“是维克托呀，爱德华。”

---

===Original===
Carefully, as if she were made of thinnest glass, she moved about the room. Nothing of Edward must be left, now that Edward himself was gone. The dent he had made in the sofa cushion, the hearthrug corner that his foot turned up. Carefully, so as not to break her fragile glass fingers, she tidied them. Then she sat in the window-seat, her eyes on the falling snow, her hands lying lightly in her lap.

She was empty, empty as the shell of a locust that still clings to a tree trunk; legs, eyes, body all there, but the bright soaring wings that were in it, the life that was in it, torn out and away.

And suddenly she ran after him, tore after him, tumbling down the stairs, leaving the hall door wide for the wind and the snow to enter, stepping on her skirt, stumbling, running through the snow in her thin slippers. He had gone to the Allens’﻿—she would go to him, they would be together again, forever and ever. “Edward, Edward, I’m coming﻿—oh, Edward, I want you!”

Edward her lover and friend to whom she had given the bread of tears.

“Victor,” sighed the wind among the pine branches. “Victor,” sighed the sifting snow.

Victor frightened. Victor homesick. Victor needing her.

Across the fields she saw Victor trudging home through the dusk, his shoulders up, his head down against the snowy wind. Passionate, enfolding tenderness flooded her, and pain beyond any pain she had ever known. It was as if her heart had been broken open so that her brother could enter in completely. But Edward was there, too, would be there forever. No matter if she never saw him again, no matter what happened. Could anything make her stop loving him? Can storms put out the stars?

===Chinese===
她小心翼翼地在屋里走动，仿佛自己是用最薄的玻璃做成的。爱德华这个人既已走了，他的痕迹便一样也不许再留下。他在沙发靠垫上压出的凹痕，他的脚尖挑翻起来的炉前毯一角。她把它们一一收拾妥帖，小心别碰坏自己那双易碎的玻璃手指。随后她坐到窗座上，眼睛望着落雪，两只手轻轻地搁在膝头。

她空了，空得像一只还攀在树干上的蝉壳：腿脚、眼睛、身子样样都在，可原先在里头的那对亮闪闪、凌空高飞的翅膀，原先在里头的那点性命，都被撕了出去，撕远了。

忽然她撒腿追起他来，发了疯似的追，连滚带跌冲下楼梯，任大厅的门四敞大开，让风雪灌进来；踩着自己的裙子，一路踉跄，穿着单薄的便鞋跑进雪地里。他到艾伦家（the Allens）去了——她要去找他，他们要重新相聚，永生永世不再分开。“爱德华，爱德华，我来了——哦，爱德华，我要你！”

爱德华——她的情人，她的朋友，她曾以泪水的面包奉养他。

“维克托，”风在松枝间叹息。“维克托，”簌簌筛落的雪叹息。

受惊的维克托。想家的维克托。需要她的维克托。

隔着田野，她望见维克托顶着卷雪的风、缩着肩膀埋着头，在暮色里一步一步往家挪。一股炽烈的、把她整个包裹起来的柔情涌遍全身，还有一阵痛，比她此生领教过的任何痛都更深。仿佛她的心被剖开了口子，好让弟弟完完全全地住进来。可是爱德华也在那里面，而且要永远在那里面。哪怕从此再见不着他，不管往后出什么事。难道有什么能叫她不再爱他吗？风暴吹得灭星星吗？

===Original===
She went back to the house, and in to the new life that still looked so much like the old. The horsehair sofa, the parlor fire smoking a little, Dicky and Downy piping in their cages, the smell of carnations and of frying chicken, the clock on the stairs, that sounded like water falling drop by drop﻿—

She must go and take off Edward’s place from the tea-table﻿—she must go﻿—as soon as she stopped shaking﻿—

The door burst open, and Victor came stamping in, all red cheeks and snow.

“Hello, Maggie! Where’s Edward?”

“He’s gone.”

“He’s coming back to tea, isn’t he?”

“No.”

“Did you tell him we were going to have charlotte russe?”

===Chinese===
她回到屋子里，走进那段新日子——它看上去还同旧日子那么相像。马鬃面子的沙发，客厅里微微冒烟的炉火，迪基（Dicky）和多尼（Downy）在笼子里啾啾地叫，康乃馨和煎鸡的香味，楼梯上那只钟——滴答，滴答，像水一滴一滴往下落——

她得去把爱德华的席位从茶桌上撤下来——她得去——等她不再发抖了就去——

门砰地推开，维克托跺着脚走了进来，红红的脸颊，满身的雪。

“哈啰，玛吉！爱德华呢？”

“他走了。”

“他要回来喝茶的，对不对？”

“不回来了。”

“你告诉他没有，咱们要吃夏洛特蛋糕？”

===Original===
And suddenly she cried in a high quivering voice:

“Victor Campion, I’ve told you one million times to scrape off your feet and not come tracking snow into the house!”

Victor looked up with mild reproach from where he sat struggling with his rubber boots.

“Well, don’t take my head off, Maggie, it isn’t *my* fault Edward couldn’t stay.”

===Chinese===
她忽然用一种尖利发颤的声音嚷起来：

“维克托·坎皮恩（Victor Campion）！我跟你说过一百万回了，进屋前把脚上的雪蹭干净，别把雪一路踩进屋子里来！”

维克托正坐在那儿跟他的胶靴较劲，闻言抬起头来，眼里带着淡淡的责备。

“得了，玛吉，犯不着这样拧下我的脑袋呀，爱德华没能留下，可怪不着我。”

## XVII / XVII

===Original===
The ladies were stealing a good many glances at the clock beneath the Arab and his prancing steed on Mrs. Leaf’s mantelpiece. Four o’clock﻿—certainly time for coffee and cake if their suppers weren’t to be spoiled. The flannel for the orphans’ nightgowns puckered more and more slowly over their needles.

And then, instead of going comfortably in and sitting around the dining-room table for their cake and coffee as they always did, always, at every meeting of the Guild, Carpus the houseman in his white jacket, with his black forehead all puckered with perplexity, brought in a tray and put it on a small spidery table by Mrs. Leaf﻿—and there wasn’t anything on it but tea and bread and butter! Tea! And bread and butter! Mrs. Talbot nearly cried, she was so disappointed, and Mrs. Pennock gave Mrs. Holly such a look! It was hardly worth the trouble of balancing the cups and plates. And poor Miss Perry, who had counted on saving her supper, gazed round at the blue velvet ottomans and chairs with their fat fringes, the grand gas chandelier like a floating bouquet of white glass tulips with twisty stems, and the bright-colored peasants dancing on large china vases, and couldn’t understand it at all. Mrs. Leaf generally gave “such an elegant entertainment.”

Mrs. Leaf knew what they were feeling, and was bright pink with self-consciousness. But her guest from New York, Mrs. Hawthorn, had afternoon tea, with bread and butter, so the Leafs were having it﻿—and the ladies of the Guild were having it.

Mrs. Hawthorn’s opulent curves, covered with tight black silk, filled an S shaped rocking-chair, white frothed from beneath her long black train, bracelets encircled her plump white wrists and a chatelaine watch clung to the steeps of her bosom. She was being charming to everyone, as a rose sheds its fragrance for all, as a queen bows to the crowd. Mrs. Almond thought she certainly washed her hair with soda to make it golden, and told Hessie Farley so; and Hessie murmured back that *that* was probably what gave her face that queer artificial look, and that very likely it wasn’t enamelled at all.

===Chinese===
太太们一次又一次地偷眼去瞟利夫太太（Mrs. Leaf）壁炉架上的座钟——钟座上是个阿拉伯人，骑着前蹄腾跃的骏马。四点了——再不上咖啡和点心，晚饭可就要被耽误了。给孤儿们做睡衣的法兰绒，在她们针下起褶起得越来越慢。

接着，非但没有像缝纫会（the Guild）每逢聚会时那样——回回如此——舒舒服服地进饭厅，围着餐桌坐下吃蛋糕喝咖啡，反而见穿白褂子的男仆卡普斯（Carpus）端进来一只托盘，搁在利夫太太手边一张细脚伶仃的小桌上——托盘上除了茶和黄油面包，什么都没有！茶！黄油面包！塔尔博特太太（Mrs. Talbot）失望得几乎要哭出来，佩诺克太太（Mrs. Pennock）朝霍利太太（Mrs. Holly）递了个多么意味深长的眼色！就这么点东西，端杯递碟地费那个平衡的劲头，简直不值当。可怜的佩里小姐（Miss Perry）——她原指望靠这顿茶点省下一顿晚饭的——只管朝四下里张望：蓝色天鹅绒的软榻和座椅，垂着鼓鼓囊囊的流苏；气派的煤气吊灯，活像一束梗子弯弯曲曲的白玻璃郁金香，浮在半空；大瓷瓶上，色彩鲜亮的庄稼人正翩翩起舞——她怎么也想不明白。要知道，利夫太太待客，向来是“多么风雅的一场款待”。

利夫太太心里明白她们是什么滋味，臊得满脸通红。可是她那位从纽约来的客人霍桑太太（Mrs. Hawthorn），午后喝的是茶，配黄油面包，于是利夫一家（the Leafs）便也喝午后茶——缝纫会的太太们便也只好跟着喝。

霍桑太太那一身富态的曲线裹在紧身的黑绸里，填满了那把S形摇椅；长长的黑裙裾底下，翻涌出白色的浪沫；一副手镯环着她丰腴白皙的手腕，一块腰链小表贴在她胸脯的陡坡上。她对每个人都和蔼可亲，像玫瑰把芬芳撒向众人，像王后向人群颔首。阿尔蒙德太太（Mrs. Almond）断定，她那头金发准是拿碱水洗出来的，便这么告诉了赫西·法利（Hessie Farley）；赫西低声回说，八成正是这个，才叫她脸上带着那股古怪的人工气，而且很可能压根儿也没搪过瓷。

===Original===
“Fannie and Prentice and their two little girls are home for a visit, May,” said Mrs. Leaf. “Fannie’s so anxious to see you. She told me to tell you you must be sure to come for lawn tennis, tomorrow morning, all three of you.”

“We couldn’t, thank you, Mrs. Leaf. Victor gets home from Harvard this evening, and we simply couldn’t leave him.”

“But, of course he must come too! Little Victor Campion a grownup Harvard gentleman—think of it! How time flies! You know you all seem children to me, and yet here’s Fannie been married four years—by the way, is she a month older than you, or a month younger? We were trying to remember. *Older!* That’s what I told them. And Robert married a year and a half, and both the children thinking they know more about bringing up babies than I do! No soothing syrup, if you please! And their stuck-up nurse-girls! But I must say the babies are lovely—remind me to show you a new photograph of Isabel holding little Robbie—he certainly looks like his daddy. Now you come tomorrow, or Fannie will feel dreadfully. I told her and Lucy Hawthorn they were bad girls not to stay for the sewing this afternoon, but they all went over to the Sandersons to play at lawn tennis—they’re nuts on it, as Robert says. Carpus! Now put the tray here—here on the table, and take the teapot out—excuse me, May—and tell Lissa to fill it up again. Carpus! Tell Lissa not so strong—not so strong—no, don’t take the tray, just the *teapot*—Mercy!”

===Chinese===
“范妮（Fannie）和普伦蒂斯（Prentice）带着他们家两个小女儿回家小住来了，梅，”利夫太太说。“范妮一心想见你呢。她特意嘱咐我告诉你，明儿上午你们姐儿仨务必来打草地网球。”

“我们去不成，谢谢您了，利夫太太。维克托（Victor）今晚就从哈佛（Harvard）回来，我们说什么也不能撂下他不管。”

“他当然也得一道来呀！小维克托·坎皮恩（Victor Campion）长成了哈佛的大人先生——想想看！光阴过得真快！要知道，你们几个在我眼里还全是孩子呢，可范妮转眼都出嫁四年了——对了，她比你大一个月还是小一个月来着？我们方才还使劲回忆呢。大一个月！我就这么跟他们说的。罗伯特（Robert）呢，结婚一年半了，小两口倒自以为带娃娃比我在行！你瞧瞧，愣是一点安神糖水都不给孩子喝！还有他们那些眼高于顶的保姆丫头！不过我得承认，娃娃是真可爱——记得提醒我给你看张新照片，伊莎贝尔（Isabel）抱着小罗比（Robbie）照的——那眉眼，活脱是他爸爸。明儿可一定来，不然范妮心里要多难受有多难受。我跟她，还有露西·霍桑（Lucy Hawthorn），都说下午缝纫不肯留下来的是坏丫头，可她们全上桑德森家（the Sandersons）打草地网球去了——照罗伯特的说法，都迷得发了疯。卡普斯！托盘搁这儿——这儿，桌上，把茶壶撤出来——失陪一下，梅——叫丽莎（Lissa）再去灌满。卡普斯！跟丽莎说别沏这么酽——别这么酽——哎，别撤托盘，就撤茶壶——老天爷！”

===Original===
“I’m afraid my little Goosey-Lucy isn’t much of a loss as far as the sewing goes,” said Mrs. Hawthorn, throwing a veil of tact over her friend’s fluster. “She belongs to a sewing circle in New York, but I don’t believe the girls do much but gossip and drink chocolate. I notice most of the sewing’s brought home for my poor Elise to do.”

“Elise is Mrs. Hawthorn’s French maid,” Mrs. Leaf explained. That made up for Carpus acting as if he’d never seen a teapot in his life before—the foolish!

===Chinese===
“只怕我家那只小呆鹅露西，就缝纫而论，走不走的也算不上什么损失，”霍桑太太说，拿一层圆通的轻纱罩住了朋友的狼狈。“她在纽约也参加了一个缝纫圈子，不过我看那帮姑娘除了闲磕牙、喝巧克力，也做不成什么。我留意到，大半的针线活都是带回家来，丢给我那可怜的艾莉斯（Elise）做的。”

“艾莉斯是霍桑太太的法国女仆。”利夫太太解释道。这一句，就把卡普斯方才那副活像一辈子没见过茶壶的蠢相给遮过去了——这个傻瓜！

===Original===
“Mrs. Hawthorn was lovely, wasn’t she?” asked Lily as the sisters walked home. “Did you see her handkerchief? It was all lace except for a piece of cambric about the size of a postage stamp, and she smelled so sweet, like that soap from Paris Cousin Lizzie gave you one Christmas, sort of heliotropy.”

May’s face flushed darkly, and her lip began to tremble.

“I couldn’t bear her! You could see every minute she was thinking, ‘I’m being charming! I’m being perfectly charming to all these country bumpkins!’ Yes, and that’s what we are—country bumpkins, and I’m *sick* of it! Aren’t we ever going to do anything all our lives but sit at home and scrimp, scrimp, scrimp, so that *Victor* can have nice clothes, *Victor* can get away, *Victor* can go to college? We’re human, too, aren’t we?”

“Why, *May*—!”

“Yes, ‘why, *May*!’ I’m sick and tired of wearing a steamed velvet iron-holder trimmed with an old duck wing Maggie’s cured on the wagon-shed wall, because I can’t afford a hat, and so Victor *can*. Who’d look at me, who’d look at any of us in these old made-overs? Who is there *to* look, anyway? There isn’t a man here who isn’t married or about a hundred—but Victor must have everything, so we’ll just sit at home and twiddle our thumbs and get older and older and older, and fifty years from now they’ll still be calling us the Campion girls—”

===Chinese===
“霍桑太太真可爱，是不是？”姐妹俩走路回家，莉莉（Lily）问道。“你看见她的手绢了吗？通身是花边，只除了邮票大小的一小块细麻纱；她身上的香气可真甜，就像丽齐表姐（Cousin Lizzie）有一年圣诞节送你的那块巴黎香皂，有点儿天芥菜花香。”

梅（May）的脸涨得发青，嘴唇开始发抖。

“我受不了她！你时时刻刻都看得出来，她心里在想：‘瞧我多和蔼可亲！我对这帮乡下土包子多客气！’不错，我们就是乡下土包子，我腻透了！难道我们这辈子，就除了坐在家里省啊省啊省，好叫维克托穿上体面衣裳，好叫维克托出门见世面，好叫维克托上大学，再没有别的事可做了？我们也是人，不是吗？”

“哎呀，梅——！”

“是呀，‘哎呀，梅！’我戴着一顶蒸汽熏过的丝绒熨斗垫改的帽子，边上缀一只玛吉（Maggie）挂在车棚墙上风干的老野鸭翅膀，我腻了，烦了——因为我置不起一顶帽子，省下这份钱，维克托就样样置得起。谁肯看我一眼？我们穿着这一身改了又改的旧衣裳，谁肯看我们一眼？再说了，又有什么人好来看我们？这地方的爷们儿，不是结了婚，就是快一百岁——可是维克托必须样样都有，于是我们就只配坐在家里搓手干等，一年老似一年，一年老似一年，一年老似一年，五十年后，人家照旧管我们叫坎皮恩家的姑娘们——”

===Original===
She began to laugh. “Did you hear Mrs. Leaf bragging to me about Fannie and Robert and their children? Why should *I* be expected to be interested in Robert Leaf’s baby, I should like to know? He and Isabel can have a million babies for all I care—it certainly isn’t of the slightest importance to me—”

“Why, May!”

“Why, Lily! I wish you could see your face!” May laughed, harder, harder, until she was almost sobbing. And then suddenly the jangle of laughter stopped, and she looked faint and exhausted, opening her hand and letting the hot crushed daisies she had snatched off by their heads fall to the ground.

===Chinese===
她笑了起来。“你听见利夫太太跟我夸范妮、夸罗伯特、夸他们的孩子了没有？我倒要请教，凭什么就该指望我去关心罗伯特·利夫（Robert Leaf）的娃娃？他跟伊莎贝尔爱生一百万个娃娃也不干我的事——那对我实在是半点也不打紧——”

“哎呀，梅！”

“哎呀，莉莉！可惜你瞧不见你自己这副脸！”梅笑着，越笑越凶，越笑越凶，笑到几乎带出哭腔来。忽然，那一串叮铃当啷的笑声戛然而止，她面色发白，筋疲力尽，摊开手掌，让那把攥得温热、揉得稀烂的雏菊——都是她掐着花头一把把揪下来的——落到地上。

===Original===
They had scraped to send Victor to Harvard. May complained, but she made their clothes just the same, turning and making over and trimming with bits of this and that from the piece bag in the entry closet, until they looked pretty enough really to have come from the grand places whose labels she ripped out of Mamma’s old gowns and sewed in. And she trimmed their hats, too, taking one by one the stuffed birds, the oriole, the bluejay, the rose-breasted grosbeak, from their branches under the glass bell on top of Papa’s secretary.

Maggie did the cooking. Martha had married her young darkie, and a life he was leading her! And Lily helped save by giving up her music lessons with Miss Martin, and working alone, with a novel propped open on the music rack while she practiced her scales, to relieve the monotony.

===Chinese===
为了送维克托上哈佛，一家人省吃俭用地攒。梅嘴上抱怨，可照旧给她们做衣裳——翻新、改制、镶边，用门厅壁橱里那袋零头碎布，东拼一块西凑一块，做到看上去真像是从那些大铺子里出来的才罢休——连铺子的商标，都是她从妈妈的旧礼服上拆下来，缝进她们衣裳里去的。帽子也是她装的点：爸爸的翻盖写字台顶上，玻璃钟罩底下，枝头上栖着一只只剥制的鸟——黄鹂、蓝松鸦、玫胸白斑翅雀——都被她一一请下来，缀上了帽檐。

做饭的是玛吉。玛莎（Martha）嫁了她那个年轻的黑小伙，瞧他领她过的那份日子！莉莉也帮着省钱——退掉了跟马丁小姐（Miss Martin）学的音乐课，一个人闷头练琴，谱架上支一本摊开的小说，好解一解练音阶的单调。

===Original===
Maggie had set her heart on Victor’s going to Harvard because Papa had gone there. And Victor wanted to go, too, while the going was still far away, while the mountains were distant waves of mist-blue, instead of steep grey rocks and slipping stones. At home, planning to go, he had been all of Harvard; but when he got to Harvard he was nothing.

Those first homesick days! He would have run home to The Maples if he could, as he had run on his first day of school.

He sat in his small room looking as lost as if he were alone on a raft in the middle of the sea. From his window he saw a thin grey cat slowly stalk a sparrow; then a man came by calling bananas. The sparrow flew away, the cat poured itself through a hole in a fence, the banana man’s cries grew fainter and were still.

===Chinese===
维克托上哈佛，是玛吉一门心思定下的，因为爸爸当年上的是哈佛。趁上哈佛还是件遥遥无期的事，维克托自己也想去——那时它还只是远处雾蒙蒙的蓝色的一道道山影，而不是陡峭的灰岩、踩上去打滑的碎石。在家里盘算着要去的时候，他就是整个哈佛；等真到了哈佛，他什么也不是了。

最初那些想家的日子啊！只要能够，他真想一路跑回枫树庄（The Maples）去，就像上学头一天那样往家跑。

他坐在自己的小屋里，一副六神无主的样子，仿佛独自一人漂在汪洋大海中央的一只木筏上。从窗口望出去，他看见一只精瘦的灰猫蹑手蹑脚地潜近一只麻雀；随后过来一个叫卖香蕉的小贩。麻雀飞走了，灰猫像一股水似的从篱笆洞里流了过去，卖香蕉的吆喝声越来越远，终于没了声息。

===Original===
At dusk he went out and bought a box of crackers. He could see that the other young men on the streets were all accustomed to the place, that everyone knew everyone else intimately. They knew just where they were going and why, as they walked along, calm and assured. He felt as if they were all looking at him, and laughing. And although he thought he was lost in the strange streets, he *couldn’t* ask the way. “Could you please tell me—” No, he couldn’t make a sound—

And in sudden terror of having really been stricken dumb, he dashed into a drug store, that seemed so friendly with its familiar smell, its bright blue boxes of the very same toothpowder that they used at The Maples, the “Pear’s Soap Boy” getting his scrubbing from his grandmother, that he could ask quite naturally for a box of shoe polish.

He came out of the drug store, drooping his eyelids and curving his mouth scornfully, then catching sight of a clock on a building, pulled out Papa’s big watch, compared the two, and suddenly hurried off with a slight, anxious frown, as if he were late for an important engagement. Lamps were being lit in the houses—he could see books, geraniums, maids pulling curtains together. Lamps were being lit for these other people, lamps were being lit at home—

Oh, the breathless relief of getting off the street into his own room! He shut the door and leaned against it, his heart pounding as if there were pursuers on the stairs.

===Chinese===
傍晚他出门，买了一盒苏打饼干。他看得出来，街上那些年轻人个个都对这地方习以为常，人人彼此熟不拘礼。他们走着，安详而笃定，自己知道这是要去哪儿、去干什么。他觉得他们全都在看他，都在笑他。虽然自觉迷失在陌生的街道上，他却开不了口问路。“劳驾，您能不能告诉我——”不，他连一点声音也发不出来——

猛然一阵恐慌，生怕自己真的哑了，他一头扎进一家药房。那铺子显得那么友好：是熟悉的气味，是那种亮蓝色盒子的牙粉——枫树庄用的正是同一款；还有画里那个“梨牌肥皂小子”，正让祖母给他浑身上下地搓澡。在这么个亲切地方，他竟能相当自然地开口，要了一盒鞋油。

他从药房出来，耷拉着眼皮，嘴角撇出讥诮的弯儿；随即瞥见一座楼上的钟，便掏出爸爸那只大怀表，两边对了对，忽然眉头轻轻一皱，急匆匆拔脚赶路，仿佛一个要紧的约会已经迟到。家家户户都在点灯——他望得见屋里的书本、天竺葵、正把窗帘拉拢的女仆。灯，是为这些别人家点的；家里，此刻也在点灯——

哦，从街上逃回自己屋里，那口不敢喘的气终于松了下来，多好啊！他关上门，背抵着门站住，心怦怦直跳，仿佛楼梯上有追兵。

===Original===
He ate the crackers, pausing for a long time between bites, his wide blue eyes fixed absently on the smoky red wall. He and the girls were coming home from a walk in the autumn dusk—a rabbit’s white tail went flashing under a hedge. The boats on the river hooted and moaned to each other through the fog, and the yellow leaves under the tulip tree were too wet to rustle. May’s chilly pink fingers lit the first fire, the thin blue and yellow flames came licking through the dry sticks and around the logs that had waited there for them ever since last April, beetles that had set up housekeeping came bursting out, the smoke poured up in one thick, greyish yellow curl. They had supper in front of the fire, cold beef and hot baked potatoes, and big, pale yellow-green grapes—

===Chinese===
他吃着饼干，一口与一口之间停顿良久，一双睁得大大的蓝眼睛恍惚地盯着那面熏红了的墙。他和姑娘们在秋天的黄昏里散步归来——树篱底下一闪，闪过一只兔子的白尾巴。河上的船隔着雾彼此呜呜地叫、呜呜地应；鹅掌楸底下的黄叶湿漉漉的，响不起来。梅那双冰凉的红润手指点起入秋第一炉火，蓝的黄的细细火苗舔过干柴枝，绕上那几根打去年四月起就一直守在那儿等它们的大木柴；安家落户的甲虫一头头蹿出来；烟浓浓地卷起一股灰黄。他们围炉吃晚饭：冷牛肉，滚烫的烤土豆，还有大串大串淡黄绿色的葡萄——

===Original===
He was getting into bed in his room at home. Everything was just as it had always been, except the new suit waiting to be put on tomorrow, the bag still open, waiting for his brush and comb and toothbrush, and the two oblongs of dark olive-green leaves and vermilion berries on the wall paper that everywhere else had faded to shades of straw color. Mamma had hung there in her sealskin sacque, caught in a photographer’s snowstorm, and Papa, with his hand thrust into the front of his coat, but now they were packed to go to Cambridge with him.

There was a tapping at the door, and Maggie’s voice, low, so as not to wake the others.

“Victor—I saw your light. Are you all right? Can I help do anything?”

And he answered, pretending to yawn, so that she shouldn’t guess that he was wide awake with nervousness and excitement:

“Aw righ’, thank you—g’nigh’—”

===Chinese===
那是还在家里的时候，他正在自己房里上床。样样东西都还是老样子，只有那套预备明天穿的新衣裳在一旁等着；旅行袋还敞着口，等他的发刷、梳子和牙刷放进去；墙纸上那两块深橄榄绿的叶子、朱红浆果拼成的长方块还鲜亮着——别处的墙纸，早就褪成了深深浅浅的麦秸色。妈妈那张照片——穿着海豹皮短袄，陷在照相师布景的一场的风雪里——和爸爸那张一手插在衣襟前的照片，原先就挂在那面墙上，如今都打好包，要跟他一道去剑桥（Cambridge）了。

门上响起轻轻的叩击，还有玛吉的声音，压得低低的，怕吵醒旁人。

“维克托——我看见你屋里亮着灯。你还好吗？有什么要我搭把手的吗？”

他答应着，假装打个哈欠，好叫她猜不出，自己其实又紧张又兴奋，清醒得很：

“挺——好，谢谢——晚——安——”

===Original===
Well, here he was. He finished his crackers. And then he blackened his shoes, slowly, thoroughly. When they were polished, that was all there was to it. He had nowhere to go in them.

But after awhile he made some friends, and they had good times together.

===Chinese===
好了，如今他就在这儿了。饼干吃完了。随后他给鞋子上油，慢慢地擦，仔仔细细地擦。擦亮了，事情也就到此为止。穿着它们，他无处可去。

不过过了一阵子，他交上了几个朋友，大家一起过了些快活日子。

===Original===
At the Holly Tree Inn, around the oilcloth covered table that red-haired John in his red shirt was wiping up with a cloth as black as a crow:

“Now I’ll tell you what it is about this fellow Zola—”

“The girl I’d like to see is Lily Langtry. Very snappy, my boy, very snappy!”

“Happy thought! What about an oyster supper and a few bottles of the rosy, one of these frosty evenings, beloved brethren?”

And John’s grubby hand setting down the wonderful poached eggs and hot, thin, buttered toast.

===Chinese===
在冬青树客栈（Holly Tree Inn），围着那张铺漆布的桌子——红衬衫、红头发的约翰（John）正拿一块乌鸦般黑的抹布擦它：

“这我就要跟你说道说道了，这个姓左拉（Zola）的家伙——”

“我想见一见的那位姑娘，是莉莉·兰特里（Lily Langtry）。真够味儿，小子，真够味儿！”

“好主意！赶一个霜冻的晚上，来一顿牡蛎夜宵，再添几瓶玫瑰红，怎么样，亲爱的弟兄们？”

还有约翰那只脏兮兮的手，端上来妙不可言的荷包蛋，和又热又薄的黄油吐司。

===Original===
Strolling together to the place on the corner of Holyoke Street, with its cigars, its soda-fountain with the slender marble pillars on either side of the mirror, its jewelled lamp that might have lighted some harem.

“Afternoon, gentlemen.”

White walrus-mustache, eyeglasses on a bit of pink string, and straw hat that seemed as much a part of him as the fungus is part of the tree, he stood there in shirtsleeves and velveteen waistcoat, mixing soft drinks.

“What’s yours, Mr. Campion?”

To have such friends! To be called by name! “Ham sandwich and chocolate ice-cream soda, General!” And in his heart he was jumping up and down like an excited little boy.

===Chinese===
再一道闲逛到霍利奥克街（Holyoke Street）拐角上那家铺子：柜上陈着雪茄，立着汽水柜台，镜子两侧竖着细细的大理石柱，还有一盏珠光宝气的灯，怕不曾照亮过哪一座后宫。

“下午好，先生们。”

一把白色的海象胡子，眼镜吊在一小截粉红细绳上，头上一顶草帽——瞧着就像菌子长在树上那样，长成了他身上的一部分——他就这么站在那儿，衬衫袖子挽着，套一件棉绒坎肩，调配着各色汽水。

“您来点什么，坎皮恩先生？”

有这样的朋友！有人叫着他的名字！“火腿三明治，加一杯巧克力冰淇淋苏打水，将军！”他心里头，活像个乐疯了的小男孩，蹦啊，跳啊。

===Original===
A night in Boston, starting with “musties” at Billy Park’s, and going on—where? Among the clouds, among the comets? All men were brave, all women beautiful.

“Ha-a-appy *thought*! Nozher bottle of oh be joyful!”

“Fixshed bayonet for me, Zhorzh.”

“Branny—shhh-*mash*!”

===Chinese===
波士顿（Boston）的一夜，从比利·帕克酒馆（Billy Park's）的陈麦酒喝起，然后——去哪儿了？到云端去了，到彗星中间去了？人人是勇士，个个女人都是美人。

“好——好主意！再——来一瓶欢喜酒！”

“给——给我上一杯‘上刺刀’，佐——治。”

“白兰地——嘶嘶——玛什！”

===Original===
And coming home in the horse car, how sweetly they sang:

> “ ‘When I drive out eash day in my little coo-pay hay—’

*hold—it—*

> ‘I tell you I’m shomehing to shee—’ ”
They sang so sweetly that Victor could not keep back his tears. Their voices died, his sobs died. He and his friends slept upon each other’s shoulders until at the Yard entrance the kind conductor called, “Good morning, gents! Sports’ Alley!”

===Chinese===
坐马拉街车回家的路上，他们唱得多甜啊：

> “‘每天我驾着小——小马车出门兜风，嘿——’

稳——住——

> ‘告诉你们，我可是个了——不起的人物——’”
他们唱得那么甜，维克托忍不住直掉泪。歌声消歇了，他的呜咽也消歇了。他和朋友们你靠着我、我靠着你地睡着了，直到哈佛园（the Yard）门口，好心的售票员喊了一声：“先生们，早安！运动员巷！”

===Original===
Then they saw each other home, back and forth, back and forth, for hours. And finally, in his own room, Victor remembered the carnation in his buttonhole. Someone had put it there—who in the dickens? Someone he’d promised never to forget. He put it in his hat in the middle of the floor and poured in all the water from his pitcher. Now and then he would lose himself in dreamy admiration of anything his eyes happened to fall on—his lamp, his soapdish—but presently he would wrench himself back, and pour in a little more water, until it was all used and, something accomplished, something done, he could go to bed in his boots.

===Chinese===
然后他们互相送回家去，你送我、我送你，来来回回，来来回回，折腾了几个钟头。最后，在自己屋里，维克托想起了纽扣眼里的那朵康乃馨。不知是谁给插上的——到底是谁呢？是他许下诺言永不忘记的什么人。他把帽子摆在地板正中央，把花搁进去，再把水罐里的水一股脑儿全倒进去。时不时地，他会对着眼睛落到的随便什么东西——他的台灯，他的肥皂盒——出神地赏玩一番，随即又把自己狠拽回来，再斟进一点水，直到水全用尽了，才算做成了什么、完成了什么，他这才可以穿着靴子上床去睡。

===Original===
But lessons—examinations—

He had tried—but before he understood the beginnings of things, the class was leaping on, and he was trying to leap with it, trying to read before he knew his letters. Clutching his hair in his hands, he would scowl at his books under his green shaded student-lamp, despairing, yawning, taking a great bite of apple, and wishing for some open-sesame that would magically unlock the door of knowledge for him.

He hadn’t passed his examinations. He had been warned after the midyears, and now he had to tell the girls that he wasn’t going back. The train lurched towards home from familiar station to familiar station. The car lamps swung and creaked.

===Chinese===
可是功课——考试——

他不是没下过力——可还没等他弄明白一件事的开头，全班已经蹦跳着往前赶了，他便也跟着蹦跳，好比还不认得字母，就硬要念书。他两手揪着头发，在绿灯罩的学生灯下对着书本愁眉苦脸，绝望，打哈欠，狠狠咬一大口苹果，一心盼着有句什么“芝麻开门”，能施起魔法，替他把知识的大门打开。

他没能通过考试。期中之后已经吃过警告，如今他得告诉姑娘们：他不回去了。火车一站一站往家晃，从熟悉的站台到熟悉的站台。车厢里的灯荡来荡去，吱呀作响。

===Original===
“Well, girls, brace yourselves for a shock—yours truly is home for good.”

The blue river with its white sails unrolled alongside the tracks, the daisies ran down to them.

“I have to tell you something. I couldn’t pass the exams., and the pleasure of my company is not requested for next year.”

===Chinese===
“呃，姑娘们，站稳了别吓着——敝人这回可是回来长住了。”

蓝色的河张着白帆，顺着铁轨一路铺展开去，雏菊一路漫下来，直漫到路基边上。

“我有件事要告诉你们。我考不过那些考试，明年，敝人奉陪的荣幸，人家已经谢绝了。”

===Original===
The church spire over the trees, the Leaf’s high hedge, Mrs. Pennock’s cows a brown fleet in the foam of daisies. But the feeling of home withheld itself, he couldn’t feel anything but that gnawing sickness at the pit of his stomach.

“Girls, I am sorry to have to tell you—”

And in the middle of Maggie’s account of the ridiculous time Aunt Priscilla was having with her guinea-chickens, he burst out, sounding loud and rough because he had been planning it so long:

“I can’t go back next year! I couldn’t pass my examinations!”

===Chinese===
树梢上的教堂尖顶，利夫家的高树篱，佩诺克太太家的奶牛——一支棕色的船队，泊在雏菊的浪沫里。可是“到家”的感觉迟迟不肯降临，他什么也感觉不到，只觉得心窝里一阵阵啃噬似的恶心。

“姑娘们，我很抱歉不得不告诉你们——”

玛吉正讲到普丽西拉婶婶（Aunt Priscilla）叫她那群珍珠鸡雏闹出来的种种笑话，他猛地脱口而出——这句话在心里演练了太久，出口的声音又响又硬：

“我明年回不去了！我没能通过考试！”

===Original===
He had told! He had told, and they didn’t scorn him. Maggie said she’d always heard, *always*, that examinations didn’t signify anything, *really*; and, of *course*, he couldn’t go back and be a freshman all over again. Lily cried, “Those *mean* professors,” and wept a little; and May said everybody knew that what you went to college for was the friends you made, not the things you learned out of books. Waves of love broke over him, waves of gratitude, and he would have died for his sisters happily.

===Chinese===
说出来了！他说出来了，她们却没有一个人轻贱他。玛吉说，她一向听人讲——一向！——说考试其实顶不了什么用，真的；再说，他当然犯不着回去从头再当一年新生。莉莉嚷道：“那些狠心的教授，”还掉了一小会儿眼泪；梅说，谁不知道，上大学图的本来就是交下的朋友，不是书本里学来的那些东西。爱的浪头一阵阵朝他扑来，感激的浪头一阵阵朝他扑来，为着姐姐妹妹们，他就是死了也甘愿，死了也快活。

===Original===
And at last the blinding, numbing tension was dissolved, and he could let the feeling of home flow through him, the sweet air, the lights moving silently on the river, the shadowy parlor and the absurd things that had been there forever, no one knew why, no one knew from where—the lacquer bowl with its strange sweet smell, the three-cornered bronze inkwell that never held any ink in its little glass hat, the penwiper of a doll’s head dressed as a nun on which no pens were wiped. Drunk with relief he plunged after Maggie, helping her shut the windows for the night, and doing such things to the curtains! It nearly drove her crazy, and yet she couldn’t say anything, it was so sweet of him to try to help.

===Chinese===
终于，那叫人眼花、叫人发麻的紧张化解开了，他可以由着“家”的感觉流遍全身——甜美的空气，河上悄然移动的灯火，幽暗的客厅，还有那些不知打什么时候起就一直摆在那儿的荒唐物件——没人知道为什么，没人知道打哪儿来——那只散发奇异甜香的漆碗，那只小玻璃帽子底下从来没盛过一滴墨水的三角形青铜墨水池，还有那个修女装束的娃娃头擦笔布——从来没有一支笔在上头擦过。他带着如释重负的醉意，颠颠地跟在玛吉身后一头扎进活计里，帮她关窗过夜，把窗帘折腾得不成体统！这几乎要把玛吉逼疯，可她一个字也说不得——他肯来搭把手，实在是甜人心。

## XVIII / XVIII

===Original===
Happiness to wake at dawn and lie listening to the birds, to bathe in the crackling copper-lined tub, and go leaping down through the soaking grass to get a brown and yellow pansy all beaded with dew for his buttonhole; to eat strawberries and mud, and presently at breakfast, with the girls beaming on him, more strawberries, and coffee with thick, yellow cream instead of chalk and water and bluing (“*Really* chalk and water and bluing?” Lily wanted to know) brown eggs Albert had brought in an hour ago, corn bread, and ham still sputtering and sizzling in the dish. Home again!

And that morning he met Lucy Hawthorn.

They all went to the Leafs, even Maggie, who said she was too old for such gadding and had a hundred things she had to do at home, anyway. But Victor wanted her to come.

The dark cedar trees half hid the blue stone house with its low bay-windows, and swept the shaven lawn that unrolled to the stone breakwater against which the river was clucking and slapping now, and from which at low tide the river mud spread like wet chocolate icing. The air above the round beds of heliotrope quivered with fragrance.

===Chinese===
多么快活啊——天蒙蒙亮就醒来，躺着听鸟儿叫；在噼啪作响的衬铜浴盆里洗个澡；再一路蹦跳着蹚过湿透的草地，去给纽扣眼摘一朵缀满露珠的棕黄三色堇；去吃草莓和泥巴；过一会儿坐上早饭桌，姑娘们笑吟吟地望着他，端上来又是草莓，咖啡里兑着厚厚的黄奶油，而不是白垩粉兑水再上点蓝颜色（“真是白垩粉兑水再上蓝颜色吗？”莉莉（Lily）想要知道），还有艾伯特（Albert）一个钟头前拿进屋的棕色鸡蛋、玉米面包，和盘子里还在噼啪滋溅油的火腿。又回到家了！

而就在那天早晨，他认识了露西·霍桑（Lucy Hawthorn）。

他们全都去了利夫家（the Leafs），连玛吉（Maggie）也去了——她嘴上说自己上了年纪，犯不上这么东跑西颠，家里反正还有一百件事等着做。可是维克托（Victor）要她去。

深色的雪松半掩着那座带低矮凸窗的蓝石宅子，枝条扫拂着修剪过的草坪；草坪一路铺展开去，直铺到石砌的防波堤跟前——这会儿河水正贴着堤岸咯咯地欢鸣、啪啪地拍打；落潮时，河泥便从堤根漫开去，像一摊湿漉漉的巧克力糖霜。一圃圃天芥菜的上空，空气让香气熏得直打颤。

===Original===
“Well, Victor! How does it seem to be home?”

“Hello, Victor! How did you leave Fair Harvard?”

“Mrs. Hawthorn, Mr. Campion.”

Mrs. Hawthorn, sitting in the lovely, liquid shadow of her lilac parasol, gave him her famous smile, while she took in every detail through the little black lace veil that came just to the tip of her nose.

Prentice Page with ferns inside his hat to keep off the sun, looking like a faun from the forest, played with May against pretty Fannie and Maggie in her manly straw sailor, and Lily good-naturedly trotted after the balls, protesting, “Oh, I like to, really, *truly* I do, I’d *loads* rather!”

The ball bounced gently from tiny racquet to tiny racquet across the casual sagging of the low net. The girls in their eelskin dresses and long ruffled skirts held on their hats as they played, but Lucy, sitting on the grass with Victor, had taken hers off, and the sun gleamed on the pale brown silk sweep of her hair from the cloud of soft frizz on her forehead to the low knot of braids like shining brown nuts. She was all pink and cream, her little ears, her tender little nose, the delicate soft curve of her chin, candid and young. The rose, fringed about with other sweetnesses, heliotrope, pinks, and rose geranium leaves, is the heart of the nosegay; and in the lovely surrounding of sunny fragrant air, kind happy voices, being home again, the relief of having told, Lucy was the rose.

===Chinese===
“哎，维克托！到家是什么滋味呀？”

“你好哇，维克托！撇下咱们美丽的哈佛（Harvard）时，它一切可好？”

“霍桑太太（Mrs. Hawthorn）——坎皮恩先生。”

霍桑太太坐在丁香紫阳伞那可爱、流动的荫影里，对他展露出她那有名的微笑，一面隔着那幅刚齐鼻尖的黑色小花边面纱，把他从头到脚每一处细枝末节都收进眼里。

普伦蒂斯·佩奇（Prentice Page）帽子里衬着蕨叶遮太阳，活像从林子里走出来的农牧神；他同梅（May）搭档，对阵俊俏的范妮（Fannie）和头戴男式平檐草帽的玛吉；莉莉好脾气地小跑着替她们追球，嘴里还一个劲儿辩白：“哦，我乐意捡，真的，真的乐意，我巴不得呢！”

小小的球拍你来我往，球轻轻地弹过那道随意松垂的低网。姑娘们一个个穿着鳗皮般贴身的连身裙、曳地的镶褶长裙，打球时得腾出一只手按住帽子；露西却坐在草地上、坐在维克托身边，把帽子摘了，阳光在她淡棕色丝绸般的头发上流淌生辉——从额前蓬起的一团软鬈，到低低绾成髻的辫子，像一颗颗亮闪闪的棕色坚果。她通身上下是粉红和乳白的颜色，她的小耳朵，她娇嫩的小鼻子，她下巴那纤柔的软弧，又坦白，又年轻。玫瑰，衬着一圈旁的甜香——天芥菜、石竹、玫瑰天竺葵的叶子——是这束花的心；而在这晴暖芬芳的空气、和蔼欢畅的语声、重返家门的踏实、把事情说出了口的如释重负之中，露西便是那朵玫瑰。

===Original===
“What funny little gold pigs on your bracelet!”

“They’re *porte-bonheurs*. Aren’t they sweet? I love my piggie-wiggies!”

“Lucky pigs! Oh, look! Three brown butterflies have settled on your skirt!”

“*Where?* Oh, you mean those bows! But real *flowers* are the most fashionable trimming now, so why not real butterflies?”

“Happy thought! How about real fruit and vegetables? A ball gown with a spinach what-do-you-call-em gored on the bias with tomatoes, and a cabbage for a bouquet!”

“Oh, Mr. Campion, don’t! You’re giving me a stitch in my side! But you mustn’t call my butterfly bows brown﻿—that color’s ‘Bottled Cloves,’ if you please, Sir!”

“Live and learn! What color is this lovely dress?”

“This old rag? What color would you say?”

“Sky-blue pink!”

“Go to the foot of the class! It’s ‘Marguerite Blue,’ and Fannie’s blue is called ‘Heavy Eyes.’ ”

“They sound like drinks﻿—‘Lady’s Smiles’ and ‘Morning-Glories.’ ”

“I’m afraid everything sounds like a drink to you fast Harvard men﻿—I suppose you’re all *dreadfully* wild. I’m frightened to death of you.”

“Oh, say not so,” said Victor, looking as wild as he could.

===Chinese===
“你手镯上这些金色的小猪真好玩！”

“它们是吉祥符（*porte-bonheurs*）呀。可爱不可爱？我最疼我的小猪猪了！”

“幸运猪！哦，瞧！三只棕色蝴蝶落在你裙子上了！”

“哪儿？哦，原来你说的是那些蝴蝶结！如今最时兴的点缀就是真花，那为什么不能来几只真蝴蝶呢？”

“好主意！干脆上真水果真蔬菜怎么样？一件舞会长裙，斜裁的裙幅镶上菠菜——那个叫什么来着——再缀上番茄，捧一棵卷心菜当花束！”

“哦，坎皮恩先生，快别说了！笑得我侧腰都抽筋了！不过你可不许管我的蝴蝶结叫棕色——那种颜色叫‘瓶装丁香’，请您记住了，先生！”

“真是活到老学到老！那这件可爱的裙子是什么颜色？”

“这块破布？你猜是什么色？”

“天蓝色的粉红！”

“答错啦，罚站到教室末排去！这是‘玛格丽特蓝’，范妮那身蓝的叫‘倦眼蓝’。”

“这些名字听着倒像酒名——‘淑女的笑靥’呀、‘清晨牵牛’呀。”

“我看哪，你们这些风流的哈佛男人，听什么都像酒——想必你们一个个都放荡得吓人。人家可被你吓死了。”

“哦，可别这么说。”维克托说，把面孔尽量扮出一副凶相。

===Original===
“Did you graduate this year, Mr. Campion?”

“Well, no﻿—”

“Oh, you’re going back!”

“Well, as a matter of fact, I don’t think I *will* go back next year. The fact is, I feel as if I ought to stay here and take care of my sisters and look after the estate, you know.”

“Oh, I do think that’s wonderful!” said Lucy’s soft voice, and Lucy’s forget-me-not eyes said, “How noble you are! How noble!”

===Chinese===
“你今年毕业了吗，坎皮恩先生？”

“呃，还没有——”

“哦，那你还要回去念完！”

“呃，说实在的，我看明年是不回去了。实话对你说吧，我总觉得应当留在家里，照顾妹妹们，照管家里的田产，你知道的。”

“哦，我觉得这真是了不起！”露西那软软的声音说；露西那双勿忘我似的眼睛说：“你多么高尚呀！多么高尚！”

===Original===
Prentice and May were cooling off in the shade, while Victor and Lucy took their places.

“More beauteous than ever, May﻿—whose heart are you breaking now?”

“Oh, I’ve settled down to being an old maid, Prentice. I’ve decided I’d rather paddle my own canoe.”

“Tell that to the marines! Those eyes will never let you.”

She crossed her slender ankles, pulling up her skirt a little, and touched his face with the spray of heliotrope she had been holding to her lips.

“The slave brushes the flies away from the face of the Sultan,” she murmured.

===Chinese===
普伦蒂斯和梅到树荫里歇凉去了，维克托和露西顶了他们的位置。

“比先前更俏丽了，梅——你如今又是在碎谁的心呐？”

“哦，我算认命当老姑娘了，普伦蒂斯。我打定了主意，宁可自己划自己的独木舟。”

“这话哄海军陆战队去吧！你那双眼睛说什么也不依。”

她交叠起一双纤细的脚踝，把裙裾稍稍撩起，拿那束一直衔在唇边的天芥菜，轻轻拂上他的脸。

“奴隶为苏丹拂去脸上的苍蝇。”她喃喃道。

===Original===
“Look at that girl, Bessie!” Mrs. Hawthorn said to Mrs. Leaf. “Look at the amount of ankle she’s showing﻿—and making such eyes at Prentice. You can see he’s laughing at her.”

“Robert was very much taken with her several years ago﻿—I thought something might come of it, but then he lost interest, for some reason﻿—that seems to be the way with May Campion and her young men, and I’m sure I don’t know why.”

“Don’t you, my dear? I do. She’s so distressingly eager.”

“But she’s very pretty and animated.”

“Much too animated. I’m sure she’s been told it suits her, so she’s always on the sparkle, no matter what one’s saying. I find her exhausting. And *can* that color be natural? I think it was a lucky escape for Robert.”

“Well, I was relieved, I must say. She gets so excited, and sort of wild looking sometimes, and there *is* a queer streak in the family﻿—her cousin Mrs. Blow, went raving crazy a couple of years ago, and died from running out into a blizzard with nothing on but her nightgown; and you know anything like that makes one so nervous.”

===Chinese===
“瞧瞧那丫头，贝西（Bessie）！”霍桑太太对利夫太太（Mrs. Leaf）说。“瞧她露出的那截脚脖子——还朝普伦蒂斯抛那样的媚眼。你也看得出来，他正在心里笑她呢。”

“罗伯特（Robert）几年前可着实地迷过她——我原当能成点儿什么呢，后来不知怎么，他又没了兴致——梅·坎皮恩同她那些年轻人，回回都是这个样儿，我真是想不透。”

“你想不透吗，亲爱的？我可想得透。她那股子急切劲儿，急得叫人心里发窘。”

“可她又漂亮又活泛呀。”

“活泛得过了头。准是有人捧过她，说这样子相宜，于是不管人家讲什么，她总是浑身亮闪闪的。我看她看得累人。再说，那脸色会是天生的么？我看哪，罗伯特这一遭脱身，脱得侥幸。”

“呃，不瞒你说，我也松了口气。她太容易激动，有时候那副神气简直像发了野，再说这一家子里头，的确有一条怪的筋——她那位表姐布洛太太（Mrs. Blow），前几年疯得狂喊乱叫，光穿着件睡袍跑进暴风雪里，人就那么没了；你知道，这种事，总叫人心里发毛。”

===Original===
Carpus came over the lawn with a clucking pitcher of lemonade, and they sat together on the grass, drinking and talking.

“Let me read your palm, Prentice﻿—oh, what a trail of broken hearts I see!”

“What do you think? My cruel husband wouldn’t take me to see Bernhardt in *La Dame aux Camelias*! He said he was afraid my French wasn’t bad enough!”

“Are you fond of the light fantastic, Miss Hawthorn?”

“A little more lemonade, *if* you please﻿—whoa, Emma!”

“Oh, my dear! Don’t mention jersey dresses! The times I’ve had getting out of mine!”

“Gracious, Maggie, how strong minded you sound! Don’t tell us you’re one of the Shrieking Sisterhood!”

===Chinese===
卡普斯（Carpus）端着一大壶咕嘟作响的柠檬水穿过草坪来，众人便一齐坐到草地上，且饮且谈。

“来，我给你看看手相，普伦蒂斯——哦，我可看见一长串碎掉的心哪！”

“你们评评理：我那狠心的丈夫，不肯带我去瞧伯恩哈特（Bernhardt）演的《茶花女》（*La Dame aux Camelias*）！他说，就怕我的法文还不够糟！”

“霍桑小姐可喜爱轻歌曼舞？”

“劳驾，再赏一点儿柠檬水——吁，艾玛！”

“哦，我的乖乖！快别提针织紧身裙！我从我那条裙子里往外挣的那几个好时辰哟！”

“老天爷，玛吉，你这口气听上去多硬朗！可别叫我们晓得，你也是‘尖叫姐妹会’里的一位！”

===Original===
Long trailing golden wings of sunlit air quivered over them﻿—over them all﻿—over Lucy.

And Victor, drunken with happiness, showing off to her, pretended his tennis racquet was a banjo, and strummed on it, singing:

===Chinese===
金色的日光舒着长长的翅膀，在他们上方微微颤动——在他们众人上方——在露西上方。

维克托快活得醉了，在她跟前卖弄起来，把网球拍权当班卓琴，一边乱弹一边唱：

===Original===
> “ ‘Ping Wing, the Pieman’s son,
 

 Was the very worst boy in all Canton;
 

 He ate his mother’s pickled mice,’ ”

(Screams from the ladies!)

> “ ‘And threw the cat on the boiling rice,
 

 And when he’d eaten her, said he:
 

 “Me wonders where the mew-cat be!” ’ ”

===Chinese===
> “‘平温，馅饼师傅家的儿郎，
 

 全广州数他最荒唐；
 

 他吃了他娘的腌老鼠，’”

（太太们尖声尖叫！）

> “‘又把猫儿扔进滚粥烫，
 

 吃完了，他说道：
 

 “俺纳闷，喵喵猫去了何方！”’”

===Original===
For the first time in his life he really saw his home, because Lucy was going to see it. He nearly drove his sisters crazy.

“Maggie! Maggie! One of the calla lilies on the fountain is broken!”

“Oh, *Victor*! That was broken off before you were born.”

“Well, did you know that the second pine tree on the drive is nearly dead?”

It seemed to him that the whole house, the whole place, was falling to pieces, that as he looked cracks and blotches appeared on walls lightly held together by cobwebs.

Fat Lily, scarlet and streaming, hurled herself up the terraces with the lawnmower, Maggie laundered the muslin curtains and rewashed the best china with its light red seaweed pattern, May cut owls and bats and crescent moons out of dark brown paper and made a new dado of them for the parlor. And Victor, drunk with nervousness, bumped into them all, ran upstairs, forgot what he had come for, ran down again, and wished he had never been born.

The day was like an accordion. First it stretched out for ever so long, and then﻿—swish!﻿—it was folded up to almost nothing. If he didn’t hurry, he wouldn’t be dressed by the time they came! Even if he did hurry, it was too late now. And he tried to decide whether to wear his blue tie that really was the color of his eyes, or the raspberry-red one that stood for a passionate nature and Harvard. With shaking fingers he tied the blue, took it off, tried the red, half untied it, tied it again, looked at himself severely in the glass, took it off in a frantic hurry. He couldn’t decide, he simply couldn’t! In a panic he saw himself changing his tie all night﻿—all his life﻿—forever and ever, red, blue, red, blue, red, blue﻿—

===Chinese===
平生头一遭，他真正看见了自己的家，因为露西就要来看它了。他差点儿把妹妹们逼疯。

“玛吉！玛吉！喷泉上有一朵马蹄莲折了！”

“哦，维克托！那朵在你出生前就折啦。”

“对了，你可知道车道边上第二棵松树快死透了？”

在他眼里，整座宅子、整个园子都在散架；他看着看着，墙壁上便现出裂痕和污斑，仿佛那墙不过是叫蛛丝轻轻缀着的。

胖莉莉涨红着脸、浑身汗津津，推着割草机一路猛冲上层层台地；玛吉把细纱窗帘浆洗了，又把那套浅红海藻纹的上好细瓷重洗了一遍；梅用深棕色的纸铰出猫头鹰、蝙蝠和一弯弯新月，替客厅拼出一道新墙裙。维克托呢，紧张得发了昏，撞了这个撞那个，跑上楼去，忘了上来要拿什么，又跑下来，只恨不得自己从来没生下来过。

这一天活像一具手风琴。先是抻啊抻啊，抻得老长老长；跟着——唰！——一下子收拢，几乎缩没了。他再不抓紧，人家来的时候他还没穿好衣裳呢！就算抓得再紧，这会儿也已经迟了。他掂量来掂量去：系那条当真和他眼睛一色的蓝领带呢，还是那条木莓红的——红的代表火热的天性和哈佛。他指尖发抖，系上蓝的，解下来，换上红的试了试，解开一半，再系上，对着镜子正颜厉色地端量自己一遭，又火急火燎地扯下来。他拿不定主意，压根儿拿不定主意！慌乱里，他瞧见自己整宿整宿地换领带——一辈子地换——永生永世，红的，蓝的，红的，蓝的，红的，蓝的——

===Original===
“*May!*”

“*What?*”

“Shall I wear my blue tie or my red?”

“Good gracious, I don’t care﻿—blue!”

Oh, what a relief!

“*Victor!*”

“*Hello!*”

“Hurry yup!”

“All right!” And he sat down on the edge of his bed, his clasped hands squeezed between his knees, and lapsed into a dream of Lucy.

===Chinese===
“梅！”

“怎么？”

“我系蓝领带还是红的？”

“老天爷，我不管——蓝的！”

哦，这可松快了！

“维克托！”

“哎！”

“快着点儿呀！”

“这就来！”说罢他在床沿上坐下，十指交叉的两手夹在膝盖当中，一头坠进了关于露西的迷梦。

---

===Original===
“Goodnight﻿—we’ve had a lovely time! Goodnight!”

“Goodnight! Goodnight!”

“I’ll push the shutters to, May, if you’ll bolt them.”

“Do you know, Fannie’s getting fat! But her dress was real pretty, wasn’t it? I loved those bands of terra-cotta ribbon embroidered with trails of jasmine. I could fix over my pale green Cashmere that way.”

“Lossie waited pretty well for just coming in, don’t you think? And ain’t it heavenly to think of the dishes all washed!”

“It seemed to me you had to do an awful lot of whispering,” May said discouragingly. “And my goodness, Lily, I was never so mortified in my life as I was at the way you kept smiling at her!”

But Lily hadn’t been able to help it, for she had remembered the way Lossie used to play “Company to Dinner” with them when they were children, in the tender green light under the beech tree, and she had almost expected to find slices of peony petal ham and maple seed fried potatoes on the plates that Lossie’s black hand set before them, and mud-and-water coffee in the cups.

“Well, I thought she did pretty well,” Maggie repeated. “But I oughtn’t to have trusted her to make the coffee﻿—how *could* she have gotten it that way? It was just like mud and water.”

“Was there any ice-cream left? Doesn’t anybody want some?”

“Lily! Don’t yawn that way﻿—you’ll dislocate your jaw! *Goodnight, Victor!* Aren’t you going to say goodnight?”

Victor, floating up the stairs, came out of his trance with a start.

“Oh﻿—goodnight!”

“*Can* you see why he’s so moony about Lucy? Really, it’s a good thing she’s going home soon. She’s a right nice little thing, but she hasn’t enough sense to say boo to a goose.”

===Chinese===
“晚安——我们今晚多尽兴！晚安！”

“晚安！晚安！”

“我来关百叶窗，梅，你来落闩。”

“你们发觉没有，范妮发福了！不过她那条裙子着实好看，是不是？我喜欢那几道陶土红的缎带，绣着一串串茉莉。我那件淡绿色的开司米也能照这个样子改。”

“洛西（Lossie）不过是临时进来帮佣的，当差当得还算不坏，你们说呢？再想想，碗碟全都洗完了，这难道不是天堂般的美事！”

“要我说，你们俩咬耳朵咬得未免太勤了，”梅泼冷水似的说。“还有我的老天，莉莉，我这一辈子从来没这么难为情过——瞧你冲着她笑个没完的那副样子！”

莉莉可由不得自己，因为她想起洛西从前陪她们玩“请客吃饭”的光景——在山毛榉嫩绿的凉影底下——她险些当真以为，洛西那双黑手端上来的盘子里，盛的会是芍药花瓣切的火腿片、枫树籽炸的土豆，杯子里斟的是泥汤咖啡呢。

“呃，我是说她当得不坏，”玛吉又说了一遍。“只是我不该托她去冲咖啡——她怎么会冲成那个样子？简直就跟泥汤一个样。”

“冰淇淋还剩得有吗？还有谁想再来点儿？”

“莉莉！别那么打哈欠——下巴颏都要脱臼了！晚安，维克托！你不道声晚安吗？”

维克托正飘飘忽忽地上着楼梯，猛地一激灵，从出神里醒了过来。

“哦——晚安！”

“你可看得出，他干吗对露西这样痴痴迷迷？说真的，她快回家去倒是件好事。她是个顶不错的小东西，可惜呀，那点见识，见了鹅也不敢‘呸’一声。”

---

===Original===
The clouds washed over the moon like thin waves, the whole sky was flowing. In the few clear patches the stars were pale in the flood of silver light. Feathery dark clouds, clouds thin as gauze, streaming over the moon.

Maggie looked out at the night, yawning, thinking how early she would have to get up to look after her newly hatched chickens. And as she looked, another moon rose in her heart, a great bubble of apricot pink floated up from the river, and she and Edward watched it, speaking to each other without words.

May looked out at the night before she lay down beside Lily, placidly sleeping with her fringe in a row of cocoons on her forehead. Oh, the moon made you feel queer! Why had she acted like such a cheap fool in the garden after supper? Oh, *why* had she let Prentice say those things, flattering and insolent? She knew she had been crazy to think he meant them when she saw him in the lamplight again, saying goodbye in the hall, with Fannie’s pretty ringed hand dangling through his arm. Perhaps, lying in bed before they went to sleep, he would tell her about it, laughing a little, and Fannie would laugh, too, pityingly, and say, “Poor old May!”

*Oh!* She crushed her cold hands against her blazing face.

Victor leaned out his window looking up at the sky. Of course, he had thought he was in love, hundreds of times, but this was different.

===Chinese===
云像一层薄浪从月亮上濯过，整个天空都在流淌。几处云隙里，星星在银光的洪流中显得苍白。羽毛似的暗云，绡纱般薄的云，一缕缕从月亮上掠过。

玛吉望着夜色，打着哈欠，心里盘算明儿得起得多早，去照看新孵的那窝小鸡。她望着望着，另一轮月亮从她心里升了起来——好大一团杏子粉红的泡沫，从河面上袅袅浮起——她和爱德华（Edward）一同望着它，交谈着，却不用言语。

梅在莉莉身边躺下之前，也望了望夜色；莉莉睡得安安稳稳，额前的刘海一卷卷的，像顶着一排小小的蚕茧。哦，这月亮叫人心里发怪！晚饭后她在花园里，干吗要做出那么一副贱兮兮的傻样？哦，她干吗要由着普伦蒂斯说那些话——又奉承，又放肆？后来在门厅的灯下再见到他，看他同人道别，范妮那只戴了戒指的漂亮手儿正挽在他臂弯里，她就明白了：自己方才竟当他是真心，简直是疯了。说不定，这会儿他们上了床还没睡着，他就会把这段事讲给她听，略略笑一笑，范妮也带着怜惜笑一笑，说一声：“可怜的老梅！”

哦！她把冰凉的双手，死死捂在自己火烧火燎的脸上。

维克托探身窗外，仰望天空。当然啦，他从前也自以为在恋爱，几百回都自以为，可这一回不一样。

===Original===
Lucy! Lucy!

“You are a dove, Lucy, my Lucy. You are a little white lamb. You are everything that is gentle and pure﻿—

“It’s like praying to think of you, Lucy﻿—

“Well, really, Lucy, I don’t call myself an authority﻿—oh, no, no, you are too kind﻿—but since you ask for my opinion﻿—”

Suddenly he put his head down in his arms, loving her.

“It means waiting, darling.”

And he could imagine her answering:

“I would wait for you forever.”

“Lucy, I come to you stained and scarred﻿—”

(Oh, those glasses of beer at Billy Park’s! Oh, the last horsecar jingling out to Cambridge!)

“Oh, Lucy, I think you’re so lovely!”

The clouds flowed over the moon, from the river he heard the wash of the tide coming in. Through all his being he felt the stir, the flow of the night, the clouds, the waves, the faintly breathing wind, the people all over the world who were awake, who were loving each other.

===Chinese===
露西！露西！

“你是一只鸽子，露西，我的露西。你是一只小白羊。你是一切温柔、一切纯洁的化身——

“想念你，就像做祷告一样，露西——

“呃，说实在的，露西，我可不敢自命权威——哦，不，不，您太客气了——不过既然您垂问鄙人的意见——”

他忽然把头埋进臂弯里，一心一意地爱着她。

“这就意味着等待，亲爱的。”

他能想见她这样回答：

“我愿意等你，等一辈子。”

“露西，我到你身边来的时候，是带着污痕、带着伤疤的——”

（哦，比利·帕克酒馆（Billy Park’s）里那几杯啤酒！哦，末一班叮叮当当出城往剑桥（Cambridge）去的马拉街车！）

“哦，露西，我觉得你真可爱！”

云从月亮上流过；从河那边，他听见涨潮的水声一阵阵涌来。他全身上下都觉出那份涌动，那夜色的流淌——云、浪、微微呼吸着的风，和普天下一切醒着的、正在彼此相爱的人们。

## XIX / XIX

===Original===
Maggie hung Victor’s dress-suit on the clothesline to get rid of the smell of camphor. He was going to New York to stay at the Fifth Avenue Hotel where Papa had stayed, and to go to Lucy Hawthorn’s ball. There had been a glazed cream-colored invitation for the girls, too, that Lily kept lying carelessly on the hall table, where everyone could see it; but Maggie said, of course, they couldn’t think of going.
