Suppose, just suppose, that Mr. Marshall arrived some evening as she came down the drive under the shadowy pines, her arms full of feathered tulips﻿—or coppery snapdragon and creamy roses, as time went on﻿—or asters﻿—chrysanthemums﻿—tulips﻿—

===Chinese===
莉莉一面应酬着，一面时刻竖起耳朵，听院门咔哒一响、小径上脚步声近、玛吉上气不接下气地喊：「莉莉！马歇尔先生到家里来啦！」可他始终没有来，白教她落下一桩乏味的差事——克拉克太太迎面便是一句「我正黑咕隆咚一个人坐在这儿，闷得慌，巴天巴地只盼着你来呢！」这时她还能怎么办呢？

每逢星期六晚上，莉莉都给克拉克太太送一束花去，好叫克拉克先生的坟——不偏不倚就在卫理公会教堂旁边——星期天受人赞叹。假如，只是假如，某个傍晚她抱着满怀羽毛似的郁金香——时令推移，便是铜红色的金鱼草配乳白的玫瑰——或是紫菀——菊花——郁金香——正从松影幢幢的车道上走下来，马歇尔先生恰好到了呢——

===Original===
“*Where are you taking your armful of flowers?*”
“*I’m just running across the road﻿—I take them every week to a poor old neighbor, to put on her husband’s grave.*”
*And he would say, a note of reverence stealing into his voice*:
“*Sweet Saint Charity!*”

===Chinese===
「*你这一怀抱的花，要送到哪儿去？*」

「*我这就过马路去——我每星期都送给一位贫苦的老婆婆，好摆在她丈夫的坟上。*」

*而他便会开口，嗓音里悄悄渗进一缕敬意*：

「*甜美的圣爱德！*」

===Original===
Mrs. Clark looked critically at each Saturday’s offering. She had a reputation to keep up.
“Thank you kindly. Them white roses last week was as beautiful as if they’d been artificial﻿—I suppose they’re all gone? Everybody always admires poor Lewis’s grave, it has the handsomest bouquets of any in the graveyard.”
“The old show-off!” Maggie exclaimed crossly, picking her prettiest roses for the Saturday bouquet.

===Chinese===
克拉克太太每个星期六都要把供品挑剔地审视一番。她是有体面要撑的。

「多谢你了。上礼拜那些白玫瑰，美得就跟假的一样——我猜这会儿都谢光了吧？人人都夸可怜的刘易斯（Lewis）的坟，满墓园里就数它的花束最气派。」

「这老爱现宝的！」玛吉没好气地嚷，一面为星期六的花束挑拣她最俊的玫瑰。

===Original===
Everybody came to The Maples for flowers, Maggie complained; but she was proud, too. Taking care of the garden used up all the time she had left from cooking and the house. Just picking the sweetpeas took hours, although all three of them picked. It was such hot work, picking out there in the truck patch, remembering to snip off old blossoms and seedpods, and not pick sprays with buds. Lily cheated sometimes, and May relieved the monotony by spelling out the names of men she knew﻿—a flower to a letter. *A*, *b*, *c*, three sweetpeas, then eight sweetpeas for *h*, then one for *a*, and so on, to spell “Charles Bradley,” or some other man. She pretended it was a little charm that would make them think of her lovingly.
They were all so bored with the work of picking and arranging, and yet complacent about it, for people were always saying to them, “*Nobody* has such sweetpeas as yours!” The milkpails full of water waited among the little green windfalls under the apple trees by the edge of the truck patch. Great milkpails full of sweetpeas, shading from white to palest pink through to almost black, and the special milkpail for the ones from light violet to deep purple. May would bury her face in them, kissing them, pouring out her love on them.

===Chinese===
人人都上枫树庄来讨花，玛吉抱怨；可她也暗自得意。做饭操持家务之外的光阴，全叫这座园子占了去。光摘香豌豆就得好几个钟头，尽管三姐妹一齐动手。在菜园里顶着日头摘花是件苦活，还得记着掐掉残花和种荚，别剪只带花苞的枝条。莉莉有时偷懒耍滑，梅便靠拼她认识的男人的名字解闷——一个字母一朵花。*a*一朵，*b*一朵，*c*一朵，三朵香豌豆；轮到*h*便是八朵；再来*a*一朵，如此这般，拼出「查尔斯·布雷德利（Charles Bradley）」，或旁的什么男人。她假装这是个小咒语，能叫他们脉脉含情地惦着她。

摘花插花的活计，人人都腻烦透了，却又都沾沾自喜，因为人们总对她们说：「*谁家*的香豌豆也比不上你家的！」菜园边上苹果树下，满满的几牛奶桶清水搁在小小的青涩落果之间等着。大大的牛奶桶插满香豌豆，从雪白到极淡的粉，一路排到近乎墨黑，另有专桶，插从浅紫到深紫的。梅会把脸埋进花里，亲吻它们，把满腔的爱倾注在它们身上。

===Original===
She had so much love to give, she longed for so much love. And the lonely years were flowing past so quickly. As she covered the enormous lampshade frames, with puffed and flounced silk, lace petticoats, and bunches of artificial flowers caught by ribbon bows, all of her but the little bit of her brain that directed her flashing fingers was lost in a dream of love. She was with her lover, cheek to cheek they spoke to each other in low and broken voices, their fingers laced together﻿—
“Oh, my darling, where are you? Come to me, save me, before it’s too late!”
“I *am* young!” she cried to herself. “Are you?” her mirror answered as she sat before it trying her hair in different ways until late, late into the night.

===Chinese===
她有那么多爱可给，又渴盼着那么多爱。而寂寞的年华正流逝得这样快。当她蒙制那些硕大的灯罩骨架——绷上蓬松带褶的绸子、蕾丝衬裙，再让缎带蝴蝶结拢住一束束人造花——她整个人，除了指挥十指翻飞的那一小点头脑，都沉入了一场爱的梦。她与她的爱人厮守在一处，脸颊贴着脸颊，低低地、断断续续地互诉衷肠，手指扣着手指——

「哦，亲爱的，你在哪儿？到我身边来，救救我，趁一切还来得及！」

「我*就是*年轻！」她对自己喊道。「是吗？」镜子这样回答——她坐在镜前，把头发试了这样梳又那样梳，直到深夜复深夜。

===Original===
There were little lines on her face﻿—yes, but only in a strong light. And what had become of the lovely warm rose of her cheeks? Well, anyone might be pale.
In one of the fashion magazines Aunt Priscilla lent them, were directions for making a collarette of pale pink chiffon. The magazine said it would cast a youthful and becoming glow. There was plenty of crumpled pink chiffon in the piece box, from the front of an evening dress Maggie had worn when she was engaged to Edward, and some odds and ends of ecru lace for trimming. It tied with broad satin ribbon﻿—she bought that. She had to buy something *sometimes*, she told herself defiantly. Hers was prettier than the one in the magazine. And yet, when it was done, and she looked at herself in the glass, she burst into tears. What was the use?

===Chinese===
她脸上有细纹了——是有，可只在强光下才现得出来。只是她颊上那可爱的暖玫瑰色到哪里去了？唉，谁没有脸色苍白的时候呢。

在普丽西拉婶婶借她们的一本时装杂志上，登着淡粉红雪纺小围领的做法。杂志说它能为面容晕上一层青春相宜的光彩。零头布匣里有的是揉皱的粉红雪纺——原是玛吉与爱德华订婚那年一条晚礼服的前襟——又有几段米色蕾丝零头可作镶边。系结用的宽缎带——那是她买的。她总得*偶尔*买点儿什么，她倔强地对自己说。她做的这条比杂志上的还俊。然而，等做好了，她往镜中一照，却放声哭了出来。有什么用呢？

===Original===
But she *would* be young. You weren’t old just because you’d lost your color and looked rather tired.
In a Philadelphia shop where no one would know her, she bought a box of rouge, asking for it in such a low voice that she had to repeat the dreadful word.
“For amateur theatricals,” she told the shopgirl haughtily.
She put a little on when she dressed to go into Wilmington to a subscription dance with Victor, who was always going to dances. Generally he went to dinners before, but this time he offered to take the girls in. Maggie only laughed, and Lily was frightened at the idea, but May wanted to go.
Her dress was pretty, she knew that, with gold-colored dots all over the white net skirt, and big balloon sleeves of gold-colored velvet, and she had brand new bronze slippers with high heels and tiny pointed toes in her slipper-bag. She and Victor had to run for the train along the frozen ruts, their breath puffing out, turning to little white clouds. Fun to run, as if you were a little girl again!

===Chinese===
可她偏要年轻。人不会只因失了血色、面带倦容，就算老了。

在费城（Philadelphia）一家没人认得她的铺子里，她买了一盒胭脂，开口要时嗓音压得那么低，不得不把那个可怕的字眼重说了一遍。

「演业余戏剧用的。」她傲然对女店员说。

她打扮停当，要同维克托一道进威尔明顿赴一场认捐舞会（subscription dance）时，便往脸上扑了那么一点儿——维克托是场场舞会都少不了的。往常他先去赴晚宴，这一回却主动提出带姑娘们进城。玛吉只一笑，莉莉一想就心里发慌，梅却是要去的。

她那条裙子是好看的，这她知道：白罗纱裙上洒满金色圆点，配大大的金丝绒灯笼袖；鞋袋里还装着一双崭新的古铜色高跟鞋，鞋尖小巧尖细。她和维克托只得顺着冻硬的车辙一路奔去赶火车，呼出的气一团团喷出，化作小小的白云。跑着真痛快，仿佛又变回了小姑娘！

===Original===
“This air﻿—like champagne﻿—It makes me feel﻿—as if I were﻿—fizzing﻿—”
“Just in time! Here she comes!”
“*Whoo!* My side! I never﻿—saw﻿—*whoo!*﻿—such bright stars!”
In the train the windows were all steamy. Her cheeks were glowing, her eyes felt big and shining. People were looking at her. Victor began to whistle “Ever of Thee” under his breath, and her feet danced a tiny waltz under her skirt. She could see herself entering the ballroom, pausing a moment, unconscious of the sensation she was making. The orchestra was playing “Ever of Thee,” and the music caught her up, she danced on its waves light as sun-gilded foam on the waves of the sea.

===Chinese===
「这空气——像香槟——叫我觉得——自己——直冒泡儿——」

「赶得正巧！车来啦！」

「*嗬！*我岔气了！我从没——见过——*嗬！*——这么亮的星星！」

车厢里，车窗上蒙满水汽。她双颊烧得绯红，眼睛自觉又大又亮。人们都在看她。维克托低声吹起口哨，吹的是《永远念你》（"Ever of Thee"）；她裙下的双脚便悄悄踏起一小段华尔兹。她能看见自己走进舞厅，略停一停，浑然不觉自己引起了怎样的轰动。乐队正奏着《永远念你》，乐声将她轻轻托起，她踏着乐声的波浪漫舞，轻盈如海浪上镀着阳光的泡沫。

===Original===
“*Gad, what a narrow escape! I almost didn’t come tonight﻿—and I might never have met you!*”
“*Your eyes are as deep as pools in a dark forest﻿—I am drowning in them.*”
*Oh!*
He *might* be there, whoever he was, wonderful, different from all the world, knowing at a glance how different she was, too.

===Chinese===
「*天哪，好险！我今晚差点儿没来——那我可就永远遇不上你了！*」

「*你的眼睛像黑森林里的深潭——我正溺在里头。*」

*哦！*

他*说不定*就在那儿——不管他是谁，那么奇妙，与全世界都不同，且一眼便看出她有多么与众不同。

===Original===
But in the dressing-room, where the debutantes were pulling on their gloves, pinning great puddings of violets on to themselves (and they needed a great deal of anchoring) and looking over their shoulders at the new glory of their trains, she felt old and cold. Just the way they stopped pushing each other aside from the pier-glass, and made way for her, just their polite changed voices when they spoke to her.
Victor was kind﻿—she didn’t want him to be. She danced three times, Victor, Raymond Line, a mature demure hop with old Judge Kelsey; and then talked brightly, feverishly to Mrs. Kelsey through one dance﻿—two dances﻿—three dances﻿—
Holding up her train and looking at it with a worried little frown, she hurried from the ballroom. It was torture to go through the ranks of “stags,” with their glove-fitting, white waistcoats, their chins propped up by their high collars, their hair parted in the middle and plastered down on either side.

===Chinese===
可是在更衣室里，初入社交界的少女们正拉上长手套，往身上别布丁似的一大蓬一大蓬紫罗兰（那可着实得下功夫别牢），又回眸顾盼自己拖裾崭新的荣光，她只觉得自己又老又冷。单凭她们不再你推我搡地争挤穿衣镜、反而给她让路的样子，单凭她们同她说话时那变了调的客客气气的嗓音。

维克托很体贴——她偏不想要他体贴。她跳了三支舞：同维克托，同雷蒙德·莱恩（Raymond Line），又同老凯尔西法官（Judge Kelsey）端端正正跳了一支持重端庄的快步舞；随后便同凯尔西太太（Mrs. Kelsey）说笑个不停，说得兴高采烈、几乎狂热，一支舞——两支舞——三支舞——

她提起拖裾，蹙着小眉头端它一眼，匆匆走出舞厅。从一排排「独行男客」中间挤过去简直是受刑：他们穿着紧窄合身的白背心，下巴叫高硬领托着，头发中分，两边抿得服服帖帖。

===Original===
“Not deserting us, Miss Campion?”
“Oh, no, indeed, but a stitch in time, you know!”
In the dressing-room she peeled off her gloves, took off a slipper and put it on again, looked in the mirror and touched her hair, drew out a hairpin and pushed it in, touched her puffed sleeves, pulled out Mamma’s lace wedding handkerchief and just touched it to her nose, tucked it away. If only the colored maid hadn’t stood there, looking at her, she could have cried a little and felt better. But she couldn’t stay in the dressing-room all night. Humming a waltz tune she drew her gloves on slowly.
Two girls burst in, warm, tingling, with melted stars in their eyes, and moist bright cheeks. They really did need repairs! One’s hair was coming down, and the other had torn off yards of flounce.

===Chinese===
「不陪我们啦，坎皮恩小姐？」

「哦，不，哪能呢，只是及时缝一针，省得将来缝九针嘛！」

在更衣室里，她褪下手套，脱下一只便鞋又穿上，照照镜子拢拢头发，抽出一枚发针再插回去，碰一碰蓬起的衣袖，掏出妈妈的蕾丝婚礼手绢，轻轻沾了沾鼻尖，又收好。若不是那个黑人女侍站在旁边瞧着她，她本可以哭上一点点，心里好受些。可她总不能在更衣室里待一整夜。她一面哼着华尔兹的调子，一面慢慢戴回手套。

两个姑娘闯了进来，热腾腾、活泼泼，眼睛里盛着化开的星子，脸颊湿润红亮。她们才是真需要修整！一个的头发散了下来，另一个扯脱了好几码荷叶边。

===Original===
“My *dear*! *Every* waltz with J.!”
“I *didn’t*! Well, anyway, *you* needn’t talk! Oh, that awful barn dance! I haven’t a hairpin left, and I look like a freak!”
“It wrecks one’s coiffure, doesn’t it?” said May eagerly, and one girl stared at her, while the other, who knew her, replied politely:
“Yes, indeed, Miss Campion.”
And then in her natural voice, a voice quivering with joy, singing with the knowledge of her prettiness, her white satin gown, her dance-card cram-jam full, and J. waiting for her, she cried to her friend:
“My dear! Look at the way these flowers have stained my new dress, if you please! I’m simply brokenhearted!”

===Chinese===
「我*亲爱的*！*每一支*华尔兹都跟J.跳！」

「我*才不*是！哼，反正*你*也没资格说嘴！哦，那个要命的谷仓舞！我一根发针也不剩了，活像个怪物！」

「那舞最毁发型了，是不是？」梅热切地说；一个姑娘直愣愣瞧着她，另一个认得她的，客气地答道：

「是啊，坎皮恩小姐。」

随后她换回了本嗓——那嗓音喜得发颤，唱得响亮，自知自己多么俏丽，自知白缎礼服多么光鲜、舞会卡记得满满当当、J.正等着她——她朝她的朋友嚷道：

「亲爱的！你倒瞧瞧这些花把我新裙子洇成什么样了，真是的！我简直心碎透了！」

---

===Original===
Maggie slept lightly, dozing, waking, until she was sure “the children” were safely home. And after she heard their doors shut, she began to wonder if they had remembered to put out the hall lamp. Oh, it was much too cold to get up! But still, she wouldn’t be able to go to sleep until she was sure.
In the hall she thought she heard the sound of sobbing from May’s room, but, when she called softly through the crack of the door, “May! Are you all right?” there was silence. She must have heard the wind that had risen and was crying around the house.
Still, it was a relief to hear from May next morning that she had had a beautiful time, when Maggie brought her breakfast up to her to have in bed, late, for a treat.

===Chinese===
玛吉睡得轻浅，一阵迷糊一阵醒，直到确信「孩子们」都平安到了家。听他们的房门关上之后，她又开始琢磨：他们记没记得熄门厅的灯。哦，天这么冷，实在爬不起来！可话虽如此，不定下心来，她是怎么也睡不着的。

在门厅里，她仿佛听见梅房里传出呜咽声；可她贴着门缝柔声唤道「梅！你没事吧？」，里头却静悄悄的。方才听见的多半是起了的风，正绕着屋子啼哭。

好在第二天早晨，玛吉把早饭端上楼，让梅赖在床上慢慢受用，算是犒赏——听梅说昨晚过得快活极了，她总算放了心。

===Original===
“Did you have a good time?”
“Wonderful! Heavenly music, and the floor was divine.”
“Who did you have for partners? Anyone I know?”
“I-don’t-believe so. Most of them were strangers. Everyone’s wearing things in their hair, Maggie﻿—I was glad I wore my gold lace butterfly, though it was very modest and meek compared to the diamond stars and crescents and things most of them had. Mrs. Kelsey had gold antennæ with diamond dangles on the tips.”
“Mrs. Kelsey with antennæ!” cried Maggie scornfully. “She’s a right hefty butterfly, is all *I* have to say. Didn’t everyone think your dress was pretty?”

===Chinese===
「玩得好吗？」

「好极了！音乐如登仙，地板也妙不可言。」

「舞伴都是谁？有我认识的吗？」

「我——想——没有吧。大多是生人。如今人人头上都戴着东西，玛吉——幸亏我戴了我那只金蕾丝蝴蝶，虽说比起她们大多数人的钻石星星呀、月牙呀什么的，我那只真是朴素谦卑。凯尔西太太戴的是金触角，角尖上还坠着钻石呢。」

「凯尔西太太戴触角！」玛吉鄙夷地叫道。「依*我*看呀，她是只蛮壮实的蝴蝶。人家不都夸你裙子好看吗？」

===Original===
“I guess so.”
“Didn’t you dance with *any*one I know?”
“Judge Kelsey and Raymond Line, but they weren’t exactly *thrilling*. Here’s my card, but they’re mostly initials.”
And there it was, as full as could be, every waltz and barn dance and *pas de quatre* filled in by May herself with different handwritings.

===Chinese===
「大概吧。」

「*一个*我认识的人你都没跟跳过？」

「凯尔西法官和雷蒙德·莱恩，可他们实在算不上*激动人心*。喏，这是我的舞会卡，上面多半是名字缩写。」

卡片果然就在那儿，满得不能再满，每一支华尔兹、谷仓舞和*四人舞*（pas de quatre），都是梅亲笔用各不相同的笔迹填上的。

===Original===
“My, you must have had fun! Victor said you were evidently having a violent flirtation in some cozy corner, he hardly saw you all evening except at supper. Was it good? What did they have? I ought to be down making doughnuts.”
“Oh, broiled oysters and salad﻿—*you* know.”
“May Campion! Who’s A. J.? *Every* waltz with A. J.! ‘Love’s Kiss,’ A. J., ‘*Je T’Aime*,’ A. J., ‘*Mia Cara*,’ A. J., ‘Love’s Confession,’ A. J.﻿—why, *May*! Well, you certainly made a conquest﻿—who in the world’s A. J.? Do I know him?”
“No, he doesn’t live in Wilmington. He came from a long way off.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，你一定玩痛快了！维克托说你八成是在哪个舒坦角落里闹了场火热的调情，一晚上除了吃夜宵，几乎没见着你的人影。夜宵好吗？都上了些什么？我该下楼炸面圈去了。」

「哦，烤牡蛎和沙拉——*你*知道的。」

「梅·坎皮恩！A. J.是谁？*每一支*华尔兹都跟A. J.！《爱之吻》（'Love's Kiss'），A. J.；《我爱你》（'Je T'Aime'），A. J.；《我亲爱的》（'Mia Cara'），A. J.；《爱的告白》（'Love's Confession'），A. J.——哎呀，*梅*！你可真叫人倾倒——这A. J.到底是谁？我认识他吗？」

「不认识。他不住在威尔明顿，是从很远很远的地方来的。」

## XXIV / XXIV

===Original===
Aunt Priscilla trailed across the fields under the soft spring sky, bringing her nieces the latest installment of “Trilby” in *Harper’s Magazine*. There were sticky circles all over the cover﻿—how had they gotten there? Jelly glasses? She licked a finger and tried to wipe them off, but it only made them look worse. “Oh, fie!” she said out loud to herself, scrubbing away with a rather grubby finger.
A bird flew from the grass at her feet with a soft whirr of wings, up into the sky. “*Goody!*” cried Aunt Priscilla, startled; and added, “Cunning little fellow!” And she went on talking to herself as she climbed over the stile and plodded up through the garden, “Willie would have gone fishing on a day like this﻿—”
The tears rose in her eyes and spilled over. They were always rolling down her cheeks now, every time anything made her think of Uncle Willie. And everything made her think of him, she was so lost without him.

===Chinese===
在柔和的春日天空下，普丽西拉婶婶拖沓着脚步穿过田野走来，给侄女们捎来《哈泼斯杂志》（*Harper's Magazine*）上新近连载的一期《特丽尔比》（Trilby）。封面上黏着一圈圈黏糊糊的圆印子——它们是打哪儿来的？果冻杯印的？她舔湿一根手指，想揩掉它们，结果只是越揩越花。「哎呀，真不害臊！」她自言自语地出了声，用一根已经相当脏的手指一个劲儿地擦。

一只鸟从她脚边的草丛里飞起，翅膀扑棱一声轻响，直飞上天。「*好家伙！*」普丽西拉婶婶吓了一跳，叫出声来；又补上一句：「机灵的小家伙！」她跨过田头的踏磴，一步一步蹭上花园，嘴里还在自言自语：「这种天气，威利原是要去钓鱼的——」

泪水涌上她的眼睛，漫了出来。如今只要有什么让她想起威利叔叔，泪水总这样顺着双颊滚滚而下。而样样东西都让她想起他；没有了他，她全然没了依傍。

===Original===
“How does anyone ever get used to their husband being dead, or their wife?” she asked Maggie miserably, sitting down in the kitchen rocking-chair, out of the way of whisking drops of the purple dye in which Maggie was stirring May’s old cream-colored challis.
“Some people get used to it easy enough,” Maggie answered briskly, her heart aching with pity. “Look at Cousin Sam and his Daisy.”

===Chinese===
「人怎么能习惯丈夫死了，或是妻子死了呢？」她凄凄惶惶地问玛吉，一面在厨房的摇椅里坐下，躲开那口紫染液锅甩溅的点点飞沫——玛吉正搅着锅，里头煮着梅的一件旧奶油色薄呢裙。

「有些人还不是轻而易举就习惯了。」玛吉利落地答道，心里却疼得发慌。「你瞧萨姆表哥和他的黛西。」

===Original===
“I know﻿—Sam’s sixty-five, the old foolish, and she’s only twenty-four.”
“So she says, but I bet she’s thirty-five if she’s a day. Poor Cousin Lizzie!”
“You know, Maggie, I see a sort of likeness to Lizzie in May, every now and then. The way Lizzie used to be when she was younger.”

===Chinese===
「我知道——萨姆六十五了，老糊涂一个，她才二十四呢。」

「她自己嘴上这么说罢了。要叫我说，她少说也有三十五了。可怜的丽齐表姐！」

「你知道吗，玛吉，我时不时会从梅身上看出几分丽齐的影子。就像丽齐年轻时候的那个样子。」

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t, Aunt Priscilla,” said Maggie, looking troubled, for she did. “Poor May! She don’t have much fun, and she’s so bright and pretty﻿—I’ve been worried about her. She’s a funny one, too; she went to a dance in Wilmington and had a wonderful time, and yet, I haven’t been able to persuade her to go to another since. But she has a new beau named Wadsworth Robinson, he seems kind of silly to me, but he certainly is devoted, and May acts happier than she has in a long time. I wish she’d get married, and Victor, too﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哦，我可没瞧出来，普丽西拉婶婶。」玛吉说，神色不安——其实她瞧出来了。「可怜的梅！她的乐子不多，偏她又那么伶俐俊俏——我一直替她悬着心。她也是个怪人儿；她去威尔明顿赴过一场舞会，玩得快活极了，可打那以后，我怎么劝她也不肯再去一场。不过她新处了一位相好，叫沃兹沃思·罗宾逊，我瞧着有点儿傻气，倒确是一往情深，梅也比好久以来都显得快活了。我巴望她早点儿嫁人，维克托也一样——」

===Original===
“Oh, Victor’s so young!”
“He’s thirty-one﻿—don’t seem possible, does it? Look, this is a pretty color, don’t you think? I believe I’ll slip up and get my old tan cape and dye that﻿—and Victor has some light ties that look kind of shabby. No, I don’t believe he’s ever gotten over Lucy Hawthorn, nasty little flirt. Of course, he’s liked lots of girls, and you know he’s *ever* so popular, Aunt Priscilla, he’s in demand for all the parties and germans and débutante dinners﻿—rosebud dinners, they call them﻿—but there hasn’t been anything serious since Lucy, and I just wish he’d fall in love﻿—goodness, is that the front door?”

===Chinese===
「哦，维克托还这么年轻！」

「他都三十一了——想想都不像，是不是？你瞧，这颜色多俊，你说呢？我看我这就溜上楼，把我那件旧的黄褐短斗篷取来，也染一染——维克托还有几条浅色领带，瞧着有点寒碜了。唉，我看他从没忘掉露西·霍桑，那个轻狂的小卖弄精。当然，他喜欢过的姑娘多着呢；你也知道他*顶顶*招人喜欢，普丽西拉婶婶，什么聚会呀、日耳曼舞（germans）呀、初入社交界的少女晚宴呀——人们管那叫『玫瑰花蕾晚宴』——处处都抢着请他；可打露西以后再没认真过一桩，我只盼他好好恋爱一场——哎呀，是不是前门铃？」

===Original===
“I’ll go,” Aunt Priscilla offered, thinking it was probably the lady selling soap and vanilla and white rose perfume who had been at her house a little earlier. But, when she opened the door, there was Sam Blow’s bride, dressed to kill in a cape of nut-brown velvet lined with gold-colored satin, with three flaring collars trimmed with gold lace, and holding her nut-brown velvet skirt high enough to show bronze shoes with sharp, tiny points and *Louis Quinze* heels. “As high as stilts,” Aunt Priscilla thought, trying to squat a little to hide her own old cloth sided boots. A hat with an openwork jet brim and a crown of brown velvet wreathed with yellow velvet roses and black ostrich tips, perched high on her much too yellow hair; and turquoise lizards and enamelled spiders and beetles with garnet and diamond eyes crawled all over her.

===Chinese===
「我去吧。」普丽西拉婶婶主动说，心想多半是那位卖肥皂、香草精和白玫瑰香水的小姐——不大工夫前还在她家来过。可她把门一开，门外立着的却是萨姆·布洛的新娘，一身盛装艳光逼人：栗棕色的天鹅绒短斗篷，里子是金黄的缎子，三道翻领镶着金蕾丝；栗棕色的天鹅绒裙裾高高提着，露出古铜色的鞋——鞋尖尖细小巧，*路易十五*（Louis Quinze）式的鞋跟。「跟踩高跷似的。」普丽西拉婶婶心想，一边悄悄往下蹲了蹲，好遮住自己那双旧布帮靴。一顶镂空黑玉帽檐的帽子，棕色天鹅绒的帽顶上缠着黄天鹅绒玫瑰和黑鸵鸟毛，高踞在她黄得过分的头发上；绿松石的蜥蜴、珐琅的蜘蛛、嵌着石榴石和钻石眼睛的甲虫，在她周身爬了一层。

===Original===
“Daisy Blow’s in the parlor!” Aunt Priscilla panted to Maggie.
“Oh, Aunt Priscilla! Oh, I *can’t* come! Oh, *bother*﻿—oh, *dear*! What did she have to come for? Go on in, like an angel, and I’ll come just as soon as ever I can﻿—”

===Chinese===
「黛西·布洛在客厅里呢！」普丽西拉婶婶气喘吁吁地对玛吉说。

「哦，普丽西拉婶婶！哦，我这副样子*不能*出去见人呀！哦，*真烦*——哦，*老天*！她跑来做什么呀？你先替我进去应着，好天使，我一收拾好马上就来——」

===Original===
And she rushed around, taking the dye off the stove﻿—*mercy*! Of course, she had to splash some out! Then up the back stairs to scrub with soap and water﻿—she was purple from top to toe﻿—give a yank to her hair, and kick into her beaded slippers﻿—that would have to do. She looked like fury, but she couldn’t help it.

===Chinese===
于是她一阵风似的团团转：把染锅从炉子上端下来——*老天爷*！当然，免不了溅出来一些！接着奔上后楼梯，拿肥皂清水一顿搓洗——她从头到脚都是紫的了——头发狠狠一拽，两脚一踢趿上珠饰便鞋——也只好这样了。她那模样要多吓人有多吓人，可她顾不上了。

===Original===
She didn’t like Daisy Blow. She looked fast, and she certainly was painted black around the eyes, for it had smudged a little. And what a lot of “Héliotrope Blanc,” perfume, the woman was drenched in it. And such airs over that huge bunch of violets, telling them that the fin de siècle girl wouldn’t consider herself dressed for out-of-doors without one. Maggie felt a sudden warm gush of affection for shabby, shy old Aunt Priscilla, with her corsets sticking out in a ridge and powdered sugar all down her front.

===Chinese===
她不喜欢黛西·布洛。她那副长相就透着轻狂，眼圈也分明描黑了——都有点儿晕开了。还有那「白天芥菜」（Héliotrope Blanc）香水，这女人通身浸了个透。还抱着那一大捧紫罗兰摆足了架子，说什么世纪末的女郎，出门若不佩一捧紫罗兰，就算不得穿好了出门的衣裳。玛吉心头忽然涌起一阵暖热的怜爱，怜爱那衣衫寒酸、怯生生的普丽西拉婶婶老好人——她紧身褡支棱出一道棱，胸前一溜儿沾着糖霜。

===Original===
Conversation creaked along, heavy and slow.
“We thought you and Cousin Sam were still in New York.”
“We came down three days ago. Sammy wanted to rusticate a bit and see his beloved horses. I tell him he loves them much better than he loves poor little me. Just between you and I, my adored Sammy is one of those social Hottentots, who thinks the conventions of Society’s charmed circle are absurd, and pines for the wilds of the country, while I am *désolée* away from town.”
“You’ll find it’s right nice here now that it’s spring,” said Aunt Priscilla, and added in a little rush of confidence, “My peach tree is in bloom!”
“Is that so?”
Everyone paused.

===Chinese===
谈话吱吱呀呀地往前挪，又沉又慢。

「我们还以为您和萨姆表哥仍在纽约呢。」

「我们三天前下来的。萨米想回乡下住一阵，看看他那些心肝马。我跟他说，他爱那些马呀，可比爱可怜的小小的我多多了。只在你我之间悄悄说：我那位敬爱的萨米，是社交场上的霍屯督野人（social Hottentots）一个，认定上流圈子里那一套繁文缛节荒唐透顶，一心渴念乡野的莽荒；而我呢，一离开城里，便*愁肠百结*（désolée）。」

「如今开了春，您会发觉这儿着实不错呢。」普丽西拉婶婶说，又按捺不住，急急补了一句知心话，「我那棵桃树开花了！」

「是吗？」

众人一时都没了话。

===Original===
“Meadowbrook’s such a nice house,” Maggie offered.
“Oh, my dear! It’s so old-*fashioned*! Of course, Sammy gives me carte blanche, as they say, and I’m going to try to brighten it up a little, but I fear me ’tis a hopeless task. Perhaps, it will be more liveable when we have a telephone put in, and electric lights.”
“*Boastie!*” thought Maggie, going into the dining-room, fiercely hospitable, to get out homemade wine and the Christmas fruitcake; and Aunt Priscilla’s eyes were nearly popping out of her head.

===Chinese===
「草溪庄园是座好宅子呢。」玛吉没话找话地说。

「哦，亲爱的！它可太*老派*啦！当然，萨米给了我全权（carte blanche），随我怎么折腾；我也打算把它拾掇得鲜亮些，只怕是一桩没指望的工程。等我们装上电话、点上电灯，兴许住着能像样些。」

「*好个夸嘴的！*」玛吉想，一面走进饭厅，怀着满腔凶猛的殷勤，取出自家酿的果酒和圣诞水果蛋糕；普丽西拉婶婶的眼睛都快从脑袋上瞪出来了。

===Original===
They sat making conversation together and taking little nibbles and sips of cake and wine; Maggie and Aunt Priscilla stiff and shy, the new Mrs. Blow grand and uneasy, until the door banged, and there was Victor. And then the caller came to life indeed. “*My!*” thought Aunt Priscilla.

===Chinese===
她们坐着没话找话，一小口一小口地抿酒咬蛋糕；玛吉和普丽西拉婶婶拘谨害臊，布洛家的新太太满身气派而不自在；直到房门砰的一响，维克托回来了。这一位客人这才当真活了过来。「*乖乖！*」普丽西拉婶婶心想。

===Original===
“*Whew!*” cried Maggie, dashing around, flinging up windows as soon as Daisy Blow drove away. “*Whew, whew, whew!* I never smelled so much perfume in my life! *Whew!*”

===Chinese===
「*嚯！*」黛西·布洛一驾车走，玛吉就叫起来，满屋子风跑，把窗子一扇一扇猛地推开。「*嚯，嚯，嚯！*我这辈子还没闻过这么冲的香水味儿！*嚯！*」

===Original===
“Methinks, my lady’s auriferous tresses are too good to be true,” said Victor.
“Hmm! Methinks, you seemed to admire them, all the same.”
“There aren’t any flies on Cousin Daisy,” Victor admitted, smiling complacently into the mantelpiece mirror.
“Well, there’s everything else﻿—turquoise *bugs* and things, I never saw anything like it. And she certainly ain’t a lady﻿—why, she don’t even speak good grammar. Here’s her handkerchief, absolutely *reeking*, now what am I going to do with that?”

===Chinese===
「依在下愚见，这位小姐满头金发，金贵得未免不像真的。」维克托说。

「哼！依在下愚见，方才你倒像是欣赏得很呢。」

「黛西表嫂可不是好糊弄的。」维克托承认，对着壁炉架上的镜子顾盼自得地笑。

「可不是嘛，旁的样样俱全——绿松石的*甲虫*什么的，我从来没见过那等阵仗。再者她也实在算不得一位淑女——喏，她连文法都说不周全。这是她的手绢，简直*熏死人*，你说叫我拿它怎么办？」

===Original===
“I have to go by there tonight, I’ll take it over,” said Victor, and he put it into his pocket and went upstairs, two steps at a time, singing at the top of his lungs:

> “ ‘Daisy, Daisy,
 

 Give me your answer true,
 

 I’m half crazy,
 

 All for the love of you﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
「我今晚正好打那儿过，我给她捎去。」维克托说。他把那方手绢揣进兜里，两步并一步跑上楼去，扯开嗓门放声唱起来：

> 「『黛西，黛西，
>
> 给我一个真心的答复，
>
> 我已经半疯，
>
> 全是为了对你的爱——』」

===Original===
Maggie and Aunt Priscilla looked at each other.
“*My!*” said Aunt Priscilla.

===Chinese===
玛吉和普丽西拉婶婶面面相觑。

「*乖乖！*」普丽西拉婶婶说。

---

===Original===
On Saturday afternoons Victor taught Daisy to ride a bicycle. He rolled her round and round the drive at “Meadowbrook,” while she, in a balloon-sleeved pink shirtwaist with a stiff white linen collar, and a small grey Fedora hat, leaned against him shrieking at the top of her lungs. And Daisy in a white silk tea-gown with lace frills and a Watteau pleat, with red silk stockings and slippers, taught Victor to make Welsh rarebits in the chafing dish.
“*Now* the beer goes in,” and she leaned against him, her perfumed hair brushing his cheek. Heavens, he was slow! But there was something sweet about him, too, and she was bored to death with her poor, dear, old Sam, snoring in his den fit to raise the roof.

===Chinese===
星期六下午，维克托教黛西骑自行车。他扶着她，在「草溪庄园」的车道上一圈又一圈地打转；她穿着泡泡袖的粉衬衫、浆硬的白亚麻领，戴一顶灰色的小费多拉软呢帽（Fedora），整个人倚在他身上，放开嗓门尖声大叫。而黛西呢，穿着缀蕾丝荷叶边、带华托褶（Watteau pleat）的白绸茶会袍，配着红丝袜红便鞋，教维克托用酒精炉锅做威尔士干酪吐司（Welsh rarebits）。

「*这会儿*该下啤酒了。」说着，她又倚到他身上，香喷喷的头发擦着他的脸颊。天哪，他可真迟钝！不过他也有一种甜甜的可亲劲儿；而她那位可怜的、亲爱的老萨姆，只在书房里打鼾，鼾声震得能掀翻屋顶——她早就腻烦得要死了。

===Original===
Or Victor took snap photographs of Daisy with her French poodle.
“Now waltz with Missy, Pompon,” Daisy would cry, seizing his paws when the posing was over, and the little black legs would scrabble as Victor whistled “*Je t’aime*.” “Muzzer’s precious pet!” Daisy murmured, covering Pompon with kisses, and looking up coquettishly at Victor. “It’s love me, love my dog, isn’t it, my poodlums?” Pompon had his own little bed, with tucked and lace-trimmed sheets and a blue silk coverlet, his own trunk to hold his ribbons and collars. “Imagine old Snap!” exclaimed Lily, awed, when Victor told the girls about it.

===Chinese===
再不然，维克托就给黛西和她的法国贵宾犬拍快照。

「现在跟小主人跳个华尔兹，蓬蓬（Pompon）。」摆姿势一拍完，黛西就抓起它的两只前爪喊道；维克托吹起《我爱你》（*Je t'aime*）的口哨，那四条小黑腿便乱蹬起来。「妈妈的小心肝儿！」黛西喃喃地说，在蓬蓬身上盖满亲吻，又抬起眼来，风骚地瞟着维克托。「爱我就得爱我的狗，是不是呀，我的小卷毛儿？」蓬蓬有自己的小床，铺着掖得平平整整、缀着蕾丝边的床单，盖着蓝绸小被子，还有自己的小箱子，装它的缎带和项圈。「想想咱们的老斯纳普（Snap）！」维克托把这些讲给姑娘们听时，莉莉咋舌惊叹。

===Original===
His sisters decided to give a party for Victor. Maggie said it was only polite when he’d gone to so many, and paid no attention to his protests. He didn’t want a party. The girls didn’t realize how elaborate parties were nowadays, and he couldn’t imagine his friends from town in the shabby dining-room, with Papa’s old desk and Mamma’s oil paintings, and the stains on the ceiling where the pipes had burst. But what could he feel but embarrassed gratitude, looking at his sisters’ glowing faces as they planned his party for him?

===Chinese===
他的姐妹们决定为维克托办一场聚会。玛吉说，人家请了他这么多回，不回请一场未免失礼，对他的抗议只当耳旁风。他并不想要什么聚会。姑娘们不明白如今的聚会多讲究；他也想象不出城里的朋友们坐进这间寒碜的饭厅——爸爸的旧书桌、妈妈的油画、水管爆裂在天花板上洇出的一块块渍痕。可是望着姐妹们为他筹划聚会时红光满面的一张张脸，他除了又窘又感激，还能有什么别的心思呢？

===Original===
The supper table was really lovely﻿—May arranged it, humming to herself, happy and light, seeing herself as Wadsworth Robinson saw her. The centerpiece was a mass of pansies tumbling from a torn straw garden hat, glistening here and there with touches of gold paint. And over the pansies flights of yellow and violet butterflies trembled on finest wires from the new lampshade of violet crêpe paper.

===Chinese===
夜宵的餐桌当真美极了——是梅布置的；她一边哼着小曲儿，又轻快又欢喜，把自己看作沃兹沃思·罗宾逊眼中的自己。桌子正中，一大丛三色堇从一顶破了边的草帽里漫泻出来，各处点着一星一星的金漆，闪闪发亮。三色堇上方，黄的紫的蝴蝶成群掠飞，从那盏新的淡紫绉纸灯罩上垂下极细的铁丝，颤颤地悬着。

===Original===
Maggie was on a rampage in the kitchen, cutting up chickens for the salad, slicing cold ham that crumbled with tenderness, buttering bread for the cucumber sandwiches. She was going to have nice things for Victor’s party, if they had to live on mush and molasses for the rest of the year. She murmured to herself a rosary of delicious dishes.
“Salmon croquettes﻿—all ready to heat﻿—beaten biscuit﻿—*they’re* done﻿—angel’s food﻿—ice-cream﻿—Jake’s going to freeze it﻿—coffee﻿—I can tend to that when I slip out to heat the croquettes﻿—Ma-ay! Front door bell!”

===Chinese===
玛吉在厨房里大逞威风：剁鸡肉拌沙拉，切酥得掉渣的冷火腿，给黄瓜三明治的面包片抹黄油。维克托的聚会上非要有些好东西不可，哪怕这一年剩下的日子全家顿顿靠玉米糊兑糖蜜过活。她像数念珠似的，给自己念叨着一串一串好吃的菜。

「鲑鱼丸子——都备好了，一热就得——拍打饼干——*那个*成了——天使蛋糕——冰淇淋——杰克管摇冻——咖啡——我溜出去热丸子的时候顺手照看——梅——呀！前门铃！」

===Original===
“Li‑ly! Door bell!”
And there was Daisy Blow, with an armful of lace tablecloth, and her coachman with baskets of candlesticks and pink china and goodness knew what.

===Chinese===
「莉——莉！门铃！」

门外正是黛西·布洛，怀里抱着一大抱蕾丝桌布，她的车夫提着一篮篮烛台、粉色瓷器和天知道些什么。

===Original===
“My dear, I simply ran over with some little things for tonight﻿—oh, Pompon, petsums, *did* his Auntie Lily step on him? He says *he* fordives her, he knows she didn’t mean to hurt poor Pompon﻿—*oh, shut up, Pomp!* Is Snap around anywheres? He and Pompon﻿—*Look!* I brought this lace tablecloth and the pink satin one for underneath to show through﻿—that’s the *dernier cri*, you know. And the table mirror and the china swan to go on it are in one of the baskets﻿—bring them in, Pete﻿—see, isn’t that *chick*? I always put that in the center, then the flowers round it, and these silver candlesticks with the pink candles and sweet little pink silk shades﻿—I said to Cousin Victor last night, ‘I’m just going to bundle up some things and bring them over﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，我简直是一溜小跑过来的，带了些今晚用的小玩意儿——哦，蓬蓬，小心肝儿，莉莉姑姑*到底*是不是踩着他啦？他说*他*原谅她了，他知道她不是存心踩疼可怜的蓬蓬——*哦，闭嘴，蓬蓬！*斯纳普这会儿在哪儿呢？它跟蓬蓬——*瞧！*我带了这条蕾丝桌布，底下还衬了一条粉缎的，好让颜色透出来——这才是*顶时新*（dernier cri）的讲究，你们是知道的。桌面镜和摆在上头的瓷天鹅都在一只篮子里——搬进来，皮特（Pete）——瞧，这多*俏*？我总把它摆在正当中，四周围一圈花，再配上这些银烛台、粉蜡烛和甜甜的小粉绸烛罩——昨晚我跟维克托表弟说来着：『我这就去捆些东西，给她们送过来——』」

===Original===
“May’s finished the table, I’m afraid.”
“Let’s see! How do﻿—oh, gracious! Too bad I was so slow, but I’m such a sleepyhead in the morning, and my old Sambo spoils me﻿—oh, *too* bad you have a violet lampshade, I brought over everything pink. Haven’t you some pink silk or something you could just run up into a little shade?”

===Chinese===
「只怕梅已经把桌子摆好了。」

「让我瞧瞧！这是怎么弄——哦，天哪！都怪我手脚太慢，可我早上就是个小瞌睡虫，我家老萨姆博又惯坏了我——哦，*真不巧*，你们这灯罩是紫的，我带来的偏偏全是粉的。你们可有粉红的绸子什么的，能随手赶出个小灯罩来？」

===Original===
The butterflies that had been quivering in a delicate living cloud turned back into paper and wire; the new lampshade looked just what it was, a makeshift.
“Aren’t you girls the smart ones? Who else in the *world* would have fixed up an old hat for a centerpiece?” Who else would want to, her expression said. And though they didn’t *want* Daisy Blow’s lace tablecloth and bisque swan, somehow there they were on the table﻿—“just to try﻿—”

===Chinese===
方才还颤巍巍的、如一朵轻云般活现的蝴蝶，又变回了纸和铁丝；那盏新灯罩也露出了本相——一件凑合的代用品。

「你们姑娘家可真够伶俐的！*普天之下*还有谁会拾掇一顶旧草帽，改作桌子正中的摆设？」还有谁乐意这么干——她的神情这么说着。虽然她们并不*想要*黛西·布洛的蕾丝桌布和素瓷天鹅，可不知怎么，它们已经端端正正上了桌——「就试一试嘛——」

===Original===
“Isn’t it a *dream*? *Sure* you don’t mind? Look at these little pink silk candleshades﻿—look! I paid ever so much for them in New York. All puffly ruffly, just like dreat bid drowed up lampshades, wasn’t they, Pompon? Pompon says, ‘yes, jus’ *zackly*’ And I brought over a box of pink and silver *dragées* I happened to have﻿—they’re not much to eat, in fact they’re left over from a couple of dinner parties, but they match the candles and candlesticks. Those butterflies﻿—hmm. They’re the cutest things I ever did see, but they don’t just exactly go with the pink, do they? I have an idea﻿—”
“Don’t let it get away,” said Lily, feeling daring. That was what Victor always said. It was exciting, to see all this pink and silver glowing and gleaming around their old dining-room﻿—horrid, if May minded, but exciting.

===Chinese===
「这难道不是一场*梦*？*真的*不介意？瞧这些小粉绸烛罩——瞧呀！我在纽约花了老大老大的价钱买的。一个个泡泡皱皱的，活脱是顶大顶大的『大人灯罩』，是不是呀，蓬蓬？蓬蓬说：『是呀，*一式一样*』我还捎来了一盒粉、银两色的糖珠（*dragées*），恰好手边有——吃着不怎么样，其实是几回晚宴剩下的，可正好配这些蜡烛烛台。那些蝴蝶嘛——嗯。它们是我这辈子见过的顶顶可爱的小玩意儿，就是跟粉色不大登对，是不是？我有个主意——」

「可别叫它跑了。」莉莉说，自觉胆大包天。这是维克托挂在嘴边的话。看着这一片粉与银，在他们那间旧饭厅四周流光漾彩，真叫人兴奋——要是梅在意，就糟心了，可真叫人兴奋。

===Original===
“I’ll dash home and get some accordion-pleated chiffon I’ve just had done for a tea-gown﻿—there’s yards and yards of it, we can catch it round the lamp﻿—we’ll all be raving beauties in its roseate glow. And Mose has a lot of pink begonias in the conservatory, they’d match better than the pansies﻿—do you want any more silver, while I’m over? Some bonbon dishes or anything? I said to Cousin Victor, I’d *adore* to bring anything, I know you live simply and why *should* you have a lot of things? Come on, Pompon, come on, Mudder’s boy﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我这就飞奔回家，取一段刚给茶会袍做的手风琴褶雪纺——有好几码好几码呢，咱们可以把它拢在灯座四周——它那玫瑰色的光一照，咱们个个都成了倾城美人。摩斯（Mose）的花房里还有好多粉色秋海棠，比三色堇更配——趁我回家，还要不要再添些银器？糖果碟什么的？我跟维克托表弟说过的，带什么都*乐意*之至；我知道你们日子过得素净，*要*那么多东西做什么呢？来吧，蓬蓬，来吧，妈妈的乖儿子——」

===Original===
Maggie came in from the kitchen, sniffing. “Daisy Blow’s been here,” she said, and then: “Oh, *May*! Your *table*!”
“Daisy’s been rearranging it,” said May listlessly.
“Why on earth did you let her? Such impertinence!”
“It’s the *dernier cri*,” Lily explained.
“Well, it looks like fury! We don’t want Mrs. Sam Blow’s things!”
“Oh, Maggie, who cares? Victor will think it’s perfect if wonderful marvellous Cousin Daisy did it.”

===Chinese===
玛吉从厨房进来，抽了抽鼻子。「黛西·布洛来过了。」她说，跟着又叫：「哦，*梅*！你的*桌子*！」

「黛西给重新摆过了。」梅没精打采地说。

「你到底怎么会由着她？这般的放肆无礼！」

「这才叫*顶时新*（dernier cri）呢。」莉莉解释道。

「哼，瞧着才吓人呢！咱们才不要萨姆·布洛太太的东西！」

「哦，玛吉，谁在乎呢？只要出自神通广大、妙不可言的黛西表嫂之手，维克托就会觉得它十全十美。」

===Original===
“*Hmp!* May Campion, where are all your butterflies?”
“I threw them away.” May opened her clenched hand, and looked at one little butterfly lying crushed on her palm. “Daisy didn’t think they went with the pink candleshades.”
“Oh, she didn’t, didn’t she? You’ve been working on those butterflies for nights and nights. Who’s giving this party, anyway?” asked Maggie furiously.

===Chinese===
「*哼！*梅·坎皮恩，你的那些蝴蝶都哪儿去了？」

「我扔了。」梅摊开攥紧的手，望着掌心里一只压瘪了的小蝴蝶。「黛西觉得它们跟粉烛罩不配。」

「哦，她觉得，她倒觉得！你那些蝴蝶，是一夜一夜熬出来做的。这聚会到底是谁在请客？」玛吉怒气冲冲地问。

===Original===
She asked it again through the evening, as Daisy dominated everything, a jewelled dagger thrust through her yellow hair, her cheeks plushy pink, as vivid as the great pink puffs of her sleeves. “That certainly is paint,” Maggie said to herself. Victor thought Daisy was all that was bright and beautiful, as she made him balance a caramel on his nose, pretending he was Pompon, slipped a piece of ice down Wadsworth Robinson’s collar, or led the chorus of groans and laughter when the booby prize was given to Raymond Line﻿—a stuffed calico rabbit, “Representing your *hare*-breadth escape from winning the first prize,” Victor said.
“Screeching like a guinea-hen!” thought Maggie. “What Victor can see in her﻿—”
Whatever it was he saw, Cousin Sam saw it too. Old and tired and silly, trying to act as young as the others, you could see his heart in his eyes as he looked at Daisy.

===Chinese===
这个问题，她一晚上问了一遍又一遍——眼看着黛西把持了一切：一柄宝石匕首插在她黄澄澄的头发里，两颊是长毛绒似的粉红，鲜亮得跟她袖子上大团大团的粉红泡泡一个样。「那分明是胭脂。」玛吉暗自说。维克托却觉得黛西集明艳于一身：她叫他鼻尖上顶一块焦糖，把他当作蓬蓬；把一块冰滑进沃兹沃思·罗宾逊的领口；末名奖颁给雷蒙德·莱恩——一只印花布填充玩具兔——的时候，她又领着满堂哄笑起哄。「象征你以一『兔』之差，惊险躲过了头奖。」维克托说。

「嚎得跟珍珠鸡似的！」玛吉心想。「维克托到底看上她哪一点——」

不管他看上的是哪一点，萨姆表哥也看上了。又老又乏又痴，偏要学着年轻人的样子闹；你瞧他望着黛西时，一颗心全摆在眼睛里。

===Original===
And just before supper, going out on the river porch to get the cream, where she had put it to keep it safe from Kitty, there were Daisy and Victor smoking together!
“I guess you think it’s real wicked for this little girl to be smoking a cigarette,” said Daisy, and Maggie answered, “No,” and stalked into the house, bursting to add, “Not wicked, just cheap and silly!” She pretended not to hear Victor call after her, “Cousin Daisy certainly has made the party a success, hasn’t she, Maggie?”

===Chinese===
就在夜宵前，她到临河的门廊去取奶油——她把奶油搁在那儿，原是防着小猫基蒂（Kitty）——却见黛西和维克托正凑在一处抽烟！

「我猜您准觉得，小姑娘家抽支香烟，简直是邪恶得很吧。」黛西说。玛吉答了声「不」，昂首阔步走进屋去，一肚子的憋闷，只想回一句：「谈不上邪恶，只是廉价又愚蠢罢了！」维克托在身后喊她：「黛西表嫂可真把这场聚会办成功了，是不是，玛吉？」她只装没听见。

===Original===
She had been afraid they would notice, when she went out to get the supper ready, and had thought what to answer if anyone asked, “Where are you going?” She would either say “To China!” or “Heigho for meddlers!” But no one noticed. “Well, I didn’t want them to,” she said, putting the croquettes into the oven and giving the door a good hard slam.
She had meant what she said when she told Aunt Priscilla that she wished Victor would fall in love with someone, but she certainly hadn’t been thinking of anyone like Daisy Blow.

===Chinese===
方才她出去预备夜宵时，原怕他们觉察，还想好了万一有谁问起「你上哪儿去？」该怎么答。她要么答「去中国！」，要么答「嘿哟，多管闲事去喽！」可谁也没有留意。「正好，我也没想叫他们瞧出来。」她说着，把鱼丸子送进烤炉，狠狠地摔上了炉门。

她跟普丽西拉婶婶说但愿维克托爱上什么人的时候，说的确是真心话；只是她心里想的，实在不是黛西·布洛这样的人。

## XXV / XXV

===Original===
“You mark my words, Lily﻿—Wadsworth Robinson is going to make May an offer, and what’s more, I believe she’s going to take him. Look﻿—he’s really not bad looking﻿—not *handsome*, but kind of nice﻿—”

Lily joined Maggie in gazing out from behind a pinch back of bedroom window curtain. Mr. Robinson propped his bicycle against a tree, took off the clips that held his white tennis trousers tight around his ankles, straightened a small white hat above his serious red-brown face, and took a box from the net hammock on his handlebars. “Chocolates,” said Lily.

===Chinese===
「你记住我的话，莉莉——沃兹沃思·罗宾逊要向梅求婚了；不但如此，我看她还会点头应下他。你瞧——他其实长得不赖——算不上*英俊*，可有几分耐看——」

莉莉凑到玛吉身旁，跟她一道从卧室窗帘捏拢的褶裥后面朝外张望。罗宾逊先生把自行车靠树支好，解下把白网球裤裤脚绷紧在脚踝上的小夹子，在那张郑重的红棕色脸孔上端端了端正那顶白色小帽，又从车把上挂着的网兜里取出一只盒子。「巧克力。」莉莉说。

===Original===
May had been watching, too, with a feeling of fingers closing around her heart, squeezing it lightly. Her knees trembled and she felt suddenly weak, and had to sit down on her bed a moment﻿—had to fling herself down with her face in the pillow.

She knew that he loved her, that he was going to ask her to marry him. And she was going to marry him. She shook with fascinated terror, dark excitement. This way, alone, with her face pressed into the pillow, she loved him.

===Chinese===
梅也一直在望着，只觉得像有几根手指攥住了她的心，轻轻地绞着。她双膝发颤，忽然浑身没了力气，只得在床沿上坐一坐——只得一头扑倒，把脸埋进枕头里。

她知道他爱她，知道他要开口求她嫁给他。而她也要嫁给他。她带着又迷醉又恐惧的战栗、幽暗的兴奋，浑身发着抖。就这样独自一人，把脸埋进枕头里，她是爱他的。

===Original===
She got up, patted a dust of powder over her flaming cheeks, and ran downstairs, and her sisters heard her company voice cry:

“Why, I didn’t know you were here! Isn’t it a heavenly day for tennis? Victor’s gone over for Daisy, they’ll be here soon, I guess.”

If only he had gone on from the place he had reached in her dream! Already she had taken him past the need of words. But he was himself, so much less real to her than her idea of him.

===Chinese===
她爬起身，往烧得通红的两颊上扑了一层薄粉，跑下楼去；姐妹们听见她换上待客的嗓音喊道：

「哎呀，我都不知道你来了呢！这天气打网球，可不是好到天上去了？维克托接黛西去了，我猜他们俩一会儿就到。」

他要是从她梦里已经走到的那一步再往前走，该有多好！在梦里，她早已带他越过了需要言语的地步。可他终归是他本人，在她眼里，远不如她心目中的那个他来得真切。

===Original===
“It certainly is a beautiful day! I’m looking forward to our game of tennis very much.”

“*Very much*”! Oh, oh, how tepid! And yet there was nothing tepid in the way he was looking at her. Wings fluttered in her breast, and her voice changed from company voice to a light shaken chime of bells, the secret of life escaped through it, though the words it was crying were only:

“Oh, for *me*? Oh, you shouldn’t have﻿—chocolates!”

“And bonbons,” he pointed out.

===Chinese===
「今天天气可真好！我非常盼望咱们这一场网球。」

「*非常*」！哦，哦，多温吞！可他望着她的那副眼色里，却没有半点儿温吞。翅膀在她胸中扑扇起来，她的嗓音从待客的腔调，化成一串轻轻发颤的铃音，生命的秘密顺着它逸了出去，尽管它喊出的字眼不过是：

「哦，给*我*的？哦，你何必破费——巧克力！」

「还有夹心糖呢。」他指出。

===Original===
Victor and Daisy played against May and Mr. Robinson. May felt like a soap bubble, bounding, gleaming, bright color swirling into bright color. Mr. Robinson couldn’t take his eyes off her, he never even looked at Daisy, except abstractedly, as if she were a cow or a tree. His faithful dog eyes followed May, you could almost see a loving tail wagging.

===Chinese===
维克托和黛西搭档，对梅和罗宾逊先生。梅觉得自己像一只肥皂泡，弹着、跳着、闪着光，亮彩旋进亮彩。罗宾逊先生的眼睛一刻离不开她，连黛西都从不看一眼——至多心不在焉地瞥一下，仿佛她是头母牛、是棵树。他那双忠犬般的眼睛一路追着梅，你几乎看得见一条深情的尾巴在摇个不停。

===Original===
“Stay to supper,” Maggie invited him and Daisy. She didn’t want Daisy, but she couldn’t ask one and not the other.

“Oh, my dear! Rapture and bliss *if* there was any way of letting my old Sambo know﻿—he’s feeling kind of mean, and most likely he’s in bed and asleep; still, I guess I ought to send word. Our telephone would certainly be a lot more useful if any other of the folks out here had one.”

===Chinese===
「留下吃晚饭吧。」玛吉向他俩——他和黛西——发出邀请。她并不想请黛西，可总不能请了这位，不请那位。

「哦，亲爱的！*要是*能有什么法子让我家老萨姆博知道，那真叫人欢喜欲狂、极乐陶陶——他这会儿身上有点儿不爽利，多半已经上床睡熟了；不过，我想总该给他捎个信儿。咱们这部电话，要是这一带还有别家也装上一部，可就顶用多啦。」

===Original===
“I’ll go over on my bicycle,” suggested Mr. Robinson in his deep serious voice, “And tell Mother on the way that I won’t be home either.”

“Oh, *would* you? Angel of light *and* mercy! I’ll love you for life!”

“There’s a moon tonight,” said Mr. Robinson in a voice vibrating with meaning. He only meant May to hear, but Daisy heard too, and burst into screams of laughter as he pedalled out of the drive, ringing his bicycle bell to the empty road.

===Chinese===
「我骑车跑一趟，」罗宾逊先生用他那深沉郑重的嗓音提议，「顺路告诉母亲一声，我也不回家吃饭了。」

「哦，你*肯*？真是光明*兼*慈悲的天使！我要爱你一辈子！」

「今晚有月亮呢。」罗宾逊先生说，声音里震颤着千钧的深意。这话他只打算说给梅一个人听，黛西却也听见了，顿时爆出一串大笑的尖叫。他蹬着车出了车道，一路按着车铃，铃声撒向空荡荡的大路。

===Original===
“ ‘There’s a moon tonight,’ ” she mimicked, deep in her throat. “*Ow!* I shall die! May, he’s going to propose! Wouldn’t you *know* he’s the kind of fellow wouldn’t think a proposal was legal unless it was by moonlight?”

“Oh, don’t﻿—!” cried Maggie.

“*Ow! Ow!* My side! Where did he get that hat?”

===Chinese===
「『今晚有月亮呢。』」她压着喉咙学舌道。「*哎哟！*我要笑死了！梅，他要求婚啦！你还不*知道*他这号人——不趁着月光，他连求婚都不信作数呢！」

「哦，别说了——！」玛吉叫道。

「*哎哟！哎哟！*笑得我胁下都疼了！他那顶帽子是打哪儿弄来的？」

===Original===
“ ‘Where did you get that hat?’ ” sang Victor, and he picked up a little white paper bonbon case from Mr. Robinson’s box, put it on his head, seized Lily’s palm leaf fan, and gave an imitation of him playing tennis that made them laugh until they cried.

“Oh! Oh!” May was nearly sobbing. “Did you ever see anything so much too small as it was, and so white, and so *new*! If it hadn’t been so *new*! But it might just as well have had ‘Bought for the occasion’ printed all over it in large black letters﻿—oh! I shall never be able to keep a straight face when I look at him again﻿—”

And she laughed and laughed, with the tears rolling down her cheeks, until she was almost screaming﻿—faint, sobbing screams, like something dying.

===Chinese===
「『你那顶帽子是打哪儿弄来的？』」维克托唱道。他从罗宾逊先生的糖盒里拾起一只小白纸糖碟，扣在自己头上，抓起莉莉的芭蕉扇，把他打网球的模样学了个惟妙惟肖，逗得大家笑出了眼泪。

「哦！哦！」梅几乎要哭出来。「你可见过那样小得不能再小、那样白、那样*新*的东西！它要不是那么*新*倒也罢了！简直不如干脆拿黑体大字从头到尾印上『专为此番场合购置』——哦！往后再看他一眼，我可再也板不住脸了——」

她笑啊笑啊，眼泪顺着两腮滚下来，直到几乎尖叫起来——微弱的、抽噎似的尖叫，像有什么东西正在死去。

===Original===
The moon turned the river to silver silk and showed frills of white flowers edging the garden paths. The air was drenched with moonlight and fragrance. And all evening May kept close to Victor and Daisy in the garden, though they certainly didn’t want her. But she wasn’t going to be left alone with that silly, solemn thing﻿—

===Chinese===
月亮把河水织成一匹银绸，照亮了花园小径两旁镶的白色花边。空气里浸透了月光和芬芳。一晚上，梅在花园里寸步不离维克托和黛西，尽管他们俩必定不情愿。可她才不要跟那件又傻气又一本正经的东西单独待在一处——

---

===Original===
Maggie could stop worrying about Daisy, for she had gone away﻿—gone away forever. Cousin Sam doubled up groaning one day on his old leather sofa, said to her, trying to smile, “Don’t you worry, honey,” and died. And Daisy cried as if her heart were broken, and, covered with crêpe, yards and yards and yards of crêpe, went back to New York as fast as she could go, hardly stopping long enough to say goodbye, even to Victor. Meadowbrook was sold to strangers now.

A year later they saw her picture in “Types of Fair Women” in *Munsey’s Magazine*, and learned that she had married again. His sisters were afraid Victor would mind, and tried to break it to him gently, but he had heard it already and didn’t seem to mind at all.

===Chinese===
玛吉不必再为黛西操心了，因为她走了——一去不回头地走了。一天，萨姆表哥在那张旧皮沙发上苦哼着蜷成一团，朝她强作笑容：「你别担心，宝贝儿。」说完就去了。黛西哭得肝肠寸断，浑身裹着黑纱（crêpe）——一码又一码又一码的黑纱——便火速赶回纽约去了，几乎没停下来好好道个别，连跟维克托也没有。草溪庄园如今卖给了外人。

一年后，她们在《芒西杂志》（*Munsey's Magazine*）的「美人典型」（Types of Fair Women）里看到她的照片，得知她又嫁了人。他的姐妹们怕维克托心里难受，想把这话婉婉地透给他，他却早已听说，而且仿佛全不在意。

===Original===
“Well, *that’s* a relief!” thought Maggie, and yet﻿—how could he be so cheerful, when he had seemed really to love Daisy? “Victor’s unselfish,” she told herself. “When he’s unhappy he keeps it to himself.”

For a few hours they were excited﻿—but God performed again his miracle of changing the water into wine, as the summer rains were bottled in the purple and pale-green globes of the grapes; the frost killed Maggie’s dahlias; Lily scalded her hand; the organ-grinder came, playing his hollow plaintive tune and jerking his monkey on its chain﻿—there were other things to think of happening all the time. They seemed so important﻿—and then they were over, and no one remembered them at all.

===Chinese===
「好嘛，*这可*叫人松了口气！」玛吉想。可是——他先前看样子是真心爱着黛西的，怎么还能这样欢欢喜喜？「维克托这个人不自私，」她对自己说，「他不快活的时候，只闷在自己心里。」

有那么几个钟头，她们激动了一场——可上帝又行了一遍他变水为酒的神迹：夏日的雨水，封存在一球球紫的、淡绿的葡萄里；霜冻死了玛吉的大丽花；莉莉烫了手；摇风琴的艺人来了，摇着他那支空落落的哀婉调子，拽着链子扯他的猴子——总还有旁的事情接二连三地挂上心头。它们一时显得那样要紧——随即过去了，谁也再不记得。

===Original===
For the second time the thunder of war came to the Campions﻿—more distant now, in Cuba. War with Spain was declared at the end of April﻿—too beautiful a time for people to be fighting, Maggie thought, pulling down an apple-tree branch and snuffing at the red buds and rose and silver blossoms. Still, one must remember the “Maine.” Victor was always singing about it.

> “ ‘Spain, Spain, Spain,
 

 Why aren’t you ashamed?
 

 Why, why, why,
 

 For blowing up the Maine?
 

 One, two, three,
 

 And Cuba will go free,
 

 There’ll be a hot time
 

 In the old town
 

 Tonight!’ ”

===Chinese===
战争的雷声第二次滚到坎皮恩家头顶——这回远了一些，在古巴。四月底，对西班牙宣了战——这么美的时节，哪是叫人打仗的日子，玛吉想，一边拉下一枝苹果树的枝条，嗅着那红色的花苞和玫瑰色、银白色的花朵。话虽如此，「缅因号」（Maine）总归不能忘。维克托总拿它唱个不停。

> 「『西班牙，西班牙，西班牙，
>
> 你怎么就不知羞？
>
> 为什么呀为什么，
>
> 炸沉了缅因号还不肯罢休？
>
> 一，二，三，
>
> 古巴就要得自由，
>
> 今夜的老城
>
> 将有一场
>
> 火热的热闹！』」

===Original===
How terrible it must be there for our poor soldiers. The sisters, looking through the dim windows of the newspapers, saw palm trees against quivering blue, and small lizards with flickering tongues darting across earth cracked open with the heat, across men who lay too still to frighten them. And men were dying of thirst and fever﻿—that was worse than having a bullet sing home to your heart. It seemed cruel to sit on the shady porch, drinking cold water, throwing what was left over the rail onto the lily-of-the-valley bed. And yet it was so hard to realize. It was as if the newspapers had made up a series of exciting stories.

“Isn’t it *dreadful*?” the Campions would say solemnly, reading, and then, kindling, “Well for pit-ty’s *sake*﻿—Raymond Line’s going to marry that Dawson girl with the funny nose!” Or Mrs. Kelsey was advertising for a cook, or, most exciting of all, Victor’s name appeared. “Look, May! Look, Maggie! Here’s Victor in *again*! ‘Mr. and Mrs. Edgar H. Snare entertained a merry party of young people in honor of their daughter Miss Lola at a picnic supper on the banks of the beautiful Brandywine, those enjoying their hospitality including’﻿—mm﻿—mm﻿—here﻿—‘Mr. V. Champion’﻿—*Champion!* How idiotic! Wouldn’t you think they’d know by *this* time? They’ve had him in often enough. Where are the scissors? I want to cut it out for him.”

===Chinese===
我们可怜的士兵们在那边，日子该多么难熬。姐妹们透过报纸这些昏蒙蒙的窗子望过去，看见摇曳的湛蓝前亭亭立着棕榈树，看见吐着细舌的小蜥蜴嗖地掠过晒得龟裂的土地，掠过那些躺着的人——他们躺得太安静，吓不着蜥蜴。还有人渴死、热病死——那比让一颗子弹呼啸着唱进你的心口还要糟。坐在背荫的门廊上喝着凉水，把剩下的水隔着栏杆泼到铃兰花床上，这样未免太狠心。然而这一切又那么难以当真，仿佛是报纸编出来的一连串紧张故事。

「这不是*可怕*嘛！」坎皮恩一家人读着报，会一本正经地这么说，随即来了精神：「哎呀，看在老天的*份上*——雷蒙德·莱恩要娶那个鼻子长得滑稽的道森家姑娘了！」再不就是凯尔西太太（Mrs. Kelsey）登广告找厨娘；或者，最叫人兴奋的——维克托的名字见了报。「快看，梅！快看，玛吉！维克托*又*上报了！『埃德加·H. 斯内尔夫妇为他们的女儿萝拉小姐，在美丽的布兰迪万河畔设野餐晚宴，款待一群快乐的年轻人，共襄盛举者包括』——呣——呣——在这儿——『V. 钱皮恩先生』——*钱皮恩*！多蠢！你以为到*这*时候他们总该记住了吧？他们登他可不是一回两回了。剪刀呢？我要把这条剪下来给他。」

===Original===
Yes, it was hard really to feel that a war was going on, although old ladies named their canaries “Dewey,” men cheerfully whistled “Goodbye, Dolly, I must leave you,” and “Just as the sun went down,” and little boys turned their hats up in front and were Rough Riders, shouting “Giddap!” to their bicycles, and hissing beds of red and yellow cannas because they were Spain’s colors.

===Chinese===
是啊，很难真正觉得一场战争正在进行，尽管老太太们给金丝雀取名「杜威」（Dewey），男人们欢欢喜喜地吹着口哨，吹的是《再见，多莉，我必须离开你》（Goodbye, Dolly, I must leave you）和《就在太阳落山的时候》（Just as the sun went down）；小男孩们把帽檐朝前翻起，扮成义勇骑兵团（Rough Riders），冲着自己的自行车喊「驾！」，朝一畦畦红的黄的美人蕉发嘘声——因为那是西班牙的国旗色。

===Original===
“Did you ever know May had a friend named Allen Jermayne?” Lily asked Maggie.

“No, I never heard of him.”

“She says he’s fighting, he’s a captain, a West Point man. She says she hasn’t any photograph of him, that he never would have one taken, but she has a drawing of a Gibson man she says is the image of him. She acts awfully funny about it. I haven’t seen any letters from Cuba, have you? But she showed me a whole pile tied up with blue ribbon, and I could just see ‘My loveliest’ something or other before she pulled them away. And she showed me some flowers he sent her from San Juan Hill﻿—she said they were tropical flowers.”

===Chinese===
「你以前知道梅有个朋友叫艾伦·杰梅因（Allen Jermayne）吗？」莉莉问玛吉。

「不知道，我从没听她说起过。」

「她说他在打仗，是位上尉，西点（West Point）出身。她说她没有他的照片，他从来不肯照相；可她有一张『吉布森男子』（Gibson man）的画像，她说跟他活脱一个模样。她在这件事上神神叨叨的，怪得很。古巴来的信我一封也没见过，你见过吗？可她给我看过一大摞，拿蓝缎带扎着，我刚来得及瞟见个什么『我最可爱的』起头，她就抽走了。她还给我看过他给她捎来的几种花，说是从圣胡安山（San Juan Hill）捎来的——她说是热带的花。」

===Original===
“What like?”

“Sort of like pressed poppies.”

“Well, she’s been tearing up for the mail every day﻿—we wouldn’t have seen them. Oh, Lily, I do wish﻿—Allen Jermayne! A. J.! I do believe that’s the man she danced with all evening that time she had such fun in Wilmington, ages and ages ago. I *think* it was A. J.”

===Chinese===
「什么样儿的？」

「有点儿像压干了的罂粟花。」

「可不是，她天天撒腿跑着去迎邮件——咱们哪看得着那些信。唉，莉莉，我真巴望——艾伦·杰梅因！A.J.！我敢保，就是老早老早以前，她在威尔明顿痛快玩了一晚上的那回，整晚跟她跳舞的那个人。我*想*那就是A.J.。」

===Original===
“I’m going to be married as soon as the war is over,” May told them one day. “I’m going to marry Captain Allen Jermayne.” And Maggie, feeling as if a fist had crashed between her eyes, knew that May was lying even before she turned scarlet and burst into tears.

Even Lily didn’t believe it. And May only pretended half-heartedly. She told them one day that Captain Jermayne had been killed, and she wore a black dress and let the others go for the mail once more. After that none of them ever spoke of it again.

===Chinese===
「仗一打完我就要结婚了，」一天，梅对她们说，「我要嫁给艾伦·杰梅因上尉。」玛吉只觉得像有一记拳头砸在自己两眼之间——还没等梅涨红了脸、眼泪夺眶而出，就知道梅是在撒谎。

连莉莉也不相信。梅自己也不过三心二意地装装样子。后来有一天，她说杰梅因上尉阵亡了；她穿起一身黑衣裙，重新让大家去取邮件。打那以后，她们谁也没有再提起这件事。

## XXVI / XXVI

===Original===
“I suppose it’s a sign a body’s getting old when time goes by so fast,” Maggie thought, putting “Victor﻿—Many Happy Returns” and a border of scallops and dots in pink icing on Victor’s birthday cake. Forty-two candles﻿—no, she wouldn’t, she’d put on four, one for each of them to blow out and make a wish on.

===Chinese===
「我看，日子过得这么快，就是人上了年纪的兆头了。」玛吉想着，一面往维克托的生日蛋糕上用粉色糖霜挤上「维克托——年年有今日」，还挤了一圈扇贝纹和圆点花边。四十二支蜡烛——不，她不放；她要插四支，他们一人一支，好吹灭了许个愿。

===Original===
Poor Aunt Priscilla had died, and people from Marcus Hook had bought Riverview, nice people, no doubt, but not Church people, and *she* looked﻿—well, plain, to say the least. But they had lots of money. May and Lily, who called, reported emperors’ heads on bronze placques, hung against grape-arbor paper, washbowls of Tiffany glass edged with wrought-iron lace, suspended by heavy chains, and holding electric lights, Turkish tabourets inlaid with mother-of-pearl, cushions (so fat and firm that they left little room in the chairs) covered with tapestry squares of gaming cavaliers and drinking monks, and a deerskin with the antlers left on hanging over the landing railing. And Mrs. Detweiler had played her phonograph to them﻿—that was wonderful. What an age they were living in﻿—really, it seemed as if there was nothing left to be invented. Think of the moving pictures that Victor had told them about﻿—*The Tailor’s Dream*, with scissors cutting out clothes by themselves, and trousers running away, *Expert Bag Punching*, *Alaska Dog Teams at Dawson City*, *Winter Sports in Norway*, *Levi and Cohen, the Irish Comedians* (that had bothered Lily until Victor explained it was a joke). How the sisters wished they could see them! But, of course, ladies couldn’t; it wouldn’t have been the thing at all to go into one of those dark mysterious places, although Maggie said she was just going to put on a thick veil and go, some day. And now here they sat and listened to Sousa’s band playing “Stars and Stripes Forever,” to someone singing a comic song about a Tattooed Man﻿—

> “ ‘It is perfectly true you can beat a tattoo,
 

 But you can’t beat the tattooed man﻿—’ ”

and to an Uncle Josh Whitcomb monologue that they couldn’t understand very well. Still, it seemed as if Uncle Josh must really and truly have his mouth at the other end of that big tin morning-glory. Mrs. Detweiler laughed so hard that little bright tears stood on her bulges of cheek, and her diamond eardrops quivered.

===Chinese===
可怜的普丽西拉婶婶去世了，河景宅卖给了一户马库斯胡克（Marcus Hook）来的人家。人想必不坏，却不是上教堂的人家；*她*嘛——呃，顶客气地说，相貌平常。不过他们有的是钱。去拜望过的梅和莉莉回来报告说：青铜牌上铸着皇帝们的头像，挂在葡萄棚花纹的墙纸跟前；盥洗盆是蒂芙尼玻璃（Tiffany）的，缘上镶着锻铁镂空花边，用沉重的链子悬吊着，盆里还托着电灯；土耳其小圆凳嵌着螺钿；靠垫蒙着一方方织锦，织的是赌钱的骑士和喝酒的修士，垫子又肥又瓷实，把椅子占得没剩多少坐处；还有一张鹿皮，连犄角原样留着，挂在楼梯口平台的栏杆上。德特韦勒太太（Mrs. Detweiler）还给她们放了留声机——那可真开眼。她们赶上的是个什么样的时代啊——真的，仿佛再没有什么留给人发明了。想想维克托给她们讲过的那些活动电影吧——《裁缝的梦》（*The Tailor's Dream*），剪刀自己裁衣裳，裤子自己逃走；《打沙包能手》（*Expert Bag Punching*）；《道森城的阿拉斯加雪橇队》（*Alaska Dog Teams at Dawson City*）；《挪威冬季运动》（*Winter Sports in Norway*）；《爱尔兰滑稽演员利维与科恩》（*Levi and Cohen, the Irish Comedians*）——这最后一个曾让莉莉犯了好一阵嘀咕，直到维克托解释说那是句玩笑。姐妹们多想亲眼看看啊！可是，太太小姐们当然看不得；走进那种黑洞洞、神神秘秘的地方，成什么体统——虽说玛吉放话，说哪天她就要蒙上一幅厚面纱去一趟。而这会儿，她们竟坐在这里，听着苏萨（Sousa）的乐队奏《星条旗永不落》（Stars and Stripes Forever），听有人唱一支说纹身人的滑稽歌——

> 「『千真万确，你尽管敲得出一片急鼓，
>
> 可你敲不倒那纹身的人——』」

还有一段乔什·惠特科姆叔叔（Uncle Josh Whitcomb）的独白，她们听不大明白。可尽管如此，那情形就仿佛乔什叔叔当真千真万确地把嘴凑在那朵大锡牵牛花（喇叭口）的另一头。德特韦勒太太笑得那样厉害，圆鼓鼓的两腮上凝起亮晶晶的小泪珠，钻石耳坠直打颤。

===Original===
“Papa and the boys certainly do delight in our talking machine! I wish you ladies could hear our Le Moyne give an imitation of it﻿—he holds his nose like this﻿—see? Like this. Laugh! We nearly die!”

And when they started to go she stopped them with a mysterious wink, flung herself back in her rocking chair, and yelled over her shoulder:

“Clarence! Cla-a-a-*runts*! (He’s our colored waiter-man.) Bring some cup﻿—there’s some in the icebox, and say! Clarence! Some pretzels﻿—you ladies like pretzels?”

===Chinese===
「爸爸和男孩子们可真爱死咱们这部说话机器了！真盼着你们几位太太能听听咱们的勒莫因（Le Moyne）学它的样子——他这样捏着鼻子——瞧见没有？就这样。笑死人了！我们简直要笑死！」

她们起身告辞时，她递一个神秘的眼色留住她们，往摇椅里一仰，扬声朝肩后大喊：

「克拉伦斯！克拉——拉——拉——*伦斯*！（他是我家的黑人侍者。）端一份果酒来——冰箱里有现成的。还有！克拉伦斯！拿些椒盐脆饼——太太们喜欢椒盐脆饼不？」

===Original===
In came a tall thin cut-glass tankard full of pieces of banana and pineapple, with claret lemonade filling the cracks, and a mountain of pretzels. She was kindness itself, but somehow they didn’t go again, although Victor went once for ping-pong and a chafing dish supper and once to a box-party they gave in Philadelphia, where he saw chinks of “Everyman” between the towering hats worn by the ladies. Mrs. Detweiler nearly cracked her jaw yawning, but she knew it was “artistic,” and genteely patted the back of her gloved wrist at the end of each act. Lily found something in a newspaper: “ ‘Everyman’ will in a measure counteract the cheapness of those fatuous frivolities, the ultra modern musical comedies.” She cut it out and pinned it to Victor’s pincushion. She almost felt, hazily, dimly, that Victor was responsible for the plays he honored by attending, the books he read, the tunes he sang in the bathroom.

===Chinese===
端进来的是一只细高的雕花玻璃大杯，盛满了香蕉块和菠萝块，缝隙里灌着红酒柠檬水，外加一座椒盐脆饼堆成的小山。她这个人再和气不过了，可不知怎么，她们后来再没登过门——虽然维克托去过一回，打乒乓球、吃了一顿小火锅夜宵；还去过一回他们家在费城开的包厢戏局，在女士们巍峨的帽子之间，窥见了《人人》（Everyman）的一线一角。德特韦勒太太呵欠打得几乎把下巴都裂了，可她知道这叫「艺术」，每一幕落幕都文文雅雅地拍一拍自己戴手套的手腕背面。莉莉在报纸上寻到一句话：「《人人》将在相当程度上，抵消那些愚妄轻浮之物——极端摩登的喜歌剧——的廉价。」她把它剪下来，别在维克托的针插上。她朦朦胧胧、隐隐约约地几乎觉得：凡维克托赏光去看的戏、他读的书、他在浴室里唱的调子，他都是要负责任的。

===Original===
Victor sang “Hiawatha” and “Any Rags?”, went to germans, collected steins (three﻿—then he gave it up) read “Storiettes” in *Munsey’s Magazine*, with heroes named Jack Meadows and heroines named Madge Van this or that, read poems about thinking you were in love with Cora, and Dora, and Dolly, and Molly, and Bessie, and Tessie, until you met the one girl (and tried writing a few himself), made fudge in chafing-dishes and played ping-pong with young ladies with pompadours, bursts of chiffon at the backs of their necks, and straight fronts, and felt that Maude Adams would really understand him if only they could meet. His mirror was stuck full of little pencilled dance-cards, and often and often he had two invitations for the same evening. Lily put his dead carnation boutonieres in water, tried unsuccessfully to lend him her mother-of-pearl opera glasses in their blue plush case, and cut out every list that held, or should have held, his name﻿—sometimes to her indignation he was hidden under the veil of “and others.” “Everybody asks Victor everywhere!” she exulted, and May replied, “Of course! He’s a bachelor. Wait till he gets married and see if he stays so popular.”

===Chinese===
维克托唱《海华沙》（Hiawatha）和《有破烂卖吗？》（Any Rags?），参加日耳曼舞会（germans），收藏德式大啤酒杯——收了三只，后来就作罢了——读《芒西杂志》（*Munsey's Magazine*）的「小故事」（Storiettes），主人公叫杰克·梅多斯，女主人公叫玛奇·范这范那；读那样的诗：你自以为爱上了科拉、多拉、多莉、莫莉、贝西、苔茜，直到你遇见了那独一无二的姑娘（他自己也试着写过几首）；用酒精灯小火锅做奶油软糖（fudge），同那些梳蓬巴杜高发髻、后颈堆一蓬雪纺、前襟束得笔直的年轻小姐们打乒乓球；还觉得，只要能同莫德·亚当斯（Maude Adams）见上一面，她准会真正懂得他。他的镜子上插满了用铅笔填好的小舞会卡，一而再、再而三地，同一个晚上接到两份邀请。莉莉把他蔫了的康乃馨钮孔花放进水里养着，想把蓝丝绒盒子装的那副螺钿观剧镜借给他，没能成功；凡是登着、或者理应登着他名字的名单，她都剪下来——有时，令她愤愤不平的是，他被藏在「及其他人等」的帷幕后头。「维克托走到哪儿都有人请！」她心花怒放。梅答道：「那还用说！他是个单身汉嘛。等他结了婚再瞧，看他还能这么吃香不。」

===Original===
Lily ate fudge, read “Richard Carvel” and “My Lady Peggy Comes To Town,” wishing that she had lived in the time of brocade and powdered hair, saw “If I Were King” and fell mildly in love with Mr. Sothern in an ermine toque, fell mildly in love with Gibson men, Christy men, and C. Allen Gilbert men, made bead chains and got her threads in terrible knots, and took up pyrography, burning crooked fleurs-de-lis and *Art Nouveau* water-lilies on wooden picture frames and glove boxes.

===Chinese===
莉莉吃奶油软糖，读《理查德·卡维尔》（Richard Carvel）和《佩吉小姐进城记》（My Lady Peggy Comes To Town），恨不得自己生在织锦缎和扑发粉的年代；看了《若我为王》（If I Were King），对着戴白鼬皮小圆帽的萨瑟恩先生（Mr. Sothern）淡淡地动了心；又对吉布森男子、克里丝蒂男子（Christy men）和 C. 艾伦·吉尔伯特男子（C. Allen Gilbert men）一一淡淡动心；编珠子链子，把线绕成可怕的乱疙瘩；又学起烙画（pyrography），在木头相框和手套盒上烙出歪歪扭扭的鸢尾花和新艺术派（*Art Nouveau*）睡莲。

===Original===
May had the straightest front and the biggest pompadour in Brandywine Hundred, made the three of them shirtwaists that were masses of handwork and lace insertion to wear with white piqué skirts, worked at a Battenberg lace bertha for Lily, holding the square of pink muslin close to her smarting eyes﻿—for she needed glasses dreadfully, but she wouldn’t wear them, she wouldn’t give in﻿—and spent hours locked in her room, lying on her bed with her arms around her pillow, whispering﻿—

===Chinese===
梅有着全布兰迪万百户区（Brandywine Hundred）最笔直的前襟、最宏伟的蓬巴杜发髻；她给姐妹三个做了几件衬衫，满身手针活和花边镶条，配白色凸纹布裙子穿；她给莉莉做一条巴滕贝格花边（Battenberg lace）的宽领，把那一方块粉色细布凑近刺痛的双眼——因为她万分需要戴眼镜，可她死也不肯戴，死也不肯服输——她还常常把自己锁在房间里，一躺几个钟头，双臂搂着枕头，喃喃低语着——

===Original===
And Maggie worked on, getting up while the east was grey to start the fire and call Victor, worked through the day worrying over May’s strangeness, the way the hens weren’t laying, the way Victor was beginning to get grey﻿—Victor! Her little brother! Admiring her own cakes, adoring her flowers, feeling patronizing towards new people in church, and frightfully annoyed with occasional squatters in their pew, and fighting off, night and day, the panic that came with the thought of how poor they were.

===Chinese===
而玛吉照旧操劳：东边天刚泛灰就起身生炉子、叫醒维克托；白天整日里操心——梅的古怪脾气啦，母鸡不下蛋啦，维克托两鬓开始见白啦——维克托！她的小弟弟！——她欣赏自己烤的蛋糕，疼爱自己的花儿，对教堂里的新面孔摆着宽厚的长辈架子，对偶尔占了他们家庭椅的生客气恼得要命；还要日日夜夜抵挡那阵恐慌——一想到家里这样穷，恐慌便涌上心头。

===Original===
At last it had to be faced.

“We just can’t go on. We’ll either have to sell the house and move into a little one, or take boarders.”

“Sell *The Maples*?”

“Well, then, we’ll have to take boarders.”

So they put an advertisement in the paper, and were sick with terror for fear someone would answer it.

===Chinese===
到头来，这件事终究躲不过去了。

「咱们实在撑不下去了。要么把房子卖掉，搬进一所小宅子，要么就招房客。」

「卖掉*枫树庄*？」

「那么，就只好招房客了。」

于是她们在报上登了广告，又吓得要命，生怕当真有人来应。

===Original===
Mr. and Mrs. Hopper and Miss Hopper came first. “Oh, don’t like it! Don’t like it!” Maggie prayed in her heart, as she showed them Mamma’s room and the tiny room opening off it, where Victor had slept when he was a little boy.

===Chinese===
先来的是霍珀先生和霍珀太太（Mr. and Mrs. Hopper），还有霍珀小姐（Miss Hopper）。「哦，可别看中！可别看中！」玛吉领他们看妈妈的房间，还有从那间屋里开门相通的小间——维克托小时候睡过的地方——心里一路这么祷告着。

===Original===
“Ho-hum!” yawned old Mrs. Hopper. Her feet were tired, and Josie kept on asking so many questions﻿—weren’t the trains noisy, and could Papa have a glass of hot milk at bedtime, and how about the bathroom? She picked up a pink plush pincushion bursting out of a silver slipper, and turned it over to see if there was a sterling mark on the bottom. “Well, Mr. Hopper, you satisfied?” she asked her husband. They both of them knew it was Josie who had to be satisfied, but they liked to pretend.

===Chinese===
「呵——呵！」老霍珀太太打着呵欠。她两只脚走乏了，乔茜（Josie）又问个没完——火车吵不吵呀？爸爸睡前能不能喝一杯热牛奶呀？浴室又怎么样呀？她拾起一只粉色长毛绒针插——从一只银鞋里鼓凸出来——翻过来，看底上有没有纯银的戳记。「怎么样，霍珀先生，你满意了吧？」她问丈夫。两口子心里都明白，要满意的非得是乔茜，可他们乐意这么装一装。

===Original===
And thin old-maid Miss Hopper, with her cheap little rings and bracelets and rhinestone combs, her beaver picture-hat, high on her head, and her bunchy suit covered with big fancy buttons and sandwiches of Irish lace and velvet, went on asking questions, so *tired* of taking care of these two helpless old babies, so tired of lagging while her slow old mother toiled along, wagging her broad behind from side to side, blocking up passageways and making audible remarks about people. “Miss Hopper is so sweet to her mother,” people said, when at a touch, Miss Hopper could have burst out screaming and *shoved* Mrs. Hopper along from behind, making her fat old legs trot, could have shrieked “Oh, shut up!” instead of mouthing, “She’ll hear you, Mama. I﻿—don’t﻿—know﻿—who﻿—she﻿—is﻿—”

===Chinese===
而清瘦的老姑娘霍珀小姐，戴着廉价的小戒指、小手镯和水钻发梳，海狸皮阔边帽高高顶在头顶，一身臃肿的套装缀满花哨的大纽扣，还有爱尔兰花边夹天鹅绒的「三明治」贴片——她只管问个不停：照看这两个离了人不行的小老宝宝，她*腻烦透了*；腻烦透了跟在后面磨蹭，等她那慢吞吞的老母亲一路挨蹭，宽阔的臀部左右摇摆，堵住过道，还把评论旁人的话说得人人都听得见。「霍珀小姐待她妈妈可真孝顺。」人家这么说。其实呢，只消碰上一下，霍珀小姐就能尖叫起来，从背后*推*着霍珀太太往前走，让她那两条老肥腿小跑起来，就能尖着嗓子喊「哦，闭嘴！」——而不是嘟哝着：「她会听见的，妈妈。我——不——知——道——她——是——谁——」

===Original===
Mrs. Mittendorf came next, because she was a friend of Mrs. Hopper’s. Just before supper, the two would put small knitted shawls around their shoulders and take little walks around the porch, their broad seats swaying, Mrs. Mittendorf calling “Hay foot! Straw foot!” Mrs. Mittendorf hadn’t been there a week before she spilled a whole bottle of essence of peppermint on the nice new mattress.

===Chinese===
接着来的是米滕多夫太太（Mrs. Mittendorf），因为她是霍珀太太的朋友。每天快开晚饭时，她们俩就往肩上披小小的针织披巾，绕着门廊小小地踱几个来回，宽大的臀部一摇一摆，米滕多夫太太一路喊着「左脚！右脚！」（Hay foot! Straw foot!）。米滕多夫太太来了不到一个星期，就把整整一瓶薄荷精洒在了那床崭新的好床垫上。

===Original===
Mr. Neff had the old schoolroom. Maggie got it ready for him with a sword turning in her heart. And after him came Miss Snaith, so quickly that Miss Hopper was sure she had followed him.

===Chinese===
内夫先生（Mr. Neff）住进了那间旧课堂。玛吉替他收拾屋子，心里像有一把剑在打转。他之后，斯奈思小姐（Miss Snaith）跟着就来了，来得那样快，霍珀小姐一口咬定她是追踪他而来的。

===Original===
Victor was hardly ever at home in the evening any more. He couldn’t stand the boarders all over the house.

“I can’t ever get into the bathroom,” he complained to Maggie.

“I know﻿—it’s Miss Snaith. She takes *hours* in there; I believe she washes out her underclothes on the sly. And then, of course, the others complain about there not being any hot water.”

===Chinese===
维克托晚上几乎再不着家了。满屋子都是房客，他受不了。

「我连浴室都进不去。」他向玛吉抱怨。

「我知道——是斯奈思小姐。她在里头一待就是*几个钟头*；我看她是躲在里头偷偷搓洗内衣。完了呢，旁人自然又要抱怨没有热水。」

===Original===
“Well, I’d like sometimes to be able to get to my room without running into a scuttling female in bedroom slippers and curl papers.”

“They’re getting ready to dazzle you and Mr. Neff, steaming their faces under towels and smoking up the lamps with their old curling irons.”

===Chinese===
「唉，我不过盼着有时候能走回自己房里，不至于撞见一个趿着卧室拖鞋、满头卷发纸、吱溜乱窜的女人。」

「人家这是拾掇停当，要迷倒你和内夫先生呢——顶着毛巾蒸脸，拿旧烫发钳把灯罩熏得乌烟瘴气。」

===Original===
“That double-distilled ass! Why are they so excited about him? He looks like something left over from a straw-ride﻿—and sticking out his chest as if he owned the house. It doesn’t seem like home any more.”

“It’s hard on you. I wish we didn’t have to.”

===Chinese===
「那条双料蒸馏的蠢驴！她们干吗为他这么起劲？他那副模样，活像草车郊游剩下没人要的物件——还挺起胸脯，仿佛这宅子是他家的。这儿再也不像家了。」

「真是苦了你。但愿咱们用不着这样才好。」

===Original===
“Well, it’s harder on you,” Victor said, and suddenly hit her cheek with his lips, quickly and shyly, and ran upstairs. Her nose tingled, tears came to her eyes. “Old foolish!” she told herself severely with a loud sniff, and hid away the moment with all the other times of especial loving kindness between herself and Victor﻿—moments to wrap in tissue of gold and hide in her heart forever.

===Chinese===
「你受的苦才更深呢。」维克托说，突然用嘴唇飞快地、羞怯地碰了碰她的脸颊，便跑上楼去。她鼻子一酸，眼泪涌上眼眶。「老犯傻！」她重重吸了一下鼻子，严厉地对自己说，随后把这一刻收拢起来，跟她与维克托之间所有那些格外温存的时刻收在一处——那样的时刻，要用金箔包好，永远藏进她的心里。

---

===Original===
“Ain’t it cold?” complained old Mrs. Hopper. “I hope I haven’t caught a cold; these ceilings are so high seems as if the heat all went up to the top, and I believe I had a draught on my back at dinner. I guess maybe I better take some camphor, I don’t want to get a cold﻿—”

The cracked, feeble, old voice droned on.

===Chinese===
「这天儿是不是冷啊？」老霍珀太太抱怨道。「但愿我没着凉才好；这天花板太高啦，热气好像全升到顶上去了，再说吃晚饭的时候，我后背八成还受了过堂风。我想我还是吃点儿樟脑的好，我可不想感冒——」

那沙哑、微弱的苍老嗓音，絮絮叨叨响个没完。

===Original===
Mrs. Mittendorf patted an enormous yawn. “Pardon *me*﻿—I have the gapes!” She billowed in the most comfortable chair, nearest the fire, her pink chins scalloped down to her great curve of bosom, her fat fingers, bulging from her dirty diamond rings, spread themselves tenderly, lovingly on the curve of her stomach. “Keep your stomach warm and you’ll be all right,” she advised. “I’m a great believer in taking care of the stomach﻿—that’s the important thing. Be good to your stomach if you want your stomach to be good to you.”

The Stomach. She sat there like some idol made of great globes of pink crystal and ebony, holding tenderly that thing that was before men were, that insatiable thing that men work all their lives to satisfy. Bring offerings of red meat, silver fish, sheaves of wheat, tear the grapes from the vines; hurry, hurry, or the Stomach will curl its lash of hunger around the bodies of its slaves.

===Chinese===
米滕多夫太太捂着一个巨大的呵欠。「恕我*失礼*——犯呵欠啦！」她在最舒服、离火炉最近的那把椅子里堆成一座肉山，粉红的双下巴一层压一层，直垂到巍峨的胸脯弧线上；她那肥胖的手指被油污的钻石戒指箍得鼓起来，温温柔柔、亲亲热热地摊在肚子的弧面上。「把胃捂暖了，就百病不生。」她规劝道。「我最讲究保养胃了——这才是顶顶要紧的。你好好待你的胃，你的胃才会好好待你。」

那胃。她坐在那儿，像一尊粉色水晶与乌木的大圆球堆成的神像，温柔地捧着那件比人类还古老的东西，那件人们劳碌一生也填不满的贪得无厌之物。快献上红肉、银亮的鱼、一捆捆麦子，快从藤上揪下葡萄；快呀，快呀，不然那胃就要把饥饿的长鞭，卷上它众奴仆的身体。

===Original===
Miss Snaith didn’t think all this talk about tummies was very delicate, with gentlemen in the room. She was sitting on the floor, showing her Teddy bear pictures in the fire, talking through it to Mr. Neff.

“See the pixtures in the fire, Teddy? See the little fire fairies? It just seems as if they were dancing, don’t it? Yes, *Teddy* sees them! Teddy says they look like they had blue and yellow skirts all going fluttery.”

===Chinese===
斯奈思小姐觉得，当着屋里先生们的面，这样大谈肚肚未免不够雅致。她正坐在地板上，给她的泰迪熊看火里的画片儿，隔着熊儿跟内夫先生说话。

「瞧见火里的画片儿了吗，泰迪？瞧见那些小火精了吗？她们就像在跳舞似的，是不是？嗯，*泰迪*瞧见了！泰迪说，她们看上去像穿着蓝裙子黄裙子，一齐飘呀飘的。」

===Original===
“A very pretty little fancy,” said Mr. Neff kindly, rousing Mrs. Hopper to call to her daughter:

“Ain’t you going to give us some music this evening, Josie?”

Miss Snaith might be full of her fancies, but she couldn’t play the piano.

===Chinese===
「好一个娇俏的小幻想。」内夫先生和气地说；这话惊动了霍珀太太，忙唤她的女儿：

「今晚你不来给我们奏点儿乐吗，乔茜？」

斯奈思小姐满脑子尽管全是她的幻想，钢琴却弹不来。

===Original===
“Everybody come and sing﻿—what shall it be? ‘Just Kiss Yourself Goodbye’ or ‘The Message of the Violet?’ Come and sing, Mr. Neff, we can’t get along without *you*.”

“Hem! Hem! I seem to have a frog in my throat.”

“Teddy says he’s goin’ to sing too, so he is!” called Miss Snaith, scrambling up. *Oop!* There went some gathers! Miss Hopper took off her rings﻿—the one like a little piece of currant jelly, the four diamond chips in a row, and the turquoise forget-me-not, and put them in the swinging candle holder. And soon they were singing:

> “ ‘Any rags, any bones, any bottles today,
 

 It’s the same old story in the same old way﻿—’ ”

Mr. Neff was perfectly killing when he sang “Any Ra-a-ags!” working Teddy’s little paw up and down as if he were an opera singer. The ladies were in raptures. Mr. Hopper tapped time to the music with a thick-soled, carefully polished old boot, Mrs. Hopper yawned and applauded, and Mrs. Mittendorf sat smiling sleepily into the fire, too comfortable to move, holding her darling stomach.

===Chinese===
「大家伙儿都来唱吧——唱哪支呢？《亲亲自己道个别》（Just Kiss Yourself Goodbye），还是《紫罗兰的音信》（The Message of the Violet）？来唱吧，内夫先生，缺了*你*可玩不转。」

「咳嗯！咳嗯！我嗓子里像是卡了只青蛙。」

「泰迪说他也要唱呢，真的要唱！」斯奈思小姐喊着，手脚并用地爬起来。*哎哟！*崩开了几道褶子！霍珀小姐摘下她的戒指——那枚像一小块红醋栗冻的、那四粒排成一排的碎钻，还有那朵绿松石勿忘我——都放进摇摆的烛台里。不多时，他们便唱了起来：

> 「『破布、骨头、瓶子，今天有卖的没？
>
> 老故事还是老样子，老调子还是老唱法——』」

内夫先生唱到「有——破——烂——儿——吗！」那一声时，真是笑煞人也：他捏着泰迪的小熊掌一上一下地挥动，活像一位歌剧大明星。太太小姐们如痴如狂。霍珀先生用一只厚底、擦得锃亮的旧靴子踏着音乐的拍子；霍珀太太打着呵欠鼓掌；米滕多夫太太坐着，昏昏欲睡地朝炉火微笑，舒服得懒得挪动，捧着她那心肝胃。

## XXVII / XXVII

===Original===
Summer came, and Miss Snaith and Miss Hopper, in piqué skirts, with chatelaine bags hanging from their straining belts, and lingerie waists with high boned collars, whose bones left cruel red marks on their necks, pretended to gather snowballs, while Mr. Neff took their photographs.

“Mercy, don’t take me, I’d spoil the picture! Really, I take an awful photograph,” Miss Hopper exclaimed, and Miss Snaith said, “I’d break the camera!” And then Miss Hopper, who had been looking at a snowball just above her head with a soulful expression, came to with a start, crying, “Oh, did you *take* it? Oh, I had no idea you were *taking* it!” They could hardly wait until Mr. Neff brought home the prints. Gracious, how dark they were! Miss Snaith could have cried, she looked so like a colored woman with a little white marble for a nose. “Oh, that’s very pretty!” Miss Hopper assured her. “I don’t think I’d know it was you, but it’s very pretty. I think they’re splendid, Mr. Neff, especially as my face hardly shows﻿—that’s a *great* advantage!”

===Chinese===
夏天来了，斯奈思小姐和霍珀小姐穿着凸纹布裙子，绷得紧紧的腰带上垂着腰链小包，上身是女式细麻衬衫，高高的骨撑领子，领骨在她们脖子上勒出一道道无情的红印；两人装模作样地采着雪球花，内夫先生给她们拍照。

「哎哟，别拍我，我会把相片糟蹋了的！真的，我照相照得可怕极了。」霍珀小姐嚷道；斯奈思小姐说：「我会把照相机弄坏的！」随后，霍珀小姐本来正含情脉脉地望着头顶上方一朵雪球花出神，猛地回过神来，喊道：「哎呀，你*拍*下来了？哎呀，我一点不知道你正*拍*着呢！」她们急不可耐地等内夫先生把印好的相片带回家来。天哪，怎么这么黑呀！斯奈思小姐简直要哭了：她那模样活像个有色人种的女人，鼻子上嵌着一粒小小的白大理石。「哦，这张好看极了！」霍珀小姐安慰她。「要不是你先说，我都认不出这是你呢，不过真好看。我觉得这些拍得再好没有了，内夫先生，尤其是我的脸几乎看不出来——这可是个*天大*的优点！」

===Original===
Miss Hopper had a Teddy bear too. Hers had a blue ribbon, and Miss Snaith’s a pink, so they wouldn’t get mixed. “Theodore bears,” Mr. Neff called them in his funny way; and through them, through loving speeches and caresses lavished on them, each tragic virgin called to him, “Help me! Help me, before it is too late!”

They had given up trying to call to that standoffish Mr. Campion, although Miss Snaith’s heart fluttered whenever she saw him﻿—just to pass the door of his empty room gave her a queer little pang, half pain, half pleasure. “He said he was fond of pink,” she thought, hooking herself into her old rose pongee princess dress. “He said he liked womenly women, just after I said I hoped women would never have to vote﻿—I wonder if he meant anything﻿—”

===Chinese===
霍珀小姐也有一只泰迪熊。她那只系蓝缎带，斯奈思小姐那只系粉的，免得搞混。「西奥多熊」（Theodore bears），内夫先生用他那有趣的腔调这样称呼它们；借着它们，借着浇在它们身上的情话和爱抚，两位可悲的老处女各自向他呼救：「帮帮我！帮帮我吧，趁现在还来得及！」

她们已经死了心，不再向那位爱理不理的坎皮恩先生呼救了，只是斯奈思小姐每次一见他，心口总要扑腾一下——哪怕只走过他那间空房的门口，也会教她隐隐一刺，半是疼，半是甜。「他说过他喜欢粉色，」她想，一面用钩扣把自己钩进那件旧玫瑰色的茧绸公主裙。「他说过他喜欢有女人味的女人——就在我说完但愿女人永远不用去投票之后——不知他这话是不是别有所指——」

===Original===
The leaves turned red; the snow fell. And in the spring Mr. Neff left.

“It’s Miss May,” he explained to Mrs. Mittendorf. “I don’t know what’s the matter with her, she just sort of smoulders all the time, and some days she won’t even answer when you speak to her. I wouldn’t mind that, if her highness don’t want to talk to me, all right, she needn’t, *I’m* satisfied; but it’s the way she cries at night, her room’s next to mine, and I can’t get my sleep﻿—I just can’t stand it.”

“She’s a funny one,” agreed Mrs. Mittendorf, rocking back and forth, her hands crossed over her belt.

“She has bats in her belfry, that’s what’s wrong with her,” said Mr. Neff darkly.

Bats in her belfry! What would Mr. Neff say next! Mrs. Mittendorf swayed backward and forward, shaking with silent laughter. Bats in her belfry!

===Chinese===
叶子红了；雪落了。到了春天，内夫先生走了。

「是梅小姐的事，」他向米滕多夫太太解释。「我不知道她是怎么了，她成天就这么阴一阵阳一阵地闷着，有的日子你跟她说话，她连搭理都不搭理。这我倒不在乎——她老人家不想跟我说话，行啊，不必说，*我*无所谓；可她夜里那个哭法，她房间就在我隔壁，我觉都睡不成——我实在受不了。」

「她是个怪人。」米滕多夫太太附和道，摇椅前后摇着，双手交叠在腰带前。

「她的钟楼里招了蝙蝠，毛病就出在这儿。」内夫先生阴沉沉地说。

钟楼里招了蝙蝠！内夫先生接下来还有什么妙语哟！米滕多夫太太前俯后仰，憋着笑抖个不停。钟楼里招了蝙蝠！

===Original===
Summer again, and boiled cherry puddings. “Dr. Mittendorf was always the greatest one for boiled cherry pudding,” said Mrs. Mittendorf, really thinking of her dead husband for the first time in ever so long. “When cherries were in market, I’d only have to say to Doctor, ‘Well, Doctor, what would you like for dinner?’ ‘Boiled cherry pudding!’ ” Her little eyes swam, partly from sentiment, partly because the steaming purple-stained dough was so hot.

The mosquitoes were dreadful. Mrs. Hopper used up bottles of citronella. And it was so hot. The boarders rocked on the river porch, slapping and flapping their palm-leaf fans. Muggy, that’s what it was. The Hoppers decided they needed a change of air﻿—they would try Asbury Park for a while.

===Chinese===
又是夏天，又是煮樱桃布丁。「米滕多夫医生生前一向顶顶爱吃煮樱桃布丁，」米滕多夫太太说——许久许久以来，她头一回当真想起了她去世的丈夫。「樱桃一上市，我只需对医生说：『那么医生，晚饭想用点儿什么？』『煮樱桃布丁！』」她的小眼睛汪起泪来，一半出于伤情，一半是因为那冒着热气、染得紫乌乌的面团实在太烫。

蚊子凶得不得了。霍珀太太用掉了一瓶又一瓶香茅油。天又那么热。房客们在临河的门廊上摇着椅子，噼噼啪啪地扑扇芭蕉扇。闷热，就是那么股闷热劲儿。霍珀一家认定需要换换空气——去阿斯伯里帕克（Asbury Park）试住一阵子。

===Original===
But the real reason was that May made them nervous. “I don’t see how you stand it, Ethel,” Miss Hopper said to Miss Snaith. They had grown quite fond of each other since Mr. Neff had gone. “She’s got a look in her eyes that sends the cold chills up my spine.”

Mrs. Mittendorf followed them. “I hate to go in a way,” she told Miss Snaith. “Miss Campion sets a lovely table, and there aren’t many places give you eggs, too, mornings you have scrapple. And I have to have plenty of good wholesome food, I’m stout, but I’m not strong. I haven’t any fault to find with Miss Campion, though she is outspoken, nor Miss Lily either, though she’s rather selfish for all she’s so religious﻿—deceitful, too. For instance, last Sunday I was just going to slip up to my room with the Sunday papers, just to glance through them, and I looked all round the parlor﻿—no papers! And there sat Miss Lily, as innocent as you please, reading ‘The Spirit of Missions.’ So I said, ‘Pardon *me*, but have you seen the Sunday papers?’ and she sat there looking around and said ‘I don’t see them.’ No, and do you know *why* she didn’t see them? She was sitting on them! I saw a corner sticking out﻿—*I* gave her a look! Still, that isn’t why I’m going, Miss Snaith, I haven’t any complaints to make about the house, though I never *was* in a place it was so hard to get hot water; but naming no names, there’s someone here that ought to be put away, and my nerves won’t stand it!”

===Chinese===
可真正的缘由，是梅让他们心里发毛。「我真不明白你怎么受得了，埃塞尔（Ethel），」霍珀小姐对斯奈思小姐说。内夫先生走后，她们俩倒你亲我热起来。「她眼睛里有那么一种神气，看得我脊梁骨直冒凉气。」

米滕多夫太太跟着也走了。「从一方面讲我也舍不得走，」她告诉斯奈思小姐。「坎皮恩小姐的饭桌摆得漂亮，肯额外给鸡蛋的地方可不多，早上还有玉米肉糕（scrapple）。我这个人，离不了大量滋补的好饭食——我是胖，可我不壮实。坎皮恩小姐呢，我没什么可挑剔的，虽说她心直口快；莉莉小姐我也没得挑，虽说她那么虔诚，为人倒有点儿自私——还会使心眼儿。比方说，上个礼拜天，我正抱着礼拜天的报纸要溜上楼去，只想大概翻一翻，我把客厅四下里瞧了个遍——报纸没了！莉莉小姐坐在那儿，没事人似的，读着《宣教精神》（The Spirit of Missions）。于是我说：『恕我*冒昧*，您瞧见礼拜天的报纸了吗？』她坐在那儿四下里张望，说：『我没瞧见。』是没瞧见，可您知道她*为什么*瞧不见吗？她就坐在报纸上！我亲眼瞧见一个角露在外头——*我*狠狠瞅了她一眼！不过呢，我不是为这个走的，斯奈思小姐，这宅子我没什么好抱怨的，虽说这么难打上热水的地方，我从来还没住过；可是，不点谁的名，这家里有一个人，该送去收起来才是，我的神经可撑不住啦！」

===Original===
And at last only Miss Snaith was left, poor Miss Snaith and her Theodore bear.

===Chinese===
到头来，只剩下斯奈思小姐一个人了——可怜的斯奈思小姐，和她的西奥多熊。

---

===Original===
May was in the garden, cutting the big solid mauve-pink roses, dew-cool and sweet. She felt happy and young, she whistled to herself. He certainly wasn’t an ordinary plumber, anyone could see that. So handsome, with those thick lashes, curling back from bright blue eyes, those strong brown hands﻿—And the way he looked at her, the way he blushed when she spoke to him﻿—*oh!*

The trouble was, he probably thought of her as a sort of Madonna, so high above him. There ought to be some way of letting people know. Perhaps when he came this morning she could drop a rose at his feet while she was saying goodmorning﻿—everyone knew a rose meant love.

===Chinese===
梅在花园里，剪那些又大又瓷实的淡紫粉色玫瑰，带着露水的清凉和香甜。她觉得自己又快乐又年轻，自顾自打着口哨。他当然不是个寻常的水管工，这谁都看得出来。那么俊——那么浓的睫毛，从亮盈盈的蓝眼睛上往后卷着，那样一双结实的棕色手——还有他望她的那副样子，她一跟他说话他就红脸的样子——*哦！*

难就难在，他多半把她当成了一位圣母，高得叫他够不着。总该有个法子让人知道才好。也许今天上午他来的时候，她可以趁道早安的当口，往他脚边丢下一朵玫瑰——谁都知道，玫瑰的意思就是爱。

===Original===
“*What became of May Campion?*”

“*Didn’t you hear? She eloped with a plumber!*”

“*Good heavens! A daughter of that proud old family!*”

“*And the strange thing is, she is radiantly happy﻿—I have never seen her look so beautiful. He is utterly mad about her, everybody says.*”

===Chinese===
「*梅·坎皮恩后来怎么样了？*」

「*你没听说吗？她跟一个水管工私奔了！*」

「*天哪！那个高傲的老门第人家的女儿！*」

「*还有桩怪事——她幸福得满面春风，我从没见她这么美过。他简直为她疯了，人人都这么说。*」

===Original===
A bee circled buzzing about a rose, and lighted on it, clinging, burrowing into it, pushing deep into its sweetness; and May, watching it, felt the blood leap to her face, began to tremble.

“Isn’t the plumber coming today?” she asked Maggie casually as she put her flowers in water.

“He’s been and gone. He’s finished the job﻿—isn’t that nice? I was afraid he’d have to come two or three times again.”

“He’s *through*?”

“Yes﻿—May, what’s the matter? *May!*”

“I don’t mind for myself,” May said through strangled sobs. “But he’ll be so disappointed!” And then she began to scream; “You did it! You did it! You sent him away because you were jealous!”

===Chinese===
一只蜜蜂嗡嗡地绕着一朵玫瑰打转，落在花上，攀住它，往里钻，深深挤进它的甜心里去；梅望着，只觉热血涌上面颊，发起抖来。

「那水管工今天不来了吗？」她把花插进水里，装作随口问玛吉。

「来过了，走啦。活儿干完了——这不是挺好？我还担心他得再跑两三趟呢。」

「他*完事了*？」

「是呀——梅，怎么了？*梅！*」

「我倒不为自己难过，」梅哽咽着抽噎道。「可他会多么失望啊！」接着她就尖叫起来：「是你干的！是你干的！你是嫉妒，才把他打发走的！」

===Original===
“*May!*”

“You always have been of everyone who was ever in love with me! You stole Edward from me﻿—you did, you did! He came to see *me*, and you stole him, but you couldn’t keep him! Well, I hope you’re satisfied, you and Victor﻿—”

“Victor never harmed you or anyone in all his life.”

“Didn’t he? Didn’t he? He kept me from marrying Wadsworth Robinson. Oh, yes, he did; if it hadn’t been for Victor I’d have been happily married today﻿—Wadsworth loved me, and I’d have been happy with him, if Victor hadn’t shown me how silly he was, laughing and making fun of him.
