And he kept Mamma from marrying Mr. Lacey, he kept you from marrying Edward﻿—he’s done nothing *but* harm, all his life.”

===Chinese===
「*梅！*」

「凡是爱过我的人，你从来样样嫉妒！你把爱德华从我身边偷走了——就是你，就是你！他是来看*我*的，你把他偷走了，可你留不住他！好啊，我但愿你称心如意，你跟维克托——」

「维克托这辈子没害过你，也没害过任何人。」

「没有吗？没有吗？是他拦着我，不让我嫁给沃兹沃思·罗宾逊。哦，不错，就是他；要不是维克托，我今天早就美满地嫁了人——沃兹沃思爱我，我本可以跟他过得很幸福，要不是维克托做给我看，他有多么蠢，笑话他、拿他打趣。还有，他拦着妈妈，不让她嫁给莱西先生，拦着你，不让你嫁给爱德华——他这辈子就没有干过别的，净干害人的事。」

===Original===
She pressed her shaking hands against her mouth, she looked at Maggie with desperate, wet, red eyes.

“Oh, Maggie, I’m so unhappy﻿—I wish I could die﻿—”

===Chinese===
她把发抖的双手紧紧压在嘴上，用一双湿漉漉、红通通的绝望眼睛望着玛吉。

「哦，玛吉，我太苦了——我恨不得死了才好——」

---

===Original===
Miss Snaith couldn’t decide whether or not to send Mr. Campion a valentine. Would he think it was funny of her? Of course, he might not guess who it came from﻿—but in that case there wouldn’t be much point in sending it.

There were some very pretty ones at Butler’s. “The is red, the blue”﻿—what did that mean? It didn’t make sense. Oh, yes, there were pictures of a rose and a violet, instead of the words. That was dainty! Quaint, too, once you caught on. And this red heart, with the border of forget-me-nots﻿—

===Chinese===
斯奈思小姐拿不定主意，要不要送坎皮恩先生一张情人卡。他会不会觉得她可笑？当然，他也可能猜不出是谁送的——可要是那样，这张卡送了也就没什么意思了。

巴特勒商店（Butler's）有几张顶好看的。「——是红的，——是蓝的」——这是什么话？讲不通嘛。哦，对了，原来玫瑰和紫罗兰是画成图画，顶替了那两个字眼。真精巧！一回过味来，还挺古雅别致。还有这张红心，四边镶着一圈勿忘我——

===Original===
A Teddy bear! What next! A Teddy bear looking through a hole in a pink heart, and a little verse:

> “Please *bear* in mind, I hope you do,
 

 A tender heart that beats for you.”

Just the thing! She would pretend it was from Theodore bear.

===Chinese===
一只泰迪熊！还有什么稀奇的！一只泰迪熊从粉红桃心上的窟窿里往外张望，还配着一首小诗：

> 「请把这*熊*记在心间，盼你记牢，
> 有一颗温柔的心，正为你而跳。」

就是它了！她要假装这是西奥多熊送的。

===Original===
She could hardly wait for Jake to bring the mail on St. Valentine’s Day. There it was, her envelope with its printed address! She’d have fun watching him when he opened it﻿—but she *knew* she would blush. Another envelope for him, that looked like a wedding invitation, a postcard from Miss Lily﻿—just a guild-meeting﻿—an envelope that looked like a valentine for Miss May, and a florist’s square box﻿—violets, if Miss Snaith wasn’t mistaken. Now who could be sending violets? Nothing for herself.

“Saint Valentine’s been good to you, Miss May.”

“A valentine?”

“Yes, you and Mr. Campion seem to be the only ones favored.”

“Who’s been sending me a valentine? Oh, and *violets*! Oh, Miss Snaith, *smell﻿—mm*!” She pressed the violets she had sent herself against Miss Snaith’s nose.

“My, what a big bunch! Somebody must like someone, sure ’nuff!” Miss Snaith said, sniffing enviously. She wished Miss May would open her envelope﻿—not that she was curious, just interested. But May took it and went upstairs to her room, humming and smelling her violets.

===Chinese===
她眼巴巴只等圣瓦伦丁节（St. Valentine's Day）那天杰克（Jake）把邮件送来。果然在——她那只印着地址的信封！看他拆信准有意思——只是她*知道*自己会脸红。他还有另一封信，看着像婚礼请柬；莉莉小姐寄来一张明信片——不过是行会开会的事；还有梅小姐的一封，看着像张情人卡；再有花店的一只方盒子——没认错的话，是紫罗兰。这会是谁送紫罗兰呢？她自个儿什么也没有。

「圣瓦伦丁待你不薄呀，梅小姐。」

「情人卡？」

「是啊，得了青睐的，好像就数你和坎皮恩先生两位。」

「谁给我送情人卡来了？哦，还有*紫罗兰*！哦，斯奈思小姐，*闻闻——嗯*！」她把那些她自己叫花店送来的紫罗兰，直凑到斯奈思小姐鼻子底下。

「哎呀，好大的一捧！准是谁喜欢上谁了，的的确确！」斯奈思小姐说，一边艳羡地嗅着。她巴不得梅小姐当场拆开那只信封——倒不是她好奇，不过是关心而已。可梅拿起信封，一路哼着歌儿、嗅着紫罗兰，上楼回房去了。

===Original===
Behind her locked door she dropped the violets on the floor and ripped open the envelope. There was a daub in red and green and yellow, a hideous sharp-nosed creature with a cat on her bony knees. “A Hopeless Old Maid,” the black letters said beneath the picture.

> “ ‘Oh, for a man’ has been your prayer
 

 For many long and weary years,
 

 But all your wiles, both sly and bold,
 

 The males have met with heartless jeers.
 

 Now, surely, you must know, yourself,
 

 That longer hope is wholly vain,
 

 And that a pitiful, sour Old Maid
 

 The rest of life you must remain.”

===Chinese===
反锁上房门，她把紫罗兰撂在地板上，一把撕开信封。里头是一幅红红绿绿黄黄的涂鸦：一个尖鼻子的丑东西，瘦骨嶙峋的膝上蹲着一只猫。图画底下印着黑字：「一个没指望的老姑娘」。

> 「『啊，给我一个男人吧』——这祷告
> 你做了多少漫长疲惫的年头；
> 可你的手段，不管明的暗的，
> 男人们只还以无情的冷嘲。
> 如今你自己总该心里有数，
> 再存指望也是全然虚飘；
> 一个可怜巴巴酸溜溜的老姑娘，
> 你的余生就只能这样到老。」

===Original===
Miss Snaith wanted to get into the bathroom﻿—of course! May had been in there for hours﻿—over an hour, anyway. The water had stopped running ever so long ago, and yet every time Miss Snaith in her old rose flannelette wrapper looked out into the hall, the bathroom door was still shut.

Well, she’d just lie down on her bed and close her eyes a few minutes. They said that made one look fresh in the evening. “I wonder what he’ll say when he opens that valentine!” she thought.

She got up and had another look. The door was still shut. What smelled so good? Gingerbread baking for dessert. She hoped they’d have whipped cream with it, instead of lemon sauce﻿—she might just say she didn’t think lemon sauce agreed with her very well. Not in front of Mr. Campion, of course.

===Chinese===
斯奈思小姐想进浴室——可不是嘛！梅在里头待了好几个钟头了——至少一个多钟头了。水声早就停了老半天，可斯奈思小姐每回穿着那件旧玫瑰色法兰绒起居袍朝过道里张望，浴室的门还是关着。

唉，那就先到床上躺一躺，闭几分钟眼睛吧。人家说，这样晚上瞧着精神。「不知道他拆开那张情人卡会说什么！」她想。

她起身又去看了一眼。门还关着。什么东西这么香？是烤姜汁饼当甜点呢。她指望能配上打发的奶油，别配柠檬酱才好——她满可以说，她觉着柠檬酱不大合她的脾胃。当然，这话不能当着坎皮恩先生说。

===Original===
She got out her demi-trained brown skirt and her best waist of tan nun’s veiling, just back from the cleaner’s. Those bishop sleeves were always getting in the butter. They’d done it very nicely, but it did smell funny. Oh, dear! Perhaps if she sprinkled on a few drops of “Cashmere Bouquet”﻿—

Which would look nicer with it, her carved sandalwood beads or the repoussé silver *bonbonnière* that swung to her knees on its long chain? The *bonbonnière* was heart-shaped﻿—that would be appropriate to Saint Valentine’s Day, and there were some cachous left in it that she could pass around.

She pinned a crescent of “rat” on the top of her head, and turned her hair back over it in a high, hard pompadour. Then she looked into the hall again.

“Tock, tock, tock,” said the clock on the stairs. Why, she wouldn’t have time to wash her hands and face for supper, not to mention washing out a few handkerchiefs and a pair of stockings or so. She tiptoed to the bathroom door and listened﻿—not a sound! Well, she’d just say something tactful through the keyhole. She rattled the knob a little, and called humourously: “Anybody drownded?”

===Chinese===
她取出那条半拖裾的棕裙，和那件最好的棕褐修女纱（nun's veiling）上衣——刚打洗衣店取回来。那对主教袖老往黄油里伸。他们收拾得很妥帖，只是那股气味实在怪。哎呀，天哪！也许洒上几滴「克什米尔花束」（Cashmere Bouquet）——

配着这身，哪样更好看呢——她那串雕花檀木珠子，还是那只凸纹银的*糖果盒*（bonbonnière），长长的链子直荡到膝头？糖果盒是心形的——这正合圣瓦伦丁节的题目，里头还剩着几粒香口的小锭子，可以分给众人。

她在头顶别好一弯头发编的「耗子」垫，把头发从上面拢过去，盘成又高又硬的蓬巴杜。然后她又朝过道里张了一眼。

「嗒，嗒，嗒，」楼梯上的钟说。哎呀，这么着，晚饭前连洗手洗脸都来不及了，更别说搓洗几块手绢、一两双长袜了。她踮起脚尖走到浴室门口，侧耳听了听——一点声儿也没有！好吧，那就隔着锁孔说句得体的话。她把门钮轻轻嗒嗒一转，打趣地喊道：「里头没人淹死吧？」

---

===Original===
Victor had stayed in town for a rehearsal at the Century Club, and was having a pleasant time with a pair of pretty Chicago ears, in Wilmington on a visit. The Maples was becoming an old plantation as it entered their sympathetic pinkness, Jake and Ida were turning into any number of old family servants, speaking in anecdotes and full of devotion for “Mr. Victor.” Things grew more Southern every minute, and Mrs. Jenkins’ voice shattered through an atmosphere of magnolia trees and mocking birds.

“Where’s the Bachelor? Bachelor! Mr. Campion!”

“Present!”

“*Well!* I’ve been calling till I﻿—Margaret! Margaret Johnson! You stay right here! We’re going to run through the ‘Bachelor’s Revery.’ Now, listen everybody, *please*, we want to go through everything the way we’re going to tomorrow night﻿—no fooling or giggling. *What*, Mr. Burnett? Yes, certainly, the firelight glow and everything﻿—”

The firelight glowed﻿—electric lights covered with red crêpe paper. Victor walked to the middle of the stage, watched coldly by Mrs. Jenkins. It was plain from the look in her eye that she wasn’t going to like it, no matter what anyone did.

===Chinese===
维克托为着世纪俱乐部（Century Club）的一场排练留在城里，正跟一双漂亮的芝加哥耳朵相处得愉快——那是两位来威尔明顿做客的芝加哥姑娘。枫树庄一走进她们那层同情的粉红色泽里，便渐渐成了一座老种植园；杰克和伊达（Ida）变成了数也数不清的老家仆，开口便是掌故，对「维克托先生」一片忠心。四下的空气一分钟比一分钟更像南方，冷不防，詹金斯太太（Mrs. Jenkins）的大嗓门撕破了这片木兰树与反舌鸟的氛围。

「单身汉在哪儿？单身汉！坎皮恩先生！」

「到！」

「*好啊！*我喊了半天，直喊得我——玛格丽特！玛格丽特·约翰逊（Margaret Johnson）！你就在这儿待着！咱们来把《单身汉的遐想》串一遍。都听着，*各位*，咱们要照明晚正式演的样子，把全套过一遍——不许胡闹，不许咯咯笑。*什么*，伯内特先生（Mr. Burnett）？对，当然，炉火的红光，全套都来——」

炉火的红光亮起来了——是蒙着红绉纸的电灯。维克托走到舞台中央，詹金斯太太冷眼看着。她眼里那副神色明明白白：不管谁卖力气，她横竖是不会满意的。

===Original===
> “ ‘Tomorrow is my wedding day,
 

 Tonight, in the firelight’s glow,
 

 I’ll sit and dream of bygone days,
 

 And the girls I used to know.’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「『明天就是我的婚礼日，
> 今夜，映着炉火的红光，
> 我要坐着梦怀逝去的岁月，
> 梦一梦旧日相识的姑娘。』」

===Original===
“You’ll have to talk louder than that.”

“All right, I will tomorrow.”

“Well, I hope you do,” said Mrs. Jenkins dubiously. “School Girl! Please *try* to be right here for your cues, or we won’t get through tonight.”

Victor sank into a Morris chair before “the firelight’s glow,” lit his pipe, gave a great stretch, and sank into the revery, while Margaret Johnson in sunbonnet, pinafore, and curls, was hit now and then by Mr. Burnett’s wavering spotlight.

“Not yet, Débutante, not yet﻿—you must wait until School Girl’s through﻿—” and she prompted the Bachelor:

===Chinese===
「你得说大声点儿。」

「行，明晚我大声。」

「哼，但愿你做得到。」詹金斯太太将信将疑地说。「女学生！请务必*守*在这儿接你的词，不然今晚咱们过不完。」

维克托在「炉火的红光」前深深坐进一把莫里斯椅（Morris chair），点上烟斗，痛快地伸了个懒腰，沉入遐想；那边玛格丽特·约翰逊戴着太阳帽、系着围涎、挂一头鬈发，时不时被伯内特先生那道摇摇晃晃的聚光灯照中。

「还不到你，初入社交界的小姐，还不到——你得等女学生唱完——」她给单身汉提词：

===Original===
> “ ‘And seem to see again
 

 The little﻿—’ ”
 

 “ ‘The little old red schoolhouse
 

 At the bottom of the lane.’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「『仿佛又重新望见
> 那小小的——』」
>
> 「『那小小的旧红校舍，
> 在小巷的尽头。』」

===Original===
“Now, Débutante﻿—”

“Of course, I’ll have a bouquet to hold tomorrow night,” the Débutante explained, giving her satin girdle a good tug down in front, and patting the mass of curls that burst out from under her coiffure wreath of cotton English daisies with cotton and rubber tubing stems. Some of them didn’t feel any too secure.

“Indian Maiden!”

They only had an Indian Maiden because Gertrude Carr had the costume. That was silly for a Bachelor’s Revery, Victor thought. But the Athletic Girl had lots of style, with her red flannel shirtwaist and white stock, her Tam o’ Shanter and flung-back plaid golf cape.

“I thought you were going to carry a golf stick, Ada.”

“I am, Mrs. Jenkins, but I can’t borrow it until tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「好了，初入社交界的小姐——」

「当然，明晚我手里还得捧一束花呢，」初入社交界的小姐解释道，一面把缎子腰带在身前好好往下拽了拽，又拍了拍从她那圈雏菊花冠底下涌出来的满头鬈发——花冠上的雏菊是棉绒做的，花茎是棉线和橡皮管。有几朵瞧着并不怎么牢靠。

「印第安少女！」

他们排印第安少女，不过是因为格特鲁德·卡尔（Gertrude Carr）现成有那身行头。演《单身汉的遐想》用这个，未免可笑，维克托想。不过那位运动女郎倒真有派头：法兰绒红衬衫、白领巾、苏格兰便帽，格纹高尔夫披风往肩后一甩。

「我还当你打算带一根高尔夫球杆呢，艾达（Ada）。」

「是要带的，詹金斯太太，可我得等到明天才借得着。」

===Original===
The Summer Girl﻿—how pretty she looked in her leghorn hat, wavy as a fluted cake-pan, trimmed with roses and set on a high bandeau. Her ingenuousness was quite a contrast to the Widow, draped in black, with her hands clasped behind her to show her fine bust and straight front.

“Bride! Bride! Where’s the Bride? Where’s Marguerite?”

“She isn’t here yet, Mrs. Jenkins, she and Scudder Tait were coming by auto, I guess that’s why they’re so late.”

Mrs. Jenkins folded her lips and sighed.

“Well, the ones of you that are going to be in the Gibson Tableaux needn’t wait any longer, but the Sunflower Belles and Beaux might go through their cakewalk again while we’re waiting for her. Please give us ‘Hello, Mah Baby,’ Mr. Sargent. *Mr. Sargent!* I said ‘Hello, Mah Baby.’ ”

===Chinese===
夏日少女——她戴着意大利麦秆草帽有多好看，帽檐波浪起伏，像只褶纹蛋糕模，缀着玫瑰，架在高高的帽箍上。她的天真烂漫，跟一身黑衣的寡妇恰成对照——寡妇双手紧扣在背后，好显出她饱满的胸脯和笔直的前襟。

「新娘！新娘！新娘在哪儿？玛格丽特呢？」

「她还没到，詹金斯太太，她跟斯卡德·泰特（Scudder Tait）坐汽车来，我猜这就是他们迟到的原因。」

詹金斯太太抿紧嘴唇，叹了口气。

「好吧，要上吉布森活人画（Gibson Tableaux）的各位，不必再等了；向日葵佳丽们和情郎们嘛，等她的当口，可以把步态舞（cakewalk）再走一遍。请给我们奏《你好吗，宝贝》，萨金特先生（Mr. Sargent）。*萨金特先生！*我说的是《你好吗，宝贝》。」

===Original===
But at last they were through, and Victor was going home on the Darby car, that connected Wilmington with Philadelphia now, was peering through the windows into the night﻿—here was his getting-off place.

The fields were white with snow, and snow fell cold and fresh on his face. He walked buoyantly up the lane, thinking of the way the Summer Girl had looked at him﻿—the Widow, too, for that matter. His mouth went up a little at the corners.

Maggie heard him singing “Hello, Mah Baby” as he climbed the porch steps, and flung the door open.

“Oh, Victor, I thought you’d never come﻿—”

“I forgot to tell you I was going to stay in for a rehearsal﻿—what’s the matter? Why are you crying?”

“May﻿—May’s drowned herself﻿—in the bathtub﻿—”

===Chinese===
好容易总算过完了，维克托坐上达比（Darby）电车回家——如今这条线把威尔明顿和费城连了起来——他隔着车窗往夜色里张望——到站了，该他下车了。

田野一片雪白，雪花又冷又新鲜地落在他脸上。他脚步轻快地走上小巷，心里想着夏日少女望他的那副眼色——说起来，寡妇的也一样。他的嘴角微微翘了起来。

玛吉听见他唱着《你好吗，宝贝》（Hello, Mah Baby）上台阶，一把推开了门。

「哦，维克托，我还当你永远不回来了——」

「忘了告诉你，我要留在城里排练——怎么了？你哭什么？」

「梅——梅把自己淹死了——在浴缸里——」

---

===Original===
“Maggie﻿—Mrs. Detweiler sent all these lilies﻿—I wish I’d never made fun of her﻿—”

“Bring them in, Victor. Doesn’t she look sweet and young? I keep thinking of the time she was the ‘Lady of Shalott’﻿—do you remember? She had lilies then, too.”

She looked from Victor’s red swollen eyes to her sister’s lovely tranquil face, and for a second it seemed as if an answer to all life’s questions had come to her, and gone before she could make it hers. May looked so young again, so happy. The room where she lay was in twilight, but out of doors sunlight on snow made a white flame too bright for mortal eyes.

===Chinese===
「玛吉——德特韦勒太太送来这些百合——但愿我当初没笑话过她——」

「拿进来吧，维克托。她看着是不是又娇甜又年轻？我总想起她扮『夏洛女郎』（Lady of Shalott）的那一回——你还记得吗？那时她也是百合。」

她看看维克托哭红肿起的眼睛，又看看妹妹那张可爱安宁的脸；有一刹那，仿佛人生一切疑问的答案都已来到她眼前，还没等她攥住、据为己有，又消逝了。梅看起来又那么年轻，那么幸福。她躺着的屋里是一片暮色，屋外却是阳光照雪，白得像一道白焰，亮得叫凡人的眼睛不敢逼视。

## XXVIII / XXVIII

===Original===
“Yes, the rooms are lovely and *big*﻿—but so little closet space! Lovely high ceilings﻿—they must make it a very hard house to heat, don’t they? See, Homer, it would have to be painted all over﻿—see, it’s all chipping off here, it comes off wherever I touch it. See? Everywhere. Careful, dear, the stairs are rather steep.”
“Here’s the kitchen,” said Maggie severely.
“Hmm. Nice and big, isn’t it? It would take a good many steps to get around *this* kitchen, wouldn’t it? Not much like the way they’re building them now. No electric light, have you, or even gas? Where do you get hired girls who’ll take care of lamps nowadays? Look out, Homer, the floor’s uneven, don’t trip.”
“Here’s the river porch.”
“Oh, yes. I guess the view must be very pretty when there isn’t quite such a glare.”
“Now I’ll show you the garden.”
“Want to see the garden, Homer? We might as well. What do you call those pink and awrnge flowers? Oh, yes, snapdragons! Remember the *beautiful* snapdragons we saw at Mrs. Proudie’s, dear? They were simply *enormous*! Oh, yes, there’s your little pool. Have you ever seen Mr. Beswick’s water garden, over near Kennett Square? Fountains and everything, and the goldfish in it are *this* big!”
And then she was saying, “Well, thank you, I’m afraid it’s a little *big*, but we’ll let you know if we﻿—ah﻿—” They were buttoning up their linen dust coats, he was pulling on his gauntlets and settling his goggles and she was tying yards and yards of chiffon veil over her visored cap, and their Royal Tourist was coughing and jerking out of the drive.

===Chinese===
「是啊，房间又可爱又*大*——就是壁橱太少了！天花板高得可爱——这样的屋子冬天准很难烧暖和，您说是不是？瞧，荷马（Homer），这房子得整个儿从头漆一遍——瞧，这儿漆皮一片片直往下剥，我手一碰就掉。瞧见没有？到处都是。当心，亲爱的，楼梯挺陡的。」

「这儿是厨房。」玛吉板着脸说。

「唔。又好又大，是不是？在*这么*个厨房里转上一圈，可得多走不少步子呢，是不是？跟如今人家盖厨房的路数可不大一样。你们没有电灯吧，连煤气也没有？如今上哪儿去找肯伺候油灯的佣人呀？留神，荷马，地板不平，别绊着。」

「这儿是临河的门廊。」

「哦，是。要是不这么晃眼，景致一定很好看吧。」

「现在带你们去看花园。」

「想看看花园吗，荷马？来都来了，看看也好。那些粉红的、橙红的花，你们管它们叫什么来着？哦，对了，金鱼草！亲爱的，还记得我们在普劳迪太太（Mrs. Proudie）家见过的那些*漂亮*金鱼草吗？简直*大*得不得了！哦，对了，你们那个小池子在这儿。贝斯威克先生（Mr. Beswick）在肯尼特广场（Kennett Square）附近那座水景园，你们见过没有？喷泉什么的样样都有，里头的金鱼有*这——么*大！」

接着只听她说：「好吧，谢谢你了，只怕这房子是稍微*大*了点儿，往后要是我们——呃——」两口子正扣着亚麻防尘外套，他拉上长手套、正了正护目镜，她往鸭舌帽上系起几码几码长的雪纺面纱，他们那辆「皇家旅行家」（Royal Tourist）汽车咳嗽着、一颠一颠地拱出车道去了。

===Original===
“Such people!” said Maggie. She felt faint with the torture of showing the place to so many, listening to their criticisms, torn between the hope of her mind that it would be bought and the hope of her heart that it wouldn’t.
And then it was sold. The Maples sold! The rich Bayard Spears bought the place. “Of course, you must consider your lovely, lovely garden just as much yours as ever,” Violet Spear told the Campions.

===Chinese===
「这路人！」玛吉说。领着一拨又一拨的人看房、听他们百般挑剔，她被折磨得直发晕；理智盼着房子卖出去，心底却盼着卖不出去，两头撕扯着。

后来房子还是卖了。枫树庄卖掉了！有钱的贝亚德·斯皮尔（Bayard Spears）买下了这地方。「当然啦，你们那座可爱的、可爱的花园，你们只管当作还跟从前一样是自家的。」薇奥莱特·斯皮尔（Violet Spear）对坎皮恩家的人说。

===Original===
Maggie and Lily were clearing out the box-room, getting ready to move.
“What are those big things done up in newspaper? Our old bustles! I guess no one will want *them* again. Here’s Cousin Jennie Blodgett’s ‘God Bless Our Home’ motto that Victor spoiled when she was making it here﻿—don’t you recollect; he was just a little fellow, and he fell off the footboard of the bed into her lap? She cried, and we were so scared﻿—see, it’s all broken﻿—why do you suppose we’ve kept it so long? Look, Lily, these old hats! Did you ever see anything so ridiculous, and yet we thought they were lovely﻿—look at this one the size of a butterplate trimmed with a wreath of white clover and a seagull﻿—and yet May looked as pretty as a picture in it! And look at this teeny, weeny muff﻿—it looks like a mouse’s muff﻿—and it’s *full* of moths﻿—I guess we’d better throw them all away﻿—”
“Shall we keep this lampshade pattern, Maggie?”
“That isn’t a lampshade, that’s Miss Snaith’s pattern for circular drawers.”
“Oh, is it? I guess that’s why I had such a hard time making that yellow lampshade from it. Here’s Victor’s old silver mug﻿—how did that get up here? And Mamma’s Wardian case﻿—”
“The Wardian case! Remember how we used to get ferns in the woods for it? And the woods are all cut down and built over now.”
“Look, Maggie, Mamma’s beadwork! Recollect how we would string the beads for her? And she let us have enough for necklaces for all the dolls﻿—oh, dear, of course, I had to spill them!”
Mothballs rattled to the floor and rolled into corners with the rolling beads, as Maggie pulled out Grandfather’s old shawl. And here were rolls of wall paper, Mamma’s moss rosebuds, the buff lozenges of the bedroom they had slept in as little girls, a diamond pattern of crossed mauve ribbons that Maggie just remembered in the parlor. Here was a box full of crêpe veils, calling up visions of sadness.

===Chinese===
玛吉和莉莉正在清理杂物间，收拾准备搬家。

「这些拿报纸裹着的大件是什么？我们从前的裙撑！我猜再不会有人要*它们*了。这是珍妮·布洛杰特表姐那幅『愿上帝保佑我们的家』——维克托在她这儿绣的时候给弄坏了——你记不记得？他才是个小不点儿，从床尾板上栽下来，一头跌进她怀里？她哭了，我们吓坏了——瞧，全破完了——你猜咱们怎么留了这么久？瞧，莉莉，这些旧帽子！你见过这么可笑的东西没有？可咱们当年还觉得漂亮得很——瞧这顶，才黄油盘那么大，缀一圈白三叶草花环，还带一只海鸥——可梅戴上它，美得跟画上人儿似的！瞧这个小不点儿手筒——活像老鼠使的手筒——里头*满是*蛀虫——我看咱们最好把这些全扔了——」

「这个灯罩纸样还留不留下，玛吉？」

「那不是灯罩纸样，那是斯奈思小姐的圆筒裤纸样。」

「哦，是吗？怪不得我照着它做那盏黄灯罩，做得那么费劲。这是维克托的旧银杯——怎么跑到这儿来了？还有妈妈的沃德箱（Wardian case）——」

「沃德箱！记得咱们从前老去树林里给它挖蕨草吗？如今树林全砍光了，都盖上房子了。」

「瞧，玛吉，妈妈的珠绣活计！还记得咱们总替她穿珠子吗？她还肯匀给咱们够每个娃娃做一条项链的珠子——哎呀，可不是嘛，我偏偏把它们打翻了！」

玛吉往外拽祖父的旧披肩，樟脑丸哗啦啦滚落一地，跟着四下乱滚的珠子一起滚进了墙角。这儿还有一卷一卷的墙纸：妈妈那色的苔蔷薇花蕾，姐妹几个小时候睡的那间卧室里的浅橙黄菱形格，还有淡紫缎带交叉编成的菱形花纹，玛吉恍惚还记得是客厅里的。这里有一匣子绉纱丧纱，唤起一幕幕伤心的旧景。

===Original===
“*Oh*﻿—”
“What is it?”
“It’s Victor’s old autograph album﻿—look, don’t you remember, May copied that dove with the banner in its mouth for him. ‘Please do not tear out any leaves. Victor Campion. The Maples,’ ‘Remember our school days at Rugby. Very truly, Your friend, J. W Harris.’ Who was he, one of the teachers?”
“No, he was a little boy who came out for over Sunday once, don’t you remember, he and Victor got a bag of black walnuts and got walnut stain over everything? And he was homesick and cried after they went to bed.”

===Chinese===
「*哦*——」

「怎么了？」

「是维克托从前的签名册——瞧，你不记得了，梅给他描过这只衔着飘带的鸽子。『请勿撕去任何一页。维克托·坎皮恩。枫树庄。』『谨忆我们在拉格比（Rugby）的求学时光。你非常忠实的朋友，J. W. 哈里斯（J. W. Harris）。』他是谁，是哪位老师吗？」

「不是，他是个来住过礼拜天的小男孩，你不记得了？他和维克托弄到一袋黑胡桃，把胡桃渍蹭得满哪儿都是？后来上了床，他想家，哭了起来。」

===Original===
> “ ‘Tell me not in mournful numbers
 

 Flirting is an empty theme,
 

 For all *school boys* have their pleasures
 

 When the *girls* upon them beam.
 

 Your friend, Emmie Holly.
 

 Did you have a nice time hunting the pump that night?’

“Well, for pity’s sake! Emmie Holly!”
“Oh, Maggie﻿—”
“What?”
“Here’s Edward﻿—‘Ever your friend, Edward Post.’ ”
“I didn’t know Victor had this book so long ago.”
“Here’s poor Aunt Priscilla﻿—‘The night is’ something﻿—a big blot﻿—‘that never finds a day.’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「『别用哀伤的诗句告诉我，
> 说调情是个空洞的题目，
> 因为*男学生*个个自有乐趣，
> 只要*女孩子*朝他们笑意盈盈。
> 你的朋友，艾米·霍利（Emmie Holly）。
> 那天夜里找水泵，你们玩得痛快吗？』」

「哎呀，我的老天！艾米·霍利！」

「哦，玛吉——」

「怎么？」

「这儿是爱德华——『你永远的朋友，爱德华·波斯特。』」

「没想到维克托这么早就有这本册子了。」

「这儿是可怜的普丽西拉婶婶——『黑夜是』什么什么——一大团墨渍——『永远等不来白昼。』」

===Original===
> “ ‘In sailing down the stream of life
 

 In your little canoe
 

 May you have a pleasant voyage
 

 And room enough for two.
 

 Your friend, Bessie Schmalsweiden.’

“Bessie Schmalsweiden! What a name! Who’s *she*? I never heard of her in my *life*! Here’s another:

> “ ‘You may dream of poetical fame
 

 But your wishes may chance to miscarry,
 

 The best way of sending one’s name,
 

 To posterity, Victor, is to marry.
 

 M. C.’

===Chinese===
> 「『愿你驾着你的小小独木舟，
> 顺人生之河一路下行，
> 愿你旅途平安愉快，
> 舟中宽宽绰绰坐得下两个人。
> 你的朋友，贝西·施马尔施维登。』」

「贝西·施马尔施维登（Bessie Schmalsweiden）！好个姓氏！她*是*谁呀？我*这辈子*从没听说过这个人！这儿还有一首：

> 「『你可以梦想诗人的声名，
> 可你的心愿未必能够如愿，
> 要把自己的名字传给后世，
> 维克托，最好的法子是成亲。
> M. C.』」

===Original===
“Who’s M. C.? Lily!

> “ ‘*Remembrance* is all I ask,
 

 And if remembrance prove a task
 

 
 *Forget me!*
 
 

 You true friend, Lucy Hawthorn.’ ”

===Chinese===
「M. C. 是谁？莉莉！

> 「『我只求一份*记得*，
> 倘若记得成了苦差，
> *那就忘了我吧！*
> 你忠实的朋友，露西·霍桑。』」

===Original===
“Here’s another book﻿—shall I give it to Jake for the children?”
“Look and see if there isn’t a picture of a little girl, carrying her baby brother across stepping stones over a brook and trying to hold on to a bunch of wild flowers﻿—yes, there it is! Why did that picture always make me feel so happy? We better keep all these old songs of Mamma’s.”
She turned over the yellow sheets of music. “All That’s Bright Must Fade.” “Where Is My Lover?” “Withered Geranium.”

> “ ‘Home of youth! all thy pleasures
 

 Are impressed on my heart﻿—
 

 Ere they fade from my mem’ry
 

 Life itself must depart﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
「这儿还有一本——拿给杰克带给孩子们，好不好？」

「找找看，里头有没有一幅画，画着一个小女孩，抱着她的小弟弟，踩着小溪里的踏脚石过河，手里还攥着一束野花——对，就在这儿！为什么那幅画从前总让我觉得那么快活？妈妈这些旧歌谱，咱们最好都留着。」

她翻动发黄的歌谱。《一切明亮终将凋谢》（All That's Bright Must Fade）。《我的爱人在哪里》（Where Is My Lover?）。《枯萎的天竺葵》（Withered Geranium）。

> 「『青春的家园！你所有的欢愉
> 都深印在我的心上——
> 趁它们还没从记忆里消逝，
> 生命本身必先离去——』」

===Original===
“ ’S Maggie! ’S Maggie!”
“I told Lossie to yell up when it was lunch time, but it *can’t* be yet. What is it, Lossie?”
“Dey aint nothin’ fo’ lunch, scusin’ some pohk chops Ah give Jake.”
“I guess fried tomatoes’ll have to do. I’ll run out and get some, and you be looking through this trimming box, Lily﻿—there’s a lot that’s not worth moving that Lossie’d love to have﻿—those jet buckles are kind of pretty, though.”
Coming in from the truck patch with the tomatoes, she saw a butterfly that had lighted on the bricks by the kitchen steps and was slowly opening and closing its quivering wings, blue in the sunshine, black in shadow. She stood gazing at it, lost.
“*Goodness!* I haven’t any time to stand looking at butterflies!”
But what did it make her think of? What was it? And then she was back through the years, back under the pear tree with Edward, watching another butterfly. The tomatoes fell to the ground and burst.

===Chinese===
「玛吉小姐！玛吉小姐！」

「我明明叫罗西到了午饭时候喊我一声，可现在*绝*还没到点儿。什么事，罗西？」

「午饭啥也没有啦，就剩我给杰克的几块猪排。」

「那只好煎番茄将就了。我出去买几个，你把这盒针头线脑翻一翻，莉莉——有一大堆不值得搬的东西，罗西保管喜欢——不过那几颗黑玉鞋扣倒怪好看的。」

她从菜地里摘了番茄回来，看见一只蝴蝶落在厨房台阶边的砖上，正慢慢开合它颤动的翅膀，阳光下蓝莹莹，阴影里黑沉沉。她站在那儿望着它出了神。

「*天哪！*我可没工夫站在这儿看蝴蝶！」

可它让她想起了什么呢？是什么来着？接着她便穿过岁月回去了，回到梨树下爱德华身边，望着另一只蝴蝶。番茄跌落在地，摔裂了。

---

===Original===
After all it was a relief when they were finally in the new house. It was a plain little place, and they couldn’t see the river; but there was a sunny bay-window for Maggie’s geraniums, and the card dish was full of calling-cards, with the grandest surreptitiously put on top by Lily. And just having a clean empty kitchen to go into was luxury. To know there wouldn’t be a slimy mass of tea-leaves and orange seeds and bits of string to be picked out of the sink drain, or a tipped-over bottle of sticky black “tonic” oozing among the tea-towels: to be able to relax from the mental pushing and shoving at the dark sisters of that long procession that strolled through the kitchen of The Maples while the Campions were keeping boarders.
The Maples was being remodelled. Maggie never went on that bit of road, but people told her about it.
It wouldn’t have been as hard if the Spears hadn’t been making the place so much more beautiful. The new walled garden; the long-spurred columbine from England; the tea-roses with their melting colors of sunsets and peaches and cream; the little lead boys holding a sundial on their heads, far older than the great trees that feathered the sky above the house; Mrs. Spear’s tool-house like a little stone house in the woods in a German fairy tale, with all the tools Maggie had always wanted, with their handles painted moss-green, and the shelter-seat under the pear trees, whose petals fell on butler and footman spreading the lace cloth, bringing out silver tea-things and plates of paper-thin bread and butter or hot crumpets melting with butter and tenderness. It would have been easier to bear, if the Spears had gone in for peanut brittle stone walls and round beds of cannas, like Mrs. Detweiler.

===Chinese===
等他们终于搬进了新房子，说到底倒松了口气。那是个简朴的小地方，望不见河；可玛吉的天竺葵有了一扇向阳的凸窗，名片碟里插满了拜客名片，顶上最气派的那一张，是莉莉悄悄摆上去的。单是能走进一间干干净净、空空荡荡的厨房，就是奢侈了。再不必从水槽的下水口里抠出黏糊糊的一团茶叶、橙子籽和碎线头，再不必眼看着一瓶倒翻的、黏糊糊的黑「补药」在擦碗布中间洇渗：坎皮恩家招房客那阵子，一长列黑皮肤的姐妹们慢悠悠地穿行在枫树庄的厨房里，如今再不必在心头对她们连推带搡地暗暗使劲了。

枫树庄正在翻修改建。玛吉再不走那一段路，可人们会讲给她听。

倘若不是斯皮尔家把宅子拾掇得越发美了，倒还好受些。新砌的围墙花园；英国来的长距耧斗菜；茶玫瑰，带着落日、蜜桃和奶油一般融融冶冶的颜色；几个小小的铅铸男孩头顶着日晷，比宅子上空轻拂着天的参天巨树还要古老得多；斯皮尔太太的工具房，活像德国童话里树林中的小石屋，里面是玛吉一心想要的全套家什，手柄都漆成苔绿色；还有梨树下的带棚座椅，花瓣落在男管家和听差身上——他们正铺开蕾丝桌布，端出银茶具和一碟碟纸一样薄的黄油面包，或是热腾腾的烤饼，化着黄油，也化着温柔。倘若斯皮尔家追求的是德特韦勒太太那一路花生脆糖式的石头院墙、一圈圈的美人蕉圆花坛，倒还好忍受些。

===Original===
Mrs. Spear was always sweet about giving the Campions the credit when people praised her garden, but to her friends she said:
“Well, of course, my dear, it *was* a *sweet* old place; but really and truly, the *condition* it was in﻿—! Did you ever *see* it before we bought it?”
No matter what changes they made, Maggie thought, she could keep The Maples unchanged in her heart. When her home had been hers, she had never been able to hold it﻿—the leaves fell, sunsets faded, darkness drank up the river, everything changed and passed like flowing water. But now that she had lost it it was hers forever, immortal.

===Chinese===
人家夸赞花园的时候，斯皮尔太太总甜甜地把功劳让给坎皮恩家，可是背地里她对朋友们说：

「唉，当然啦，亲爱的，这儿*本来*是座*可爱*的老宅子；可是说实在的，我们买下的时候它那个*状况*哟——！你*见过*我们买它之前的模样吗？」

随便他们怎么改吧，玛吉想，她都能把枫树庄原封不动地存在心里。家还归她的时候，她从来没能留住它——叶子落了，晚霞褪了，黑暗饮干了河水，一切都变了、过去了，像流水一样。可如今她失去了它，它反倒永远归她了，不朽了。

===Original===
Lily wanted dreadfully to go to the housewarming the Spears gave, when all the changes had been made. “It would hurt their feelings so if none of us came,” she thought. And she did want to see what they had done to the house, and then there were sure to be wonderful refreshments. Of course, *if* she went she wouldn’t mention it to Maggie, who would rather die than go, she knew, and who couldn’t stand the Spears just because they had bought The Maples.
“*If* I go﻿—” Lily said to herself, up to the time when she put on her hat and her new dotted veil﻿—goodness, how stiff and scratchy! And then with her heart thumping she crept downstairs and out of doors, while Maggie, with her head tied up in a dustcloth and her mouth full of tacks, was covering the old schoolroom sofa with a pair of the parlor curtains, too busy to notice what anyone else was doing.
They had taken away the fountain with its iron calla-lilies﻿—how queer! All it needed was a little paint, and one broken lily fixed. But there was another fountain, a new one, in the garden, white against the hemlock hedge, a slender column splintering into rainbows, veiling itself in thin crystal.
Sunlight drenched through the trees, the red tulips were sun-filled cups﻿—Holy Grails at a garden party. She had a new hat, bought secretly in Wilmington. “It’s my own money!” she told herself, but she knew she ought to save it for the dentist. It was a sort of peach-basket, only it had grapes on it instead of peaches. She had tried on almost every hat in the shop before she found it, and her mild deliberation nearly drove the shopgirl mad. “You’ve got to remember you have a stout face, dear,” she said, plunging hat after hat on Lily’s head, bending down an ear. “That’s why you don’t think they look good on you, but the hats themselves are really lovely; I wouldn’t tell you so if they weren’t.” But Lily loved this one﻿—white grapes that looked good enough to eat. Their light tapping cheered her now, she put up her hand from time to time to feel them. But even with the new hat, even though she had enlivened her old brown silk dress with pectoral fins of brown chiffon, and cleaned her white gloves, among the other women in their frilly flower-colored organdies and muslins, she felt like a hop-toad in a petunia bed. She almost wished she hadn’t come﻿—but the *café frappé* was delicious, and an orchestra, under a striped red and white awning over near the beech tree, was playing that lovely new waltz from “The Merry Widow” that Victor was always whistling. And then everyone was being so kind.

===Chinese===
斯皮尔家把一切改造停当之后，开了场乔迁聚会，莉莉恨不得去。「要是咱们家一个人也不露面，多伤他们的感情呀。」她想。她也实在想看看他们把宅子弄成了什么样，何况准有可口的茶点。当然，*要是*去，她不会跟玛吉提一个字——她知道玛吉宁死也不肯去；就因为斯皮尔家买了枫树庄，玛吉就见不得他们。

「*要是我去*——」莉莉一路这么对自己说，一直说到戴上帽子、蒙上那块新的点纹面纱的时候——天哪，又硬又扎人！随后她心口怦怦直跳，蹑手蹑脚下得楼来、出了门；那边的玛吉，头上包着掸灰布，嘴里衔着图钉，正拿一对客厅窗帘给旧书房的沙发蒙面，忙得顾不上理会旁人在做什么。

他们把那座铁马蹄莲的旧喷泉拆走了——多奇怪！它分明只需刷一道漆、再修好一枝断了的莲花就成。可花园里另有一座喷泉，新的，白白的衬着铁杉树篱，一柱纤细的水流迸碎成道道彩虹，给自己蒙上薄薄一层水晶纱。

阳光透过树木倾泻而下，红郁金香是斟满了阳光的杯盏——游园会上的圣杯。她有顶新帽子，是在威尔明顿偷偷买的。「这是我自己的钱！」她对自己说，可她知道这钱本该留着看牙。那是顶桃篮帽，只是上头缀的不是桃子，是葡萄。她几乎试遍了店里的每一顶帽子才寻着它，她那慢吞吞的挑拣差点儿把女店员逼疯。「你得记住你是张胖脸儿，亲爱的，」女店员说着，把一顶顶帽子往莉莉头上扣，压弯一只耳朵。「所以你才觉得它们戴着不好看，其实这些帽子本身真是漂亮；要是不漂亮，我不会这么说的。」可莉莉偏爱这一顶——上头的白葡萄看着就好想咬一口。这会儿那些葡萄轻轻磕碰着，她心里一阵受用，不时抬起手去摸一摸。可就算有了新帽子，就算她给那件旧棕绸裙装点了两片棕色雪纺的「胸鳍」，又把手套洗得干干净净，夹在满园花团锦簇的太太小姐中间——人家穿的是皱边蝉翼纱、细洋纱，一色花草般娇嫩的颜色——她自觉活像矮牵牛花坛里的一只癞蛤蟆。她几乎要后悔来了——可那*冰镇咖啡*（café frappé）实在好喝，山毛榉树旁红白条纹凉棚底下，一支乐队正奏着《风流寡妇》（The Merry Widow）里那支好听的新圆舞曲，就是维克托总挂在嘴边吹的那支。何况人人又都那么亲热客气。

===Original===
“Sweet of you to come,” Mrs. Spear murmured, her eyes wandering absently while she clung to Lily’s hand. “*Do* you like our little changes? Of course, really *you* did the whole thing﻿—aren’t the tulips heavenly, if I do say so myself? You know my little Dorothy, don’t you? Just think, Dotty, Miss Lily used to live here when she was a little girl like you, and maybe some day she’ll tell you all about what she used to do then﻿—won’t that be *exciting*?”
“Would you like to hear all about what I did when I was a little girl like you, darling?” Lily asked in a voice dripping with honey.
“No-o.”
“Dorothy! If you can’t speak politely to Mother’s friends, you can go up to Fraulein! Now stop kicking the grass with your pretty new slippers﻿—*Dorothy*﻿—and take Miss Lily’s hand and show her where the refreshments are, that’s Mother’s big girl!”
So Lily had some more *café frappé*. And while she was eating it she gave such a jump that she spilled a spoonful down her front. There was Victor! She was pretty sure he didn’t want her to see him; she knew she didn’t want him to see her. And it made her sad to see him there, a guest in the home that should have been his own.

===Chinese===
「你能来，真好。」斯皮尔太太喃喃地说，一边紧握着莉莉的手，眼睛却心不在焉地四处飘。「你*到底*喜欢不喜欢我们这些小改动？当然啦，这一切其实还都是*你们*的功劳——那些郁金香，可不是我自夸，是不是美上天了？你认识我们的小多萝西（Dorothy），对不对？多蒂，你想想，莉莉小姐从前就住在这儿，那时她跟你一样是个小姑娘，说不定哪天她会讲给你听，她那时候都干些什么——那该多*带劲*呀？」

「你想不想听听，我像你这么大的时候都干过些什么呀，宝贝儿？」莉莉用浸透了蜜糖的声音问。

「不——想。」

「多萝西！再这么没规矩地跟妈妈的朋友说话，你就上楼找弗罗莱因（Fraulein）去！别拿你那双漂亮的新鞋踢草——*多萝西*——快拉住莉莉小姐的手，领她到茶点那边去，这才是妈妈的大姑娘！」

于是莉莉又用了一份*冰镇咖啡*。正喝着，她猛地一惊，一匙咖啡泼在了前襟上。维克托在那儿！她十拿九稳，他不愿让她瞧见；她也知道自己不愿让他瞧见自己。看着他站在那儿，在本该属于他自己的家里做客人，她心里一酸。

===Original===
She puffed off to the house as fast as she could go. People were swarming like bees in a hive. “Just poke around,” urged Mrs. Spear, hurrying past. “See what we’ve done upstairs﻿—”
She didn’t want to look into the bathroom, but something pushed her, pulled her. How changed﻿—it was a blue and silver fairy tale of a bathroom now! The crackling copper-lined tub was gone, and a white porcelain one was in its place. And here was the room where she and Maggie and May used to sleep when they were small. The place where Victor did the decalcomania goose, upside down to make it funnier, all the dark brown wood, was painted over, the walls and soft chairs were pink, the bed and dressing table were all pink ruffles. She was like a bee in the heart of a rose. How changed﻿—
The white fur rugs, the pink walls, blurred and faded. Three little girls in round combs and striped stockings played together in the pool of yellow sunlight on the floor. Her mouth jerked, she fumbled for her handkerchief, but they did not look at her as she stood in the doorway.

===Chinese===
她鼓足劲儿，尽快朝宅子那边挪。人们密密麻麻，像蜂巢里的蜜蜂。「随便到处转转。」斯皮尔太太匆匆走过时招呼她。「上楼瞧瞧我们改了些什么——」

她本不想朝浴室里张望，可有什么东西推着她、拽着她。变了样了——如今是一间蓝白二色的童话浴室！那口噼啪作响的铜衬浴缸搬走了，原处换上了一只白瓷的。这是她和玛吉、梅小时候同睡的房间。维克托贴贴花画那只鹅——头朝下贴着，好更逗乐——的旧地界，连同满屋深棕色的木器，全给漆盖住了；墙壁和软椅是粉红的，床和梳妆台缀满粉红的褶边。她像钻进玫瑰花心的一只蜜蜂。变了多少——

白毛皮小毯、粉红的墙壁，模糊了，淡去了。三个戴圆梳、穿条纹长袜的小女孩，在地板上那一汪黄色阳光里一起玩耍。她的嘴角抽动了一下，慌忙去摸手绢，可她站在门口，她们并不朝她看。

## XXIX / XXIX

===Original===
Fannie Page tilted the side of her face to her husband’s casual kiss, as she went on reading her letters. “Frances says little Francie’s been chosen to be Wild Rose in the school play,” she told him across his fence of newspaper. “She’s making her a costume of pink and green crêpe paper﻿—seems to me *studying’s* the last thing they think of in school nowadays. Elizabeth says as far as she can see the twins never do anything but model in clay and sing folk-songs. Let’s see. Both Freddie’s front teeth are out. Poor little fellow, how funny he must look! What’s the matter with your canteloupe, dear? Isn’t it good? I *told* that man﻿—”
“Where’s Pren?”
“He had breakfast earlier. He’s going to Springfield with the Bangs boys, I only hope they don’t break down and have to be towed home again. I told him I’d tell you he’d taken the automobile, I knew you wouldn’t mind.”
“Well, I do mind.”
“No, you don’t.” She heaved her violet billows out of her chair, and came around to kiss the bald place on the top of his head. She must remember to get him some more hair tonic﻿—not that it seemed to do much good.
“Have another cup of coffee﻿—of *course*, it won’t hurt you. Mary, ask Ella for some hot waffles.”

===Chinese===
范妮·佩奇（Fannie Page）把半边脸侧过去，领受丈夫漫不经心的一吻，手上照旧读着信。「弗朗西丝（Frances）说，小弗朗西（Francie）在学校演戏里选中了扮野玫瑰。」她隔着丈夫那道报纸扎成的篱笆告诉他。「她正用粉绿两色的绉纸给她做戏装呢——依我看，如今学校里*念书*这档子事，是他们顶顶想不到的。伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）说，照她瞧见的，那对双生子除了捏泥巴、唱民歌，旁的什么都不干。我瞧瞧。弗雷迪（Freddie）两颗门牙都掉了。可怜的小家伙，那模样一定逗死了！你的香瓜怎么了，亲爱的？不好吃么？我*跟*那个卖瓜的说过——」

「普伦呢？」

「他早饭吃得早。他要跟邦斯家的男孩们上斯普林菲尔德（Springfield）去，但愿别再半道抛锚，让人拖着车送回家来。我跟他讲好了，由我来告诉你他开了汽车去，我知道你不会介意。」

「我还就是介意。」

「不，你不。」她鼓荡起一身紫罗兰色的波涛，从椅子里挣出身来，绕到丈夫跟前，吻了吻他头顶那块光溜溜的地方。她得记着再给他添几瓶生发油——虽说那东西似乎没多大用处。

「再来一杯咖啡——*当然*不妨事，伤不着你。玛丽（Mary），去请埃拉（Ella）上些热华夫饼。」

===Original===
“Here’s about my speech at the banquet, Fannie.”
“Mmm﻿—”
“Well, if you aren’t interested﻿—”
“What? Oh, darling, I *am*, *intensely* interested!”
“What in?” he asked suspiciously.

===Chinese===
「这儿是我在宴会上那篇演讲，范妮。」

「唔——」

「哼，你要是不感兴趣——」

「什么？哦，亲爱的，我*是*感兴趣呀，*热烈*得很！」

「对什么感兴趣？」他狐疑地问。

===Original===
“Well, to tell you the truth, Prentice, I wasn’t listening, I was reading a letter from Margery, and *guess* who she’s met! Victor Campion! Did you ever? Listen﻿—
“ ‘Betty and I went to a dinner before a dance at the Century Club, I wore my candleshade﻿—’ ”
“Her what?”
“Oh, her pink dress with the crystal fringe﻿—let’s see﻿—‘and split the skirt again﻿—’
“*Tc!* Why *will* they try to dance in hobble skirts? Wait, where is it that she tells about Victor?
“ ‘I had something aged but sprightly on one side of me, named Mr. Victor Campion. When I say sprightly, I mean he’s the kind that’s still begging for a rosebud; and one of the other men told me at the dance that after dinner he told them the world’s mildest limerick about a split skirt that he evidently considered absolutely devilish. He was pretty old for the party, but Betty says everybody uses him for filling in, though the debutantes groan when they get next him; and, of course, he’s *not* exactly thrilling. She says he’s a regular town institution, you haven’t properly come out until Mr. Campion comes to your tea and says ‘What charming blossoms and what still more charming buds!’ You’ll see he really *is* old when I tell you he says he knew you when you were a girl﻿—*Well!*”

===Chinese===
「唉，实话跟你说吧，普伦蒂斯（Prentice），我方才没在听，我在读玛杰丽（Margery）的信——*猜猜*她碰上谁了！维克托·坎皮恩（Victor Campion）！你说稀奇不稀奇？听着——

「『贝蒂（Betty）和我去世纪俱乐部（the Century Club）赴宴，完了跳舞，我穿了我那条烛罩色（candleshade）——』」

「她的什么色？」

「哦，就是她那条缀水晶流苏的粉裙子呀——我瞧瞧——『又把裙衩绷开了——』」

「*啧！*她们干吗*偏要*穿窄底裙去跳舞呢？等等，她讲到维克托的地方在哪儿——」

「『我身旁坐了一位上了年纪却还挺活泛的，名叫维克托·坎皮恩先生。我说活泛，是指他至今还是那种追着人讨一朵玫瑰花苞的主儿；舞会上另一位男客告诉我，饭罢他讲了一支说开衩裙的打油诗给他们听——天底下最斯文的一支——他分明还当它放肆透顶呢。在那一场合里，他算是老得很了，不过贝蒂说，人人都拿他补空缺，虽然初入社交界的姑娘们挨着他坐就叫苦连天；当然啦，他也*实在*说不上叫人心动。她说他是本城公认的一件老古董，坎皮恩先生不亲临你的茶会、道一声「多么迷人的花朵，还有更迷人的花苞呀」，你就不算正式出来过。等我告诉你，他竟说他认识你的时候你还是个小姑娘——*好家伙！*』」

===Original===
“A polite daughter you have!”
“Now, Prentice, of course, we seem a thousand to those young things﻿—just a lit‑tle *bit* of egg on your chin, darling!
“ ‘He seemed inclined to give me a rush at the dance﻿—and, oh, Mother, his idea of the Boston! Hop is no word for it! He’s *much* worse than Daddy. But a perfect *peach* from Princeton who was on my other side came to my rescue﻿—’

===Chinese===
「你养的好一位懂礼貌的女儿！」

「好了，普伦蒂斯，在那些年轻东西眼里，咱们可不就跟一千岁的人似的——你下巴上沾了小小*一丁点儿*鸡蛋，亲爱的！

「『舞会上他倒像有几分要追我的意思——还有，哦，妈妈，他心里那个波士顿舞哟！岂是「蹦跶」二字了得！比爸爸*还*要糟。亏得我另一边坐了位普林斯顿来的十足*可人儿*，救了我——』」

===Original===
“Prentice, why do you suppose Victor Campion never married?”
“Oh, I don’t know. He was too much of a spoiled baby, I guess, first his mother and then his sisters, always protecting him and admiring him until he was too tender to do anything but run home and hide, when anything real started to happen to him. And then he was supposed to be pretty much cut up over Lucy Hawthorn, wasn’t he?”
“Yes, I guess he was; but mercy, that was ages ago, and she certainly wasn’t the only pebble on the beach. And anyway, no man’s going to be faithful to one woman all his life if she’s out of reach﻿—you needn’t tell me! Women do, but not men﻿—it isn’t their nature. No, I guess he just wasn’t the marrying kind, or else as you say they made him too comfortable at home.”

===Chinese===
「普伦蒂斯，你说维克托·坎皮恩怎么会一直没结婚？」

「哦，我说不上来。我想他是太给惯坏了，十足一个娇宝贝，起先是他母亲，后来是他妹妹们，总护着他、夸着他，把他护得太娇嫩，真有什么事儿落到他头上，他只会往家里跑、往里头躲。再说，当初他为露西·霍桑（Lucy Hawthorn）不是也伤得不轻么？」

「是啊，我想是伤着来着；可是老天，那是八百年前的事了，她也绝不是海滩上独一份的卵石。再说了，一个女人反正够不着边儿，没有哪个男人会对她守一辈子——这你不用跟我说！女人守得住，男人守不住——不是他们的天性。不，我想他天生就不是结婚的那号人，要不就像你说的，家里人把他伺候得太舒坦了。」

===Original===
Prentice made a vague conversational sound, his eyes straying to his newspaper, but Fannie went on, interested:
“You know it isn’t only Victor himself, but I believe the girls wouldn’t have been old maids if it hadn’t been for him. Maggie never said so, but everyone knew she broke her engagement so she could stay and take care of him; and they never could go anywhere or have pretty clothes or anything, because everything had to be spent on Victor. You can’t exactly blame him for being selfish, they never gave him a chance to be anything else. Did they? Did they, Prentice?”
“Mm,” said Prentice, reading.
“Mother always said Mrs. Campion would have married again if it hadn’t been for Victor. I remember there was someone. Mrs. Campion came to see Mother one day, and cried and said Victor was so sensitive he needed especially tender treatment, and Father said afterwards he needed a hairbrush applied to the seat of his trousers.”
“That’s about right.”

===Chinese===
普伦蒂斯含含糊糊哼了一声，眼睛早飘回他的报纸上去了，范妮却兴致勃勃，只管往下说：

「你知道，不光是维克托他自己，我总觉得，那姐妹几个要不是为了他，也不至于都当一辈子老姑娘。玛吉自己从没这么说过，可谁都知道，她就是为了留下来照顾他，才把婚约退了的；她们哪儿也去不成，漂亮衣裳什么的一件也没有，因为什么都得花在维克托身上。他自私，倒也真怪不得他，她们压根儿没给过他机会做第二种人。是不是？是不是这样，普伦蒂斯？」

「唔。」普伦蒂斯读着报，应了一声。

「妈妈生前总说，坎皮恩太太要不是为了维克托，本来是会再嫁的。我记得是有那么一个人。有一天坎皮恩太太来看妈妈，哭着说维克托太敏感，得格外温存地待他；爸爸事后说，他需要的是拿一把发刷，往他裤子的后裆上好好招呼招呼。」

「这话差不离。」

===Original===
“Well, it seems queer to think of such a mild little man as Victor Campion having any influence on anybody. But I can’t help feeling sorry for him. An old bachelor seems so sad, somehow. Sort of forlorn. And can you imagine﻿—even imagine life without any children, Prentice?”
“I can easily, and it sounds like a paradise of peace.”
“Oh, go on! Skidoo!” she cried, slapping him affectionately.
“Fannie! What language!”
“Well, it’s what Pren and Bobby say all the time﻿—‘twenty-three, skidoo.’ ” She returned his goodbye kiss, as she put a pansy in his buttonhole, and then went back for just one more waffle with butter and maple syrup. She knew she oughtn’t to eat waffles, she was so fat. Elizabeth was always telling her so. “You mustn’t let yourself go to seed, Mother.” Oh, well! She poured out just a little more coffee, with three lumps and cream.

===Chinese===
「唉，想想维克托·坎皮恩这么个温吞和气的小个子男人，居然能左右什么人的命数，总觉得挺蹊跷。可我就是忍不住替他难过。一个老光棍，不知怎么的，总显得那么凄凉。孤苦伶仃的。你能想象吗——哪怕光是想象一下，一辈子没有孩子，普伦蒂斯？」

「我随便就想象得出来，而且听着简直像太平无事的乐园。」

「哦，去你的！二十三，快走人！」她嚷道，亲亲热热拍了他一下。

「范妮！这算什么话！」

「还不就是普伦和博比（Bobby）成天挂在嘴上的——『二十三，快走人（twenty-three, skidoo）。』」她回了他一个道别的吻，往他扣眼儿里别上一朵三色堇，然后又回去添了一只华夫饼，抹上黄油，浇上枫糖浆。她知道自己不该再吃华夫饼了，她都这么胖了。伊丽莎白总这么劝她。「妈妈，你可不能由着自己花谢结籽呀。」唉，罢了罢了！她又给自己斟了点儿咖啡，搁上三块方糖，兑点奶油。

===Original===
Victor Campion! Poor old thing! She was sorry for the girls, too. Isabel Leaf said they had had a dreadful time, with May’s suicide, and being poor, and having to sell The Maples. The Maples without the Campions, the Campions without The Maples﻿—impossible to imagine! “We used to have such fun there!” she thought. “Poor old Maggie! It would be nice to ask her to Hartford for a visit﻿—give her a good rest, and feed her up.” She would do it sometime﻿—or at least she would if ever a time came when she wasn’t too occupied with her family. The family came first, of course.
Poor Maggie! Poor Lily! Poor Victor! Never to marry, never to have children and grandchildren﻿—how awful! No one to love. Perhaps, she had gone to seed, as Elizabeth said, but what did it matter, when such fresh young flowers were springing up so thickly around her?
Her thoughts floated from the Campions to little Francie in her wild rose costume﻿—to the dress she was embroidering for Elizabeth’s baby﻿—the day’s menus﻿—Pren would be bringing the Bangs boys home for dinner﻿—Bobby back from Andover tomorrow﻿—the sunshine wrapping her feet in soft warm gold﻿—the pot of pansies on the breakfast table. One was dead, and in her mind she stretched out her hand and pinched it off, but her body was too comfortable to move. She sank into an agreeable torpor.

===Chinese===
维克托·坎皮恩！可怜的老家伙！她也替那姐妹几个难过。伊莎贝尔·利夫（Isabel Leaf）说，她们熬过了一段可怕的日子：梅寻了短见，家里又穷，末了连枫树庄也只得卖掉。枫树庄里没了坎皮恩家，坎皮恩家没了枫树庄——没法想象！「我们从前在那儿玩得多开心呀！」她想。「可怜的老玛吉！请她上哈特福德（Hartford）来住些日子倒好——让她好好歇一歇，补养补养。」改天她一定请——或者说，只要有一天她能从自家人堆里腾出身来，她就请。当然喽，自家人永远是头一位。

可怜的玛吉！可怜的莉莉！可怜的维克托！一辈子不结婚，一辈子没有儿孙——多可怕呀！没有个可疼爱的人。就算她真如伊丽莎白说的，花谢结籽了，又有什么打紧？她身边这么鲜嫩水灵的年轻花朵，正密密匝匝一个劲儿往上冒呢。

她的思绪从坎皮恩家飘开去，飘到穿野玫瑰戏装的小弗朗西——飘到她正给伊丽莎白的宝宝绣的那条小裙子——今天的三餐——普伦要带邦斯家的男孩们回家吃晚饭——博比明天从安多弗（Andover）回来——阳光把她的一双脚裹在软乎乎、暖烘烘的金子里——早餐桌上一盆三色堇。有一朵已经谢了，她在心里伸出手去，想把它掐掉，可身子实在舒服，懒得动弹。她沉进了一种适意的昏沉里。

---

===Original===
Victor lay in his bath, dreamily blowing bubbles. Nice to sleep late and dawdle over dressing on Sunday morning. “ ‘Oh, waltz me around again, Willie.’ ” he sang, making a beautiful lather.

> “ ‘Around, around, around!
 

 The music so dreamy, like peaches and creamy﻿—’ ”

He was going to do exercises every morning﻿—that was the way to keep fit. Sunday was the day to be lazy, but he’d start tomorrow. Twenty minutes brisk exercises every morning﻿—what if it did mean getting up a little earlier? The very thought made him feel as glowing and strong-willed as if he had gone through them already.

===Chinese===
维克托泡在浴缸里，悠悠地吹着泡泡。星期天早晨能睡个懒觉，又能磨磨蹭蹭地穿衣裳，真好。他一边搓出漂亮的皂沫，一边唱起来：「『哦，再搂着我转一圈圆舞吧，威利（Willie）。』」

> 「『转啊，转啊，转啊！
> 乐声这般如梦，像蜜桃儿和奶油——』」

他打定主意，往后每天早晨都做体操——这才是强身的正道。星期天本是偷懒的日子，可明天他就开工。每天早晨二十分钟爽利的体操——就算得稍微早起一忽儿，又何妨？单这么一想，他便觉得浑身发光、意志坚毅，倒像已经做了一遍似的。

===Original===
> “ ‘Oh, Willie Fitzgibbons he used to sell ribbons,
 

 And stand up all day on his feet﻿—’ ”

Where was his blue tie with the cream-colored dots?

> “ ‘He got very spoony on Madeleine Mooney﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「『哦，威利·菲茨吉本斯（Willie Fitzgibbons）从前是卖缎带的，
> 成天到晚，站定一双脚——』」

他那条带奶油色圆点儿的蓝领带，搁到哪儿去了？

> 「『他一头痴上了马德琳·穆尼（Madeleine Mooney）——』」

===Original===
Last night he had come home old and tired. The dinner had been hard work. He had told all his funniest stories, and waited with an ear anxiously cocked for the laughter that did not come. Polite, vague smiles﻿—“Oh, that’s *perfect*!” And afterwards, at the dance! No one really danced any more.
But at home again, where all things﻿—the burning lamp, the doughnuts and milk waiting under a napkin on the hall table, his turned-back bed, light, and food, and rest﻿—were symbols of love for him, he revived, he grew happy again.

===Chinese===
昨儿夜里，他回到家里时，又老又乏。那顿晚宴真是苦差。他把压箱底的笑话讲了一个遍，支棱着耳朵焦心地等那阵笑声，笑声却迟迟不来。只有客客气气、心不在焉的微笑——「哦，真*妙*极了！」还有后头的舞会呢！如今压根儿没有人真跳舞了。

可一回到家里——在那儿，样样东西：亮着的灯，餐巾底下搁在门厅桌上等他的炸面圈和牛奶，替他翻折好被角的床，光、吃食、安歇——于他都是爱的表征——他便缓了过来，又快活起来了。

===Original===
Maggie was watering her plants in the bay window. A rubber bulb sprinkler filled itself﻿—blub-blub-blub﻿—in a pail of warm water standing on the newspapers spread on the floor under the dripping fringe of ivy, and the room was full of sunshine and the fragrance of sprinkled geraniums.
“You sound as lively as a cricket. Have a good time last night? Wait, I’ll bring in your breakfast﻿—”
She loved Sunday mornings, when she could let him sleep late, when he had time to talk with her as he lingered over his breakfast. She made him a brown and gold puff of omelet while he was eating his orange, and brought it in to him still sputtering and heaving.

===Chinese===
玛吉正在凸窗里给她的花草浇水。一只橡皮球喷壶浸在一桶温水里，咕噜、咕噜、咕噜，自己灌着水——水桶立在地板上铺开的报纸上，正顶着滴水的常春藤垂帘——满屋子都是阳光，和洒过水的天竺葵的清香。

「你听着可欢实了，活像只蟋蟀。昨儿晚上玩得好吗？等着，我把你的早饭端进来——」

她顶喜欢星期天早晨，这样的早晨她可以由着他睡懒觉，由着他磨着早饭慢慢吃、有工夫陪她说话。趁他吃橙子的当儿，她给他摊了一份褐中透金、蓬松喧软的蛋饼，端进去时还滋滋作响、一鼓一鼓地颤。

===Original===
“Guess who I sat next at dinner﻿—Fannie Leaf’s daughter, the youngest one. She’s visiting Robert and Isabel.”
“Fannie Leaf’s daughter! Well, I never! What’s she like?”
“A pretty little thing﻿—looks the way Fannie used to, pink cheeks and dimples and curly light hair.”
“Well, wasn’t that nice for her, to sit next you and have you know her mother and father and everything﻿—here, let me give you some more coffee. Did she give you any news of Fannie and Prentice?”

===Chinese===
「猜猜昨儿晚宴上我旁边坐的是谁——范妮·利夫（Fannie Leaf）的女儿，顶小的那一个。她这阵子住在罗伯特（Robert）和伊莎贝尔家里。」

「范妮·利夫的女儿！哎呀，真是没想到！她长什么样儿？」

「俊俏的一个小东西——长得跟范妮从前一个模子，粉扑扑的脸蛋儿，笑起来有酒窝，一头浅色鬈发。」

「哎呀，那她多好呀，挨着你坐，你连她爸爸她妈妈、连她家的事一样样全认得——来，我再给你添点儿咖啡。她讲了范妮和普伦蒂斯的什么消息没有？」

===Original===
“Some new grandchildren since last we heard. A grandmother! Poor old Fannie, I bet she doesn’t like that. Makes her sound pretty old, don’t it?”
“It don’t seem possible. I always think of her the way she was on her wedding day, so slender and laughing﻿—the prettiest bride I ever saw, I think, and the youngest looking. But Isabel Leaf says she’s aged awfully, and gotten so fat and settled﻿—what do you want? Matches? Wait a minute, I’ll get them for you. Well, I can’t get over how nice it was for her having you for a partner!”
“She’s not much of a dancer, though. We didn’t get on very well together.”
“Well, if she couldn’t dance with you, she couldn’t dance with anyone.”

===Chinese===
「打上回听信儿以来，又添了几个孙儿孙女。她都当上外婆了！可怜的老范妮，我敢说她准不乐意。这听着多显老，可不是么？」

「简直叫人不敢信。我总记着她做新娘子那天的模样儿，那么苗条，笑吟吟的——我想那是我见过的顶好看的新娘，也顶显年轻。可伊莎贝尔·利夫说她老得吓人，发了福，定了形——你要什么？火柴？等一等，我给你拿去。唉，她能有你做舞伴，真真是好福气，我想起来都替她高兴！」

「不过她跳舞可不怎么了起。我们俩处得不大来。」

「唉，她要是跟你都跳不来，跟谁也跳不来了。」

===Original===
He went happily into the living-room, to the sunshine and the Sunday papers. Last night he had been hurt by Margery Page’s straying attention, vague answers, and frequent stiffenings of the jaw that meant yawns suppressed. She had had plenty to say to the Princeton sophomore on her other side, plenty of things to giggle about. And although she and Victor certainly had not gotten on very well when they were dancing together, he had seen her swooping and dipping almost professionally with other partners. But no matter what suspicion stole on him in the outside world that he was not as young as he used to be, that he was not as fascinating as he wanted to be, at home where they loved and admired him so he found the Fountain of Youth.
He hunted out the funny papers, and lit a fresh cigarette. She had been as pretty as a picture, with all those yellow curls. Perhaps, she had just been shy with an older man, “A man of the world,” he thought rather complacently. He hadn’t anything especial to do today﻿—he might go in to Wilmington after lunch and call on her. It would be polite, and she would be pleased.

===Chinese===
他欢欢喜喜走进起居室，走到阳光和星期天的报纸跟前。昨儿夜里，玛杰丽·佩奇（Margery Page）那副四处飘忽的心思、含含糊糊的答话，还有频频绷紧的腮帮子——那是强忍住的呵欠——都叫他心里受伤。她跟她另一边那位普林斯顿的大二学生倒有的是话说，有的是可笑着意的玩意儿。虽说她跟维克托俩一块儿跳舞时委实也没跳到来，可他分明瞧见她跟别的舞伴回旋俯仰，几乎赶上行家里手。不过，任凭外头的世界怎样悄悄叫他疑心——疑心自己不如从前年轻了、不如自己指望的那么招人喜欢了——一回到家里，回到这人人都疼他、人人夸他的地方，他便寻见了青春之泉。

他翻出滑稽连环画版，点上一支新烟。她那满头黄鬈发，俊得跟画儿上下来似的。兴许她不过是跟上了年纪的男人坐在一起，害臊罢了，「到底是位见过世面的男人嘛」，他颇为自得地想道。他今天没什么特别要紧的事——午饭后不妨进城去威尔明顿（Wilmington）走一趟，拜望拜望她。这是礼貌，她也一定欢喜。

## XXX / XXX

===Original===
War swept the world. “Oh, if *only* I could go over and drive an ambulance!” Maggie thought, longing to be in the thick of things, longing to fling out her life in service in the mud, under the star-shells, and having to be content with knitting socks and going without sugar. Lily couldn’t manage socks, but she knitted sweaters﻿—sweaters for giants, vast, enormous, tiny sweaters for brownies. And yet the directions were always the same﻿—she couldn’t understand why they turned out so different, or why they had so many openwork places. “More holy than righteous,” said Maggie, picking up Lily’s dropped stitches. Lily wanted to fasten little cheering notes to the sweaters, but she was too shy. She talked all the time about “Our Boys,” and sang, with pleasurable tears in her eyes:

> “ ‘There’s a long long trail a-winding
 

 To the land of my dreams,
 

 Where the nightingale is singing,
 

 And a white moon beams﻿—’ ”

She thought it was the sweetest song she had ever heard. It was dashing to have Maggie say one day,

“I don’t think so much of that song﻿—or maybe it’s the way you’re singing it?”

Victor was on committees for raising money, for giving patriotic balls, for arranging benefit performances. He had never been so important and busy in his life, and when peace came he was a pricked balloon.

===Chinese===
战争席卷了整个世界。「唉，我*要是*能上前线去开救护车就好了！」玛吉想。她渴望置身事发的漩涡中心，恨不得把这条性命豁出去效力——在泥泞里，在照明弹的星光下——却只得织织袜子、戒着糖不吃，聊以自安。莉莉的袜子织不来，就织毛衣——给巨人织的毛衣，一件比一件宽大、浩瀚；还有给棕仙（brownies）织的小不点儿毛衣。可说明书偏偏回回一个样——她弄不懂织出来怎么回回不相同，也弄不懂哪儿来这许多镂空的花样。「圣洁倒是圣洁——窟窿百出。」玛吉说，一面替莉莉拾起漏掉的针脚。莉莉想在毛衣上别几张鼓劲的小条子，又太害羞。她成天「我们的孩子兵」长「我们的孩子兵」短，还噙着欢喜的泪花唱：

> 「『有一条长长的路啊，弯弯蜒蜒，
> 蜿蜒到我梦里的家园，
> 那儿夜莺正在歌唱，
> 一轮白月洒着清辉——』」

她觉得这是自己平生听过的顶甜美的一支歌。所以有一天玛吉来了那么一句——那真是兜头一瓢凉水：

「我看这支歌不怎么样——要不，兴许是你唱的那味儿不行？」

维克托挂上了一连串委员的头衔——筹款的委员会，举办爱国舞会的委员会，张罗义演的委员会。他有生以来从没这么举足轻重、这么忙碌过；等和平一到，他成了只泄了气的气球。

---

===Original===
Saturday Market in Wilmington! The farmers’ wagons were pulled up along the curb, chickens and eggs, butter, and boxes of blackberries, and tight bunches of red and yellow flowers were spread out for sale. Women with baskets on their arms priced, bargained, tasted, stopped to gossip in midstream.

“What you asking for peaches? Kinda green, ain’t they?”

“Let’s try a piece of your cheese﻿—”

Rich women, poor women, white women, black women, filling their market baskets﻿—sea green cabbages, limp-necked poultry, a little wooden boat of cottage-cheese, a fist of yellow banana fingers, sometimes for the spirit’s sake a bunch of marigolds and bee-balm, solid as worsted work.

Maggie shifted her heavy basket from arm to arm. A couple of canteloupes, and then she could go over to the library and rest until it was time to catch the car for home.

===Chinese===
威尔明顿（Wilmington）的周六集市！农夫的板车一辆挨一辆停在路缘，鸡呀蛋呀、黄油呀、一盒盒黑莓，还有扎得紧紧的红花黄花，摊开待售。挎着篮子的女人们问价、还价、尝味儿，话说半截又停下脚来唠闲嗑。

「你这桃子要价多少？还青着呢，可不？」

「尝一块你家的奶酪——」

阔太太、穷女人，白人妇女、黑人妇女，都在往菜篮里装货——海绿色的卷心菜，脖颈耷拉的宰禽，一小木舟里盛的农家鲜酪，一小把黄澄澄的指头似的香蕉，有时为着叫心里欢喜，再添一束万寿菊和香蜂草，像绒线绣的活计那样瓷实。

玛吉把沉甸甸的篮子从一只胳膊挪到另一只胳膊。再买两个甜瓜，她就可以上图书馆去歇歇脚，等着赶电车回家。

===Original===
While she was pressing her thumb into the cantaloupes and smelling them, the pain came again, so that she could hardly stand. She set her teeth, feeling the sweat spring out on her upper lip. If she could just live through this second﻿—the next﻿—the next﻿—

The pain became the center of everything, gathered all creation into itself. Streets, houses, forests, seas, the sun and sky, concentrated in that one spot of torture. Then it ebbed away, left her. She managed to get her basket over to Market Street and up the long flight of library steps, she managed to change Lily’s library book.

Each time she thought it wouldn’t come again. But it was coming oftener, and it was worse. She faced it, sitting with her basket at her feet, turning over the pages of something﻿—“The Musical Courier.”

She was afraid to go to the doctor, that was the truth. But how silly, when probably he’d say “Don’t eat tomatoes,” or “Drink hot water,” and she’d be all right again. She had nearly fainted on the street just now﻿—she certainly would have to do something.

And she made up her mind to go to the doctor now, before she lost her courage. What doctor? She didn’t know. They hadn’t had one for so long. She would telephone Isabel Leaf and ask her who was good.

===Chinese===
她正用拇指按着甜瓜、凑上去闻的时候，那疼痛又来了，疼得她几乎站立不住。她咬紧牙关，觉出上唇沁出一层汗珠。只要能熬过这一秒——再一秒——再一秒——

疼痛成了万物的中心，把整个造化都收拢进它自身。街道、房屋、森林、海洋、太阳和天空，统统汇聚到那一点酷刑上。随后它退潮似的退走，撇下了她。她撑着把篮子挪到市场街（Market Street），挪上图书馆那一长段台阶，又撑着替莉莉换好了图书馆的书。

每回她都想，它不会再来了。可它来得越来越勤，也越来越凶。她硬着头皮受着，坐在那儿，篮子搁在脚边，随手翻着一本什么——《音乐信使》（The Musical Courier）。

实话实说，她是怕去见医生。可这有多傻呢，说不定他只会说「别吃番茄」，或者「喝点热水」，她就又好好的了。方才在街上她差点儿晕过去——看来总得想个办法了。

于是她打定主意，趁着勇气还没散，现在就去看医生。看哪一位呢？她不知道。家里已经那么久没请过医生了。她要打电话问伊莎贝尔·利夫（Isabel Leaf），问问哪位医生好。

===Original===
“Oh, I hope it’s nothing serious!” Isabel said.

“Oh, no, nothing at all, really﻿—”

“We always go to Dr. Henderson on Delaware Avenue. Why don’t you come out to lunch, and then I’ll send you down to him in the motor?”

But now that she had made up her mind she wanted to get to the doctor’s right away. It would be more restful to have some ice cream at Jones’s, afterwards, all reassured and happy. She might even go on a spree and see the Charlie Chaplin movie at the Queen, if she didn’t have to wait too long.

She felt so well by the time she got to Dr. Henderson’s that it seemed ridiculous to go in﻿—still, she was here, and she might as well. “I wish I’d asked Isabel what he charges,” she thought, with a nervous look into her purse.

The waiting room was quite full of people﻿—masked people, hiding from each other, covering themselves up, coughing now and then, or speaking in low voices, but so still most of the time that the clock sounded loud. Waiting patients, as much a part of the furnishing of the room as the sepia photographs of cathedrals on the walls, the aspidistra in its brass jardinière, the pile of *National Geographic* Magazines﻿—only showing that they were real by the frightened eyes that looked out sometimes through the slits in the masks.

Maggie looked at pictures of the fishing industry of Norway. She looked at pictures of Yellowstone Park. She looked at a copy of *Life*, reading the jokes without taking in a word. My, it was getting late! She glared suspiciously at newcomers﻿—suppose they got in ahead of her, somehow! At this rate she’d be lucky if she caught the twenty minutes to four, without stopping for anything to eat.

And then it was her turn﻿—too soon, after all! Too soon! She picked up her market basket and went in to Dr. Henderson.

===Chinese===
「哦，但愿不是什么大毛病！」伊莎贝尔说。

「哦，不是不是，压根儿没事，真的——」

「我们一向都看特拉华大道（Delaware Avenue）上的亨德森医生（Dr. Henderson）。你何不上我家来吃顿午饭，完了再用汽车送你上他那儿去？」

可她主意既定，就恨不得马上到医生那儿。回头一颗心放回肚里、欢欢喜喜地，再去琼斯冷饮店（Jones's）吃份冰淇淋，那才叫舒坦呢。要是不用等太久，她兴许还要放个纵，上女王影院（the Queen）看一场查理·卓别林（Charlie Chaplin）的电影去。

到了亨德森医生诊所门口，她又觉着好好的了，进去倒像是多此一举——不过人既然来了，也就进去吧。「早知道该问问伊莎贝尔他收费多少。」她一边想，一边提心吊胆地往钱包里瞟了一眼。

候诊室里人满满的——都是戴口罩的人，彼此躲着，把自己遮得严严实实，不时咳嗽一两声，或低声说话，多半时候静得连时钟的嘀嗒都显得响。候诊的病人们，同墙上深褐色的教堂照片、铜套盆里的蜘蛛抱蛋、那摞《国家地理》（*National Geographic*）杂志一样，都成了这屋子陈设的一部分——只是偶尔有惊惶的眼睛从口罩的缝隙里露出来，才显出他们是活人。

玛吉看了挪威渔业的照片，又看了黄石公园的照片，又翻了一份《生活》（*Life*）杂志，笑话读了一条又一条，一个字也没进脑子。哎呀，天不早了！她狐疑地瞪着新来的人——万一一不留神叫他们抢到前头去了呢！照这么磨蹭，能赶上三点四十那班电车、连口吃的都不停下来买，就算她走运了。

接着就轮到她了——到底是太快了！太快了！她拎起菜篮子，走进亨德森医生的诊室。

---

===Original===
Lily had a sore throat and had spent the night with a stocking-compress around it. But she was still as hoarse as a crow in the morning, and dreadfully dejected griddlecakes for breakfast, and it hurt her too much to swallow them! So Maggie told her to stay home from church, and she would put the flowers on the altar.

She walked along the road to the church, the great sheaf of white cosmos jutting out at one side, wagging with every step.

She had cancer, and it was too late to operate. That was his verdict, stripped of the kindness and encouragement he had wrapped it in. Cancer﻿—too late to operate﻿—

===Chinese===
莉莉嗓子疼，脖子上箍着长袜扎的敷布熬了一夜。可早晨起来还是哑得像只乌鸦，早餐的软饼也烙得垂头丧气，她咽一口都疼得受不了！于是玛吉叫她待在家里别上教堂，鲜花由她来摆上圣坛。

她顺着大路往教堂走，一大捧白波斯菊从身侧戳出来，一步一晃。

她得的是癌，已经迟了，不能开刀了。这就是他的诊断，剥去他裹在外头的宽慰与鼓励，剩下的就是这么一句。癌——迟了，不能开刀了——

===Original===
What pretty single dahlias in the Worthingtons’ garden﻿—the color of raspberries and candlelight. Not much like the dahlias she used to grow at The Maples painted tin rosettes﻿—

That meant she was dying. How queer! Maggie Campion dying! But you weren’t dying when you were walking to church in your best voile dress; when you bothered to put on your best stockings, silk to just below the knees, because it was Sunday; when you got up earlier than anyone else in the house, and started the fire and made griddlecakes for breakfast.

Dying﻿—dying. The word repeated itself to her, monotonous as the beat of waves.

> “Blow, bugle, blow, set the wild echoes flying,
 

 Blow, bugle; answer, echoes, dying, dying, dying.”

===Chinese===
沃辛顿家（the Worthingtons）园子里那些单瓣大丽花多好看呀——覆盆子掺烛光的颜色。可不大像她从前在枫树庄（The Maples）种的那一路——一朵朵漆过的铁皮玫瑰花——

这就是说，她快死了。多古怪呀！玛吉·坎皮恩（Maggie Campion）要死了！可是穿着自己顶好的巴里纱裙子往教堂走的时候，不算快死；因为是礼拜天，肯费事换上顶好的长袜——丝袜齐到膝盖下面一点——的时候，不算快死；比全家谁都起得早、生了火、烙了早饭的软饼的时候，也不算快死呀。

垂死——垂死。这个字眼儿在她心里自行反复，单调得像波浪一记一记的拍打。

> 「吹吧，号角，吹吧，送野性的回声飞扬，
> 吹吧，号角；应答吧，回声，垂死，垂死，垂死。」

===Original===
The Tennyson tableaux﻿—so long ago. Edward in a tam-o’-shanter trimmed with his Aunt Jo’s plume﻿—May in silvery white, holding the silvery lilies﻿—poor Aunt Priscilla’s blue tail﻿—Victor teasing May about Edward﻿—Edward﻿—

There went the Willie Trewhitts all packed into their new Ford coupé﻿—not going towards church, either. She nodded severely, and Willie, who had been in her Sunday school class, reddened and looked like an embarrassed little boy in spite of being a married man and father of a family.

Communion Sunday. She went into the musty little robing-room with its piles of dusty prayer books, its framed yellowed photograph of old Mr. Page in his vestments, and got out the white hangings. The cosmos were almost lost in their feathery foliage﻿—not that many people would be there to see them. Everybody went automobiling on Sundays nowadays, or stayed at home and read the Sunday papers.

She brushed the fallen petals into the newspaper she had wrapped around the wet stems, pausing to read an advertisement for a marked-down sale of overcoats at Gimbels’﻿—Victor needed an overcoat badly. She shook the petals out of the chancel window, put the folded newspaper under her pew, and knelt, thinking of nothing. Through the service she stood up, sat down, knelt, not thinking, not feeling, numb.

===Chinese===
那一场丁尼生（Tennyson）活人画——多久远的事了。戴苏格兰圆帽的爱德华（Edward），帽上镶着乔姨妈（Aunt Jo）那根翎毛——梅一身银白，捧着银白的百合——可怜的普丽西拉婶婶那条蓝尾巴——维克托拿爱德华打趣梅——爱德华——

瞧，威利·特雷维特（Willie Trewhitt）一家子挤在他们那辆新福特双门小车里驶了过去——去的还不是教堂的方向。她板着脸点了点头；当过她主日学学生的威利霎时红了脸，活像个害臊的小男生，全不像个成了家、儿女成行的大男人。

这天是圣餐主日。她走进那间霉味儿扑鼻的小圣衣室——里头堆着一摞摞落灰的祈祷书，挂着老佩奇先生（Mr. Page）穿圣衣的泛黄镶框照片——取出白色的帷幔。波斯菊几乎没入自己羽状的叶丛——反正也不会有几个人来看。如今一到礼拜天，人人不是开车兜风去，就是窝在家里读星期天的报纸。

她把落下的花瓣扫进裹花茎的湿报纸里，中途停了停，读一条吉姆贝尔斯百货（Gimbels'）大衣减价的广告——维克托正缺一件大衣呢。她把花瓣从圣坛的窗子抖出去，折好的报纸塞到自己长椅底下，跪了下来，什么也没想。整个礼拜式中她起立、坐下、跪倒，不想，不感，麻木不仁。

===Original===
> “Bread of the world, in mercy broken,
 

 Wine of the soul, in mercy shed,
 

 By whom the words of life were spoken,
 

 And in whose death our sins are dead.
 

 

 “Look on the heart by sorrow broken,
 

 Look on the tears by sinners shed;﻿—”

And suddenly the beating waves crashed through, drenching her with realization, with terror, submerging her. Anguish and death﻿—

“I can’t bear it! I can’t bear it! I don’t want to die!”

And the answer came﻿—did her ears hear it, or her heart? She didn’t know.

“This is the bread which cometh down from heaven, that a man may eat thereof, and not die.”

===Chinese===
> 「世上的粮，怜悯中擘开，
> 灵魂的酒，怜悯中倾流，
> 生命之言凭他启口，
> 我众罪愆随他命休。
>
> 「垂顾这忧伤破碎的心，
> 垂顾罪人洒落的泪；——」

猛然间，那阵阵拍击的浪涛轰然决堤而过，把她浇了个透——浇来的是醒悟，是恐怖，把她整个淹没。痛苦与死亡——

「我受不住！我受不住！我不想死啊！」

回答来了——是耳朵听见的，还是心听见的？她不知道。

「这就是从天上降下来的粮，叫人吃了就不死。」

===Original===
> “Bread of the world, in mercy broken,
 

 Wine of the soul﻿—”

He had died, so that she need never die. He had promised!

“I am the bread of life: he that cometh to me shall never hunger; and he that believeth on me shall never thirst.” The bread of the body, the wine of the soul. The wheat and grapes had had their roots in the earth, but they had grown up towards heaven. The white bread, the red wine; white as snow and red as fire﻿—snow and fire, winter and summer, death and life, opposite and yet the same, part of eternity’s circle.

But before the wheat becomes bread it must be ground, before the grapes become wine they must be crushed. They must be sacrificed, as Christ on the Cross was sacrificed.

Winter must come, and death. But the seed lives, and rises again from the dark earth, from the grave. The only way to life is through the door of death.

She knelt, hiding her streaming face in her arms, shaken by terrible weeping. Her legs were too weak to stand, but the Son of God, the Son of Man, came down to her from his altar, bringing her his divine gift of terror and beauty, his gift of sacrifice. And as she accepted his gift he entered into her, and she into him, he was everything, the bread, the wine, the sun in the sky, the dust that danced in the sunbeams, her tear-soaked handkerchief. “Lo, I am with you alway﻿—”

===Chinese===
> 「世上的粮，怜悯中擘开，
> 灵魂的酒——」

他死过了，好叫她永远不必死。他许诺过！

「我就是生命的粮。到我这里来的，必定不饿；信我的，永远不渴。」身体的饼，灵魂的酒。麦子和葡萄的根扎在土里，茎秆却向上朝着天空生长。白的饼，红的酒；白如雪，红如火——雪与火，冬与夏，死与生，相对却相同，同是永恒圆环上的一环。

可麦子要先磨成粉，才能成饼；葡萄要先碾碎，才能成酒。它们必须被献祭，正如基督在十字架上被献祭。

冬天总要来的，死亡也总要来的。可种子活着，会从黑暗的泥土里、从坟墓里重新升起来。通往生命的路，只有一扇门，那就是死。

她跪在那里，把涕泪横流的脸埋进臂弯，一阵阵可怕的呜咽摇撼着她。她的两腿软得站不住了，可是神的儿子、人的儿子，从他的圣坛上降临到她身边，把他神圣的礼物——那又可畏又美的礼物，那牺牲的礼物——递给她。她领受他的礼物时，他进入了她，她也进入了他；他是一切，是饼，是酒，是天上的太阳，是在日光里飞舞的微尘，是她那块泪水浸透的手帕。「看哪，我常与你们同在——」

## XXXI / XXXI

===Original===

“*Where* you going, Maggie?”

Lily squatted back on her heels. A small limp pancake of homemade raffia hat, trimmed with rosettes of red raffia topped her mild apple face, every hair pin seemed about to spring from her hair, she bristled with them like a hedgehog. She was digging the holes for the new tulip bulbs﻿—the bulbs of the red and yellow parrot tulips, all feathered and fringed, that Mrs. Detweiler had sent from her garden.

“Heigho for meddlers!”

That was all Maggie would say. Where *could* she be going, in her best voile, and with her card-case? “I think it’s real mean in you not to tell me!” Lily called after her placidly.

It was quite a relief to have her go out, she had been so cross and brisk for the last week or two, cleaning the house, putting up preserves﻿—what had gotten into her? “I think I’ll go and have a little lie-down while she’s gone,” Lily said to herself, scrambling to her feet. She found the new *Ladies’ Home Journal*, tucked a lump of sugar into her cheek, and lowered herself onto the sofa. Oh, how nice to be able to rest and enjoy herself without feeling guilty, as she did when Maggie was dragging the furniture around or struggling to cut up quinces.

===Chinese===

「你*上哪儿*去呀，玛吉？」

莉莉往后蹲坐到脚跟上。她那顶自家编的拉菲草帽软塌塌的，像块小煎饼，缀着一朵朵红拉菲草的玫瑰花结，扣在她那温顺的苹果脸顶上；满头发夹一根根都像要蹦出来，她顶着一头小刺，活像只刺猬。她正在给新到的郁金香球茎挖坑——德特韦勒太太（Mrs. Detweiler）从自家花园里送来的红黄两色鹦鹉郁金香的球茎，那一路花瓣羽毛般重重、流苏般簇簇的。

「嘿，爱管闲事的家伙万岁！」

玛吉只肯说这一句。穿着顶好的巴里纱裙子，带着名片盒，她*究竟*能上哪儿去呢？「你不告诉我，可真小气！」莉莉在她背后慢悠悠地喊。

她出了门，莉莉倒着实松了口气——这一两个礼拜，玛吉脾气又坏、手脚又急，又是打扫屋子，又是封罐存果酱——她这是着了什么魔了？「趁她不在，我去躺一小会儿吧。」莉莉对自己说，手脚并用地爬起身来。她找来新一期的《女士家庭杂志》（*Ladies' Home Journal*），往腮帮子里塞了块方糖，在沙发上落了座。哦，能这样歇着受用、心里又不觉得亏欠，多好呀——每逢玛吉拖着家具挪来挪去、或是较着劲儿切榅桲的时候，她可是要觉得亏欠的。

===Original===

Mrs. Spear was in the midst of a rubber of bridge when the maid brought in Maggie’s calling card, ivory with age. “Oh, bother!” she said. “Still, I suppose I’ll have to see the poor old thing.” And she called to her daughter, who was sprawling on the sofa reading “Town Topics” and wishing they’d have more about Wilmington people in it, “Take my hand, Dot.”

Maggie had come to say goodbye to The Maples while she could. She knew the time was growing short. The maid had prudently hooked the screen door, leaving the shabby stranger outside in case she was just trying to sell something, but it hadn’t been like being locked out of her own home. Everything was so changed. The fountain gone, the peony beds gone, the grey house painted white, with awnings of orange Italian sailcloth. “Pity’s sake!” thought Maggie as she waited. “I don’t think much of their wonderful improvements!” Homesick for The Maples, on hot days remembering it veiled in snow, on cold days aching for it gilded with sunshine, netted down with shadows, she had felt that she would know a leaf from home, out of all the other leaves in the world. And now here was home itself, and it was strange to her.

This Mamma’s parlor? This room full of sleek rich-looking women, white terriers with green leather collars, magazines, open candy boxes, cigarette smoke, noise﻿—

===Chinese===

斯皮尔太太（Mrs. Spear）正打到一盘桥牌的半中间，女仆进来递上玛吉的名片——名片年深日久，已泛成象牙黄色。「哎，真麻烦！」她说。「不过这可怜的老家伙，我想总得见一见。」说着她朝女儿喊了一声——女儿正摊手摊脚躺在沙发上看《城中话题》（Town Topics），直盼着里头多登些威尔明顿人的动静——「多蒂（Dot），接我的牌。」

玛吉这是趁还走得动，回来向枫树庄（The Maples）道别的。她知道时日无多了。女仆很有心计地把纱门的搭钩挂好，把这个寒酸的外人撂在门外，防着她不过是来兜售什么货色的；可她心里，并不觉得像是被人锁在了自家门外。一切都变了样。喷泉没了，牡丹花坛没了，灰房子刷成了白色，搭着橙红色意大利帆布的遮阳篷。「老天爷！」玛吉一边等一边想。「他们那些了不得的改良，我可不敢恭维！」她害着对枫树庄的相思病：热天里，惦记它蒙着白雪的模样；冷天里，渴望它镀满阳光、笼着网一般的荫翳。她一向觉得，普天下的叶子千千万万，哪一片是家里长的，她都认得出。可如今家本身就在眼前，她却觉得它陌生。

这是妈妈的客厅？这满屋子衣着光鲜的阔太太、戴绿皮项圈的白梗犬、杂志、敞开的糖果盒、香烟的烟气、喧闹——

===Original===

“I know Miss Campion wants to see her garden. Miss Campion’s the most *marvellous* gardener, I’m petrified to have her see all the mistakes I *know* we’ve made. Of course, I needn’t tell *you* that at this time of the year﻿—oh, Simpson! Turn on the fountain for Miss Campion, will you? And I wonder if you could cut us a few flowers?” She added aside, “I’m terrified of my gardener! I wouldn’t dare pick a flower without his permission.”

“No, no! Stop him! No, don’t pick me any﻿—I have to go. Thank you just the same. Don’t come with me﻿—I’ll cut across﻿—”

She was desperate to get away. “What’s the matter with the crazy old thing?” Violet Spear wondered. Keeping away for twelve years, and then acting like this. And not a single word about all the improvements. Not much like her brother, who was almost painfully polite when he came to dine or to play bridge. But she did look sick﻿—such a color, and so thin that her clothes hung on her as if they were hanging from the wooden shoulders of a coat-hanger. She almost asked her to stay to tea﻿—then she thought irritably, “No, if she’s so crazy to go, let her!” She was a kind woman, but her new rubber reducing corset wasn’t by any means the dream of comfort she had expected, her satin slippers were soaking from the wet grass, and she felt that little muffled beat in her temple that meant one of her bad headaches was coming.

Maggie hurried up the lawn, straining towards escape. The beech tree was the same, anyway. And as she looked at it the mist of strangeness lifted, blew away, she saw her home again. Nothing was changed, really. Nothing was lost. Childhood’s sky arched up from childhood’s river, exquisitely reassuring. Small, chunky sunset clouds filled the west, cobblestones from the golden streets. Red leaves fell about her in a sudden shower﻿—the red of the wine and the blood of sacrifice. They were falling back to the earth from which they had sprung, making ready for winter’s death﻿—and yet nothing died, nothing! In the spring the buds would swell again.

===Chinese===

「我知道坎皮恩小姐是想看看她的花园。坎皮恩小姐是位*顶了不得*的园艺家，我们园里那些我*明知*犯下的错处，要给她看了去，我都吓僵了。当然，这个时节的光景，用不着我来告诉*您*——哦，辛普森（Simpson）！替坎皮恩小姐把喷泉打开，好吗？再劳驾给我们剪几枝花来，行不行？」她又侧过身去加了一句：「我怕我那园丁怕得要命！没他点头，我连一朵花也不敢摘。」

「不，不！别让他开！不用给我剪——我该走了。还是多谢。别送我——我抄近道——」

她只想赶快脱身。「这个怪老婆子到底是怎么了？」薇奥莱特·斯皮尔（Violet Spear）心里直纳闷。一走十二年不登门，来了又是这么个做派。人家这么些改良，她一句好话也没有。半点不像她弟弟——弟弟来吃饭、来打桥牌，客气得几乎叫人吃不消。不过她瞧着的确像有病——那样一副脸色，瘦得衣服挂在身上，活像挂在木头衣架那两条肩膀上。她险些开口留她喝茶——转念又没好气地想：「不留！她这么急着要走，由她去！」她本是位好心肠的太太，只是新买的橡胶减肥紧身褡穿在身上，全然不是她指望的那场舒舒服服的美梦，缎面便鞋叫湿草浸了个透湿，太阳穴里又一下一下闷闷地跳——那是她的偏头风又要发作了。

玛吉匆匆走上草坪，一门心思只想着逃出去。好在那棵山毛榉还是老样子。她望着它，那层陌生感的薄雾升起来、飘散了，她又看见了自己的家。其实什么都没有变。什么都不曾失去。童年的天空从童年的河上拱起，妥帖得叫人心安。小朵小朵敦实的晚霞云挤满了西天，像金街上的鹅卵石。红叶忽然一阵急雨，洒落在她四周——那是酒的红、祭血的红。它们正落回自己所从出的泥土，为冬天的死亡做着安排——然而没有谁真会死，没有！到了春天，芽苞自会再鼓起来。

---

===Original===

“Lily!”

But Lily’s light whistling snores went on peacefully, so Maggie dragged herself out of bed, and downstairs to the pantry, for some cracked ice.

They had Lossie’s daughter Rose to do the cooking now that Maggie had to stay in bed. And her beau was there again﻿—there was a light in the kitchen shining through a haze of tobacco smoke. Rose’s alarm clock, waiting on a pantry chair to be taken up to bed, held up its black hands in horror at the lateness of the hour. “He ought to have gone home ever so long ago!” it said.

It was a panicky moment for the young ones. No mice were ever more still, and then﻿—*crash!* One of the mice knocked something over with its tail. “I ought to speak to them,” Maggie thought, getting her ice. But she put out the pantry light and climbed up the stairs.

===Chinese===

「莉莉！」

可莉莉那轻悠悠、吹哨似的鼾声只管安安稳稳地响着，于是玛吉撑着自己爬下床，下楼到配餐室去，弄点碎冰。

玛吉既不得不卧床，家里便雇了洛西（Lossie）的女儿罗斯（Rose）来做饭。她的情郎又来了——厨房里亮着灯，光透过一团烟草的青雾照出来。罗斯的闹钟搁在配餐室的椅子上，等着被带上楼去睡觉，它惊恐地举起两只黑指针，指着这个过晚的时辰。「他老早老早就该回家了！」它说。

这对年轻人一时慌了神。再没有比他们更安静的老鼠了，紧接着——*哗啦！*一只「老鼠」用尾巴扫倒了什么东西。「我该说说他们才是。」玛吉想，一面取着冰。可她还是熄了配餐室的灯，爬上楼去了。

===Original===

The trained nurse was coming in the morning. Maggie had had to give up fighting against having her. Long before Lily was awake, she was up tidying her room, making the bed with fresh sheets, putting on her best nightgown, getting ready to be nursed. Lily had tried hard, but things were in an awful muddle. From time to time Maggie stopped, crouched in a knot of pain, her wet forehead pressed against the marble slab of the bureau. Then she straightened herself, gathered up sticky medicine spoons with her nose wrinkling in disgust, fished the wrapped circles of combings from the wastebasket, went to the linen closet for a fresh towel to cover her bedside table, before she climbed back into bed and gave herself up to the flame of anguish that was consuming her.

It was better after Miss McMurtrie came. Sometimes Maggie was given something to stop the pain, and she could smile at Lily’s and Victor’s scared, solemn faces that stretched into wide answering smiles when they saw she was looking at them.

“Kind of nice to lie here in a nice warm bed and not have to do anything, ain’t it?” she asked them. Nice not to have the alarm clock slash across her sleep, not to have to stagger up into the cold dark mornings, not to have to keep going when her legs felt like butter in the sun, nice to lie watching the gently falling snow. Oh, if she could only make herself believe that it was!

She wasn’t afraid of dying any more, for herself, but how could she leave the children?

===Chinese===

职业护士明天一早就要来了。玛吉只得认输，不再抵制请护士这件事。天还没亮、莉莉还没醒，她就已经起身收拾屋子，铺上干净的床单，换上自己最好的睡衣，一切备妥，单等人家来护理。莉莉已经很尽力了，可屋里还是乱得一塌糊涂。玛吉不时停下来，疼得蜷作一团，汗湿的额头抵着五斗橱的大理石台面。缓过劲来，她又直起身子，皱着鼻子、一脸嫌恶地拾掇起黏糊糊的药匙，从废纸篓里捞出包成一小卷一小卷的落发，再到放床品的壁橱取条干净毛巾盖在床头小几上，这才爬回床上，把自己交付给那团正吞噬着她的痛苦的烈焰。

麦克默特里小姐（Miss McMurtrie）来了以后，情形好些了。有时给玛吉用上一点止痛的东西，她便能朝莉莉和维克托那两张惶惶的、绷着的小脸微笑；那两张脸见她望着自己，便也舒展成大大的笑脸来回应。

「躺在暖暖和和的好床上，什么也不必干，也挺美的，是不是？」她问他们。闹钟不再一刀斩断她的睡眠，多好；不必再挣扎着爬进又冷又黑的清晨，多好；两腿软得像太阳地里的黄油时也不必硬撑着往前赶，多好；躺着看雪花轻轻飘落，多好。唉，她要真能叫自己相信这是真的就好了！

她不再怕死了——就她自己而言，是不怕了；可是这几个孩子，她怎么撇得下？

===Original===

The love that had lighted her life shone for her as she looked back through the years﻿—Edward﻿—Papa﻿—Victor. Memories came like the great silver bubbles that waver slowly up from the dark depths of a spring. Going for chestnuts with Edward when the air smelt of frost and the dead grasses were gold and silver﻿—there was a film of ice over the fallen leaves in the shadowy places, the small green hedgehogs of burs spilled out their brown satin nuts. Everywhere was the feeling of hidden life﻿—warm furry little bodies, bright eyes, and pattering feet. And suddenly he and she were dropping their baskets, rushing into each other’s arms, not able to stay apart another instant﻿—

Papa lifting her up to ride in front of him on his horse﻿—

“I won’t let you fall, Muggins.”

“Pooh! I’m not a bit scared! I could ride Gipsy bareback, Papa! I could ride standing up, if you’d let me!”

She and the children in the spring woods, where the dogwood trees in blossom floated like wreaths of cloud. She was trying to get unsteady little Victor across the stream on the wobbly stepping stones﻿—*splash!* In they both went, while May and Lily screamed with excitement, and Trusty barked fit to kill himself﻿—

And then she was holding the baby close to her heart. Mamma had given him to her wrapped in shawls. “Be careful of Baby, Maggie﻿—”

===Chinese===

她回首流年，那照亮了她一生的爱又为她亮起来——爱德华（Edward）——爸爸——维克托。记忆浮上心头，像大颗大颗的银色气泡，从幽深的泉底慢慢摇曳着升上来。和爱德华去捡栗子——空气里有霜的味道，枯草一片金、一片银——背阴处的落叶上覆着一层薄冰，一只只绿刺猬似的栗蓬，摊出它们棕色缎子的坚果。四下里到处是藏着的生命的气息——毛茸茸暖乎乎的小身子，亮晶晶的眼睛，嗒嗒轻响的小脚。忽然间，他和她扔下了篮子，奔进彼此的怀抱，一刻也不能再分开——

爸爸把她抱上马，让她坐在自己身前——

「我不会让你摔下来的，小傻瓜（Muggins）。」

「呸！我一点儿也不怕！不用鞍子我也能骑吉普赛（Gipsy），爸爸！你要是肯，我站着骑都行！」

她和孩子们在春天的树林里，满树开花的山茱萸，像一环环云朵浮着。她正扶着脚步不稳的小维克托，踩着摇摇晃晃的踏脚石过溪——*扑通！*两个人一齐栽了进去，梅和莉莉兴奋得尖声大叫，特拉斯蒂吠得简直要把自己吠死——

再后来，她把那个婴儿紧紧搂在心口。妈妈把他裹在披巾里，交到她手上。「小心宝宝，玛吉——」

===Original===

“Miss Lily! Miss Lily!”

“She’s just gone over to the store, Miss McMurtrie.”

“I think you’d better come right away, Mr. Campion﻿—”

He ran upstairs, his heart knocking against his side, and tiptoed into Maggie’s room. And something tore him, made him fling himself down by her bed, crying, “Maggie, don’t die! Don’t die!” But for the first time in his life she did not answer him.

===Chinese===

「莉莉小姐！莉莉小姐！」

「她刚去铺子了，麦克默特里小姐。」

「坎皮恩先生，我想您最好马上过来——」

他跑上楼来，心咚咚地撞着肋骨，蹑手蹑脚走进玛吉的房间。有什么东西把他撕开了，他扑倒在她床边，喊道：「玛吉，别死！别死啊！」可是生平头一次，她没有回答他。

## XXXII / XXXII

===Original===

Lily loved the Sunday papers. Lying on the sofa covered like an elderly Babe in the Wood with papers instead of leaves, she read every word about Queen Mary’s toques and Princess Mary Viscountess Lascelle’s baby, how to arrange salad in green pepper canoes, whether skirts were going to be long or short, and what “Doug and Mary” were doing at the moment. She read selected poems, and often cut them out raggedly with a hair pin and lost them down the crack of the sofa. She looked at the funny pictures and made baffled tries at the puzzles, like a moth bumping softly against a window pane. She read anything about Harvard, in the sports section, on account of Papa and Victor, and looked at pictures of football heroes, thinking how their mothers must worry. She read special articles about chorus girls winning the mystic love of Hindu Swamis. And she always read straight through the society columns. In fact she read everything in the newspapers except the news.

So it was she who discovered that Lucy Hawthorn was back in America. It was quite a shock to come upon the name of someone who was a real person to her, among all the well known but unseen Vanderbilts and Whitneys and Astors. “Countess de la Villeblanche, who will be remembered as Lucy Hawthorn﻿—”

Her first impulse was not to tell Victor. Lucy home again and a widow﻿—she’d be certain to grab him if he gave her half a chance! And she saw herself alone and old. Her better nature triumphed in a minute﻿—besides, she would burst if she kept such an exciting bit of news untold. But it would be just as well to have things especially pleasant when she told him. Somehow, though she tried so hard, home wasn’t the same without Maggie.

He was having Sunday supper somewhere in Wilmington. She would sit up for him, and make some candy to have for a surprise when he came in.

===Chinese===

莉莉顶爱星期天的报纸。她躺在沙发上，周身盖满报纸，活像个上了年纪的「林中稚子」（Babe in the Wood）——只是盖的不是树叶，是报纸。玛丽王后（Queen Mary）的各款无檐小帽、玛丽公主——拉塞尔斯子爵夫人（Princess Mary Viscountess Lascelle）——的小宝宝、怎样把沙拉摆进青椒独木舟里、裙子往后是行长还是行短、「道格和玛丽」（Doug and Mary）眼下的一举一动，她一个字一个字地全读了。选登的诗她也读，还常用发夹毛毛糙糙地把诗剜下来，末了掉进沙发缝里没了踪影。她看滑稽画，对猜谜游戏懵里懵懂地试上几回，活像只轻扑窗玻璃的飞蛾。体育版里凡是哈佛的消息，为着爸爸和维克托的缘故，她一条不落；她端详橄榄球健将们的照片，心里想他们的母亲该怎样牵肠挂肚。她读特稿，读歌舞队姑娘怎样赢得印度教斯瓦米大师（Hindu Swamis）的神秘爱情。社交栏更是回回从头直读到尾。其实，报纸上的东西她样样都读——唯独新闻例外。

于是，露西·霍桑（Lucy Hawthorn）回美国来了这个消息，头一个发现的便是她。在那满纸赫赫有名、却素未谋面的范德比尔特家、惠特尼家、阿斯特家（the Vanderbilts and Whitneys and Astors）中间，猛可里撞见一个在自己算是活生生真人的名字，着实叫她吃了一惊。「德·拉·维尔布朗什伯爵夫人——诸位想必还记得，她就是当年的露西·霍桑——」

她头一个冲动，是不告诉维克托。露西回了国，又是个寡妇——只要他给她半点机会，她准保一把把他抓住不放！她看见自己孤零零地老去。不过她那点更好的天性转眼便打了胜仗——再说，这么叫人兴奋的一条新闻闷在肚里不说，她可要憋破了。只是告诉他的当口，顶好把家里安排得格外妥帖些。不知怎么，任凭她怎样费劲，家里没有了玛吉，到底不是原先那个家了。

他这会儿正在威尔明顿城里哪一家吃星期天的晚饭。她要坐着等他回来，再做点糖果，好在他进门时给他个惊喜。


===Original===

Out in the kitchen she and a little mouse gave each other a good scare﻿—she jumped so that she knocked over the vanilla bottle. Oh dear! Tears came to her eyes. But she got up quite a lot of vanilla with a spoon.

It was so still that she could hear the tinkle of falling icicles, pure and exquisite sound. Alone in the house at night. It was sort of scary when you let yourself think about it.

Alone in the house. And she and Victor weren’t as young as they used to be. Suppose﻿—something should happen to one of them? What would the other do? Alone in the house, alone in the world.

They said it was a sign of getting old when time slipped past faster and faster. It made her dizzy, the world was spinning around so these days, spinning from blue and gold to black and silver, so fast that not a drop was spilled from the rivers and seas, not a pot of flowers fell from a window sill, not a bird’s egg fell from its nest. Standing in the quiet kitchen that suddenly opened out vast and unfamiliar, she felt herself clinging head down to the whirling globe that would some day spin her off altogether.

In a panic she began to clatter pots and spoons, to sing a hymn they had sung when they were children.

===Chinese===

外头厨房里，她和一只小老鼠结结实实互吓了一跳——她一蹦，把香草精瓶儿撞翻了。哎呀！眼泪都涌上来了。可她还是拿汤匙，把洒了的香草精舀回来不少。

四下里静极了，静得她听得见冰锥坠地的叮咚声，又纯净，又清脆。夜里，独自守着一座房子。你要是放任自己去想，还真有几分吓人。

独自守着一座房子。她和维克托又不比从前年轻了。万一——他们俩哪一个出了什么事呢？剩下那一个可怎么办？独自守着一座房子，独自在这世上。

人家说，日子溜过去一天快似一天，便是人上了年纪的兆头。她直发晕——如今这世界转得多快呀，由蓝与金旋入黑与银，转得那样急，江河湖海竟洒不出一滴水，窗台上落不下一盆花，鸟巢里跌不出一颗蛋。她站在静悄悄的厨房里，厨房忽然向四下里敞开来，空旷而陌生；她只觉得自己头朝下攀在这旋转的地球上，总有一天，会教它整个儿甩出去。

她一时惊慌，便把锅子汤匙弄得叮当乱响，唱起一支他们小时候唱过的赞美诗来。


===Original===

> > “ ‘Jesus, tender Shepherd, hear me;

> Bless Thy little lamb tonight;

> Through the darkness be Thou near me﻿—’ ”
